						海沃德（Hayward），接杀者沃利（Wooley），投杀者卡尔（Carr）
						67
						
						
						霍布斯（Hobbs），跑动出局
						0
						
						
						海斯（Hayes），截杀者休伊什（Huish），投杀者菲尔德（Fielder）
						12
						
						
						杜卡特（Ducat），投杀者菲尔德（Fielder）
						33
						
						
						哈里森（Harrison），未出局
						11
						
						
						桑德姆（Sandham），未出局
						6
						
						
						额外分
						10
						
						
						总计（四人出局）
						139
						
					
					
				

贝利斯恭恭敬敬地审视起这份天书。

「您要我解释的是哪一处，先生？」

「嗐，全部。这到底说的是什么？」

===Original===
“It’s perfectly simple, sir. Surrey won the toss, and took first knock. Hayward and Hobbs were the opening pair. Hayward called Hobbs for a short run, but the latter was unable to get across and was thrown out by mid-on. Hayes was the next man in. He went out of his ground and was stumped. Ducat and Hayward made a capital stand considering the stickiness of the wicket, until Ducat was bowled by a good length off-break and Hayward caught at second slip off a googly. Then Harrison and Sandham played out time.”

Mr. Crocker breathed heavily through his nose.

“Yes!” he said. “Yes! I had an idea that was it. But I think I’d like to have it once again, slowly. Start with these figures. What does that sixty-seven mean, opposite Hayward’s name?”

“He made sixty-seven runs, sir.”

“Sixty-seven! In one game?”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
「简单得很，先生。萨里掷币获胜，先攻。海沃德和霍布斯打头阵。海沃德喊霍布斯跑个短打，后者没来得及赶过去，被中路野手传杀出局。下一个上的是海斯。他出了击球线，被截杀。杜卡特和海沃德顾念着球道发黏，打出一段漂亮的搭档，直到杜卡特被一记落点极刁的内转折球投杀，海沃德又在二号截击位吃了一记 googly 变向球被接杀。此后哈里森和桑德姆便磨光了时辰。」

克罗克先生从鼻孔里沉重地呼了口气。

「好！」他说。「好！我原先就琢磨着是这么回事。不过我想劳你驾再慢慢来上一遍。先从这些数儿说起。海沃德名字对面那个六十七，是什么讲究？」

「他得了六十七分，先生。」

「六十七分！一场里？」

「是的，先生。」

===Original===
“Why, Home-Run Baker couldn’t do it!”

“I am not familiar with Mr. Baker, sir.”

“I suppose you’ve never seen a ballgame?”

“Ballgame, sir?”

“A baseball game?”

“Never, sir.”

===Chinese===
「嚯，『本垒打』贝克（Home-Run Baker）也办不到啊！」

「贝克先生其人，我不熟，先生。」

「我估摸你压根儿没瞧过球赛？」

「球赛，先生？」

「棒球赛？」

「从来没有，先生。」

===Original===
“Then, Bill,” said Mr. Crocker, reverting in his emotion to the bad habit of his early London days, “you haven’t lived. See here!”

Whatever vestige of respect for class distinctions Mr. Crocker had managed to preserve during the opening stages of the interview now definitely disappeared. His eyes shone wildly and he snorted like a warhorse. He clutched the butler by the sleeve and drew him closer to the table, then began to move forks, spoons, cups, and even the contents of his plate about the cloth with an energy little short of feverish.

“Bayliss!”

“Sir?”

“Watch!” said Mr. Crocker, with the air of an excitable high priest about to initiate a novice into the Mysteries.

He removed a roll from the basket.

===Chinese===
「那么，比尔，」克罗克先生说——情急之下，他又犯起了初到伦敦那些日子的老毛病——「你算是白活了。瞧着！」

会谈开场阶段克罗克先生好不容易维持下来的那一点阶级体统，至此彻底报销。他两眼放光，直冒野火，像匹战马似的打着响鼻。他一把攥住管家的衣袖，把他拽近桌边，随即开始在桌布上调度刀叉、汤匙、茶杯，连自己盘中的吃食也一并调度，那股热劲只差一点点就赶上热病了。

「贝利斯！」

「先生？」

「看好！」克罗克先生说，活像一位亢奋的大祭司，正要领一位新手入门，传授奥秘。

他从篮子里取出一枚小圆面包。

===Original===
“You see this roll? That’s the home plate. This spoon is first base. Where I’m putting this cup is second. This piece of bacon is third. There’s your diamond for you. Very well, then. These lumps of sugar are the infielders and the outfielders. Now we’re ready. Batter up? He stands here. Catcher behind him. Umps behind catcher.”

“Umps, I take it, sir, is what we would call the umpire?”

“Call him anything you like. It’s part of the game. Now here’s the box, where I’ve put this dab of marmalade, and here’s the pitcher, winding up.”

“The pitcher would be equivalent to our bowler?”

“I guess so, though why you should call him a bowler gets past me.”

“The box, then, is the bowler’s wicket?”

===Chinese===
「瞧见这枚面包没有？这是本垒板。这把汤匙是一垒。我搁这只杯子的地儿是二垒。这片培根是三垒。喏，你的内场这就成了。很好。这几块方糖，就是内野手和外野手。齐活，可以开张了。击球手上场？他站这儿。捕手蹲他后头。老裁蹲捕手后头。」

「『老裁』，据我理解，先生，就是我们所说的裁判（umpire）？」

「爱怎么叫怎么叫。这也是比赛的一部分。喏，这块是投手区，我抹了这一坨橘子酱的地方，这位是投手，正要绕臂。」

「投手想必就相当于我们的 bowler（投球员）了？」

「大概是吧，不过你们干嘛偏偏管他叫 bowler，我可就纳闷了。」

「那么投手区，便是 bowler 的球道（wicket）了？」

===Original===
“Have it your own way. Now pay attention. Play ball! Pitcher’s winding up. Put it over, Mike, put it over! Some speed, kid! Here it comes, right in the groove. Bing! Batter slams it and streaks for first. Outfielder—this lump of sugar—boots it. Bonehead! Batter touches second. Third? No! Get back! Can’t be done. Play it safe. Stick around the sack, old pal. Second batter up. Pitcher getting something on the ball now besides the cover. Whiffs him. Back to the bench, Cyril! Third batter up. See him rub his hands in the dirt. Watch this kid. He’s good! He lets two alone, then slams the next right on the nose. Whizzes around to second. First guy, the one we left on second, comes home for one run. That’s a game! Take it from me, Bill, that’s a *game*!”

Somewhat overcome with the energy with which he had flung himself into his lecture, Mr. Crocker sat down and refreshed himself with cold coffee.

“Quite an interesting game,” said Bayliss. “But I find, now that you have explained it, sir, that it is familiar to me, though I have always known it under another name. It is played a great deal in this country.”

Mr. Crocker started to his feet.

“It is? And I’ve been five years here without finding it out! When’s the next game scheduled?”

“It is known in England as Rounders, sir. Children play it with a soft ball and a racquet, and derive considerable enjoyment from it. I had never heard of it before as a pastime for adults.”

===Chinese===
「随你怎么说。现在专心听着。开球！投手绕臂了。投过去呀迈克，投过去呀！来点速度，小子！球来啦，正钻好球带。砰！击球手猛捶一记，蹽向一垒。外野手——这块方糖——漏接了。榆木脑袋！击球手踩上二垒。三垒？不行！回去！没门儿。求稳要紧。守着垒包别挪窝，老伙计。第二位击球手上场。投手这回投的球总算不光是层皮儿了。三振！回板凳去吧，西里尔！第三位击球手上场。瞧他往土里搓手。盯紧这小子。他是块料！放过两个，下一球正中门面轰了出去。嗖地绕上二垒。头一位——咱们撂在二垒上那位——一溜烟回了本垒，得一分。这才叫比赛！信我的话，比尔，这才叫 *比赛*！」

这一堂课讲得用力过猛，克罗克先生颇有些虚脱，坐下来灌了口凉咖啡滋补元气。

「相当有意思的运动，」贝利斯说。「不过经您一讲我才明白，先生，这东西我原来熟得很，只是一向知道的是它的另一个名字。在我们这个国家，玩它的多得很。」

克罗克先生腾地跳了起来。

「是吗？我在这儿待了五年，愣是没察觉！下一场定在什么时候？」

「它在英国叫做绕圈球（Rounders），先生。小孩子们拿软球和球拍玩它，玩得不亦乐乎。拿它当大人的消遣，我以前倒从未听说过。」

===Original===
Two shocked eyes stared into the butler’s face.

“Children?” The word came in a whisper.

“A racquet?”

“Yes, sir.”

“You—you didn’t say a soft ball?”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
一双震骇的眼睛直勾勾瞪进了管家的脸。

「小孩子？」这两个字是用气音说出来的。

「还使球拍？」

「是的，先生。」

「你——你方才说的是，软球？」

「是的，先生。」

===Original===
A sort of spasm seemed to convulse Mr. Crocker. He had lived five years in England, but not till this moment had he realised to the full how utterly alone he was in an alien land. Fate had placed him, bound and helpless, in a country where they called baseball Rounders and played it with a soft ball.

He sank back into his chair, staring before him. And as he sat the wall seemed to melt and he was gazing upon a green field, in the centre of which a man in a grey uniform was beginning a Salome dance. Watching this person with a cold and suspicious eye, stood another uniformed man, holding poised above his shoulder a sturdy club. Two Masked Marvels crouched behind him in attitudes of watchful waiting. On wooden seats all around sat a vast multitude of shirt-sleeved spectators, and the air was full of voices.

One voice detached itself from the din.

“Peanuts! Get y’r peanuts!”

Something that was almost a sob shook Bingley Crocker’s ample frame. Bayliss the butler gazed down upon him with concern. He was sure the master was unwell.

===Chinese===
一阵痉挛似乎击穿了克罗克先生。他在英格兰住了五年，可直到这一刻，他才彻底明白，自己在异邦的土地上是何等地道的孤家寡人。命运把他五花大绑、动弹不得地搁在了一个把棒球唤作绕圈球、还使软球来打的国家。

他跌回椅子里，直着眼望着前方。他这么坐着，那面墙仿佛消融了，他正望进一片绿茵场，场子中央，一个灰制服汉子正开始跳莎乐美之舞。另一个制服汉子冷眼瞅着他，满脸狐疑，一根结实的球棒高举在肩头蓄势待发。他身后蹲着两位蒙面奇人，摆出一副守望以待的功架。四周木看台上黑压压坐满了挽着衬衫袖子的看客，空气里沸沸扬扬全是人声。

一个嗓门从嘈杂里杀了出来。

「花生！来买花生嘞！」

一样几乎算得上呜咽的东西撼动了宾利·克罗克肥硕的身躯。管家贝利斯满腹忧虑地低头望着他。他断定主人是身子不爽。

===Original===
The case of Mr. Bingley Crocker was one that would have provided an admirable “instance” for a preacher seeking to instil into an impecunious and sceptical flock the lesson that money does not of necessity bring with it happiness. And poetry has crystallised his position in the following stanza.

An exile from home splendour dazzles in vain.
						

						Oh, give me my lowly thatched cottage again;
						

						The birds singing gaily, that came at my call,

						Give me them, and that peace of mind dearer than all.

Mr. Crocker had never lived in a thatched cottage, nor had his relations with the birds of his native land ever reached the stage of intimacy indicated by the poet; but substitute “Lambs Club” for the former and “members” for the latter, and the parallel becomes complete.

===Chinese===
克罗克先生的这桩案子，倘若有位牧师正想给一群囊中羞涩、满腹疑窦的教友灌输「金钱未必附带幸福」的道理，拿去当个「实例」再妙不过。而诗歌早已把他的处境凝练成了下面这一节：

背井离乡的人，眼前繁华徒然晃眼；

啊，还我那低矮的茅舍来；

还有那闻唤即来、啁啾欢唱的鸟雀，

把它们还我，还有那贵过一切的心中安泰。

克罗克先生从未住过茅草屋，同故国禽鸟的交情也从来不曾达到诗人笔下的那种亲密境界；不过，只要把前者换成「羔羊俱乐部」（Lambs Club），后者换成「诸位会员」，这份对仗便严丝合缝了。

===Original===
Until the time of his second marriage Bingley Crocker had been an actor, a snapper-up of whatever small character-parts the gods provided. He had an excellent disposition, no money, and one son, a young man of twenty-one. For forty-five years he had lived a hand-to-mouth existence in which his next meal had generally come as a pleasant surprise: and then, on an Atlantic liner, he met the widow of G. G. van Brunt, the sole heiress to that magnate’s immense fortune.

What Mrs. van Brunt could have seen in Bingley Crocker to cause her to single him out from all the world passes comprehension: but the eccentricities of Cupid are commonplace. It were best to shun examination into first causes and stick to results. The swift romance began and reached its climax in the ten days which it took one of the smaller Atlantic liners to sail from Liverpool to New York. Mr. Crocker was on board because he was returning with a theatrical company from a failure in London, Mrs. van Brunt because she had been told that the slow boats were the steadiest. They began the voyage as strangers and ended it as an engaged couple—the affair being expedited, no doubt, by the fact that, even if it ever occurred to Bingley to resist the onslaught on his bachelor peace, he soon realised the futility of doing so, for the cramped conditions of shipboard intensified the always overwhelming effects of his future bride’s determined nature.

The engagement was received in a widely differing spirit by the only surviving blood-relations of the two principals. Jimmy, Mr. Crocker’s son, on being informed that his father had plighted his troth to the widow of a prominent millionaire, displayed the utmost gratification and enthusiasm, and at a little supper which he gave by way of farewell to a few of his newspaper comrades and which lasted till six in the morning, when it was broken up by the flying wedge of waiters for which the selected restaurant is justly famous, joyfully announced that work and he would from then on be total strangers. He alluded in feeling terms to the Providence which watches over good young men and saves them from the blighting necessity of offering themselves in the flower of their golden youth as human sacrifices to the Moloch of capitalistic greed: and, having commiserated with his guests in that a similar stroke of luck had not happened to each of them, advised them to drown their sorrows in drink. Which they did.

===Chinese===
直到第二次结婚之前，宾利·克罗克一直是个演员，诸神抛下什么小配角，他就顺手捡什么。他性情一流，存款为零，膝下一子，是个年方二十一的年轻人。四十五年来他过的都是现挣现吃的日子，下一顿饭通常以惊喜的面目从天而降：然后，在一条横渡大西洋的邮轮上，他遇见了 G. G. 范布伦特的遗孀——那位大亨万贯家财的唯一继承人。

范布伦特太太究竟在宾利·克罗克身上看出了什么名堂，以至于从普天之下单单把他挑了出来，这件事超出了人类悟性的边界：不过丘比特的古怪行径本属家常便饭。上策莫过绕开第一因，单看结果。这场迅雷不及掩耳的罗曼史，从萌芽到瓜熟蒂落，恰好占了一条较小的横渡大西洋邮轮从利物浦驶到纽约所需的那十天。克罗克先生在船上，是因为他随一个剧团在伦敦砸了戏、正打道回府；范布伦特太太在船上，则是因为有人告诉她，慢船最稳当。他们以陌生人的身份启航，以订下婚约的情侣身份靠岸——此事之所以一路顺风，无疑有赖于如下事实：即便宾利一度起过念头，想抵挡这场对他单身清净的强攻，他也旋即悟出此举纯属徒劳，因为船上逼仄的空间，把他未婚妻那素来势不可挡的坚决天性，效果又放大了几分。

这桩婚约，在男女双方仅存的血亲那里，激起的反应判若云泥。克罗克先生的儿子吉米，听说父亲已同一位鼎鼎大名的百万富翁遗孀山盟海誓订了终身，表现出极度的欣慰与热忱。他摆了一桌小小的夜宵，权作与几位报界同行的话别，这顿夜宵一直吃到早晨六点，才被那家指定餐馆素负盛名的楔形攻势服务生方阵冲散——席间他喜气洋洋地宣布，从今以后，工作与他将形同陌路。他满怀感情地提到那眷顾好青年的天意，如何拯救他们免于在黄金年华的盛花期，把自己当牺牲供奉到资本贪婪的摩洛克祭坛上：随后他对在座诸君深表同情，感慨类似的好运不曾降临到他们每个人头上，并劝他们借酒浇愁。他们也确实浇了。

===Original===
Far different was the attitude of Mrs. Crocker’s sister, Nesta Pett. She entirely disapproved of the proposed match. At least, the fact that in her final interview with her sister she described the bridegroom-to-be as a wretched mummer, a despicable fortune-hunter, a broken-down tramp, and a sneaking, grafting confidence-trickster lends colour to the supposition that she was not a warm supporter of it. She agreed wholeheartedly with Mrs. Crocker’s suggestion that they should never speak to each other again as long as they lived: and it was immediately after this that the latter removed husband Bingley, stepson Jimmy, and all her other goods and chattels to London, where they had remained ever since. Whenever Mrs. Crocker spoke of America now, it was in tones of the deepest dislike and contempt. Her friends were English, and every year more exclusively of England’s aristocracy. She intended to become a leading figure in London Society, and already her progress had been astonishing. She knew the right people, lived in the right square, said the right things, and thought the right thoughts: and in the Spring of her third year had succeeded in curing Bingley of his habit of beginning his remarks with the words “Say, lemme tell ya something.” Her progress, in short, was beginning to assume the aspect of a walkover.

Against her complete contentment and satisfaction only one thing militated. That was the behaviour of her stepson, Jimmy.

It was of Jimmy that she spoke when, having hung the receiver on its hook, she returned to the breakfast-room. Bayliss had silently withdrawn, and Mr. Crocker was sitting in sombre silence at the table.

===Chinese===
克罗克太太的妹妹内斯塔·佩特，态度则相去霄壤。她对这桩拟议中的婚事全然不以为然。至少，在她与姐姐最后一次会面时，她把那位准新郎形容成一个下三滥的戏子、一个可鄙的逐金之徒、一个垮掉的流浪汉、外加一个阴损讹诈的老千——这一事实，为她并非这桩婚事热心拥趸的推测平添了几分色彩。克罗克太太提议，姐妹二人只要一息尚存，便永不再交谈，对此她衷心拥护：紧接着，后者便带着丈夫宾利、继子吉米，连同其余全部动产杂物，迁往伦敦，从此定居至今。如今克罗克太太一提起美国，口气里总浸着最深切的厌恶与鄙夷。她的朋友全是英国人，而且一年比一年更是清一色的英国贵族。她立志要当伦敦社交界的一位头面人物，而她的进展已然惊人。她结识该结识的人，住该住的广场，说该说的话，想该想的心思：到第三年春天，她又成功治愈了宾利用「我说，俺给你唠两句」起头的痼疾。一言以蔽之，她的进展已渐渐显出一场不战而胜的气象。

在她那份十全十美的称心如意之中，唯独有一件事作梗。那就是她继子吉米的行径。

她把听筒挂回钩上，回到早餐室时，谈的正是吉米。贝利斯已悄然退下，克罗克先生正坐在桌边，阴沉着脸一言不发。

===Original===
“A most fortunate thing has happened, Bingley,” she said. “It was most kind of dear Lady Corstorphine to ring me up. It seems that her nephew, Lord Percy Whipple, is back in England. He has been in Ireland for the past three years, on the staff of the Lord Lieutenant, and only arrived in London yesterday afternoon. Lady Corstorphine has promised to arrange a meeting between him and James. I particularly want them to be friends.”

“Eugenia,” said Mr. Crocker in a hollow voice, “do you know they call baseball Rounders over here, and children play it with a soft ball?”

“James is becoming a serious problem. It is absolutely necessary that he should make friends with the right kind of young men.”

“And a racquet,” said Mr. Crocker.

“Please listen to what I am saying, Bingley. I am talking about James. There is a crude American strain in him which seems to grow worse instead of better. I was lunching with the Delafields at the Carlton yesterday, and there, only a few tables away, was James with an impossible young man in appalling clothes. It was outrageous that James should have been seen in public at all with such a person. The man had a broken nose and talked through it. He was saying in a loud voice that made everybody turn round something about his left-scissors hook—whatever that may have been. I discovered later that he was a low professional pugilist from New York—a man named Spike Dillon, I think Captain Wroxton said. And Jimmy was giving him lunch—at the *Carlton*!”

Mr. Crocker said nothing. Constant practice had made him an adept at saying nothing when his wife was talking.

===Chinese===
「出了件大好事，宾利，」她说。「亲爱的科斯托芬夫人打来电话，真是热心。她的侄子珀西·惠普尔勋爵（Lord Percy Whipple）回英格兰了。过去三年他都在爱尔兰，在总督手下当差，昨天下午才到伦敦。科斯托芬夫人答应安排他同詹姆斯见上一面。我特别盼望他们俩能交成朋友。」

「尤金妮娅，」克罗克先生用空洞的嗓音说，「你知不知道，他们这儿管棒球叫绕圈球，还让小孩子拿软球打？」

「詹姆斯正日益成为一个严重的问题。他非结交上一批正派人家的青年不可，这一点绝对必要。」

「还使球拍，」克罗克先生说。

「请你留意听我说话，宾利。我说的是詹姆斯。他身上有一股粗鄙的美国血脉，非但不见收敛，反倒愈演愈烈。昨天我在卡尔顿饭店（the Carlton）同德拉菲尔德夫妇（the Delafields）吃午饭，隔着没几张桌子就是詹姆斯，同一个不成体统的青年坐在一起，那身穿着简直骇人听闻。詹姆斯居然被人撞见在公开场合同这号人厮混，真是岂有此理。那人塌了半边鼻子，说话全打鼻孔里出。他正扯着个大嗓门，嚷得人人回头，嚷的似乎是他的什么『左剪钩拳』——天知道那是路数。后来我才打听明白，他是纽约来的下三滥职业拳师——名叫斯派克·狄龙（Spike Dillon），我记得是罗克斯顿上尉（Captain Wroxton）说的。而吉米居然请他吃午饭——还是在 *卡尔顿* 饭店！」

克罗克先生一言未发。勤学苦练之下，他早已修成一门绝技：太太说话的时候，一言不发。

===Original===
“James must be made to realise his responsibilities. I shall have to speak to him. I was hearing only the other day of a most deserving man, extremely rich and lavishly generous in his contributions to the party funds, who was only given a knighthood, simply because he had a son who had behaved in a manner that could not possibly be overlooked. The present Court is extraordinarily strict in its views. James cannot be too careful. A certain amount of wildness in a young man is quite proper in the best set, provided that he is wild in the right company. Everyone knows that young Lord Datchet was ejected from the Empire Music-Hall on Boat-Race night every year during his residence at Oxford University, but nobody minds. The family treats it as a joke. But James has such low tastes. Professional pugilists! I believe that many years ago it was not unfashionable for young men in Society to be seen about with such persons, but those days are over. I shall certainly speak to James. He cannot afford to call attention to himself in any way. That breach-of-promise case of his three years ago, is, I hope and trust, forgotten, but the slightest slip on his part might start the papers talking about it again, and that would be fatal. The eventual successor to a title must be quite as careful as—”

It was not, as has been hinted above, the usual practice of Mr. Crocker to interrupt his wife when she was speaking, but he did it now.

“Say!”

Mrs. Crocker frowned.

“I wish, Bingley—and I have told you so often—that you would not begin your sentences with the word ‘Say’! It is such a revolting Americanism. Suppose some day when you are addressing the House of Lords you should make a slip like that! The papers would never let you hear the end of it.”

===Chinese===
「必须让詹姆斯明白他肩上的责任。我得同他谈一谈。前几天我才听说有这么一位极有功德的人物，家资巨万，向政党经费捐款一掷千金，到头来只得了个骑士封号，就因为他有个儿子，行事让人想装看不见都办不到。如今的宫廷，眼光严格得出奇。詹姆斯再怎么小心也不为过。年轻人放浪一点，在最上等的圈子里原本无可厚非，只要他放浪得结交对伴。谁都知道年轻的达切特勋爵（Lord Datchet）在牛津大学念书那几年，每年划船大赛之夜都要被帝国音乐厅（Empire Music-Hall）撵出来一回，可没一个人介意。他们全家还拿它当笑话讲呢。可詹姆斯的口味竟这般低级。职业拳师！我相信许多年前，社交界的青年同这路人相伴出入，倒也未必有失体面，可那样的年头早过去了。我务必同詹姆斯谈谈。他经不起以任何方式惹人注目。三年前他那桩毁弃婚约的官司，我期望并且相信早已被人遗忘，但他只要有半点闪失，报纸就可能旧话重提，那便是致命一击。爵位日后的继承人，行事必须同样地小心，恰如——」

前文已有暗示，打断太太讲话并非克罗克先生的惯技，可他此刻破例了。

「听着！」

克罗克太太蹙起了眉。

「我希望，宾利——这话我不知嘱咐过你多少回了——你不要用『听着』（Say）这个字眼来起你的句子！这是种叫人作呕的美国腔。设想有朝一日你正在上议院致辞，冷不丁漏出这么一句！报纸准会揪住不放，叫你一辈子不得安生。」

===Original===
Mr. Crocker was swallowing convulsively, as if testing his larynx with a view to speech. Like Saul of Tarsus, he had been stricken dumb by the sudden bright light which his wife’s words had caused to flash upon him. Frequently during his sojourn in London he had wondered just why Eugenia had settled there in preference to her own country. It was not her wont to do things without an object, yet until this moment he had been unable to fathom her motives. Even now it seemed almost incredible. And yet what meaning would her words have other than the monstrous one which had smitten him as a blackjack?

“Say—I mean, Eugenia—you don’t want—you aren’t trying—you aren’t working to—you haven’t any idea of trying to get them to make me a Lord, have you?”

“It is what I have been working for all these years!”

“But—but why? Why? That’s what I want to know. Why?”

Mrs. Crocker’s fine eyes glittered.

“I will tell you why, Bingley. Just before we were married I had a talk with my sister Nesta. She was insufferably offensive. She referred to you in terms which I shall never forgive. She affected to look down on you, to think that I was marrying beneath me. So I am going to make you an English peer and send Nesta a newspaper clipping of the Birthday Honours with your name in it, if I have to keep working till I die! Now you know!”

===Chinese===
克罗克先生喉咙一阵阵痉挛性地上下吞动，像是在测验喉管还能不能出声。他就像大数城的扫罗，被太太的言语骤然打出的那道强光震得哑口无言。旅居伦敦期间，他常常纳闷，尤金妮娅何以偏偏选定此地落脚，而不回她自己的国家。她做事从不无的放矢，可直到此刻，他始终没能参透她的动机。即便是现在，这也几乎令人难以置信。然而，她那番话若不是那个像短棍一样劈中他的可怕意思，还能有什么别的意思呢？

「听着——我是说，尤金妮娅——你不见得——你不会是想——你不会是在奔走——你总不至于当真动过念头，要让他们把我弄成个勋爵吧，啊？」

「这些年我东奔西走，忙活的就是这件事！」

「可是——可是为什么？为什么？我就想知道这个。为什么？」

克罗克太太那双俊眼灼灼生辉。

「我这就告诉你为什么，宾利。就在我们结婚之前，我同我妹妹内斯塔谈过一次话。她放肆得叫人忍无可忍。她提起你时所用的那些字眼，我永远不能原谅。她装出一副俯看你的做派，认定我这是屈尊下嫁。所以，我要把你造就成英国贵族，还要给内斯塔寄一份登着你名字的诞辰授勋剪报——哪怕我得为此操劳到死！这下你明白了！」

===Original===
Silence fell. Mr. Crocker drank cold coffee. His wife stared with gleaming eyes into the glorious future.

“Do you mean that I shall have to stop on here till they make me a lord?” said Mr. Crocker limply.

“Yes.”

“Never go back to America?”

“Not till we have succeeded.”

“Oh Gee! Oh Gosh! Oh Hell!” said Mr. Crocker, bursting the bonds of years.

===Chinese===
沉默落下。克罗克先生喝着凉咖啡。他的太太目光炯炯，凝望着那片光辉灿烂的前程。

「你是说，我就得在这儿一直蹲下去，蹲到他们把我扶正成勋爵为止？」克罗克先生绵软无力地说。

「是的。」

「再也不回美国了？」

「功成之前不回。」

「哦老天！哦上帝！哦活见鬼！」克罗克先生说，一举崩断了束缚他多年的绳索。

===Original===
Mrs. Crocker though resolute, was not unkindly. She made allowances for her husband’s state of mind. She was willing to permit even American expletives during the sinking-in process of her great idea, much as a broad-minded cowboy might listen indulgently to the squealing of a mustang during the branding process. Docility and obedience would be demanded of him later, but not till the first agony had abated. She spoke soothingly to him.

“I am glad we have had this talk, Bingley. It is best that you should know. It will help you to realise your responsibilities. And that brings me back to James. Thank goodness Lord Percy Whipple is in town. He is about James’ age, and from what Lady Corstorphine tells me will be an ideal friend for him. You understand who he is, of course? The second son of the Duke of Devizes, the Premier’s closest friend, the man who can practically dictate the Birthday Honours. If James and Lord Percy can only form a close friendship, our battle will be as good as won. It will mean everything. Lady Corstorphine has promised to arrange a meeting. In the meantime, I will speak to James and warn him to be more careful.”

Mr. Crocker had produced a stump of pencil from his pocket and was writing on the tablecloth.

===Chinese===
克罗克太太意志虽坚，心地却不坏。她体恤丈夫的心境。在她那伟大构想的消化吸收期里，就连美国式的惊叹词她也愿意网开一面，好比一位胸襟开阔的牛仔，在打烙印的当口，也会宽厚地由着野马嘶叫。温顺与服从往后自然要向他索取，但总得捱到第一阵剧痛消退之后。她柔声安抚他。

「咱们这番话谈得好，宾利。你知道了总是好的。这有助于你掂量自己的责任。这就又绕回詹姆斯身上了。谢天谢地，珀西·惠普尔勋爵进城了。他与詹姆斯年纪相仿，据科斯托芬夫人相告，正是他的一位理想伙伴。他是谁你自然清楚？德维泽斯公爵的次子——公爵是首相最亲密的朋友，那位几乎能一手钦定诞辰授勋名单的人物。只要詹姆斯和珀西勋爵能缔结一段亲密的友谊，我们这一仗便等于打赢了九成九。这将意味着一切。科斯托芬夫人已答应安排会面。在此期间，我会找詹姆斯谈一谈，告诫他多加检点。」

克罗克先生从口袋里摸出一截铅笔头，在桌布上写将起来。

-

He blanched as he read the frightful words. A sudden thought stung him.

“Eugenia!”

“Well?”

克罗克勋爵

他一面读着这些骇人的字眼，一面脸色发白。一个念头猛地螫了他一下。

「尤金妮娅！」

「什么事？」

===Original===
“What will the boys at the Lambs say?”

“I am not interested,” replied his wife, “in the boys at the Lambs.”

“I thought you wouldn’t be,” said the future baron gloomily.

===Chinese===
「羔羊俱乐部那帮老哥们儿会怎么说？」

「羔羊俱乐部那帮老哥们儿怎么说，」他的太太答道，「我毫无兴趣。」

「我就料定你没兴趣，」这位未来的男爵阴郁地说。

## III / 三

Family Jars / 家庭内讧

===Original===
It is a peculiarity of the human mind that, with whatever apprehension it may be regarding the distant future, it must return after a while to face the minor troubles of the future that is immediate. The prospect of a visit to the dentist this afternoon causes us to forget for the moment the prospect of total ruin next year. Mr. Crocker, therefore, having tortured himself for about a quarter of an hour with his meditations on the subject of titles, was jerked back to a more imminent calamity than the appearance of his name in the Birthday Honours﻿—the fact that in all probability he would be taken again this morning to watch the continuation of that infernal cricket-match, and would be compelled to spend the greater part of today, as he had spent the greater part of yesterday, bored to the verge of dissolution in the pavilion at Lord’s.
One gleam of hope alone presented itself. Like baseball, this pastime of cricket was apparently affected by rain, if there had been enough of it. He had an idea that there had been a good deal of rain in the night, but had there been sufficient to cause the teams of Surrey and Kent to postpone the second instalment of their serial struggle? He rose from the table and went out into the hall. It was his purpose to sally out into Grosvenor Square and examine the turf in its centre with the heel of his shoe, in order to determine the stickiness or non-stickiness of the wicket. He moved towards the front door, hoping for the best, and just as he reached it the bell rang.
One of the bad habits of which his wife had cured Mr. Crocker in the course of the years was the habit of going and answering doors. He had been brought up in surroundings where every man was his own doorkeeper, and it had been among his hardest tasks to learn the lesson that the perfect gentleman does not open doors but waits for the appropriate menial to come along and do it for him. He had succeeded at length in mastering this great truth, and nowadays seldom offended. But this morning his mind was clouded by his troubles, and instinct, allaying itself with opportunity, was too much for him. His fingers had been on the handle when the ring came, so he turned it.
At the top of the steps which connect the main entrance of Drexdale House with the sidewalk three persons were standing. One was a tall and formidably handsome woman in the early forties whose appearance seemed somehow oddly familiar. The second was a small, fat, blobby, bulging boy who was chewing something. The third, lurking diffidently in the rear, was a little man of about Mr. Crocker’s own age, grey-haired and thin with brown eyes that gazed meekly through rimless glasses.
Nobody could have been less obtrusive than this person, yet it was he who gripped Mr. Crocker’s attention and caused that homesick sufferer’s heart to give an almost painful leap. For he was clothed in one of those roomy suits with square shoulders which to the seeing eye are as republican as the Stars and Stripes. His blunt-toed yellow shoes sang gaily of home. And his hat was not so much a hat as an effusive greeting from Gotham. A long time had passed since Mr. Crocker had set eyes upon a biped so exhilaratingly American, and rapture held him speechless, as one who after long exile beholds some landmark of his childhood.
The female member of the party took advantage of his dumbness﻿—which, as she had not unnaturally mistaken him for the butler, she took for a silent and respectful query as to her business and wishes﻿—to open the conversation.

===Chinese===
人心有这么一桩怪脾气：无论它对遥远的未来怀着多大的忧惧，过不了多久，总得回过头来，应付那迫在眉睫的未来的种种小麻烦。今天下午要去看牙医的前景，能叫我们一时忘却明年彻底破产的前景。于是，克罗克先生拿爵位问题把自己煎熬了一刻钟左右之后，便被猛地拽回一场比名字登上「诞辰授勋名录」更迫近的灾殃面前——多半今天上午他又要被押去观赏那场该死板球赛的下文，而且注定要把今天的大半天，像昨天的大半天一样，消磨在罗兹板球场（Lord's）的看台棚里，无聊得直逼油尽灯枯的边缘。
唯有一线希望独自亮着。跟棒球一样，板球这项消遣看来也吃雨水的摆布——倘若雨下得够多的话。他记得夜里似乎下了不少雨，可那雨到底够不够，能让萨里郡（Surrey）和肯特郡（Kent）两队把他们那部连载长战的第二回顺延？他从餐桌旁起身，走进门厅。他打算冲到格罗夫纳广场（Grosvenor Square），用鞋跟检验广场中央的草皮，以测定三柱门到底是黏还是不黏。他朝前门走去，心里专往好里盼，刚到门前，门铃响了。
这些年来，克罗克先生被太太治好的坏毛病之一，便是亲自跑去应门的毛病。他从小生长在人人自任门房的环境里，因此「完美的绅士不开门，只等合宜的仆役前来代劳」这一课，是他修得最吃力的功课之一。他总算修成了这条伟大的真理，如今已很少再犯。可是今天早晨，他的脑子叫烦恼搅浑了，本能又同机会暗中勾结，他招架不住。铃响时他的手指本就搭在门把上，于是他拧了下去。
在德雷斯代尔府（Drexdale House）正门与便道之间的台阶顶上，站着三个人。一位是高挑个子、俊美得咄咄逼人的女人，四十岁刚出头，那模样不知怎的透着眼熟。第二位是个矮胖滚圆、浑身鼓胀的男孩，嘴里正嚼着什么。第三位怯生生地缩在后头，是个同克罗克先生年纪相仿的小个子男人，头发花白，身形清瘦，一双棕色眼睛隔着无边眼镜温顺地望着人。
天底下再没有比此人更不惹眼的人了，然而偏偏是他攫住了克罗克先生的注意力，害得这位害思乡病的受难者心口几乎发疼地一跳。因为他身上套的是那种肩方袖宽的宽大西服——在识货的眼里，它和星条旗一样共和得地道。他那双圆头黄皮鞋快活地唱着家乡的歌。他头上那顶物件，与其说是帽子，不如说是「哥谭」（Gotham，纽约的别名）寄来的一封热情洋溢的问候信。克罗克先生已经很久没见过一只如此提神醒脑的美国两足动物了，狂喜攫住了他，叫他哑口无言，恰如久客异邦的游子蓦然望见童年的旧地标。
来客中的女性成员趁他这阵哑然开了腔——她把他错当成了管家，这倒也合乎情理，于是便把这哑然当作一声无声而恭敬的「您有何贵干」。

===Original===
“Is Mrs. Crocker at home? Please tell her that Mrs. Pett wishes to see her.”
There was a rush and scurry in the corridors of Mr. Crocker’s brain, as about six different thoughts tried to squash simultaneously into that main chamber where there is room for only one at a time. He understood now why this woman’s appearance had seemed familiar. She was his wife’s sister, and that same Nesta who was some day to be pulverised by the sight of his name in the Birthday Honours. He was profoundly thankful that she had mistaken him for the butler. A chill passed through him as he pictured what would have been Eugenia’s reception of the information that he had committed such a bourgeois solecism as opening the front door to Mrs. Pett of all people, who already despised him as a low vulgarian. There had been trouble enough when she had found him opening it a few weeks before to a mere collector of subscriptions for a charity. He perceived, with a clarity remarkable in view of the fact that the discovery of her identity had given him a feeling of physical dizziness, that at all costs he must foster this misapprehension on his sister-in-law’s part.
Fortunately he was in a position to do so. He knew all about what butlers did and what they said on these occasions, for in his innocently curious way he had often pumped Bayliss on the subject. He bowed silently and led the way to the morning-room, followed by the drove of Petts: then, opening the door, stood aside to allow the procession to march past the given point.
“I will inform Mrs. Crocker that you are here, madam.”
Mrs. Pett, shepherding the chewing child before her, passed into the room. In the light of her outspoken sentiments regarding her brother-in-law, it is curious to reflect that his manner at this, their first meeting, had deeply impressed her. After many months of smouldering revolt she had dismissed her own butler a day or so before sailing for England, and for the first time envy of her sister Eugenia gripped her. She did not covet Eugenia’s other worldly possessions, but she did grudge her this supreme butler.
Mr. Pett, meanwhile, had been trailing in the rear with a hunted expression on his face. He wore the unmistakable look of a man about to be present at a row between women, and only a wet cat in a strange backyard bears itself with less jauntiness than a man faced by such a prospect. A millionaire several times over, Mr. Pett would cheerfully have given much of his wealth to have been elsewhere at that moment. Such was the agitated state of his mind that, when a hand was laid lightly upon his arm as he was about to follow his wife into the room, he started so violently that his hat flew out of his hand. He turned to meet the eyes of the butler who had admitted him to the house, fixed on his in an appealing stare.

===Chinese===
「克罗克太太在家吗？请禀报她，佩特太太求见。」
克罗克先生脑袋里的走廊间登时一片横冲直撞，大约六个不同的念头想同时挤进那间一次只容一人的正殿。他如今明白这女人的模样为何眼熟了。她是他太太的妹妹，正是那位有朝一日要被他名字登上「诞辰授勋名录」的景象碾成齑粉的内斯塔（Nesta）。他深深庆幸她把自己错当成了管家。他一想象，假如尤金妮娅（Eugenia）得知他犯下这等市侩的礼数差错——偏偏给佩特太太这号人开正门，而此人早把他鄙视为下流俗物——会作何发落，便有一股寒气窜遍全身。几个星期前，她撞见他给一个区区慈善募捐员开门，风波就已经够瞧的了。他察觉——考虑到认出她身份这件事已经叫他天旋地转，这份清醒实属难得——自己无论如何必须把妻妹的这个误会精心培育下去。
所幸他恰好有办法。管家这类场合做些什么、说些什么，他一概门儿清，因为他素来怀着天真无害的好奇，常在这方面盘问贝利斯（Bayliss）。他默默一躬，在前引路直奔起居室，身后跟着佩特家这一小群牲口：随后他推开门，闪身一旁，让这支队伍从指定地点列队行进而过。
「我去禀报克罗克太太，说您到了，夫人。」
佩特太太把那个嚼个不停的孩子赶在前头，像牧羊似的进了屋。念及她对这位姐夫向来直言不讳的看法，说来也怪：在这头一回的会面里，他的举止竟给她留下了极深的印象。经过好几个月郁积的暗涌，她在启程赴英前一两天辞退了自家的管家，于是平生头一遭，对姐姐尤金妮娅的嫉妒攫住了她。尤金妮娅别的身外之物她一概不眼红，唯独舍不得她这位登峰造极的管家。
这时，佩特先生一直吊在队尾，脸上挂着一副遭人追捕的神情。他分明是一副即将列席女人拌嘴的男人模样，而面临这种前景的男人还能保住几分神气，天底下只有误入陌生后院的落汤猫比他更惨。佩特先生这位「百万」字要念上好几遍的百万富翁，当时情愿捐出大半家财，只求换个地方待着。他心绪激荡到这个地步，以致当他正要跟太太进屋时，一只手轻轻搭上他的手臂，他惊得那样猛，帽子竟从手里飞了出去。他转过身，正撞上那位引他进门的管家的眼睛——那双眼睛正牢牢盯住他，目光里带着恳求。

===Original===
“Who’s leading in the pennant race?” said this strange butler in a feverish whisper.
It was a question, coming from such a source, which in another than Mr. Pett might well have provoked a blank stare of amazement. Such, however, is the almost superhuman intelligence and quickness of mind engendered by the study of America’s national game that he answered without the slightest hesitation.
“Giants!”
“Wow!” said the butler.
No sense of anything strange or untoward about the situation came to mar the perfect joy of Mr. Pett, the overmastering joy of the baseball fan who in a strange land unexpectedly encounters a brother. He thrilled with a happiness which he had never hoped to feel that morning.
“No signs of them slumping?” enquired the butler.

===Chinese===
「锦旗赛现在谁领先？」这位古怪的管家用滚烫的耳语说。
这样的问题，从这样的出处冒出来，换了旁人而不是佩特先生，多半要瞠目结舌好一阵。然而，研习美国国球所能孕育的那种近乎超人的智力与急智，其功效便是如此：他毫不含糊、应声而答。
「巨人队（Giants）！」
「嚯！」管家说。
丝毫没有半点「此情此景有何蹊跷、有何不妙」的感觉，来败坏佩特先生的圆满喜悦——那是一个棒球迷在异邦他乡意外撞见一位自家弟兄的压倒性喜悦。他周身战栗，满怀一种当天早晨从未奢望过的幸福。
「没露出疲软的迹象吧？」管家问道。

===Original===
“No. But you never can tell. It’s early yet. I’ve seen those boys lead the league till the end of August and then be nosed out.”
“True enough,” said the butler sadly.
“Matty’s in shape.”
“He is? The old souper working well?”
“Like a machine. He shut out the Cubs the day before I sailed!”
“Fine!”

===Chinese===
「没有。不过这事说不准。赛季还早着呢。我见过那帮小子一路领先到八月底，末了差一口气被人家挤下去。」
「说的是啊。」管家悲从中来地说。
「马蒂（Matty）状态正好。」
「是吗？那锅老汤还熬得滚吧？」
「跟机器似的。我开船前一天，他把小熊队（the Cubs）完封了！」
「好极了！」

===Original===
At this point an appreciation of the unusualness of the proceedings began to steal upon Mr. Pett. He gaped at this surprising servitor.
“How on earth do you know anything about baseball?” he demanded.
The other seemed to stiffen. A change came over his whole appearance. He had the air of an actor who has remembered his part.
“I beg your pardon, sir. I trust I have not taken a liberty. I was at one time in the employment of a gentleman in New York, and during my stay I became extremely interested in the national game. I picked up a few of the American idioms while in the country.” He smiled apologetically. “They sometimes slip out.”
“Let ’em slip!” said Mr. Pett with enthusiasm. “You’re the first thing that’s reminded me of home since I left. Say!”
“Sir?”

===Chinese===
到这时候，一种「眼前这场面未免反常」的意识，才开始悄悄爬上佩特先生的心头。他张口结舌地望着这位出人意表的仆役。
「你到底打哪儿知道的棒球？」他质问道。
对方似乎全身一挺。他的整个外表为之一变。他那副神气，活像个猛然记起自己角色的演员。
「请您恕罪，先生。但愿我没有失礼。我从前在纽约一位绅士府上做过事，旅居期间，对贵国国球发生了极其浓厚的兴趣。在那边的时候，我顺带拾得了几句美国成语。」他歉然一笑。「它们有时会溜出口来。」
「让它们溜好了！」佩特先生热情洋溢地说。「自从我离开家，你是头一样叫我想起家的东西。我说！」
「先生？」

===Original===
“Got a good place here?”
“Er﻿—oh, yes, sir.”
“Well, here’s my card. If you ever feel like making a change, there’s a job waiting for you at that address.”
“Thank you, sir.” Mr. Crocker stooped.
“Your hat, sir.”

===Chinese===
「你在这儿的位置还不错吧？」
「呃——哦，是的，先生。」
「那么，这是我的名片。你哪天想换换东家，那个地址上有一份差事等着你。」
「谢谢您，先生。」克罗克先生躬下身去。
「您的帽子，先生。」

===Original===
He held it out, gazing fondly at it the while. It was like being home again to see a hat like that. He followed Mr. Pett as he went into the morning-room with an affectionate eye.
Bayliss was coming along the hall, hurrying more than his wont. The ring at the front door had found him deep in an extremely interesting piece of news in his halfpenny morning paper, and he was guiltily aware of having delayed in answering it.
“Bayliss,” said Mr. Crocker in a cautious undertone, “go and tell Mrs. Crocker that Mrs. Pett is waiting to see her. She’s in the morning-room. If you’re asked, say you let her in. Get me?”
“Yes, sir,” said Bayliss, grateful for this happy solution.
“Oh, Bayliss!”
“Sir?”

===Chinese===
他把帽子递过去，一面深情地凝视着它。能再见到这样一顶帽子，就像又回了一趟家。他满眼慈爱，目送佩特先生走进起居室。
贝利斯正顺着门厅过来，比平时跑得急。前门的铃声找到他时，他正埋头于那份半便士晨报上一条极有意思的新闻，此刻他心虚地自知应门迟了。
「贝利斯，」克罗克先生用谨慎的低声说，「去告诉克罗克太太，佩特太太等着见她。她在起居室。要是有人问起，就说是你放她进来的。懂我的意思吗？」
「是，先生。」贝利斯说，对这个两全其美的解决办法感激不已。
「哦，贝利斯！」
「先生？」

===Original===
“Is the wicket at Lord’s likely to be too sticky for them to go on with that game today?”
“I hardly think it probable that there will be play, sir. There was a great deal of rain in the night.”
Mr. Crocker passed on to his den with a lighter heart.

===Chinese===
「罗兹的三柱门今天会不会黏得没法接着赛？」
「我看多半赛不成了，先生。夜里雨下得可不少。」
克罗克先生心头一轻，往他的私室去了。

---

===Original===
It was Mrs. Crocker’s habit, acquired after years of practice and a sedulous study of the best models, to conceal beneath a mask of well-bred indifference any emotion which she might chance to feel. Her dealings with the aristocracy of England had shown her that, while the men occasionally permitted themselves an outburst, the women never did, and she had schooled herself so rigorously that nowadays she seldom even raised her voice. Her bearing, as she approached the morning-room was calm and serene, but inwardly curiosity consumed her. It was unbelievable that Nesta could have come to try to effect a reconciliation, yet she could think of no other reason for her visit.
She was surprised to find three persons in the morning-room. Bayliss, delivering his message, had mentioned only Mrs. Pett. To Mrs. Crocker the assemblage had the appearance of being a sort of Old Home Week of Petts, a kind of Pett family mob-scene. Her sister’s second marriage having taken place after their quarrel, she had never seen her new brother-in-law, but she assumed that the little man lurking in the background was Mr. Pett. The guess was confirmed.
“Good morning, Eugenia,” said Mrs. Pett.
“Peter, this is my sister, Eugenia. My husband.”
Mrs. Crocker bowed stiffly. She was thinking how hopelessly American Mr. Pett was, how baggy his clothes looked, what absurdly shaped shoes he wore, how appalling his hat was, how little hair he had and how deplorably he lacked all those graces of repose, culture, physical beauty, refinement, dignity, and mental alertness which raise men above the level of the common cockroach.
Mr. Pett, on his side, receiving her cold glance squarely between the eyes, felt as if he were being disembowelled by a clumsy amateur. He could not help wondering what sort of a man this fellow Crocker was whom this sister-in-law of his had married. He pictured him as a handsome, powerful, robust individual with a strong jaw and a loud voice, for he could imagine no lesser type of man consenting to link his lot with such a woman. He sidled in a circuitous manner towards a distant chair, and, having lowered himself into it, kept perfectly still, pretending to be dead, like an opossum. He wished to take no part whatever in the coming interview.

===Chinese===
克罗克太太的习惯——那是多年练习加潜心揣摩最佳范本养成的——是把偶然泛起的任何情绪，都藏进一副教养良好的淡漠面具底下。同英格兰贵族打交道的阅历告诉她：男人偶尔还容许自己发作一场，女人却从不。她把自己锤炼得如此严苛，如今连嗓音都很少抬高。她走向起居室时，仪态从容安详，内心却被好奇心烧灼着。内斯塔竟是来设法言归于好的，这难以置信；然而此行的缘由，她又想不出别的。
起居室里竟有三个人，她吃了一惊。贝利斯传话时只提了佩特太太一位。在克罗克太太眼里，这一群人的架势，像是佩特家族办了个「返乡团圆周」，一场佩特家的群氓大戏。姐妹俩闹翻之后她妹妹才改嫁，故而这位新妹夫她从未谋面，但她揣想那个缩在暗处的小个子男人便是佩特先生。这一猜被证实了。
「早安，尤金妮娅。」佩特太太说。
「彼得，这是我姐姐尤金妮娅。我丈夫。」
克罗克太太僵硬地一躬。她心里转的是：佩特先生这个美国人当得多么不可救药，他那身衣裳看上去多么松垮兜风，他那双鞋的形状多么荒唐，他那顶帽子多么骇人听闻，他头发多么稀少，还有他多么可悲地欠缺那一切能把人抬举到普通蟑螂水准之上的优雅——诸如恬静、教养、仪表之美、风雅、尊严，以及心智的机敏。
佩特先生这一边，正正地挨了她一眼冷冷的对视，感觉像是被一个手艺笨拙的业余刽子手开膛破肚。他不由得纳闷，他这位姨姐嫁的这位姓克罗克的老兄，究竟是怎样一号人物。他把他想象成一个英俊、魁伟、壮硕的汉子，下巴坚毅，嗓门洪亮，因为比这更逊一等的男人肯把命运同这样的女人拴在一起，他想象不出来。他侧着身子绕了个大圈子，蹭到远处一把椅子跟前，把自己放低进去之后便一动不动，像只负鼠似的装死。即将开场的这场会晤，他一星半点也不想沾边。

===Original===
“Ogden, of course, you know,” said Mrs. Pett.
She was sitting so stiffly upright on a hard chair and had so much the appearance of having been hewn from the living rock that every time she opened her mouth it was as if a statue had spoken.
“I know Ogden,” said Mrs. Crocker shortly. “Will you please stop him fidgeting with that vase? It is valuable.”
She directed at little Ogden, who was juggling aimlessly with a handsome *objet d’art* of the early Chinese school, a glance similar to that which had just disposed of his stepfather. But Ogden required more than a glance to divert him from any pursuit in which he was interested. He shifted a deposit of candy from his right cheek to his left cheek, inspected Mrs. Crocker for a moment with a pale eye, and resumed his juggling. Mrs. Crocker meant nothing in his young life.
“Ogden, come and sit down,” said Mrs. Pett.
“Don’t want to sit down.”

===Chinese===
「奥格登（Ogden）你自然是认识的。」佩特太太说。
她在一把硬椅子上坐得笔管条直，大有整块从天然岩石里凿出来的架势，以致她每回一开口，都像是一尊石像开了腔。
「我认识奥格登。」克罗克太太没好气地说。「劳驾叫他别摆弄那只花瓶了。那东西值钱。」
小奥格登正漫无目的地把一件早期中国流派的漂亮*艺术品*（objet d'art）耍得团团转，她朝他射去一眼，与方才料理他继父的那一眼如出一辙。然而要把奥格登从任何一项他感兴趣的营生里引开，光靠一眼是不够的。他把右腮里囤积的糖挪到左腮，用一只淡漠的眼打量了克罗克太太片刻，又接着耍他的了。克罗克太太在他年轻的生命里一文不值。
「奥格登，过来坐下。」佩特太太说。
「不想坐。」

===Original===
“Are you making a long stay in England, Nesta?” asked Mrs. Crocker coldly.
“I don’t know. We have made no plans.”
“Indeed?”
She broke off. Ogden, who had possessed himself of a bronze paper-knife, had begun to tap the vase with it. The ringing note thus produced appeared to please his young mind.
“If Ogden really wishes to break that vase,” said Mrs. Crocker in a detached voice, “let me ring for the butler to bring him a hammer.”
“Ogden!” said Mrs. Pett.

===Chinese===
「你们要在英国久住吗，内斯塔？」克罗克太太冷冷地问。
「不知道。我们没订计划。」
「是吗？」
她把话头打住。奥格登已经把一柄青铜裁纸刀弄到了手，正开始用它敲那只花瓶。由此敲出的铿锵之音，似乎颇悦他那颗幼小的灵魂。
「要是奥格登真想砸那只花瓶，」克罗克太太用一种置身事外的声口说，「我这就摇铃叫管家给他拿把锤子来。」
「奥格登！」佩特太太说。

===Original===
“Oh Gee! A fellow can’t do a thing!” muttered Ogden, and walked to the window. He stood looking out into the square, a slight twitching of the ears indicating that he still made progress with the candy.
“Still the same engaging child!” murmured Mrs. Crocker.
“I did not come here to discuss Ogden!” said Mrs. Pett.
Mrs. Crocker raised her eyebrows. Not even Mrs. Otho Lanners, from whom she had learned the art, could do it more effectively.
“I am still waiting to find out why you did come, Nesta!”
“I came here to talk to you about your stepson, James Crocker.”

===Chinese===
「好家伙！想干点儿什么都不成！」奥格登咕哝着，走到了窗前。他站在那儿眺望广场，耳朵微微翕动，表明糖果工程仍在稳步推进。
「还是那么招人疼的一个孩子！」克罗克太太低声说。
「我不是来这儿讨论奥格登的！」佩特太太说。
克罗克太太扬起了眉毛。这门功夫她师承奥托·兰纳斯太太（Mrs. Otho Lanners），可就连老师本人，也扬不出比这更有力的效果。
「我还在等着领教，你到底是为什么来的呢，内斯塔！」
「我来这儿，是要同你谈谈你的继子詹姆斯·克罗克。」

===Original===
The discipline to which Mrs. Crocker had subjected herself in the matter of the display of emotion saved her from the humiliation of showing surprise. She waved her hand graciously﻿—in the manner of the Duchess of Axminster, a supreme hand-waver﻿—to indicate that she was all attention.
“Your stepson, James Crocker,” repeated Mrs. Pett. “What is it the New York papers call him, Peter?”
Mr. Pett, the human opossum, came to life. He had contrived to create about himself such a defensive atmosphere of nonexistence that now that he reentered the conversation it was as if a corpse had popped out of its tomb like a jack-in-the-box.
Obeying the voice of authority, he pushed the tombstone to one side and poked his head out of the sepulchre.
“Piccadilly Jim!” he murmured apologetically.
“Piccadilly Jim!” said Mrs. Crocker. “It is extremely impertinent of them!”

===Chinese===
克罗克太太在表露情绪一事上施加给自己的修炼，使她免于露出一副惊讶之态、失了体面。她雍容地摆了摆手——摆法一遵阿克斯敏斯特公爵夫人（the Duchess of Axminster），摆手界的至高圣手——表示自己正洗耳恭听。
「你的继子，詹姆斯·克罗克。」佩特太太重复道。「纽约的报纸管他叫什么来着，彼得？」
佩特先生这只人形负鼠复活了。他在自己四周营造出一股防御性的「本人不存在」气氛，功夫如此到家，以致如今他重新加入谈话，就像一具尸体从坟墓里弹簧玩偶似的蹦了出来。
他遵从权威之声，把墓碑推到一旁，从墓穴里探出头来。
「皮卡迪利·吉姆！」他抱歉地喃喃道。
「皮卡迪利·吉姆！」克罗克太太说。「他们放肆得太不成话了！」

===Original===
In spite of his misery, a wan smile appeared on Mr. Pett’s death-mask at this remark.
“They should worry about﻿—!”
“Peter!”
Mr. Pett died again, greatly respected.
“Why should the New York papers refer to James at all?” said Mrs. Crocker.
“Explain, Peter!”

===Chinese===
尽管满腹凄惶，听了这话，佩特先生那张死人脸面上还是掠过一丝惨笑。
「人家才犯不着操心呢——！」
「彼得！」
佩特先生又死了，极尽哀荣。
「纽约的报纸到底为什么要提詹姆斯呢？」克罗克太太说。
「你解释解释，彼得！」

===Original===
Mr. Pett emerged reluctantly from the cerements. He had supposed that Nesta would do the talking.
“Well, he’s a news-item.”
“Why?”
“Well, here’s a boy that’s been a regular fellow﻿—raised in America﻿—done work on a newspaper﻿—suddenly taken off to England to become a London dude﻿—mixing with all the dukes, playing pinochle with the King﻿—naturally they’re interested in him.”
A more agreeable expression came over Mrs. Crocker’s face.
“Of course, that is quite true. One cannot prevent the papers from printing what they wish. So they have published articles about James’ doings in English Society?”

===Chinese===
佩特先生不情愿地从裹尸布里钻出来。他原以为谈话的事自有内斯塔包办。
「呃，他算个新闻人物。」
「为什么？」
「呃，你想想：一个小伙子，本来是个地道的好小伙子——在美国长大——在报馆里做过事——忽然被带到英国去，当起了伦敦纨绔——同各路公爵厮混，跟国王打皮纳克尔牌（pinochle）——人家对他感兴趣，自然不过。」
克罗克太太脸上浮起一阵比较愉快的神色。
「这话当然不假。报纸爱登什么，谁也拦不住。这么说，他们登过文章，讲詹姆斯在英国社交界的种种行状了？」

===Original===
“Doings,” said Mr. Pett, “is right!”
“Something has got to be done about it,” said Mrs. Pett.
Mr. Pett endorsed this.
“Nesta’s going to lose her health if these stories go on,” he said.
Mrs. Crocker raised her eyebrows, but she had hard work to keep a contented smile off her face.
“If you are not above petty jealousy, Nesta.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「『行状』，」佩特先生说，「这个词儿可算说到了点子上！」
「这事非得想个办法不可了。」佩特太太说。
佩特先生对此深表赞同。
「这些报道再登下去，内斯塔的身体要垮的。」他说。
克罗克太太扬起眉毛，可是她费了好大的劲，才把一抹心满意足的微笑从脸上驱赶下去。
「要是你还不至于屑于搞那套小家子气的妒忌，内斯塔……」

===Original===
Mrs. Pett laughed a sharp, metallic laugh.
“It is the disgrace I object to!”
“The disgrace!”
“What else would you call it, Eugenia? Wouldn’t you be ashamed if you opened your Sunday paper and came upon a full page article about your nephew having got intoxicated at the races and fought a bookmaker﻿—having broken up a political meeting﻿—having been sued for breach-of-promise by a barmaid.﻿ ﻿…”
Mrs. Crocker preserved her well-bred calm, but she was shaken. The episodes to which her sister had alluded were ancient history, horrors of the long-dead past, but it seemed that they still lived in print. There and then she registered the resolve to talk to her stepson James when she got hold of him in such a manner as would scourge the offending Adam out of him for once and for all.
“And not only that,” continued Mrs. Pett. “That would be bad enough in itself, but somehow the papers have discovered that I am the boy’s aunt. Two weeks ago they printed my photograph with one of these articles. I suppose they will always do it now. That is why I have come to you. It must stop. And the only way it can be made to stop is by taking your stepson away from London where he is running wild. Peter has most kindly consented to give the boy a position in his office. It is very good of him, for the boy cannot in the nature of things be of any use for a very long time, but we have talked it over and it seems the only course. I have come this morning to ask you to let us take James Crocker back to America with us and keep him out of mischief by giving him honest work. What do you say?”

===Chinese===
佩特太太发出一声尖利的、金属质感的笑。
「我反对的是丢人现眼！」
「丢人现眼！」
「不叫丢人现眼叫什么，尤金妮娅？要是你翻开星期天的报纸，迎面撞上整版文章，说你外甥在赛马场上喝得酩酊大醉、跟赌马经纪人动过武——搅散过一场政治集会——还被酒吧女侍告过悔婚——你脸上挂得住吗……」
克罗克太太保持住了她那教养良好的镇定，可是内里被震动了。她妹妹影射的那些事端，本已是远古史，是早已作古的恐怖旧闻，不料看来它们仍在印刷品里活着。她当场立下决断：等抓到继子詹姆斯，要同他好好谈上一谈，谈法须能把他身上那个作孽的亚当，一劳永逸地鞭挞出去。
「还不止呢。」佩特太太继续说。「光这些本来已经够糟了，可不知怎么，报纸竟查出我是这孩子的姨妈。两周前，他们登这类文章的时候，还配上了我的照片。我料想今后他们回回都要这么干了。所以我才来找你。这事必须叫停。而唯一能叫它停的办法，就是把你继子从他撒野撒欢的伦敦带走。彼得已慨然应允，给孩子在他事务所里安插一个职位。他真是大好人，因为这孩子照事理而言，很长一段时期里根本顶不了什么用，不过我们商量过了，似乎唯有此路一条。我今天上午来，是求你让我们把詹姆斯·克罗克一起带回美国，给他一份正派的活计，叫他再也闯不了祸。你说怎么样？」

===Original===
Mrs. Crocker raised her eyebrows.
“What do you expect me to say? It is utterly preposterous. I have never heard anything so supremely absurd in my life.”
“You refuse?”
“Of course I refuse.”
“I think you are extremely foolish.”
“Indeed!”

===Chinese===
克罗克太太扬起了眉毛。
「你要我说什么？这简直是岂有此理。我平生从没听见过比这更荒谬绝伦的事。」
「你拒绝？」
「我当然拒绝。」
「我看你蠢得无以复加。」
「哦——是嘛！」

===Original===
Mr. Pett cowed in his chair. He was feeling rather like a nervous and peace-loving patron of a wild western saloon who observes two cowboys reach for their hip-pockets. Neither his wife nor his sister-in-law paid any attention to him. The concluding exercises of a duel of the eyes was in progress between them. After some silent, age-long moments, Mrs. Crocker laughed a light laugh.
“Most extraordinary!” she murmured.
Mrs. Pett was in no mood for Anglicisms.
“You know perfectly well, Eugenia,” she said heatedly, “that James Crocker is being ruined here. For his sake, if not for mine﻿—”
Mrs. Crocker laughed another light laugh, one of those offensive rippling things which cause so much annoyance.
“Don’t be so ridiculous, Nesta! Ruined! Really! It is quite true that, a long while ago, when he was much younger and not quite used to the ways of London Society, James was a little wild, but all that sort of thing is over now. He knows”﻿—she paused, setting herself as it were for the punch﻿—“he knows that at any moment the government may decide to give his father a Peerage.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
佩特先生缩在椅子里。他此刻的心情，颇像西部蛮荒酒馆里一位神经质而热爱和平的老主顾，眼看着两个牛仔各自把手伸向裤后袋。他的太太和他的姨姐，谁也不拿正眼瞧他。一场目光对决的收尾功课，正在她们二人之间进行。默默熬过几个洪荒般漫长的时刻之后，克罗克太太轻轻笑了两声。
「真是咄咄怪事！」她喃喃道。
佩特太太这会儿全无心情领教英式做派。
「你肚里雪亮，尤金妮娅，」她火气冲冲地说，「詹姆斯·克罗克在这儿正在走向毁灭。就算不为我着想，也该为他——」
克罗克太太又轻笑了一声——正是那种一波波漾开、最教人窝火的可恶东西。
「别这么荒唐，内斯塔！毁灭！真是的！不错，很久以前，他年纪还小、又不很习惯伦敦社交界的规矩时，詹姆斯是荒唐过一阵，可那一套如今全都过去了。他知道」——她顿了顿，仿佛凝神蓄势，准备发出致命一击——「他知道，政府随时可能决定，给他父亲一个贵族爵位……」

===Original===
The blow went home. A quite audible gasp escaped her stricken sister.
“What!”
Mrs. Crocker placed two ringed fingers before her mouth in order not to hide a languid yawn.
“Yes. Didn’t you know? But of course you live so out of the world. Oh yes, it is extremely probable that Mr. Crocker’s name will appear in the next Honours List. He is very highly thought of by the Powers. So naturally James is quite aware that he must behave in a suitable manner. He is a dear boy! He was handicapped at first by getting into the wrong set, but now his closest friend is Lord Percy Whipple, the second son of the Duke of Devizes, who is one of the most eminent men in the kingdom and a personal friend of the Premier.”
Mrs. Pett was in bad shape under this rain of titles, but she rallied herself to reply in kind.
“Indeed?” she said. “I should like to meet him. I have no doubt he knows our great friend, Lord Wisbeach.”

===Chinese===
这一击正中要害。挨了这一击的妹妹倒抽了一口气，声音清晰可闻。
「什么！」
克罗克太太将两根戴戒指的指头掩在唇前，以免遮住一个慵懒的呵欠。
「是啊。你不知道吗？也难怪，你们住得那样与世隔绝。哦没错，克罗克先生的名字极有可能出现在下一期授勋名录上。当权诸公对他评价极高。所以詹姆斯自然十分明白，举止必须处处得体。他是个可人疼的孩子！起初他吃了交错朋友圈子的亏，如今他最贴心的朋友是珀西·惠普尔勋爵（Lord Percy Whipple），德维泽斯公爵（the Duke of Devizes）的次子——公爵乃本王国最显赫的人物之一，又是首相的私交。」
在这场头衔的弹雨之下，佩特太太被打得七零八落，但她重整旗鼓，照样回敬了一发。
「是吗？」她说。「我倒愿意见见他。他准认得我们的至交威斯比奇勋爵。」

===Original===
Mrs. Crocker was a little taken aback. She had not supposed that her sister had even this small shot in her locker.
“Do you know Lord Wisbeach?” she said.
“Oh yes,” replied Mrs. Pett, beginning to feel a little better. “We have been seeing him every day. He always says that he looks on my house as quite a home. He knows so few people in New York. It has been a great comfort to him, I think, knowing us.”
Mrs. Crocker had had time now to recover her poise.
“Poor dear Wizzy!” she said languidly.
Mrs. Pett started.

===Chinese===
克罗克太太微微一怔。她没料到妹妹的弹药箱里，竟还藏着这么一发小子弹。
「你认识威斯比奇勋爵？」她说。
「哦，认识。」佩特太太答道，自觉元气稍有恢复。「我们天天见得着他。他总说，他把我的宅子当成个家一样看待。他在纽约认得的人实在太少。我想，认得了我们，对他是个莫大的安慰。」
此刻，克罗克太太已重新稳住了阵脚。
「可怜的、亲爱的小威齐！」她懒洋洋地说。
佩特太太悚然一惊。

===Original===
“What!”
“I suppose he is still the same dear, stupid, shiftless fellow? He left here with the intention of travelling round the world, and he has stopped in New York! How like him!”
“Do you know Lord Wisbeach?” demanded Mrs. Pett.
Mrs. Crocker raised her eyebrows.
“Know him? Why, I suppose, after Lord Percy Whipple, he is James’ most intimate friend!”
Mrs. Pett rose. She was dignified even in defeat. She collected Ogden and Mr. Pett with an eye which even Ogden could see was not to be trifled with. She uttered no word.

===Chinese===
「什么！」
「我猜他还是那副又可爱、又愚蠢、又没长性的老样子吧？他离开此地的时候，立志要周游世界，结果在纽约就停了窝！多像他呀！」
「你认识威斯比奇勋爵？」佩特太太质问道。
克罗克太太扬起了眉毛。
「认识？嘿，我想，除了珀西·惠普尔勋爵，就该数他是詹姆斯最贴心的朋友了！」
佩特太太站起身来。纵是败局，她亦败得尊严。她用一道眼色把奥格登和佩特先生归拢到一起——那道眼色的分量，连奥格登都看得出不是闹着玩的。她一个字也没说。

===Original===
“Must you really go?” said Mrs. Crocker. “It was sweet of you to bother to come all the way from America like this. So strange to meet anyone from America nowadays. Most extraordinary!”
The *cortège* left the room in silence. Mrs. Crocker had touched the bell, but the mourners did not wait for the arrival of Bayliss. They were in no mood for the formalities of polite Society. They wanted to be elsewhere, and they wanted to be there quick. The front door had closed behind them before the butler reached the morning-room.
“Bayliss,” said Mrs. Crocker with happy, shining face, “send for the car to come round at once.”
“Very good, madam.”
“Is Mr. James up yet?”
“I believe not, madam.”

===Chinese===
「当真非走不可吗？」克罗克太太说。「难为你费心，这么大老远地从美国跑一趟。如今竟能碰上个美国来的人，真是稀罕。真是咄咄怪事！」
这支*送葬行列*（cortège）默然离开了房间。克罗克太太已经按过铃，但哀悼的人们等不及贝利斯驾到。他们全无心情讲究上流社会的繁文缛节。他们只愿身在别处，而且恨不得立刻就到。管家还没走到起居室，前门已在他们身后关上了。
「贝利斯，」克罗克太太满面春风、光彩照人地说，「叫车立刻开过来。」
「好的，夫人。」
「詹姆斯先生起来了吗？」
「应该还没有，夫人。」

===Original===
Mrs. Crocker went upstairs to her room. If Bayliss had not been within earshot, she would probably have sung a bar or two. Her amiability extended even to her stepson, though she had not altered her intention of speaking eloquently to him on certain matters when she could get hold of him. That, however, could wait. For the moment, she felt in vein for a gentle drive in the Park.
A few minutes after she had disappeared, there was a sound of slow footsteps on the stairs, and a young man came down into the hall. Bayliss, who had finished telephoning to the garage for Mrs. Crocker’s limousine and was about to descend to those lower depths where he had his being, turned, and a grave smile of welcome played over his face.
“Good morning, Mr. James,” he said.

===Chinese===
克罗克太太上楼回房去了。倘若贝利斯不在听力所及的范围之内，她多半要高歌上一两句。她的和颜悦色甚至普泽到了继子身上，尽管她并未更改计划：一旦抓到他，还要就某些事项对他发表一篇雄辩的训话。不过那可以等。此刻她雅兴大发，只想到公园里悠悠地兜一趟风。
她走后没几分钟，楼梯上响起一阵迟缓的脚步声，一个年轻人下楼来到门厅。贝利斯刚给车库打完电话、叫克罗克太太的豪华轿车备好，正要下到楼下那片他赖以生存的底层天地去，闻声转过身来，庄重的欢迎微笑在他脸上荡漾开来。
「早安，詹姆斯先生。」他说。

## IV / 四

Jimmy's Disturbing News / 吉米的惊人消息

===Original===
Jimmy Crocker was a tall and well-knit young man who later on in the day would no doubt be at least passably good-looking. At the moment an unbecoming pallor marred his face, and beneath his eyes were marks that suggested that he had slept little and ill. He stood at the foot of the stairs, yawning cavernously.

“Bayliss,” he said, “have you been painting yourself yellow?”

“No, sir.”

“Strange! Your face looks a bright gamboge to me, and your outlines wobble. Bayliss, never mix your drinks. I say this to you as a friend. Is there anyone in the morning-room?”

“No, Mr. James.”

“Speak softly, Bayliss, for I am not well. I am conscious of a strange weakness. Lead me to the morning-room, then, and lay me gently on a sofa. These are the times that try men’s souls.”

===Chinese===
吉米·克罗克（Jimmy Crocker）是个高挑而结实的青年，待到当天晚些时候，无疑又会恢复到至少不难看的模样。此刻，一脸不合时宜的苍白毁了他的面容，眼睛底下的印记表明他睡得又少又糟。他站在楼梯脚下，哈欠打得山洞一般。

「贝利斯，」他说，「你是不是把自己漆成黄色了？」

「没有，先生。」

「奇怪！在我看来，你的脸是一派鲜亮的藤黄色，轮廓还一个劲儿地晃悠。贝利斯啊，千万别把几种酒掺着喝。我这是拿你当朋友才说这话。起居室里有人吗？」

「没有，詹姆斯先生。」

「说话轻着点儿，贝利斯，我不大舒服。我只觉得浑身没来由地发虚。那就领我去起居室吧，再把我轻轻安放在沙发上。如今正是考验人们灵魂的时代啊。」

===Original===
The sun was now shining strongly through the windows of the morning-room. Bayliss lowered the shades. Jimmy Crocker sank onto the sofa, and closed his eyes.

“Bayliss.”

“Sir?”

“A conviction is stealing over me that I am about to expire.”

“Shall I bring you a little breakfast, Mr. James?”

A strong shudder shook Jimmy.

===Chinese===
此刻，太阳正劲头十足地照进起居室的窗户。贝利斯放下了遮帘。吉米·克罗克瘫进沙发，闭上了眼睛。

「贝利斯。」

「先生？」

「一个信念正悄悄爬上我的心头：我眼看就要咽气了。」

「要我给您端一小点早餐来吗，詹姆斯先生？」

一阵剧烈的战栗晃动了吉米的全身。

===Original===
“Don’t be flippant, Bayliss,” he protested. “Try to cure yourself of this passion for being funny at the wrong time. Your comedy is good, but tact is a finer quality than humour. Perhaps you think I have forgotten that morning when I was feeling just as I do today and you came to my bedside and asked me if I would like a nice rasher of ham. I haven’t and I never shall. You may bring me a brandy-and-soda. Not a large one. A couple of bathtubs full will be enough.”

“Very good, Mr. James.”

“And now leave me, Bayliss, for I would be alone. I have to make a series of difficult and exhaustive tests to ascertain whether I am still alive.”

When the butler had gone, Jimmy adjusted the cushions, closed his eyes, and remained for a space in a state of coma. He was trying, as well as an exceedingly severe headache would permit, to recall the salient events of the previous night. At present his memories refused to solidify. They poured about in his brain in a fluid and formless condition, exasperating to one who sought for hard facts.

It seemed strange to Jimmy that the shadowy and inchoate vision of a combat, a fight, a brawl of some kind persisted in flitting about in the recesses of his mind, always just far enough away to elude capture. The absurdity of the thing annoyed him. A man has either indulged in a fight overnight or he has not indulged in a fight overnight. There can be no middle course. That he should be uncertain on the point was ridiculous. Yet, try as he would, he could not be sure. There were moments when he seemed on the very verge of settling the matter, and then some invisible person would meanly insert a red-hot corkscrew in the top of his head and begin to twist it, and this would interfere with calm thought. He was still in a state of uncertainty when Bayliss returned, bearing healing liquids on a tray.

===Chinese===
「别这么轻薄，贝利斯。」他抗议道，「想法子治一治你这毛病——总爱在不该逗乐的当口犯逗。你的喜剧功夫确实不赖，可是分寸感比幽默是更高级的品质。你也许以为我忘了那天早上——我那会儿跟今天一样难受，你走到我床边，问我想不想来一片喷香的火腿。我没忘，永远也忘不了。你可以给我端杯白兰地苏打来。不用大杯。满满两浴缸就尽够了。」

「好的，詹姆斯先生。」

「现在你退下吧，贝利斯，我要独自待着。我还得做上一系列艰巨而彻底的测试，查明自己是否依然活着。」

管家退下之后，吉米调了调靠垫，闭上眼睛，有一阵子处于昏迷状态。他在竭力回忆昨夜的重大事件，只可惜一场极其剧烈的头痛处处掣肘。眼下，他的记忆拒绝凝固成形，只以液态而无形的姿态在脑子里四下流淌，直叫一个一心寻求铁的事实的人火冒三丈。

让吉米觉得蹊跷的是，一场搏斗、干仗、某种混战的影影绰绰、尚未成形的幻象，总在他心底深处扑棱来扑棱去，偏偏又始终保持那么一点点距离，教人逮它不着。这件事的荒唐劲儿叫他恼火。一个人隔夜要么打过一架，要么没打过一架，绝无中间路线可走。他居然对此心里没底，未免可笑。然而任凭他怎么使劲，就是拿不准。有那么几刻，他眼看就要把这桩公案弄个水落石出，随即便有个看不见的家伙卑鄙地把一根烧红的螺丝锥插进他头顶，开始拧动起来，平静的思考就此告吹。他仍在这般没着没落的状态里，贝利斯回来了，端着的托盘上盛着一汪疗伤圣液。

===Original===
“Shall I set it beside you, sir?”

Jimmy opened one eye.

“Indubitably. No mean word, that, Bayliss, for the morning after. Try it yourself next time. Bayliss, who let me in this morning?”

“Let you in, sir?”

“Precisely. I was out and now I am in. Obviously I must have passed the front door somehow. This is logic.”

“I fancy you let yourself in, Mr. James, with your key.”

===Chinese===
「我把它搁在您手边吗，先生？」

吉米睁开了一只眼睛。

「无可置疑地。这词儿可不含糊，贝利斯，正配宿醉后的早晨。下回你自己也试试。贝利斯，今天早上是谁放我进来的？」

「放您进来，先生？」

「正是。我先前人在外面，如今人在里面。显然，我必定以某种方式通过了正门。这就叫逻辑。」

「我想是您自己开门进来的，詹姆斯先生，用的您自己的钥匙。」

===Original===
“That would seem to indicate that I was in a state of icy sobriety. Yet, if such is the case, how is it that I can’t remember whether I murdered somebody or not last night? It isn’t the sort of thing your sober man would lightly forget. Have you ever murdered anybody, Bayliss?”

“No, sir.”

“Well, if you had, you would remember it next morning?”

“I imagine so, Mr. James.”

“Well, it’s a funny thing, but I can’t get rid of the impression that at some point in my researches into the night life of London yestreen I fell upon some person to whom I had never been introduced and committed mayhem upon his person.”

===Chinese===
「那似乎表明，我当时处于一种冰镇过的清醒状态。可是，倘若当真如此，我怎么竟想不起来昨晚自己到底杀没杀人？这种事，可不是清醒人士会随随便便忘掉的。你杀过人吗，贝利斯？」

「没有，先生。」

「那么，你要是杀过，第二天早上总还记得吧？」

「我想是的，詹姆斯先生。」

「唉，说来也怪，我总甩不掉这么个印象：昨夜晚间，我在考察伦敦夜生活的某个当口，扑倒了一位素未谋面的人士，并对他人身犯下了暴行。」

===Original===
It seemed to Bayliss that the time had come to impart to Mr. James a piece of news which he had supposed would require no imparting. He looked down upon his young master’s recumbent form with a grave commiseration. It was true that he had never been able to tell with any certainty whether Mr. James intended the statements he made to be taken literally or not, but on the present occasion he seemed to have spoken seriously and to be genuinely at a loss to recall an episode over the printed report of which the entire domestic staff had been gloating ever since the arrival of the halfpenny morning paper to which they subscribed.

“Do you really mean it, Mr. James?” he enquired cautiously.

“Mean what?”

“You have really forgotten that you were engaged in a fracas last night at the Six Hundred Club?”

Jimmy sat up with a jerk, staring at this omniscient man. Then the movement having caused a renewal of the operations of the red-hot corkscrew, he fell back again with a groan.

“Was I? How on earth did you know? Why should you know all about it when I can’t remember a thing? It was my fault, not yours.”

===Chinese===
在贝利斯看来，此刻该向詹姆斯先生透露一则消息了——他原以为这消息根本用不着人透露。他低头端详着年轻主人横陈的身形，神情庄重，满怀怜悯。诚然，他从来吃不准詹姆斯先生说出口的话究竟是不是打算让人照字面理解，可这一回，他似乎说得郑重其事，而且当真想不起那桩事件——他们订阅的那份半便士晨报一到，全体家仆便对着报上那段铅字报道乐不可支，一直咂摸到现在。

「您这话是当真的吗，詹姆斯先生？」他小心翼翼地问道。

「什么当真不当真？」

「您当真忘了，昨天夜里您在六百俱乐部（Six Hundred Club）跟人干了一架？」

吉米猛地坐起，直勾勾瞪着这位无所不知的奇人。紧接着，这个动作害得那根烧红的螺丝锥重新开工，他又呻吟一声倒了回去。

「我打架了？你究竟是怎么知道的？我自己一件事都想不起来，你怎么倒知道得底儿掉？惹祸的是我，又不是你。」

===Original===
“There is quite a long report of it in today’s *Daily Sun*, Mr. James.”

“A report? In the *Sun*?”

“Half a column, Mr. James. Would you like me to fetch the paper? I have it in my pantry.”

“I should say so. Trot a quick heat back with it. This wants looking into.”

===Chinese===
「今天的《每日太阳报》（*Daily Sun*）上有好长一篇报道呢，詹姆斯先生。」

「报道？登在《太阳报》（*Sun*）上？」

「足足半栏呢，詹姆斯先生。要我去把报纸取来吗？就搁在我的配膳室里。」

「那还用说。这就撒开腿飞跑一趟把它取来。此事不可不查。」

===Original===
Bayliss retired, to return immediately with the paper. Jimmy took it, gazed at it, and handed it back.

“I overestimated my powers. It can’t be done. Have you any important duties at the moment, Bayliss?”

“No, sir.”

“Perhaps you wouldn’t mind reading me the bright little excerpt, then?”

“Certainly, sir.”

“It will be good practice for you. I am convinced I am going to be a confirmed invalid for the rest of my life, and it will be part of your job to sit at my bedside and read to me. By the way, does the paper say who the party of the second part was? Who was the citizen with whom I went to the mat?”

===Chinese===
贝利斯退出去，转眼间便拿着报纸回来了。吉米接过，端详了一阵，又递了回去。

「我高估了自己的本领。这事儿办不到。你这会儿有什么要紧的公务吗，贝利斯？」

「没有，先生。」

「那么，也许你不介意把这则精彩的短文念给我听听？」

「当然可以，先生。」

「这对你是个难得的练习。我确信，我这后半辈子要做一名铁定治不好的病人了，坐在病榻旁给我念书念报，将成为你分内的职责。对了，报上有没有说『乙方』是哪一位？跟我上垫子交手的那位公民，究竟是谁？」

===Original===
“Lord Percy Whipple, Mr. James.”

“Lord who?”

“Lord Percy Whipple.”

“Never heard of him. Carry on, Bayliss.”

Jimmy composed himself to listen, yawning.

===Chinese===
「珀西·惠普尔勋爵（Lord Percy Whipple），詹姆斯先生。」

「什么勋爵？」

「珀西·惠普尔勋爵。」

「从没听说过这号人物。继续念吧，贝利斯。」

吉米打着哈欠，安顿好身子，准备听讲。

## V / 五

The Morning After / 宿醉翌晨

===Original===
Bayliss took a spectacle-case from the recesses of his costume, opened it, took out a pair of gold-rimmed glasses, dived into the jungle again, came out with a handkerchief, polished the spectacles, put them on his nose, closed the case, restored it to its original position, replaced the handkerchief, and took up the paper.

“Why the hesitation, Bayliss? Why the coyness?” enquired Jimmy, lying with closed eyes. “Begin!”

“I was adjusting my glasses, sir.”

“All set now?”

“Yes, sir. Shall I read the headlines first?”

“Read everything.”

===Chinese===
贝利斯从他那身行头的深处摸出一只眼镜盒，打开盒盖，取出一副金丝边眼镜，随后再度潜入那片丛林，摸出一块手帕，把眼镜擦拭一番，架到鼻梁上，合上眼镜盒，放回原位，收好手帕，然后拿起了报纸。

「干吗这么迟疑，贝利斯？干吗这么羞羞答答？」吉米闭着眼睛躺着，问道。「开始！」

「我刚才是在调眼镜，先生。」

「这回都归置妥当了？」

「是的，先生。先念大标题吗？」

「全都念。」

===Original===
The butler cleared his throat.

“Good Heavens, Bayliss,” moaned Jimmy, starting, “don’t gargle. Have a heart! Go on!”

Bayliss began to read.

Fracas in Fashionable Nightclub

					Sprigs of Nobility Brawl

Jimmy opened his eyes, interested.

===Chinese===
管家清了清嗓子。

「老天爷，贝利斯，」吉米惊起，哼哼道，「别在那儿咕噜咕噜漱嗓子。积德行好吧！快念！」

贝利斯开始念了。

上流夜总会爆发混战

贵族嫩枝大打出手

吉米来了兴趣，睁开眼睛。

===Original===
“Am I a sprig of nobility?”

“It is what the paper says, sir.”

“We live and learn. Carry on.”

The butler started to clear his throat, but checked himself.

===Chinese===
「我是一根贵族嫩枝？」

「报上是这么写的，先生。」

「真是活到老学到老。接着念。」

管家又开始清嗓子，但硬生生忍住了。

===Original===
Sensational International Contest

Battling Percy (England)

				v.

				Cyclone Jim (America)

				Full description by our expert

Jimmy sat up.

===Chinese===
轰动全球的国际大赛

「斗士」珀西（英格兰）

对阵

「旋风」吉姆（美国）

本报专家全程详报

吉米坐直了身子。

===Original===
“Bayliss, you’re indulging that distorted sense of humour of yours again. That isn’t in the paper?”

“Yes, sir. Very large headlines.”

Jimmy groaned.

“Bayliss, I’ll give you a piece of advice which may be useful to you when you grow up. Never go about with newspaper men. It all comes back to me. Out of pure kindness of heart I took young Bill Blake of the *Sun* to supper at the Six Hundred last night. This is my reward. I suppose he thinks it funny. Newspaper men are a low lot, Bayliss.”

“Shall I go on, sir?”

===Chinese===
「贝利斯，你那个歪扭的幽默感又发作了。报上不会真登了这个吧？」

「登了，先生。标题字号大得很。」

吉米呻吟一声。

「贝利斯，我赠你一句忠告，等你长大成人说不定用得着：千万别跟报馆的人厮混。我全想起来了。昨晚我纯粹出于心肠好，带《太阳报》（*Sun*）的小伙子比尔·布莱克去六百俱乐部吃夜宵。这就是我的报答。我估计他还觉得挺逗。报馆的人是一帮下等货色，贝利斯。」

「接着念吗，先生？」

===Original===
“Most doubtless. Let me hear all.”

Bayliss resumed. He was one of those readers who, whether their subject be a murder case or a funny anecdote, adopt a measured and sepulchral delivery which gives a suggestion of tragedy and horror to whatever they read. At the church which he attended on Sundays, of which he was one of the most influential and respected members, children would turn pale and snuggle up to their mothers when Bayliss read the lessons. Young Mr. Blake’s account of the overnight proceedings at the Six Hundred Club he rendered with a gloomy gusto more marked even than his wont. It had a topical interest for him which urged him to extend himself.

“At an early hour this morning, when our myriad readers were enjoying that refreshing and brain-restoring sleep so necessary to the proper appreciation of the *Daily Sun* at the breakfast table, one of the most interesting sporting events of the season was being pulled off at the Six Hundred Club in Regent Street, where, after three rounds of fast exchanges, James B. Crocker, the well-known American welterweight scrapper, succeeded in stopping Lord Percy Whipple, second son of the Duke of Devizes, better known as the Pride of Old England. Once again the superiority of the American over the English style of boxing was demonstrated. Battling Percy has a kind heart, but Cyclone Jim packs the punch.

“The immediate cause of the encounter had to do with a disputed table, which each gladiator claimed to have engaged in advance over the telephone.”

“I begin to remember,” said Jimmy meditatively. “A pill with butter-coloured hair tried to jump my claim. Honeyed words proving fruitless, I soaked him on the jaw. It may be that I was not wholly myself. I seem to remember an animated session at the Empire earlier in the evening, which may have impaired my self-control. Proceed!”

===Chinese===
「毫无疑问，念。让我从头听到尾。」

贝利斯接着往下念。世上有一路读报人，不论念的是凶杀案还是逗乐的段子，一律采取那种四平八稳、坟腔墓调的念法，不管念什么，都给它添上一层惨剧加恐怖的色彩——贝利斯正是此中翘楚。每逢星期天，在他做礼拜的那座教堂——他是那里最有势力、最受敬重的会众之一——只要轮到贝利斯诵读经文，孩子们便个个脸色煞白，直往母亲怀里蹭。至于小布莱克先生这篇关于六百俱乐部昨夜盛况的报道，他念得更是阴风阵阵、津津有味，比平素还多添三分卖力。这桩新闻于他正赶着时效，不由得他不施展浑身解数。

「今日凌晨，正当本报万千读者享受着那一场提神醒脑、养精蓄锐的酣睡——要在早餐桌上恰如其分地品读《每日太阳报》（*Daily Sun*），这一觉必不可少——摄政街六百俱乐部正上演本季度最有趣的体育盛事之一：经过三个回合的快速对攻，知名的美国次中量级打手詹姆斯·B·克罗克，成功将珀西·惠普尔勋爵击倒在地——此君乃德维泽斯公爵次子，江湖人称『旧英格兰的骄傲』。美式拳击对英式拳击的优越性，由此再度得到证明。『斗士』珀西心肠软，『旋风』吉姆拳头硬。

「这场交锋的直接起因，是一张归属成疑的桌子——两位角斗士各自声称，已提前打电话订下了它。」

「我开始想起来了，」吉米若有所思地说。「有个满头黄油色头发的小药丸想抢占我的地盘。甜言蜜语宣告无效之后，我一拳结结实实印在他下巴上。当时我恐怕不完全是平常的自己。我似乎记得，头天傍晚我在帝国音乐厅先过了一段热火朝天的时光，那兴许削弱了我的自制力。继续！」

===Original===
“One word leading to others, which in their turn led to several more, Cyclone Jim struck Battling Percy on what our rude forefathers were accustomed to describe as the mazzard, and the gong sounded for

“Round One

“Both men came up fresh and eager to mix things, though it seems only too probable that they had already been mixing more things than was good for them. Battling Percy tried a right swing which got home on a waiter. Cyclone Jim put in a rapid one-two punch which opened a large gash in the atmosphere. Both men sparred cautiously, being hampered in their movements by the fact, which neither had at this stage of the proceedings perceived, that they were on opposite sides of the disputed table. A clever Fitzsimmons’ shift on the part of the Battler removed this obstacle, and some brisk work ensued in neutral territory. Percy landed twice without a return. The Battler’s round by a shade.

“Round Two

“The Cyclone came out of his corner with a rush, getting home on the Battler’s shirtfront and following it up with a right to the chin. Percy swung wildly and upset a bottle of champagne on a neighbouring table. A good rally followed, both men doing impressive in-fighting. The Cyclone landed three without a return. The Cyclone’s round.

===Chinese===
「一言引出又一言，那一言又引出好几言，随后『旋风』吉姆一拳打在『斗士』珀西的——用我们粗野的老祖宗惯常的说法——脑袋瓜上，锣声随之敲响，宣告

「第一回合

「两位拳手精神抖擞地登场，巴不得大『掺和』一场——尽管十有八九，他们事先已经掺和进去的东西超出了有益健康的剂量。『斗士』珀西试了一记右摆拳，稳稳落在一名侍者身上。『旋风』吉姆打出一套疾速的一二连拳，在空气里豁开一道大口子。两人小心翼翼地相互试探，动作大受掣肘，原因是——在这个阶段双方都还没瞧出来——他们正隔着那张争议桌子分立两端。『斗士』施展一记巧妙的菲茨西蒙斯式移位，扫除了这一障碍，随后在中立地带展开一阵利落交火。珀西两度命中，未遭回敬。本回合『斗士』以毫厘之差占先。

「第二回合

「『旋风』从场角猛冲而出，一拳命中『斗士』的胸襟，紧接着一记右拳直取下巴。珀西挥拳落空，反倒掀翻了邻桌一瓶香槟。随后是一轮漂亮的拉锯，两人的近身缠斗颇见功夫。『旋风』三度命中，未遭回敬。本回合『旋风』胜。

===Original===
“Round Three

“Percy came up weak, seeming to be overtrained. The Cyclone waded in, using both hands effectively. The Battler fell into a clinch, but the Cyclone broke away and, measuring his distance, picked up a haymaker from the floor and put it over. Percy down and out.

“Interviewed by our representative after the fight, Cyclone Jim said: ‘The issue was never in doubt. I was handicapped at the outset by the fact that I was under the impression that I was fighting three twin-brothers, and I missed several opportunities of putting over the winning wallop by attacking the outside ones. It was only in the second round that I decided to concentrate my assault on the one in the middle, when the affair speedily came to a conclusion. I shall not adopt pugilism as a profession. The prizes are attractive, but it is too much like work.’ ”

Bayliss ceased, and silence fell upon the room.

===Chinese===
「第三回合

「珀西登场时脚下发虚，像是训练过了头。『旋风』挥师直进，双拳左右开弓，成效显著。『斗士』抱住对方缠斗一番，但『旋风』挣脱出来，目测好距离，从地板上捞起一记大摆拳，干干净净送了过去。珀西倒地不起。

「赛后本报代表采访『旋风』吉姆，他说：『胜负从来不成问题。开局我吃了点亏，因为我一直以为自己是在跟三胞胎兄弟交手，有几次本可制胜的重拳机会，都白白浪费在攻打边上那两位身上了。直到第二回合，我才决定集中火力专攻中间那个，战局随即迅速了结。我不会把拳击当作职业。奖金固然诱人，可这活儿实在太像干活了。』」

贝利斯念完了，一片寂静笼罩了房间。

===Original===
“Is that all?”

“That is all, sir.”

“And about enough.”

“Very true, sir.”

“You know, Bayliss,” said Jimmy thoughtfully, rolling over on the couch, “life is peculiar, not to say odd. You never know what is waiting for you round the corner. You start the day with the fairest prospects, and before nightfall everything is as rocky and ding-basted as stig tossed full of doodlegammon. Why is this, Bayliss?”

===Chinese===
「就这些？」

「就这些，先生。」

「也够瞧的了。」

「千真万确，先生。」

「你知道吗，贝利斯，」吉米若有所思地说，在沙发上一个翻身，「生活这东西真是离奇，简直可以说古怪。你永远不知道拐角后面等着你的是什么。你一大早揣着最光明的前景出门，还没到天黑，一切就已变得坎坷嶙峋、混账透顶，活像一只被扔满了杜德尔加蒙的斯提格。这是为什么呢，贝利斯？」

===Original===
“I couldn’t say, sir.”

“Look at me. I go out to spend a happy evening, meaning no harm to anyone, and I come back all blue with the blood of the aristocracy. We now come to a serious point. Do you think my lady stepmother has read that sporting chronicle?”

“I fancy not, Mr. James.”

“On what do you base these words of comfort?”

“Mrs. Crocker does not read the halfpenny papers, sir.”

===Chinese===
「我说不上来，先生。」

「瞧瞧我。我出门本想消磨一个快活的夜晚，对谁都无敌意，回来时却让贵族的血染得浑身发蓝。现在咱们谈一个严肃的问题。你以为我那位继母大人读到那份体育纪事了么？」

「我想还没有，詹姆斯先生。」

「你这句安慰话，有何根据？」

「克罗克太太不看半便士报，先生。」

===Original===
“True! She does not. I had forgotten. On the other hand the probability that she will learn about the little incident from other sources is great. I think the merest prudence suggests that I keep out of the way for the time being, lest I be fallen upon and questioned. I am not equal to being questioned this morning. I have a headache which starts at the soles of my feet and gets worse all the way up. Where is my stepmother?”

“Mrs. Crocker is in her room, Mr. James. She ordered the car to be brought round at once. It should be here at any moment now, sir. I think Mrs. Crocker intends to visit the Park before luncheon.”

“Is she lunching out?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Then, if I pursue the excellent commonsense tactics of the lesser sand-eel, which as you doubtless know buries itself tail upwards in the mud on hearing the baying of the eel-hounds and remains in that position till the danger is past, I shall be able to postpone an interview. Should you be questioned as to my whereabouts, inflate your chest and reply in a clear and manly voice that I have gone out, you know not where. May I rely on your benevolent neutrality, Bayliss?”

“Very good, Mr. James.”

===Chinese===
「不错！她不看。我把这一茬给忘了。但另一方面，她从别的渠道听说这桩小小意外的可能性也极大。我认为，哪怕纯粹出于谨慎，眼下我也该暂避锋芒，免得被人扑住盘问。今天上午我实在无力应答盘问。我的头疼是从脚底板起头的，一路向上，越走越凶。我继母在哪儿？」

「克罗克太太在她房里，詹姆斯先生。她吩咐马上把车开过来，这会儿应该随时候着了，先生。我想克罗克太太打算午饭前去公园散散心。」

「她午饭在外头吃？」

「是的，先生。」

「那么，只要我效法小玉筋鱼那套绝妙而合乎常识的战术——你想必知道，那鱼一听见鳗猎犬的吠叫，便把自己尾巴朝天埋进泥里，保持这个姿势直到危险过去——我就能把一场会面往后推延。倘若有人问起我的行踪，你就挺起胸膛，用清亮而男子汉的嗓音答道：他出门去了，去向不明。我能仰仗你善意的中立吗，贝利斯？」

「好的，詹姆斯先生。」

===Original===
“I think I will go and sit in my father’s den. A man may lie hid there with some success as a rule.”

Jimmy heaved himself painfully off the sofa, blinked, and set out for the den, where his father, in a deep armchair, was smoking a restful pipe and reading the portions of the daily papers which did not deal with the game of cricket.

Mr. Crocker’s den was a small room at the back of the house. It was not luxurious, and it looked out onto a blank wall, but it was the spot he liked best in all that vast pile which had once echoed to the tread of titled shoes; for, as he sometimes observed to his son, it had the distinction of being the only room on the ground floor where a fellow could move without stubbing his toe on a countess or an honourable. In this peaceful backwater he could smoke a pipe, put his feet up, take off his coat, and generally indulge in that liberty and pursuit of happiness to which the Constitution entitles a freeborn American. Nobody ever came there except Jimmy and himself.

He did not suspend his reading at his son’s entrance. He muttered a welcome through the clouds, but he did not raise his eyes. Jimmy took the other armchair, and began to smoke silently. It was the unwritten law of the den that soothing silence rather than aimless chatter should prevail. It was not until a quarter of an hour had passed that Mr. Crocker dropped his paper and spoke.

===Chinese===
「我想我还是去我爸的书房坐坐。一般说来，一个人躲在那儿，成功率还相当可观。」

吉米龇牙咧嘴、费了九牛二虎之力才从沙发上撑起身来，眨了眨眼，向书房进发。他父亲正坐在书房一把深陷的扶手椅里，抽着一管安神烟斗，读着日报里不谈板球的那几部分。

克罗克先生的书房是屋后一间小屋。屋子算不上豪华，窗外正对一堵光秃秃的墙，但在那座曾经回荡过有头衔的鞋履声的巍峨大宅里，这是他最中意的一隅。因为，正如他偶尔对儿子说的那样，这间屋子有一桩与众不同之处：它是底层唯一一间，让人走路不必被哪位伯爵夫人或哪位「尊敬的」大人绊了脚趾头。在这条太平的后水湾里，他可以抽抽烟斗，把脚翘起来，脱掉外衣，尽情享受宪法赋予一个生而自由的美国人的那种自由，以及对幸福的追求。除了吉米和他自己，从来没人踏进这里。

儿子进来，他也没有中断读报。他在烟雾缭绕中咕哝了一句欢迎的话，眼睛却不曾抬起。吉米坐进另一把扶手椅，默默抽起烟来。书房里有一条不成文的规矩：此处盛行的是安神的静默，而非漫无边际的闲聊。直到一刻钟过去，克罗克先生才放下报纸，开了口。

===Original===
“Say, Jimmy, I want to talk to you.”

“Say on. You have our ear.”

“Seriously.”

“Continue﻿—always, however, keeping before you the fact that I am a sick man. Last night was a wild night on the moors, dad.”

“It’s about your stepmother. She was talking at breakfast about you. She’s sore at you for giving Spike Dillon lunch at the Carlton. You oughtn’t to have taken him there, Jimmy. That’s what got her goat. She was there with a bunch of swells and they had to sit and listen to Spike talking about his half-scissors hook.”

===Chinese===
「我说，吉米，我有话跟你讲。」

「往下讲。咱的耳朵已经交给你了。」

「正经点。」

「那就继续——不过，请始终把一件事摆在眼前：我是个病人。昨夜可是荒原上狂野的一夜啊，老爸。」

「是关于你继母的事。今天吃早饭的时候，她说起你来着。你请斯派克·狄龙在卡尔顿饭店吃午饭，她为这个火冒三丈。你本不该带他去那儿，吉米。坏就坏在这件事上。她当时正跟一帮达官显贵坐在一处，只好干坐着听斯派克大谈他那手半剪刀勾拳。」

===Original===
“What’s their kick against Spike’s half-scissors hook? It’s a darned good one.”

“She said she was going to speak to you about it. I thought I’d let you know.”

“Thanks, dad. But was that all?”

“All.”

“All that she was going to speak to me about? Sure there was nothing else?”

“She didn’t say anything about anything else.”

===Chinese===
「他们跟斯派克的半剪刀勾拳有什么过不去的？那可是顶顶好的一拳。」

「她说要找你谈谈这事儿。我想还是先给你递个信儿。」

「多谢，老爸。不过就这些？」

「就这些。」

「她要找我谈的就这一件事？敢肯定没别的了？」

「她没提别的什么。」

===Original===
“Then she *doesn’t* know! Fine!”

Mr. Crocker’s feet came down from the mantelpiece with a crash.

“Jimmy! You haven’t been raising Cain again?”

“No, no, dad. Nothing serious. High-spirited Young Patrician stuff, the sort of thing that’s expected of a fellow in my position.”

Mr. Crocker was not to be comforted.

===Chinese===
「那她就是*不*知道！好极了！」

克罗克先生的双脚哐当一声从壁炉架上放了下来。

「吉米！你该不会又出去惹是生非、大闹天宫了吧？」

「没有，没有，老爸。不是什么大事。无非是意气风发的贵族青年的那套行径，我这种身份的小伙子，人家正巴望着有这种表现呢。」

克罗克先生却怎么也宽慰不下来。

===Original===
“Jimmy, you’ve got to pull up. Honest, you have. I don’t care for myself. I like to see a boy having a good time. But your stepmother says you’re apt to queer us with the people up top, the way you’re going on. Lord knows I wouldn’t care if things were different, but I’ll tell you exactly how I stand. I didn’t get wise till this morning. Your stepmother sprang it on me suddenly. I’ve often wondered what all this stuff was about, this living in London and trailing the swells. I couldn’t think what was your stepmother’s idea. Now I know. Jimmy, she’s trying to get them to make me a peer!”

“What!”

“Just that. And she says﻿—”

“But, dad, this is rich! This is comedy of a high order! A peer! Good Heavens, if it comes off, what shall I be? This title business is all so complicated. I know I should have to change my name to Hon. Rollo Cholmondeley or the Hon. Aubrey Marjoribanks, but what I want to know is which? I want to be prepared for the worst.”

===Chinese===
「吉米，你得收敛点儿。说真的，必须收敛。为我自己，我倒无所谓。我爱看小伙子玩得痛快。可你继母说，照你这么闹下去，咱们保不齐会在上头那帮人跟前砸了锅。天知道，事情若是另一个样子，我才懒得管呢。不过我把我的处境原原本本告诉你。我也是今天早上才恍然大悟。你继母突然跟我摊了牌。我一直纳闷这一切到底图什么——住在伦敦，成天追着一帮贵人转。我想不出你继母打的什么主意。现在我明白了。吉米，她正张罗着让他们封我当爵爷呢！」

「什么！」

「就是这么回事。她说——」

「可是，老爸，这可太妙了！这是最高级的喜剧！爵爷！老天爷，真要是成了，我又该算什么？这头衔的事简直乱成一团麻。我知道我准得把名字改了，要么叫『罗洛·乔姆利阁下』，要么叫『奥布里·马乔里班克斯阁下』，可我想知道到底是哪一个？总得先做好最坏的打算啊。」

===Original===
“And you see, Jimmy, these people up top, the guys who arrange the giving of titles, are keeping an eye on you, because you would have the title after me and naturally they don’t want to get stung. I gathered all that from your stepmother. Say, Jimmy, I’m not asking a lot of you, but there is just one thing you can do for me without putting yourself out too much.”

“I’ll do it, dad, if it kills me. Slip me the info’!”

“Your stepmother’s friend Lady Corstorphine’s nephew﻿ ﻿…”

“It’s not the sort of story to ask a man with a headache to follow. I hope it gets simpler as it goes along.”

“Your stepmother wants you to be a good fellow and make friends with this boy. You see, his father is in right with the Premier and has the biggest kind of a pull when it comes to handing out titles.”

===Chinese===
「而且你瞧，吉米，上头那帮人——就是张罗着发头衔的家伙们——正盯着你呢，因为头衔日后要落到你头上，他们自然不愿看走了眼。这些话都是我从你继母那儿听来的。我说吉米，我不求你什么大事，只求你替我办一件事，也费不了你太大的劲。」

「就算要了我的命我也办，老爸。快把情报递过来！」

「你继母的朋友科斯托芬夫人的侄子……」

「这种故事，可不该讲给一个头疼的人听。但愿它越往下越简单。」

「你继母想请你做个好小伙，跟这孩子交个朋友。你瞧，他父亲跟首相打得火热，到了发放头衔的时候，那手眼可就大了去了。」

===Original===
“Is that all you want? Leave it to me. Inside of a week I’ll be playing kiss-in-the-ring with him. The whole force of my sunny personality shall be directed towards making him love me. What’s his name?”

“Lord Percy Whipple.”

Jimmy’s pipe fell with a clatter.

“Dad, pull yourself together! Reflect! You know you don’t seriously mean Lord Percy Whipple.”

“Eh?”

Jimmy laid a soothing hand on his father’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
「就这事？包在我身上。不出一个星期，我就跟他手拉手玩起转圈吻了。我那阳光灿烂的人格的全部力量，都将用来叫他爱上我。他叫什么名字？」

「珀西·惠普尔勋爵。」

吉米的烟斗哐啷一声掉在地上。

「老爸，你定定神！三思！你总不会当真是说珀西·惠普尔勋爵吧。」

「嗯？」

吉米把一只安抚的手搭上父亲的肩膀。

===Original===
“Dad, prepare yourself for the big laugh. This is where you throw your head back and roar with honest mirth. I met Lord Percy Whipple last night at the Six Hundred Club. Words ensued. I fell upon Percy and beat his block off! How it started, except that we both wanted the same table, I couldn’t say. ‘Why, that I cannot tell,’ said he, ‘but ’twas a famous victory!’ If I had known, dad, nothing would have induced me to lay a hand upon Perce, save in the way of kindness, but, not even knowing who he was, it would appear from contemporary accounts of the affair that I just naturally sailed in and expunged the poor, dear boy!”
