The stunning nature of this information had much the same effect on Mr. Crocker as the announcement of his ruin has upon the Good Old Man in melodrama. He sat clutching the arms of his chair and staring into space, saying nothing. Dismay was written upon his anguished countenance.

His collapse sobered Jimmy. For the first time he perceived that the situation had another side than the humorous one which had appealed to him. He had anticipated that Mr. Crocker, who as a general thing shared his notions of what was funny and could be relied on to laugh in the right place, would have been struck, like himself, by the odd and pleasing coincidence of his having picked on for purposes of assault and battery the one young man with whom his stepmother wished him to form a firm and lasting friendship. He perceived now that his father was seriously upset. Neither Jimmy nor Mr. Crocker possessed a demonstrative nature, but there had always existed between them the deepest affection. Jimmy loved his father as he loved nobody else in the world, and the thought of having hurt him was like a physical pain. His laughter died away and he set himself with a sinking heart to try to undo the effect of his words.

===Chinese===
「老爸，请做好捧腹大笑的准备。现在就该您向后仰起脑袋，发出真诚的轰然大笑了。昨晚我在六百俱乐部遇见了珀西·惠普尔勋爵。随之发生了一些言语交流。我扑到珀西身上，把他的脑袋揍了个稀巴烂！事情怎么起的头，我说不上来，只知道我们俩都想要同一张桌子。『唉，这个我可说不上，』他说，『不过那是一场辉煌的大胜仗！』老爸，我要是早知道，天底下没有任何东西能诱我对小珀动一根指头——除非出于好意——可我当时连他是谁都不知道，从关于此事的当代记载来看，我显然是顺风扬帆杀将进去，把那可怜的、亲爱的孩子一笔勾销了！」

这一消息五雷轰顶的性质，对克罗克先生的作用，恰如宣告破产之于情节剧里的善良老人。他坐在那里，死死攥住椅子扶手，两眼直勾勾望着虚空，一言不发。惊慌写满了他那张痛楚的脸。

他的崩溃让吉米清醒了。他头一回意识到，这局面除了吸引他的滑稽一面，还有另外一面。他原本料想，克罗克先生——一般说来，老头儿跟他笑点相投，该笑的地方绝对靠得住——也会像他自己一样，被这个又古怪又可喜的巧合击中：他偏偏挑中继母盼着他与之缔结牢固持久友谊的那一位青年，对之施加了殴打。他现在看明白了，父亲是真难过坏了。吉米和克罗克先生都不是感情外露的性子，可父子之间一直存着最深厚的感情。吉米爱父亲，胜过爱世上任何一个人，一想到伤了他的心，便如肉体疼痛一般。他的笑声敛去了，他怀着下沉的心，着手设法挽回自己那番话的影响。

===Original===
“I’m awfully sorry, dad. I had no idea you would care. I wouldn’t have done a fool thing like that for a million dollars if I’d known. Isn’t there anything I can do? Gee whiz! I’ll go right round to Percy now and apologise. I’ll lick his boots. Don’t you worry, dad. I’ll make it all right.”

The whirl of words roused Mr. Crocker from his thoughts.

“It doesn’t matter, Jimmy. Don’t worry yourself. It’s only a little unfortunate, because your stepmother says she won’t think of our going back to America till these people here have given me a title. She wants to put one over on her sister. That’s all that’s troubling me, the thought that this affair will set us back, this Lord Percy being in so strong with the guys who give the titles. I guess it will mean my staying on here for a while longer, and I’d liked to have seen another ballgame. Jimmy, do you know they call baseball Rounders in this country, and children play it with a soft ball!”

Jimmy was striding up and down the little room. Remorse had him in its grip.

===Chinese===
「真是对不住，老爸。我没想到你会往心里去。早知道的话，出一百万美元我也不干那种蠢事。有什么我能补救的吗？哎呀！我这就去找珀西赔不是。他的靴子我都肯舔。您别担心，老爸。我一定把这事摆平。」

这一连串旋风般的话语，把克罗克先生从沉思里刮醒了。

「不打紧，吉米。你别自寻烦恼。只是有点儿不走运，因为你继母说了，这边的老爷们不给我封个头衔，回美国的事她想都别想。她要压她妹妹一头。让我犯愁的也就这个——就怕这件事拖了我们的后腿，这位珀西勋爵在发头衔那帮人跟前正吃得开呢。我估摸着，这就意味着我还得在这儿再耗上一阵子，可我原还指望再看一场棒球赛呢。吉米，你知道吗，在这个国家，他们管棒球叫绕圈球，小孩子还拿软球玩！」

吉米在小屋里来回踱步。悔恨把他攥在了手心里。

===Original===
“What a damned fool I am!”

“Never mind, Jimmy. It’s unfortunate, but it wasn’t your fault. You couldn’t know.”

“It was my fault. Nobody but a fool like me would go about beating people up. But don’t worry, dad. It’s going to be all right. I’ll fix it. I’m going right round to this fellow Percy now to make things all right. I won’t come back till I’ve squared him. Don’t you bother yourself about it any longer, dad. It’s going to be all right.”


===Chinese===
「我真是天字第一号的大混蛋！」

「别往心里去，吉米。事情是不巧，可这不怪你。你又没法知道。」

「就是怪我。天底下也只有我这样的傻瓜，才会到处把人揍一顿。不过您别担心，老爸。会没事的。我来搞定。我这就去找那位珀西老兄，把事情弄妥帖。不把他哄顺了我就不回来。您往后再别为这事操心了，老爸。一切都会好起来的。」

## VI / 六

Jimmy Abandons Piccadilly / 吉米告别皮卡迪利

===Original===
Jimmy removed himself sorrowfully from the doorstep of the Duke of Devizes’ house in Cleveland Row. His mission had been a failure. In answer to his request to be permitted to see Lord Percy Whipple, the butler had replied that Lord Percy was confined to his bed and was seeing nobody. He eyed Jimmy, on receiving his name, with an interest which he failed to conceal, for he too, like Bayliss, had read and heartily enjoyed Bill Blake’s spirited version of the affair of last night which had appeared in the *Daily Sun*. Indeed, he had clipped the report out and had been engaged in pasting it in an album when the bell rang.

In face of this repulse, Jimmy’s campaign broke down. He was at a loss to know what to do next. He ebbed away from the Duke’s front door like an army that has made an unsuccessful frontal attack on an impregnable fortress. He could hardly force his way in and search for Lord Percy.

He walked along Pall Mall, deep in thought. It was a beautiful day. The rain which had fallen in the night and relieved Mr. Crocker from the necessity of watching cricket had freshened London up.

The sun was shining now from a turquoise sky. A gentle breeze blew from the south. Jimmy made his way into Piccadilly, and found that thoroughfare a-roar with happy automobilists and cheery pedestrians. Their gaiety irritated him. He resented their apparent enjoyment of life.

===Chinese===
吉米怏怏不乐地从克利夫兰街（Cleveland Row）德维泽斯公爵宅邸的门阶上撤了下来。他的使命以失败告终。他请求获准拜见珀西·惠普尔勋爵，对方管家却答称，珀西勋爵卧床静养，概不见客。听到吉米自报家门，那管家打量他的目光里透着一股按捺不住的兴趣，因为他同贝利斯一样，也拜读了《每日太阳报》上比尔·布莱克那篇妙笔生花的昨夜纪事，而且读得心花怒放。不仅如此，门铃响起的当口，他正把剪下来的那篇报道往剪贴簿里贴呢。

遭此回绝，吉米的攻势全线崩溃。他不知道下一步该如何是好。他从公爵家大门前退将下去，活像一支对坚不可摧的堡垒发动正面强攻却铩羽而归的大军。他总不能硬闯进去，把珀西勋爵搜出来吧。

他沿着帕尔马尔街一路走去，心事重重。天气好极了。夜里下过一场雨，免去了克罗克先生守着板球赛的苦差，也把伦敦洗刷得焕然一新。

此刻阳光正从一碧如洗的绿松石色天空里倾泻下来。南风习习。吉米踱进皮卡迪利，只见这条通衢大道上一片欢腾——驾车出游的兴高采烈，步行路人的喜气洋洋。这份欢乐看得他心头火起。瞧他们那副享受生活的样子，他简直气不打一处来。

===Original===
Jimmy’s was not a nature that lent itself readily to introspection, but he was putting himself now through a searching self-examination which was revealing all kinds of unsuspected flaws in his character. He had been having too good a time for years past to have leisure to realise that he possessed any responsibilities. He had lived each day as it came in the spirit of the Monks of Thelema. But his father’s reception of the news of last night’s escapade and the few words he had said had given him pause. Life had taken on of a sudden a less simple aspect. Dimly, for he was not accustomed to thinking along these lines, he perceived the numbing truth that we human beings are merely as many pieces in a jigsaw puzzle and that our every movement affects the fortunes of some other piece. Just so, faintly at first and taking shape by degrees, must the germ of civic spirit have come to Prehistoric Man. We are all individualists till we wake up.

The thought of having done anything to make his father unhappy was bitter to Jimmy Crocker. They had always been more like brothers than father and son. Hard thoughts about himself surged through Jimmy’s mind. With a dejectedness to which it is possible that his headache contributed he put the matter squarely to himself. His father was longing to return to America﻿—he, Jimmy, by his idiotic behaviour was putting obstacles in the way of that return﻿—what was the answer? The answer, to Jimmy’s way of thinking, was that all was not well with James Crocker, that, when all the evidence was weighed, James Crocker would appear to be a fool, a worm, a selfish waster, and a hopeless, low-down skunk.

Having come to this conclusion, Jimmy found himself so low in spirit that the cheerful bustle of Piccadilly was too much for him. He turned, and began to retrace his steps. Arriving in due course at the top of the Haymarket he hesitated, then turned down it till he reached Cockspur Street. Here the Transatlantic steamship companies have their offices, and so it came about that Jimmy, chancing to look up as he walked, perceived before him, riding gallantly on a cardboard ocean behind a plate-glass window, the model of a noble vessel. He stopped, conscious of a curious thrill. There is a superstition in all of us. When an accidental happening chances to fit smoothly in with a mood, seeming to come as a direct commentary on that mood, we are apt to accept it in defiance of our pure reason as an omen. Jimmy strode to the window and inspected the model narrowly. The sight of it had started a new train of thought. His heart began to race. Hypnotic influences were at work on him.

===Chinese===
吉米的性子本不惯于自省，此刻却给自己来了一场彻头彻尾的自我检视，检出了性格里种种前所未见的毛病。这些年来他日子过得太滋润，哪有闲工夫意识到自己肩上还担着什么责任。他过一天算一天，奉行的是泰勒玛修道僧的宗旨。可父亲听闻昨夜荒唐事之后的反应，以及他说的那寥寥几句话，让吉米顿住了。生活猛然间换上了一副不那么简单的面孔。他隐隐约约地——因为不惯于在这类思路上费脑筋——悟出一个令人麻木的真理：我们人类不过是一副拼图里的一块块小板块，我们的一举一动都牵动着别块板子的命运。远古人的公民精神之萌芽，起初想必也是这样隐隐约约、渐渐成形的。睡醒之前，我们人人都是个人主义者。

一想到自己竟做出了让父亲伤心的事，吉米·克罗克心里就发苦。他们爷俩历来不像父子，倒更像一对兄弟。种种刻薄的自责在吉米脑海里翻腾。他闷闷不乐——这或许也有他头疼的一份功劳——把事情直截了当地摆到了自己面前：父亲一心想回美国；而他吉米，正用自己愚蠢透顶的行径给这归途设置路障；答案是什么？按吉米的思路，答案是：詹姆斯·克罗克很不妙；把所有证据掂量一遍，詹姆斯·克罗克会被判定为一个大傻瓜、一条虫豸、一个自私的败家子，外加一只不可救药的下三滥臭鼬。

得出这个结论之后，吉米发现自己情绪低落得厉害，皮卡迪利那片欢腾的喧闹他实在消受不起。他转过身，开始原路折返。走到海马克特街街口时他犹豫了一下，随即拐了进去，一直走到科克斯珀街。跨大西洋轮船公司的办事处都集中在这里，于是就有了这么一幕：吉米边走边无意间抬头，瞥见一家店铺的橱窗玻璃后面，一艘巨轮的模型正威风凛凛地航行在一片硬纸板做的海洋上。他停住脚步，心头掠过一阵异样的激动。我们每个人心里都埋着几分迷信。当一件偶然的事碰巧同某种心境严丝合缝地咬合在一起，仿佛专为这心境而发的注解，我们往往会抛开纯粹的理性，把它当作天启的预兆加以领受。吉米大步走到橱窗前，细细端详那模型。这景象引出了一条新的思路。他的心开始狂跳。催眠术一般的力量正在他身上发作。

===Original===
Why not? Could there be a simpler solution of the whole trouble?

Inside the office he would see a man with whiskers buying a ticket for New York. The simplicity of the process fascinated him. All you had to do was to walk in, bend over the counter while the clerk behind it made dabs with a pencil at the illustrated plate of the ship’s interior organs, and hand over your money. A child could do it, if in funds. At this thought his hand strayed to his trouser-pocket. A musical crackling of banknotes proceeded from the depths. His quarterly allowance had been paid to him only a short while before, and, though a willing spender, he still retained a goodly portion of it. He rustled the notes again. There was enough in that pocket to buy three tickets to New York. Should he?﻿ ﻿… Or, on the other hand﻿—always look on both sides of the question﻿—should he not?

It would certainly seem to be the best thing for all parties if he did follow the impulse. By remaining in London he was injuring everybody, himself included.﻿ ﻿… Well, there was no harm in making enquiries. Probably the boat was full up anyway.﻿ ﻿… He walked into the office.

===Chinese===
何乐而不为呢？整个乱局还能有比这更简单的解法吗？

办事处里想必会有个蓄着络腮胡的男人，正在买去纽约的船票。这道程序的简单把他迷住了。你只需走进去，俯身靠在柜台上，听柜台后面的职员拿铅笔在轮船五脏六腑的剖面图上东戳西点，然后把钱包奉上，就完事了。只要兜里有钱，小孩子也办得来。想到这里，他的手不由得摸向裤袋。钞票在深处发出一阵悦耳的窸窣声。他的季度津贴不久前刚刚到手，他虽然花钱大方，眼下还留存着可观的一份。他又把那叠钞票捻得哗哗响。那口袋里的钱足够买三张去纽约的船票。买不买？……抑或换个角度——凡事总得两面瞧瞧——不买？

如果顺从这股冲动，对各方来说无疑都是上策。留在伦敦，他坑的是所有人，连他自己也在被坑之列。……不过，探探行情总归无妨。说不定船票早就售罄了。……他走进了办事处。

===Original===
“Have you anything left on the *Atlantic* this trip?”

The clerk behind the counter was quite the wrong sort of person for Jimmy to have had dealings with in his present mood. What Jimmy needed was a grave, sensible man who would have laid a hand on his shoulder and said “Do nothing rash, my boy!” The clerk fell short of this ideal in practically every particular. He was about twenty-two, and he seemed perfectly enthusiastic about the idea of Jimmy going to America. He beamed at Jimmy.

“Plenty of room,” he said. “Very few people crossing. Give you excellent accommodation.”

“When does the boat sail?”

“Eight tomorrow morning from Liverpool. Boat-train leaves Paddington six tonight.”

Prudence came at the eleventh hour to check Jimmy. This was not a matter, he perceived, to be decided recklessly, on the spur of a sudden impulse. Above all, it was not a matter to be decided before lunch. An empty stomach breeds imagination. He had ascertained that he could sail on the *Atlantic* if he wished to. The sensible thing to do now was to go and lunch and see how he felt about it after that. He thanked the clerk, and started to walk up the Haymarket, feeling hardheaded and practical, yet with a strong premonition that he was going to make a fool of himself just the same.

===Chinese===
「这班*大西洋号*还有余票吗？」

柜台后面那位职员，偏偏是吉米此刻这种心情最不该打交道的一种人。吉米需要的是一位严肃持重的长者，会把手搭上他的肩膀，说一句「年轻人，切莫鲁莽行事！」而这位职员在这一理想上几乎处处走了样。他约莫二十二岁，而且似乎对吉米远航美国这一构想热心得无以复加。他冲吉米眉开眼笑。

「位子多得很，」他说，「这阵子坐船的人少。给您安排顶好的舱位。」

「船什么时候开？」

「明早八点，从利物浦启航。船票列车今晚六点从帕丁顿发车。」

谨慎之心赶在最后关头赶来拦阻吉米。他省悟到，这件事不可凭着一时的冲动贸然定夺。尤其不可在午饭之前定夺。空肚子最会滋生想象。他已经探明，只要愿意，随时可以搭*大西洋号*出海。眼下明智的做法是先去吃午饭，饭后再看看心里是什么滋味。他谢过职员，动身往海马克特街上走，自觉头脑冷静、讲究实际，却又怀着一种强烈的预感：到头来他还是要出一次大洋相。

===Original===
It was halfway up the Haymarket that he first became conscious of the girl with the red hair.

Plunged in thought, he had not noticed her before. And yet she had been walking a few paces in front of him most of the way. She had come out of Panton Street, walking briskly, as one going to keep a pleasant appointment. She carried herself admirably, with a jaunty swing.

Having become conscious of this girl, Jimmy, ever a warm admirer of the sex, began to feel a certain interest stealing over him. With interest came speculation. He wondered who she was. He wondered where she had bought that excellently fitting suit of tailor-made grey. He admired her back, and wondered whether her face, if seen, would prove a disappointment. Thus musing, he drew near to the top of the Haymarket, where it ceases to be a street and becomes a whirlpool of rushing traffic. And here the girl, having paused and looked over her shoulder, stepped off the sidewalk. As she did so a taxicab rounded the corner quickly from the direction of Coventry Street.

===Chinese===
就是在海马克特街走到一半的地方，他头一回留意到那个红头发的姑娘。

他一路想心事，先前竟没注意到她。而她其实大半路都走在他前面几步远的地方。她从潘顿街出来，步履轻快，像是正要去赴一场惬意的约会。她的仪态好极了，走起来一摇一摆，俏丽洒脱。

既已留意到这姑娘，吉米——他向来是女性的热烈崇拜者——开始感到某种兴趣悄悄爬上心头。兴趣一起，猜测随之而来。他纳闷她是谁。他纳闷她那身熨帖之至的灰色定制套装是在哪儿置办的。他欣赏她的背影，又寻思她的正脸若是瞧见了，会不会令人失望。他正这般胡思乱想，已走近海马克特街的街顶——街道到此不再是街道，而成了一口车流汹涌的漩涡。就在这里，那姑娘停步回头望了一眼，跨下了人行道。说时迟那时快，一辆出租车从考文垂街方向飞快地拐过街角。

===Original===
The agreeable surprise of finding the girl’s face fully as attractive as her back had stimulated Jimmy, so that he was keyed up for the exhibition of swift presence-of-mind. He jumped forward and caught her arm, and swung her to one side as the cab rattled past, its driver thinking hard thoughts to himself. The whole episode was an affair of seconds.

“Thank you,” said the girl.

She rubbed the arm which he had seized with rather a rueful expression. She was a little white, and her breath came quickly.

“I hope I didn’t hurt you,” said Jimmy.

“You did. Very much. But the taxi would have hurt me more.”

===Chinese===
发现姑娘的正脸同她的背影一样动人，这份意外的惊喜使吉米精神大振，恰好为施展当机立断的身手调好了弦。他一个箭步蹿上前，抓住她的胳膊，把她往旁边一抡，出租车擦着他们轰隆而过，司机在心里狠狠咒骂着。整个事件前后不过几秒钟的事。

「多谢。」姑娘说。

她揉着被他抓住的那条胳膊，神情颇为懊丧。她脸色有点发白，呼吸急促。

「希望我没弄疼你。」吉米说。

「疼，疼得很。可出租车站我身上碾过去，只会更疼。」

===Original===
She laughed. She looked very attractive when she laughed. She had a small, piquant, vivacious face. Jimmy, as he looked at it, had an odd feeling that he had seen her before﻿—when and where he did not know. That mass of red-gold hair seemed curiously familiar. Somewhere in the hinterland of his mind there lurked a memory, but he could not bring it into the open. As for the girl, if she had ever met him before, she showed no signs of recollecting it. Jimmy decided that, if he had seen her, it must have been in his reporter days. She was plainly an American, and he occasionally had the feeling that he had seen everyone in America when he had worked for the *Chronicle*.

“That’s right,” he said approvingly. “Always look on the bright side.”

“I only arrived in London yesterday,” said the girl, “and I haven’t got used to your keeping-to-the-left rules. I don’t suppose I shall ever get back to New York alive. Perhaps, as you have saved my life, you wouldn’t mind doing me another service. Can you tell me which is the nearest and safest way to a restaurant called the Regent Grill?”

“It’s just over there, at the corner of Regent Street. As to the safest way, if I were you I should cross over at the top of the street there and then work round westward. Otherwise you will have to cross Piccadilly Circus.”

“I absolutely refuse even to try to cross Piccadilly Circus. Thank you very much. I will follow your advice. I hope I shall get there. It doesn’t seem at all likely.”

She gave him a little nod, and moved away. Jimmy turned into that drugstore at the top of the Haymarket at which so many Londoners have found healing and comfort on the morning after, and bought the pink drink for which his system had been craving since he rose from bed. He wondered why, as he drained it, he should feel ashamed and guilty.

===Chinese===
她笑了。她笑起来的模样十分动人。她有一张小小的、俏皮活泼的脸。吉米望着这张脸，心头涌起一种奇异的感觉：他从前见过她——何时何地却想不起来了。那一头红金色的秀发似乎莫名地眼熟。在他脑海的后院深处潜伏着一段记忆，可他怎么也请不出它来见客。至于那姑娘，倘若她从前见过他，可没流露出丝毫认得他的迹象。吉米断定，若是真见过，必是在他跑新闻的那些年月。她分明是个美国人，而他给《纪事报》当差那阵子，时不时会觉得全美国的人都叫他见遍了。

「这就对了，」他嘉许道，「凡事总往亮堂的一面看。」

「我昨天才到伦敦，」姑娘说，「还没习惯你们靠左行驶的规矩。我看我活着回纽约的希望渺茫。既然您救了我的命，想必不会介意再效一次劳。请问去一家叫摄政烧烤餐厅的馆子，最近又最保险的走法是哪条？」

「就在那边，摄政街拐角上。说到最保险的走法，我要是你，就从这街顶那儿横穿马路，然后往西绕过去。不然的话，你就得横穿皮卡迪利广场了。」

「横穿皮卡迪利广场这种事，我连试都拒绝试。多谢您指点。我这就照办。但愿我到得了。看起来可能性微乎其微。」

她朝他微微颔首，走开了。吉米转身走进海马克特街街顶的那家药房——不知多少伦敦人在大醉初醒的早晨，在那里寻得过疗救与慰藉——买了一杯粉色饮料，他从起床那一刻起五脏六腑就在渴念它。他一面把它喝干，一面纳闷自己何以感到又羞耻又心虚。

===Original===
A few minutes later he found himself, with mild surprise, going down the steps of the Regent Grill. It was the last place he had had in his mind when he had left the steamship company’s offices in quest of lunch. He had intended to seek out some quiet, restful nook where he could be alone with his thoughts. If anybody had told him then that five minutes later he would be placing himself of his own free will within the range of a restaurant orchestra playing “My Little Grey Home in the West”﻿—and the orchestra at the Regent played little else﻿—he would not have believed him.

Restaurants in all large cities have their ups and downs. At this time the Regent Grill was enjoying one of those bursts of popularity for which restaurateurs pray to whatever strange gods they worship. The more prosperous section of London’s Bohemia flocked to it daily. When Jimmy had deposited his hat with the robber-band who had their cave just inside the main entrance and had entered the grillroom, he found it congested. There did not appear to be a single unoccupied table.

From where he stood he could see the girl of the red-gold hair. Her back was towards him, and she was sitting at a table against one of the pillars with a little man with eyeglasses, a handsome woman in the forties, and a small stout boy who was skirmishing with the olives. As Jimmy hesitated, the vigilant headwaiter, who knew him well, perceived him, and hurried up.

===Chinese===
几分钟后，他发现自己正拾级而下，走进摄政烧烤餐厅，心里微微觉得诧异。他离开轮船公司办事处出来觅食时，脑子里最没有的就是这个地方。他本想寻个清静安逸的角落，好独自陪着自己的心事消磨。倘若那时有人告诉他，五分钟后他就会心甘情愿地把身子送进一支饭店乐队的射程之内，听它奏《我在西部的小灰屋》——而摄政餐厅的乐队别的曲目几乎一概不奏——他是断然不会信的。

大城市里的餐馆各有起落沉浮。此时的摄政烧烤餐厅，正享受着一段餐馆老板向他们信奉的各路怪神苦苦祈求的人气暴涨。伦敦波希米亚圈里较为阔绰的一拨天天涌到这里来。吉米把帽子交给盘踞在正门内侧洞窟里的那伙强盗，走进烧烤厅，只见里面人满为患。放眼望去，竟没有一张空桌。

从他站的地方望过去，正看得见那个红金色头发的姑娘。她背朝着他，同几个人围坐在一根柱子旁的桌边：一个戴夹鼻眼镜的小个子男人，一位四十来岁、容貌标致的妇人，还有一个正在拿橄榄果练兵的小胖墩。吉米正踌躇间，那位眼尖的领班——老早认得他——瞧见了他，急忙迎上前来。

===Original===
“In one moment, Mister Crockaire!” he said, and began to scatter commands among the underlings. “I will place a table for you in the aisle.”

“Next to that pillar, please,” said Jimmy.

The underlings had produced a small table﻿—apparently from up their sleeves, and were draping it in a cloth. Jimmy sat down and gave his order. Ordering was going on at the other table. The little man seemed depressed at the discovery that corn on the cob and soft-shelled crabs were not to be obtained, and his wife’s reception of the news that clams were not included in the Regent’s bill-of-fare was so indignant that one would have said that she regarded the fact as evidence that Great Britain was going to pieces and would shortly lose her place as a world power.

===Chinese===
「稍候片刻，克罗凯尔先生！」他说着便朝下手们撒开号令，「我给您在过道里安一张桌子。」

「就挨着那根柱子吧，劳驾。」吉米说。

下手们已经变出一张小桌——简直像从袖筒里抖出来的——正往上铺桌布。吉米落座，点了菜。邻桌也在点菜。小个子男人发现玉米棒子和软壳蟹均无供应，顿时满面愁云；他的太太听闻蛤蜊也不在摄政餐厅的菜单之列，反应之愤慨，叫人一眼断定：她已把这一事实视作大英帝国正在土崩瓦解、行将丧失世界强国地位的铁证。

===Original===
A selection having finally been agreed upon, the orchestra struck up “My Little Grey Home in the West,” and no attempt was made to compete with it. When the last lingering strains had died away and the violinist-leader, having straightened out the kinks in his person which the rendition of the melody never failed to produce, had bowed for the last time, a clear, musical voice spoke from the other side of the pillar.

“Jimmy Crocker is a *worm*!”

Jimmy spilled his cocktail. It might have been the voice of Conscience.

“I despise him more than anyone on earth. I hate to think that he’s an American.”

Jimmy drank the few drops that remained in his glass, partly to make sure of them, partly as a restorative. It is an unnerving thing to be despised by a red-haired girl whose life you have just saved. To Jimmy it was not only unnerving; it was uncanny. This girl had not known him when they met on the street a few moments before. How then was she able to display such intimate acquaintance with his character now as to describe him﻿—justly enough﻿—as a worm? Mingled with the mystery of the thing was its pathos. The thought that a girl could be as pretty as this one and yet dislike him so much was one of the saddest things Jimmy had ever come across. It was like one of those Things Which Make Me Weep In This Great City so dear to the hearts of the sob-writers of his late newspaper.

===Chinese===
菜品总算商定，乐队奏起了《我在西部的小灰屋》，无人试图与之争锋。待到袅袅余音彻底消散，乐队首席小提琴手把演奏此曲必会在他身上引发的几处筋骨错位一一扳正，最后一次鞠躬之后，柱子那头响起一个清脆悦耳的声音。

「吉米·克罗克是一条*虫豸*！」

吉米把鸡尾酒泼了。那简直可以说是良知的声音。

「天底下我最瞧不起的就是他。一想到他是美国人，我就难受。」

吉米把杯底剩下的几滴喝了，一来是确保不糟蹋，二来是定定神。被一个你刚救了命的红发姑娘所鄙夷，本就是一件叫人腿软的事。对吉米来说，这不止腿软，简直骇人。这姑娘方才在街上遇见时分明不认得他。可这会儿，她何以能对他的品格表现出如此知己般的熟稔，管他叫——叫得倒也并不冤——一条虫豸？这件事的神秘里头还掺着几分悲怆。一个姑娘家竟能生得这般标致，却又这般讨厌他——这个念头是吉米平生所遇最伤心的事情之一，堪比他前东家那帮专写煽情文字的记者们最钟爱的「这座大都市里令我落泪的种种」。

===Original===
A waiter bustled up with a highball. Jimmy thanked him with his eyes. He needed it. He raised it to his lips.

“He’s always drinking﻿—”

He set it down hurriedly.

“﻿—and making a disgraceful exhibition of himself in public! I always think Jimmy Crocker﻿—”

===Chinese===
一名侍者端着一杯威士忌苏打匆匆而至。吉米以目致谢。他正需要它。他把它举向唇边。

「他成天泡在酒里——」

他慌忙把它放下了。

「——还在大庭广众之下出尽洋相！我一向认为，吉米·克罗克——」

===Original===
Jimmy began to wish that somebody would stop this girl. Why couldn’t the little man change the subject to the weather, or that stout child start prattling about some general topic? Surely a boy of that age, newly arrived in London, must have all sorts of things to prattle about? But the little man was dealing strenuously with a breaded cutlet, while the stout boy, grimly silent, surrounded fish-pie in the forthright manner of a starving python. As for the elder woman, she seemed to be wrestling with unpleasant thoughts, beyond speech.

“﻿—I always think that Jimmy Crocker is the worst case I know of the kind of American young man who spends all his time in Europe and tries to become an imitation Englishman. Most of them are the sort any country would be glad to get rid of, but he used to work once, so you can’t excuse him on the ground that he hasn’t the sense to know what he’s doing. He’s deliberately chosen to loaf about London and make a pest of himself. He went to pieces with his eyes open. He’s a perfect, utter, hopeless *worm*!”

Jimmy had never been very fond of the orchestra at the Regent Grill, holding the view that it interfered with conversation and made for an unhygienic rapidity of mastication; but he was profoundly grateful to it now for bursting suddenly into *La Bohême*, the loudest item in its repertory. Under cover of that protective din he was able to toy with a steaming dish which his waiter had brought. Probably that girl was saying all sorts of things about him still but he could not hear them.

===Chinese===
吉米开始盼着有谁能叫这姑娘住口。那小个子男人何不把话题岔到天气上去？那胖孩子何不喋喋不休地念叨点什么天下大事？一个那种年纪、初到伦敦的男孩子，肚里总该攒着好些可念叨的东西吧？可小个子男人正同一块面包粉炸肉排展开苦斗，那胖孩子则闷头不响，以一条饿蟒般干脆利索的架势把鱼馅饼团团围住、逐块歼灭。至于那位年长的妇人，她似乎正同某些不愉快的念头角力，连话都顾不上说了。

「——我一向认为，吉米·克罗克是我所知登峰造极的一号病例：那种把全副光阴消磨在欧洲、一心想把自己改造成英国人仿制品的美国青年。这路人多半是任何国家都乐意拱手相送的货色，可他好歹曾经正经工作过，所以你还没法体谅他一句，说他蠢得不知道自己在干什么。他是存心闲荡在伦敦城里，存心把自己活成一个人见人嫌的祸害。他是睁着眼睛自甘堕落的。他是一条彻头彻尾、不可救药的*虫豸*！」

吉米一向不大喜欢摄政烧烤餐厅的这支乐队，认为它妨碍交谈，还催得人咀嚼过快、有违卫生；可这会儿它突然爆发出《波希米亚人》——其曲目单上最震耳欲聋的一件——他对它简直感激涕零。趁着这片护体的喧嚣，他总算能同侍者端来的一盘冒着热气的菜肴周旋一番了。那姑娘多半还在数落他的种种不是，可他听不见了。

===Original===
The music died away. For a moment the tortured air quivered in comparative silence; then the girl’s voice spoke again. She had, however, selected another topic of conversation.

“I’ve seen all I want to of England,” she said, “I’ve seen Westminster Abbey and the Houses of Parliament and His Majesty’s Theatre and the Savoy and the Cheshire Cheese, and I’ve developed a frightful homesickness. Why shouldn’t we go back tomorrow?”

For the first time in the proceedings the elder woman spoke. She cast aside her mantle of gloom long enough to say “Yes,” then wrapped it round her again. The little man, who had apparently been waiting for her vote before giving his own, said that the sooner he was on board a New York-bound boat the better he would be pleased. The stout boy said nothing. He had finished his fish-pie, and was now attacking jam roll with a sort of morose resolution.

“There’s certain to be a boat,” said the girl. “There always is. You’ve got to say that for England﻿—it’s an easy place to get back to America from.” She paused. “What I can’t understand is how, after having been in America and knowing what it was like, Jimmy Crocker could stand living﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
乐声止息。受尽折磨的空气在相对的寂静中颤了一颤；随后姑娘的声音再度响起。不过，她已另择了话题。

「英格兰我想看的都看够了，」她说，「威斯敏斯特教堂、议会大厦、陛下剧院、萨伏伊饭店、柴郡奶酪酒馆，我都看过了，我还染上了严重的思乡病。咱们干吗不明天就回去？」

整个谈话过程中，那位年长的妇人头一回开腔。她把阴郁的斗篷掀开一条缝，够说一声「好」，随即又把自己裹了回去。小个子男人——他显然一直在等她投了票再亮自己的票——表示自己越早上开往纽约的船就越称心如意。胖孩子一言不发。他已解决了鱼馅饼，此刻正以一种阴沉沉的决心攻打果酱卷。

「一定会有船的，」姑娘说，「总归会有。这一点你得替英格兰说句公道话——从这儿回美国，路是好走的。」她顿了顿。「我没法理解的是，一个人既然到过美国、知道那儿是什么光景，吉米·克罗克怎么还受得了住在……」

===Original===
The waiter had come to Jimmy’s side, bearing cheese; but Jimmy looked at it with dislike and shook his head in silent negation. He was about to depart from this place. His capacity for absorbing home-truths about himself was exhausted. He placed a noiseless sovereign on the table, caught the waiter’s eye, registered renunciation, and departed soft-footed down the aisle. The waiter, a man who had never been able to bring himself to believe in miracles, revised the views of a lifetime. He looked at the sovereign, then at Jimmy, then at the sovereign again. Then he took up the coin and bit it furtively.

A few minutes later, a hat-check boy, untipped for the first time in his predatory career, was staring at Jimmy with equal intensity, but with far different feelings. Speechless concern was limned on his young face.

The commissionaire at the Piccadilly entrance of the restaurant touched his hat ingratiatingly, with the smug confidence of a man who is accustomed to getting sixpence a time for doing it.

“Taxi, Mr. Crocker?”

===Chinese===
侍者端着奶酪来到吉米身畔；吉米厌恶地看了它一眼，摇头默拒。他要离开这个地方了。他吸收关于自己的逆耳忠告的容量已然告罄。他把一枚不声不响的金镑放在桌上，接上侍者的目光，登记下放弃找零，然后踮着脚尖，沿过道飘然而去。那侍者——一个这辈子怎么也说服不了自己相信奇迹的人——就此修正了毕生的见解。他看看金镑，看看吉米，又看看金镑。然后他拿起那枚硬币，鬼鬼祟祟地咬了一口。

几分钟后，一个寄帽间的男孩——在他巧取豪夺的生涯里头一回没拿到小费——正以同样的专注凝视着吉米，心境却截然不同。无言的忧虑涂满了他年轻的脸。

餐厅皮卡迪利门口的门童讨好地碰了碰帽子，带着一种惯于靠这个动作每次挣六便士的人的沾沾自喜。

「要出租车吗，克罗克先生？」

===Original===
“A worm,” said Jimmy.

“Beg pardon, sir?”

“Always drinking,” explained Jimmy, “and making a pest of himself.”

He passed on. The commissionaire stared after him as intently as the waiter and the hat-check boy. He had sometimes known Mr. Crocker like this after supper, but never before during the luncheon hour.

===Chinese===
「一条虫豸。」吉米说。

「您说什么，先生？」

「成天喝酒，」吉米解释道，「外加到处招人嫌。」

他自顾自走了。门童目送他的背影，盯得同那侍者和寄帽间男孩一样紧。他从前也见过克罗克先生晚饭后这副模样，可午饭时分如此，还是破天荒头一遭。

===Original===
Jimmy made his way to his club in Northumberland Avenue. For perhaps half an hour he sat in a condition of coma in the smoking-room; then, his mind made up, he went to one of the writing-tables. He sat awaiting inspiration for some minutes, then began to write.

The letter he wrote was to his father:

Dear Dad:

I have been thinking over what we talked about this morning, and it seems to me the best thing I can do is to drop out of sight for a brief space. If I stay on in London, I am likely at any moment to pull some boner like last night’s which will spill the beans for you once more. The least I can do for you is to give you a clear field and not interfere, so I am off to New York by tonight’s boat.

===Chinese===
吉米去了自己那家坐落在诺森伯兰大道的俱乐部。他在吸烟室里以昏迷状态枯坐了约莫半个钟头；随后主意拿定，他走到一张写字台前。他坐等灵感降临，等了几分钟，然后落笔。

他写的那封信，是给他父亲的：

亲爱的老爸：

今早咱们谈的那些事，我反复琢磨过了，依我看，我能想出的上策就是暂且从人们的视线里蒸发一阵子。我要是继续赖在伦敦，随时可能再闹出一桩昨夜那样的昏招，把你的事再一次搅黄。我至少能为你做的，就是给你腾出一条清净跑道，不来碍手碍脚，所以我搭今晚的船去纽约。

===Original===
I went round to Percy’s to try to grovel in the dust before him, but he wouldn’t see me. It’s no good grovelling in the dust of the front steps for the benefit of a man who’s in bed on the second floor, so I withdrew in more or less good order. I then got the present idea. Mark how all things work together for good. When they come to you and say “No title for you. Your son slugged our pal Percy,” all you have to do is to come back at them with “I know my son slugged Percy, and believe me I didn’t do a thing to him! I packed him off to America within twenty-four hours. Get me right, boys! I’m anti-Jimmy and pro-Percy.” To which their reply will be “Oh, well, in that case arise, Lord Crocker!” or whatever they say when slipping a title to a deserving guy. So you will see that by making this getaway I am doing the best I can to put things straight. I shall give this to Bayliss to give to you. I am going to call him up on the phone in a minute to have him pack a few simple toothbrushes and so on for me. On landing in New York, I shall instantly proceed to the Polo Grounds to watch a game of Rounders, and will cable you the full score. Well. I think that’s about all. So goodbye﻿—or even farewell﻿—for the present.

J.

P.S. I know you’ll understand, dad. I’m doing what seems to me the only possible thing. Don’t worry about me. I shall be all right. I’ll get back my old job and be a terrific success all round. You go ahead and get that title and then meet me at the entrance of the Polo Grounds. I’ll be looking for you.

===Chinese===
我去过珀西府上，想在他面前匍匐谢罪，可他不肯见我。对一个躺在二楼床上的人来说，趴在前门台阶的尘土里谢罪是白费工夫，所以我以多少还算体面的队形撤退了。随后我便想到了眼下这个主意。且看万事如何互相效力、终成好事。等他们找上门来对你说「封号没你的份，你儿子把我们哥们儿珀西揍了」，你只管回敬他们：「我明白我儿子揍了珀西，而且信不信由你，我可没轻饶他！我二十四小时之内就把他打包发配美国了。把话听仔细了，各位！我是反吉米、亲珀西的。」他们一听准会答道：「哦，这样啊，那么就请起身，克罗克勋爵！」——或者随便他们给一个够格的好汉奉上爵位时都念叨些什么。所以你看得出来，我这一走，是尽我所能把局面扳正。这封信我会交给贝利斯转呈给你。我过一分钟就打电话，叫他替我收拾几支简单的牙刷之类的东西。一到纽约，我将即刻赶赴波罗球场，观摩一场绕圈球比赛，并把全场比分电告给你。好了，我想就这些。那么再见——或者说永别了您呐——暂时。

吉。

又及：我知道你会理解的，老爸。我这是在做在我看来唯一可行的事。别为我操心。我会好好的。我要把老差事讨回来，方方面面都大放异彩。你只管去挣你的爵位，然后到波罗球场大门口同我会合。我会一直留意等你的。

===Original===
P.P.S. I’m a worm.

The young clerk at the steamship offices appeared rejoiced to see Jimmy once more. With a sunny smile he snatched a pencil from his ear and plunged it into the vitals of the *Atlantic*.

“How about E. a hundred and eight?”

===Chinese===
再又及：我是一条虫豸。

轮船公司那位年轻职员见吉米去而复返，显得喜出望外。他满面春风地从耳朵上抽下一支铅笔，一头扎进*大西洋号*的要害。

「E 一百零八怎么样？」

===Original===
“Suits me.”

“You’re too late to go in the passenger-list, of course.”

Jimmy did not reply. He was gazing rigidly at a girl who had just come in, a girl with red hair and a friendly smile.

===Chinese===
「正合我意。」

「乘客名单上自然是赶不上您了。」

吉米没有作答。他直勾勾地盯着一位刚进门的姑娘——一个红头发、面带友好微笑的姑娘。

===Original===
“So you’re sailing on the *Atlantic*, too!” she said, with a glance at the chart on the counter. “How odd! We have just decided to go back on her too. There’s nothing to keep us here and we’re all homesick. Well, you see I wasn’t run over after I left you.”

A delicious understanding relieved Jimmy’s swimming brain, as thunder relieves the tense and straining air. The feeling that he was going mad left him, as the simple solution of his mystery came to him. This girl must have heard of him in New York﻿—perhaps she knew people whom he knew﻿—and it was on hearsay, not on personal acquaintance, that she based that dislike of him which she had expressed with such freedom and conviction so short a while before at the Regent Grill. She did not know who he was!

Into this soothing stream of thought cut the voice of the clerk.

“What name, please?”

===Chinese===
「这么说，您也搭*大西洋号*！」她说着，瞥了一眼柜台上的舱位图。「真巧！我们刚决定也搭这班船回去。这儿没什么好留恋的，我们全都想家想得不行。喏，您瞧，我离开您之后到底没被车碾着。」

一股妙不可言的顿悟流过吉米眩晕的脑袋，好比一声惊雷松开了绷紧欲裂的天空。他要疯了的感觉消失了，谜底的简单解法自己送上了门。这姑娘准是在纽约听说过他——说不定她认识他认识的人——方才在摄政烧烤餐厅，她那番毫无顾忌、言之凿凿的贬损，依据的是传闻，而非亲自过从。她不知道他是谁！

打断这条舒心思路的，是职员的声音。

「请问尊姓大名？」

===Original===
Jimmy’s mind rocked again. Why were these things happening to him today of all days, when he needed the tenderest treatment, when he had a headache already?

The clerk was eyeing him expectantly. He had laid down his pencil and was holding aloft a pen. Jimmy gulped. Every name in the English language had passed from his mind. And then from out of the dark came inspiration.

“Bayliss,” he croaked.

The girl held out her hand.

“Then we can introduce ourselves at last. My name is Ann Chester. How do you do, Mr. Bayliss?”

===Chinese===
吉米的脑筋又晃了一晃。这些事情为什么偏偏找上他，偏偏是今天——他最需要温柔相待的一天，他本来就头疼欲裂的一天？

职员正一脸期待地瞅着他。他已放下铅笔，高擎着一支钢笔。吉米倒抽一口气。英语里所有的姓名都从他的脑子里跑光了。就在这时，灵感从黑暗里冒了出来。

「贝利斯。」他哑着嗓子说。

姑娘伸出手来。

「那我们总算可以正式认识一下了。我叫安·切斯特。您好，贝利斯先生。」

===Original===
“How do you do, Miss Chester?”

The clerk had finished writing the ticket, and was pressing labels and a pink paper on him. The paper, he gathered dully, was a form and had to be filled up. He examined it, and found it to be a searching document. Some of its questions could be answered offhand, others required thought.

“Height?” Simple. Five foot eleven.

“Hair?” Simple. Brown.

“Eyes?” Simple again. Blue.

===Chinese===
「您好，切斯特小姐。」

职员已开好票，正往他手里塞标签和一张粉红色的纸。他懵懵懂懂地琢磨出，那纸是一份表格，必须填写。他审视了一番，发现这是一份刨根问底的文件。有些问题可以脱口而出，另一些则需要斟酌。

「身高？」简单。五英尺十一。

「头发？」简单。棕色。

「眼睛？」又是一个简单的。蓝色。

===Original===
Next, queries of a more offensive kind.

“Are you a polygamist?”

He could answer that. Decidedly no. One wife would be ample﻿—provided she had red-gold hair, brown-gold eyes, the right kind of mouth, and a dimple. Whatever doubts there might be in his mind on other points, on that one he had none whatever.

“Have you ever been in prison?”

Not yet.

===Chinese===
接下来，是性质较为冒犯的一类问题。

「您是一夫多妻主义者吗？」

这个他答得上来。断然不是。一位太太就绰绰有余了——前提是她得有一头红金色的头发、一双棕金色的眼睛、恰到好处的一张嘴，外加一个酒窝。在其他各点上，他心里或许还有疑虑，唯独这一条，他半点疑虑也没有。

「您坐过牢吗？」

还没有。

===Original===
And then a very difficult one. “Are you a lunatic?”

Jimmy hesitated. The ink dried on his pen. He was wondering.

---

In the dim cavern of Paddington Station the boat-train snorted impatiently, varying the process with an occasional sharp shriek. The hands of the station clock pointed to ten minutes to six. The platform was a confused mass of travellers, porters, baggage, trucks, boys with buns and fruits, boys with magazines, friends, relatives, and Bayliss the butler, standing like a faithful watchdog beside a large suitcase. To the human surf that broke and swirled about him he paid no attention. He was looking for the young master.

Jimmy clove the crowd like a one-man flying-wedge. Two fruit and bun boys who impeded his passage drifted away like leaves on an Autumn gale.

===Chinese===
然后是一道非常难答的题。「您是精神病患者吗？」

吉米踌躇了。笔尖上的墨水干了。他正在琢磨。

---

在帕丁顿车站昏暗的洞窟里，船票列车不耐烦地喷着气，间或变个花样，发出一声凄厉的尖啸。车站大钟的指针指向六点差十分。月台上乱糟糟挤作一团：旅客、搬运工、行李、行李车、卖面包水果的男孩、卖杂志的男孩、朋友、亲戚，还有管家贝利斯，像一条忠心的看家犬，立在一口大衣箱旁边。对他脚边冲刷回旋的人浪，他一概不予理会。他在找少主人。

吉米劈开人群，好似一支单人楔形冲锋队。两个挡道的卖水果面包的男孩，像秋风里的两片落叶，飘到一边去了。

===Original===
“Good man!” He possessed himself of the suitcase. “I was afraid you might not be able to get here.”

“The mistress is dining out, Mr. James. I was able to leave the house.”

“Have you packed everything I shall want?”

“Within the scope of a suitcase, yes, sir.”

“Splendid! Oh, by the way, give this letter to my father, will you?”

“Very good, sir.”

===Chinese===
「好样的！」他接过衣箱。「我还怕你赶不来呢。」

「太太今晚在外面用饭，詹姆斯先生。我才能离宅。」

「我要用的东西都装齐了吗？」

「在一只衣箱装得下的范围内，齐了，先生。」

「好极了！哦，对了，把这封信交给我父亲，劳驾。」

「遵命，先生。」

===Original===
“I’m glad you were able to manage. I thought your voice sounded doubtful over the phone.”

“I was a good deal taken aback, Mr. James. Your decision to leave was so extremely sudden.”

“So was Columbus’. You know about him? He saw an egg standing on its head and whizzed off like a jackrabbit.”

“If you will pardon the liberty, Mr. James, is it not a little rash﻿—?”

“Don’t take the joy out of life, Bayliss. I may be a chump, but try to forget it. Use your willpower.”

===Chinese===
「真高兴你办妥了。电话里听你声音，可有点没底。」

「我着实吃了一惊，詹姆斯先生。您这走的主意来得实在太突然。」

「哥伦布的主意也突然。你知道他吧？他瞧见一只鸡蛋倒立着，立马像只长耳兔似的窜出去了。」

「恕我冒昧，詹姆斯先生，这是不是有欠仓促——？」

「别夺走生活的乐趣，贝利斯。我兴许是个蠢货，可请设法忘掉这一点。动用你的意志力。」

===Original===
“Good evening, Mr. Bayliss,” said a voice behind them. They both turned. The butler was gazing rather coyly at a vision in a grey tailor-made suit.

“Good evening, miss,” he said doubtfully.

Ann looked at him in astonishment, then broke into a smile.

“How stupid of me! I meant this Mr. Bayliss. Your son! We met at the steamship offices. And before that he saved my life. So we are old friends.”

Bayliss, gaping perplexedly and feeling unequal to the intellectual pressure of the conversation, was surprised further to perceive a warning scowl on the face of his Mr. James. Jimmy had not foreseen this thing, but he had a quick mind and was equal to it.

“How are you, Miss Chester? My father has come down to see me off. This is Miss Chester, dad.”

===Chinese===
「晚上好，贝利斯先生。」身后一个声音说道。两人都转过身去。管家正相当扭捏地凝视着一位一身灰色定制套装的丽人。

「晚上好，小姐。」他将信将疑地说。

安诧异地望着他，随即绽开了笑容。

「瞧我多糊涂！我指的是这一位贝利斯先生。您的儿子！我们在轮船公司碰过面。在那之前，他还救过我的命。所以我们算是老朋友了。」

贝利斯张口结舌，自觉应付不了这场谈话的智力重压，更叫他吃惊的是，他瞧见他那位詹姆斯先生脸上罩着一层警告式的怒容。吉米没料到这一出，但他脑子快，应付得来。

「你好吗，切斯特小姐？我父亲来送我。这是切斯特小姐，爸。」

===Original===
A British butler is not easily robbed of his poise, but Bayliss was frankly unequal to the sudden demand on his presence of mind. He lowered his jaw an inch or two, but spoke no word.

“Dad’s a little upset at my going,” whispered Jimmy confidentially. “He’s not quite himself.”

Ann was a girl possessed not only of ready tact but of a kind heart. She had summed up Mr. Bayliss at a glance. Every line of him proclaimed him a respectable upper servant. No girl on earth could have been freer than she of snobbish prejudice, but she could not check a slight thrill of surprise and disappointment at the discovery of Jimmy’s humble origin. She understood everything, and there were tears in her eyes as she turned away to avoid intruding on the last moments of the parting of father and son.

“I’ll see you on the boat, Mr. Bayliss,” she said.

“Eh?” said Bayliss.

“Yes, yes,” said Jimmy. “Goodbye till then.”

===Chinese===
一位英国管家是不轻易失态的，可面对这突如其来的急智考验，贝利斯实在招架不住。他把下巴放低了一两英寸，却一言不发。

「爸见我走，心里有点难受，」吉米推心置腹地悄声说。「他有点不大对劲。」

安这姑娘不但急智俱全，还有一副好心肠。她一眼就把贝利斯先生看了个通透。他浑身上下每一根线条都在宣告：这是一个体面的上等仆役。天底下没有哪个姑娘比她更少势利眼，可发现了吉米寒微的出身，她仍禁不住微微打了个惊讶与失望的寒噤。她全明白了；她转过身去时眼里噙着泪，免得打扰这对父子诀别的最后时刻。

「船上见，贝利斯先生。」她说。

「啊？」贝利斯说。

「是，是。」吉米说。「那就到时候见。」

===Original===
Ann walked on to her compartment. She felt as if she had just read a whole long novel, one of those chunky younger-English-novelist things. She knew the whole story as well as if it had been told to her in detail. She could see the father, the honest steady butler, living his life with but one aim, to make a gentleman of his beloved only son. Year by year he had saved. Probably he had sent the son to college. And now, with a father’s blessing and the remains of a father’s savings, the boy was setting out for the New World, where dollar-bills grew on trees and no one asked or cared who anyone else’s father might be.

There was a lump in her throat. Bayliss would have been amazed if he could have known what a figure of pathetic fineness he seemed to her. And then her thoughts turned to Jimmy, and she was aware of a glow of kindliness towards him. His father had succeeded in his life’s ambition. He had produced a gentleman! How easily and simply, without a trace of snobbish shame, the young man had introduced his father. There was the right stuff in him. He was not ashamed of the humble man who had given him his chance in life. She found herself liking Jimmy amazingly.﻿ ﻿…

The hands of the clock pointed to three minutes to the hour. Porters skimmed to and fro like water-beetles.

“I can’t explain,” said Jimmy. “It wasn’t temporary insanity; it was necessity.”

“Very good, Mr. James. I think you had better be taking your seat now.”

“Quite right, I had. It would spoil the whole thing if they left me behind. Bayliss, did you ever see such eyes? Such hair! Look after my father while I am away. Don’t let the dukes worry him. Oh, and, Bayliss”﻿—Jimmy drew his hand from his pocket﻿—“as one pal to another﻿—”

===Chinese===
安走向自己的车厢。她觉得自己刚读完一整部长篇小说，还是新兴英国青年小说家笔下那种大部头。整个故事她了如指掌，仿佛有人掰开揉碎讲给她听过。她看得见那位父亲——那位诚实稳重的管家——一辈子只抱着一个目标过活：把他疼爱的独子栽培成一位绅士。他一年一年地攒。多半还送儿子上了大学。如今，带着一位父亲的祝福和一位父亲积蓄的余烬，这孩子动身去新大陆了——在那儿，钞票长在树上，谁也不会过问、也不会在意别人的父亲是干什么的。

她喉咙里哽着一团东西。贝利斯若是知道，自己在她眼里成了一副何等哀婉动人的形象，准会大吃一惊。接着她的念头转向吉米，她觉出一股对他的暖意。他父亲毕生的抱负成功了。他造就了一位绅士！这位青年介绍自己父亲时，多么轻松自然，没有一丝一毫势利的羞惭。他骨子里有好料。他不以那个给了他人生起点、出身微贱的人为耻。她发现自己竟惊人地喜欢起吉米来。……

大钟的指针指向六点差三分。搬运工们像水虫似的来回疾行。

「我没法解释，」吉米说。「那不是一时精神失常；那是势在必行。」

「遵命，詹姆斯先生。我看您现在最好入座了。」

「说得对，是该入了。他们要是把我落下了，整台戏就砸了。贝利斯，你见过那样的眼睛吗？那样的头发！我不在的时候，替我照看父亲。别让那些公爵搅扰他。哦，还有，贝利斯」——吉米从口袋里抽出手来——「咱们哥俩好，一点心意——」

===Original===
Bayliss looked at the crackling piece of paper.

“I couldn’t, Mr. James, I really couldn’t! A five-pound note! I couldn’t!”

“Nonsense! Be a sport!”

“Begging your pardon, Mr. James, I really couldn’t. You cannot afford to throw away your money like this. You cannot have a great deal of it, if you will excuse me for saying so.”

“I won’t do anything of the sort. Grab it! Oh, Lord, the train’s starting! Goodbye, Bayliss!”

===Chinese===
贝利斯望着那张哗哗作响的纸片。

「我不能收，詹姆斯先生，真的不能！一张五镑钞票！我不能！」

「胡说！拿出点运动家的风度来！」

「恕我直言，詹姆斯先生，我实在不能。您可不能这样把钱白白扔掉。恕我斗胆说一句，您手头想必也没有多少。」

「我才不干那一套呢。拿着！哎呀，天哪，车开了！再见了，贝利斯！」

===Original===
The engine gave a final shriek of farewell. The train began to slide along the platform, pursued to the last by optimistic boys offering buns for sale. It gathered speed. Jimmy, leaning out the window, was amazed at a spectacle so unusual as practically to amount to a modern miracle﻿—the spectacled Bayliss running. The butler was not in the pink of condition, but he was striding out gallantly. He reached the door of Jimmy’s compartment, and raised his hand.

“Begging your pardon, Mr. James,” he panted, “for taking the liberty, but I really couldn’t!”

He reached up and thrust something into Jimmy’s hand, something crisp and crackling. Then, his mission performed, fell back and stood waving a snowy handkerchief. The train plunged into the tunnel.

Jimmy stared at the five-pound note. He was aware, like Ann farther along the train, of a lump in his throat. He put the note slowly into his pocket.

The train moved on.

===Chinese===
机车发出一声诀别的长啸。列车开始沿月台滑行，乐观的男孩们穷追不舍，兜售面包直到最后一刻。车越开越快。吉米探身窗外，为一幅奇景所骇——那奇景罕见得几乎称得上当代奇迹：戴着眼镜的贝利斯在奔跑。这位管家并不处于巅峰状态，但他迈开双腿，跑得英姿勃发。他赶到吉米车厢的门口，举起了手。

「恕我冒昧，詹姆斯先生，」他喘着气说，「斗胆了，可我真的不能收！」

他探起身，把一样东西塞进吉米手里——一样干干脆脆、哗哗作响的东西。然后，使命既毕，他退后站定，挥动一方雪白的手帕。列车一头扎进隧道。

吉米凝视着那张五镑钞票。他同这列车更前头的安一样，喉咙里哽着一团东西。他把钞票慢慢放进口袋。

列车继续前行。

## VII / 七

On the Boat-Deck / 在艇甲板上

===Original===
Rising waters and a fine flying scud that whipped stingingly over the side had driven most of the passengers on the *Atlantic* to the shelter of their staterooms or to the warm stuffiness of the library. It was the fifth evening of the voyage. For five days and four nights the ship had been racing through a placid ocean on her way to Sandy Hook: but in the early hours of this afternoon the wind had shifted to the north, bringing heavy seas. Darkness had begun to fall now. The sky was a sullen black. The white crests of the rollers gleamed faintly in the dusk, and the wind sang in the ropes.

Jimmy and Ann had had the boat-deck to themselves for half an hour. Jimmy was a good sailor: it exhilarated him to fight the wind and to walk a deck that heaved and dipped and shuddered beneath his feet; but he had not expected to have Ann’s company on such an evening. But she had come out of the saloon entrance, her small face framed in a hood and her slim body shapeless beneath a great cloak, and joined him in his walk.

Jimmy was in a mood of exaltation. He had passed the last few days in a condition of intermittent melancholy, consequent on the discovery that he was not the only man on board the *Atlantic* who desired the society of Ann as an alleviation of the tedium of an ocean voyage. The world, when he embarked on this venture, had consisted so exclusively of Ann and himself that, until the ship was well on its way to Queenstown, he had not conceived the possibility of intrusive males forcing their unwelcome attentions on her. And it had added bitterness to the bitter awakening that their attentions did not appear to be at all unwelcome. Almost immediately after breakfast on the very first day, a creature with a small black moustache and shining teeth had descended upon Ann and, vocal with surprise and pleasure at meeting her again﻿—he claimed, damn him! to have met her before at Palm Beach, Bar Harbor, and a dozen other places﻿—had carried her off to play an idiotic game known as shuffleboard. Nor was this an isolated case. It began to be borne in upon Jimmy that Ann, whom he had looked upon purely in the light of an Eve playing opposite his Adam in an exclusive Garden of Eden, was an extremely well-known and popular character. The clerk at the shipping-office had lied absurdly when he had said that very few people were crossing on the *Atlantic* this voyage. The vessel was crammed till its sides bulged, it was loaded down in utter defiance of the Plimsoll law, with Rollos and Clarences and Dwights and Twombleys who had known and golfed and ridden and driven and motored and swum and danced with Ann for years. A ghastly being entitled Edgar Something or Teddy Something had beaten Jimmy by a short head in the race for the deck-steward, the prize of which was the placing of his deck-chair next to Ann’s. Jimmy had been driven from the promenade deck by the spectacle of this beastly creature lying swathed in rugs reading bestsellers to her.
===Chinese===
上涨的海水，连同鞭子似的抽得人生疼、越过船舷横扫进来的飞沫，已经把*大西洋号*（Atlantic）上的大部分乘客赶进了各自特等客舱的荫庇里，或是赶进了图书室那温暖而闷浊的空气里。这是航程的第五个傍晚。五天四夜以来，这艘船一直在赶路，穿过一片平静的海洋，驶向桑迪胡克；可是今天下午才过不多时，风就转了北，掀起了汹涌的巨浪。此刻黑暗已经开始降临。天空是一派阴沉的黑。长浪的白色浪峰在暮色中微微闪光，风在缆索间歌唱。

艇甲板上，吉米和安已经独占了半个钟头。吉米是个好水手：跟风搏斗一番，走在一块儿在脚下起伏、倾侧、发颤的甲板上，使他意气风发；但他没指望这样的夜晚还能有安作伴。可她偏偏从大厅入口走了出来——小小的脸蛋嵌在风帽的框子里，纤细的身躯裹在一件大斗篷下面没了轮廓——加入了他的散步。

吉米正处在一种欢欣鼓舞的心境之中。前几天，他一直在断断续续的忧郁里熬着，起因是他发现，在*大西洋号*上，渴望以安的陪伴来缓解横渡大洋之乏味的男人，并不是只有他一个。想当初他着手这桩冒险的时候，世界在他心目中简直清一色地由安和他两人组成，以至于直到轮船开往昆斯敦的路上走了老远，他都没设想过会有闯入的男性硬把她并不欢迎的殷勤塞给她。而这番苦涩的醒悟又平添一层苦味：那些殷勤在她那里似乎根本不受嫌弃。就在头一天，早饭后几乎立刻，一个留着小黑胡子、牙齿锃亮的家伙就朝安俯冲下来，嘴里连声惊呼，说再次遇见她是多么惊喜——他声称，该死的家伙！从前在棕榈滩、巴尔港以及其他一打地方都见过她——然后就把她掳走，去玩一种名叫沙狐球的蠢游戏。这还不是孤例。吉米渐渐被灌输了这样一个认识：安——这位他纯然当作专属伊甸园里与他的亚当配戏的夏娃的姑娘——竟是个大大有名、广受欢迎的人物。船票代办处那个职员说这班*大西洋号*没什么人横渡，真是荒谬透顶的谎言。这条船被塞得两舷发鼓，载重全然无视《普林索尔法》，满船的罗洛、克拉伦斯、德怀特和图姆布尔——这些年来他们跟安相识、打过高尔夫、骑过马、赶过车、开过汽车、游过泳、跳过舞。一个名唤埃德加什么或者泰迪什么的可怕家伙，还在争夺甲板侍者的竞赛里以一鼻之差击败了吉米——该项竞赛的奖品，便是把自家的甲板躺椅安放在安的躺椅旁边。眼见这头讨厌的畜生裹着毛毯躺在那儿念畅销书给她听，吉米被逼得从散步甲板上撤了下来。

===Original===
He had scarcely seen her to speak to since the beginning of the voyage. When she was not walking with Rolly or playing shuffleboard with Twombley, she was down below ministering to the comfort of a chronically seasick aunt, referred to in conversation as “poor aunt Nesta.” Sometimes Jimmy saw the little man﻿—presumably her uncle﻿—in the smoking-room, and once he came upon the stout boy recovering from the effects of a cigar in a quiet corner of the boat-deck: but apart from these meetings the family was as distant from him as if he had never seen Ann at all﻿—let alone saved her life.

And now she had dropped down on him from heaven. They were alone together with the good clean wind and the bracing scud. Rollo, Clarence, Dwight, and Twombley, not to mention Edgar or possibly Teddy, were down below﻿—he hoped, dying. They had the world to themselves.

“I love rough weather,” said Ann, lifting her face to the wind. Her eyes were very bright. She was beyond any doubt or question the only girl on earth. “Poor aunt Nesta doesn’t. She was bad enough when it was quite calm, but this storm has finished her. I’ve just been down below, trying to cheer her up.”
===Chinese===
启航以来，他几乎没有跟她攀谈的机会。她不是在陪罗洛散步，就是在跟图姆布尔玩沙狐球，再不就是在下层舱里照料一位晕船晕成慢性的姨妈——言谈之间称作「可怜的内斯塔姨妈」。吉米有时在吸烟室里瞧见那位小个子男人——想必是她的姨父——还有一回在艇甲板一个僻静角落撞见那胖小子，正从一支雪茄的余威里缓过劲儿来。可除了这些照面，这一家人疏远得就好像他压根儿没见过安似的——更别提救过她的命了。

而现在，她竟从天上降落到了他身边。他们俩单独待在一起，守着这又好又干净的风和提神的浪沫。罗洛、克拉伦斯、德怀特和图姆布尔，更不必说埃德加或者说不定是泰迪，统统待在下面——他希望，正在咽气。整个世界归他们俩独享。

「我爱恶劣的天气，」安迎着风仰起脸说。她的眼睛亮极了。她毫无疑问、无可争议地是世上独一无二的姑娘。「可怜的内斯塔姨妈可不爱。风平浪静的时候她就够受的了，这场风暴干脆把她了结了。我刚从下面上来，正试着给她打气。」

===Original===
Jimmy thrilled at the picture. Always fascinating, Ann seemed to him at her best in the role of ministering angel. He longed to tell her so, but found no words. They reached the end of the deck, and turned. Ann looked up at him.

“I’ve hardly seen anything of you since we sailed,” she said. She spoke almost reproachfully. “Tell me all about yourself, Mr. Bayliss. Why are you going to America?”

Jimmy had had an impassioned indictment of the Rollos on his tongue, but she had closed the opening for it as quickly as she had made it. In face of her direct demand for information he could not hark back to it now. After all, what did the Rollos matter? They had no part in this little windswept world: they were where they belonged, in some nether hell on the C. or D. deck, moaning for death.
===Chinese===
这幅图景让吉米心头发热。安素来就迷人，而在抚慰天使这个角色里，他觉得她更是好上加好。他渴望把这话告诉她，却找不到词儿。他们走到甲板尽头，转身往回走。安抬眼望着他。

「开船以后，我几乎没怎么见着你，」她说，语气里几乎带着责备。「跟我讲讲你自己的事吧，贝利斯先生。你为什么要去美国？」

吉米舌尖上本来压着一篇控诉罗洛之流的激昂檄文，可她把这个话口开得有多快，关得就有多快。面对她直截了当的问询，他此刻没法再绕回那篇檄文去。说到底，罗洛之流算什么东西？他们在这疾风横扫的小世界里没有份儿：他们待在应待的地方——C 层或 D 层甲板上的哪座地狱里，正哼哼着求死呢。

===Original===
“To make a fortune, I hope,” he said.

Ann was pleased at this confirmation of her diagnosis. She had deduced this from the evidence at Paddington Station.

“How pleased your father will be if you do!”

The slight complexity of Jimmy’s affairs caused him to pause for a moment to sort out his fathers, but an instant’s reflection told him that she must be referring to Bayliss the butler.

“Yes.”
===Chinese===
「我希望是去发财，」他说。

自己的诊断得到了证实，安很是满意。这是她根据帕丁顿车站的证据推演出来的。

「你要是真发了财，你父亲该多高兴啊！」

吉米的境遇有那么一点儿复杂，害他停顿片刻，好把他的几位父亲捋一捋；但一转念就明白了，她说的准是管家贝利斯。

「是啊。」

===Original===
“He’s a dear old man,” said Ann. “I suppose he’s very proud of you?”

“I hope so.”

“You must do tremendously well in America, so as not to disappoint him. What are you thinking of doing?”

Jimmy considered for a moment.

“Newspaper work, I think.”
===Chinese===
「他是个可爱的老头儿，」安说。「我猜他一定非常为你骄傲吧？」

「但愿如此。」

「你在美国必须干得极其出色，才不至于让他失望。你打算干什么呢？」

吉米考虑了片刻。

「我想是干新闻这一行。」

===Original===
“Oh? Why, have you had any experience?”

“A little.”

Ann seemed to grow a little aloof, as if her enthusiasm had been damped.

“Oh, well, I suppose it’s a good enough profession. I’m not very fond of it myself. I’ve only met one newspaper man in my life, and I dislike him very much, so I suppose that has prejudiced me.”

“Who was that?”

“You wouldn’t have met him. He was on an American paper. A man named Crocker.”
===Chinese===
「哦？怎么，你有过经验？」

「有过一点儿。」

安似乎变得有些疏远，仿佛她的热忱被浇湿了。

「哦，嗯，我想这也算个体面的职业。我自己不大喜欢它。我这辈子只遇见过一个报人，又非常讨厌他，所以大概是这缘故让我带了成见。」

「是谁？」

「你不会遇见过他。他在一家美国报纸做事。一个姓克罗克的男人。」

===Original===
A sudden gust of wind drove them back a step, rendering talk impossible. It covered a gap when Jimmy could not have spoken. The shock of the information that Ann had met him before made him dumb. This thing was beyond him. It baffled him.

Her next words supplied a solution. They were under shelter of one of the boats now and she could make herself heard.

“It was five years ago, and I only met him for a very short while, but the prejudice has lasted.”

Jimmy began to understand. Five years ago! It was not so strange, then, that they should not recognise each other now. He stirred up his memory. Nothing came to the surface. Not a gleam of recollection of that early meeting rewarded him. And yet something of importance must have happened then, for her to remember it. Surely his mere personality could not have been so unpleasant as to have made such a lasting impression on her!
===Chinese===
一阵骤风把他们逼退一步，说话成了不可能的事。这阵风恰好盖住了吉米本来就出不了声的空当。安从前遇见过他——这一情报的冲击把他震哑了。这件事超出了他的理解力。这把他难住了。

她的下一句话给出了解答。他们此刻正待在一艘救生艇的遮蔽之下，她的话音可以让人听清了。

「那是五年前的事了，我跟他只照面了短短一会儿，可这成见一直留到了今天。」

吉米开始明白了。五年前！这么说，他们如今彼此认不出来，也就不那么奇怪了。他把记忆搅了又搅。什么也没浮上来。对那次早年会面，连一星回忆的微光也没回报给他。可是，当年必定发生过什么要紧的事，否则她不会记得。总不至于单凭他这个人的气性，就讨厌到能在她心里留下这般经久不褪的印象吧！

===Original===
“I wish you could do something better than newspaper work,” said Ann. “I always think the splendid part about America is that it is such a land of adventure. There are such millions of chances. It’s a place where anything may happen. Haven’t you an adventurous soul, Mr. Bayliss?”

No man lightly submits to a charge, even a hinted charge, of being deficient in the capacity for adventure.

“Of course I have,” said Jimmy indignantly. “I’m game to tackle anything that comes along.”

“I’m glad of that.”

Her feeling of comradeship towards this young man deepened. She loved adventure and based her estimate of any member of the opposite sex largely on his capacity for it. She moved in a set, when at home, which was more polite than adventurous, and had frequently found the atmosphere enervating.
===Chinese===
「真希望你能干点儿比新闻更好的营生，」安说。「我一向觉得美国妙就妙在，它是一片冒险的国土。那儿有几百万几百万的机会。那是个任何事都可能发生的地方。贝利斯先生，你难道没有一颗爱冒险的灵魂吗？」

没有哪个男人会轻易忍受一项指控——哪怕是暗示性的指控——说他缺乏冒险的能耐。

「我当然有，」吉米愤慨地说。「来什么我都奉陪到底。」

「那我很高兴。」

她对这位青年的同志之谊又深了一层。她热爱冒险，对任何一个异性的估价，大体就取决于对方的冒险能耐。她在家的时候来往的那个圈子，礼数有余而冒险不足，她常常觉得那气氛软绵绵地磨人。

===Original===
“Adventure,” said Jimmy, “is everything.”

He paused. “Or a good deal,” he concluded weakly.

“Why qualify it like that? It sounds so tame. Adventure is the biggest thing in life.”

It seemed to Jimmy that he had received an excuse for a remark of a kind that had been waiting for utterance ever since he had met her. Often and often in the watches of the night, smoking endless pipes and thinking of her, he had conjured up just such a vision as this﻿—they two walking the deserted deck alone, and she innocently giving him an opening for some low-voiced, tender speech, at which she would start, look at him quickly, and then ask him haltingly if the words had any particular application. And after that﻿—oh, well, all sorts of things might happen. And now the moment had come. It was true that he had always pictured the scene as taking place by moonlight and at present there was a half-gale blowing, out of an inky sky; also on the present occasion anything in the nature of a low-voiced speech was absolutely out of the question owing to the uproar of the elements. Still, taking these drawbacks into consideration, the chance was far too good to miss. Such an opening might never happen again. He waited till the ship had steadied herself after an apparently suicidal dive into an enormous roller, then, staggering back to her side, spoke.
===Chinese===
「冒险，」吉米说，「就是一切。」

他顿了顿。「或者说，是一桩很大的事，」他有气无力地补了一句。

「干吗要这么打折扣？听着多没劲。冒险才是人生头等大事。」

在吉米看来，他总算得到了一个借口，可以说出一句自打遇见她以来就一直等着出口的话。多少个不眠的夜里，他抽着一斗又一斗没完没了的烟斗，心里想着她，唤出的正是这样一幅幻景——他们两个独自走在空寂的甲板上，她天真无邪地递给他一个话口，让他低声说出一句温柔的话；她会一怔，飞快地看他一眼，然后吞吞吐吐地问，这话是不是另有所指。再然后——哦，那可就什么事都可能发生了。而现在，这一刻到来了。诚然，他一向把这一幕想象成发生在月光底下，而此刻正从墨黑的天上刮下来半场大风；再说在眼下这个场合，任何低声细语性质的发言都绝对无从谈起，全拜狂风暴雨的喧闹所赐。不过，把这些不利因素统统考虑在内，这个机会仍旧好得不容错过。这样的话题口也许再也不会有了。他一直等到轮船一个显然自杀式地扎进一道巨浪、又挣扎着稳住船身之后，才踉踉跄跄回到她身边，开了口。

===Original===
“Love is the biggest thing in life!” he roared.

“What is?” shrieked Ann.

“Love!” bellowed Jimmy.

He wished a moment later that he had postponed this statement of faith, for their next steps took them into a haven of comparative calm, where some dimly seen portion of the vessel’s anatomy jutted out and formed a kind of nook where it was possible to hear the ordinary tones of the human voice. He halted here, and Ann did the same, though unwillingly. She was conscious of a feeling of disappointment and of a modification of her mood of comradeship towards her companion. She held strong views, which she believed to be unalterable, on the subject under discussion.

“Love!” she said. It was too dark to see her face, but her voice sounded unpleasantly scornful. “I shouldn’t have thought that you would have been so conventional as that. You seemed different.”
===Chinese===
「爱情才是人生头等大事！」他吼道。

「什么才是？」安尖声叫道。

「爱情！」吉米雷鸣般喊道。

过了一会儿，他就后悔没把这句信仰宣言推迟发布，因为他们再迈几步，就进了一个相对平静的避风港——船身上某个影影绰绰的部位在这里凸出来，构成一个类似壁龛的角落，人声的寻常音量在这儿是听得见的。他在这儿停住，安也不情愿地跟着停住。她心里泛起一阵失望，对同伴的那份同志情谊也打了折扣。对于眼下讨论的题目，她持有一套强硬的观点，并且相信它们绝无更改。

「爱情！」她说。天太黑，看不见她的脸，可她的嗓音听上去满是讥诮，叫人不好受。「真没想到你会俗套到这个地步。你原先看着不像这种人。」

===Original===
“Eh?” said Jimmy blankly.

“I hate all this talk about Love, as if it were something wonderful that was worth everything else in life put together. Every book you read and every song that you see in the shopwindows is all about Love. It’s as if the whole world were in a conspiracy to persuade themselves that there’s a wonderful something just round the corner which they can get if they try hard enough. And they hypnotise themselves into thinking of nothing else and miss all the splendid things of life.”

“That’s Shaw, isn’t it?” said Jimmy.

“What is Shaw?”

“What you were saying. It’s out of one of Bernard Shaw’s things, isn’t it?”
===Chinese===
「啊？」吉米茫然地说。

「我讨厌这一切关于爱情的高谈阔论，把它说成什么了不得的奇珍，抵得上人生里其余一切加在一起。你读的每一本书，你在橱窗里看见的每一支歌，谈的全是爱情。这就好像全世界串通好了要劝自己相信：拐个弯就有一件妙不可言的什么，只要肯下力气就到手。他们把自己催眠得什么都不想，于是错过了生活里一切美妙的东西。」

「这是萧伯纳的调子，对不对？」吉米说。

「什么萧伯纳？」

「你方才说的那套话。是从萧伯纳哪部作品里出来的，对不对？」

===Original===
“It is not.” A note of acidity had crept into Ann’s voice. “It is perfectly original.”

“I’m certain I’ve heard it before somewhere.”

“If you have, that simply means that you must have associated with some sensible person.”

Jimmy was puzzled.

“But why the grouch?” he asked.
===Chinese===
「才不是。」安的嗓音里爬进了一丝酸味。「这是彻头彻尾的独创。」

「我敢肯定从前在哪儿听过。」

「就算你听过，那也只能说明，你必定结交过什么明白人。」

吉米被弄糊涂了。

「可是，哪来这么大的火气？」他问。

===Original===
“I don’t understand you.”

“I mean, why do you feel that way about it?”

Ann was quite certain now that she did not like this young man nearly as well as she had supposed. It is trying for a strong-minded, clear-thinking girl to have her philosophy described as a grouch.

“Because I’ve had the courage to think about it for myself, and not let myself be blinded by popular superstition. The whole world has united in making itself imagine that there is something called love which is the most wonderful happening in life. The poets and novelists have simply hounded them on to believe it. It’s a gigantic swindle.”

A wave of tender compassion swept over Jimmy. He understood it all now. Naturally a girl who had associated all her life with the Rollos, Clarences, Dwights, and Twombleys would come to despair of the possibility of falling in love with anyone.
===Chinese===
「我不明白你的意思。」

「我是说，你为什么对这事抱这种看法？」

安现在十分确定，她远不像自己原先以为的那样喜欢这位青年。一个有主见、头脑清楚的姑娘，自己的哲学被人形容成一股无名火，这滋味够呛。

「因为我有勇气自己把这事想明白，不肯让自己被大众迷信蒙住眼睛。全世界齐心协力，让自己想象出一种叫爱情的东西，说它是人生里最了不得的奇遇。诗人和小说家又一味撺掇大家去信。这是一场天大的骗局。」

一阵温柔的怜悯涌过吉米全身。他这下全明白了。一个打小到大连身边都是罗洛、克拉伦斯、德怀特和图姆布尔的姑娘，自然会灰心绝望，觉得跟谁谈情说爱都没指望。

===Original===
“You haven’t met the right man,” he said. She had, of course, but only recently: and, anyway, he could point that out later.

“There is no such thing as the right man,” said Ann resolutely, “if you are suggesting that there is a type of man in existence who is capable of inspiring what is called romantic love. I believe in marriage.﻿ ﻿…”

“Good work!” said Jimmy, well satisfied.

“… But not as the result of a sort of delirium. I believe in it as a sensible partnership between two friends who know each other well and trust each other. The right way of looking at marriage is to realise, first of all, that there are no thrills, no romances, and then to pick out someone who is nice and kind and amusing and full of life and willing to do things to make you happy.”

“Ah!” said Jimmy, straightening his tie, “Well, that’s something.”
===Chinese===
「你只是还没遇上那个对的男人，」他说。她其实遇上了，当然，只是时日尚浅；再说，这一点尽可以往后再向她指明。

「世上根本不存在什么对的男人，」安斩钉截铁地说，「如果你是想暗示有那么一类男人，有能力激发所谓浪漫爱情的话。我信婚姻。……」

「好极了！」吉米说，心满意足。

「……但不是当成一种谵妄的产物。我信的是一种明智的伙伴关系：两个知根知底、彼此信任的朋友。看待婚姻的正确方式，是首先认清这里面没有销魂荡魄，没有罗曼史，然后挑一个和气、善良、有趣、生气勃勃、又乐意费心让你高兴的人。」

「啊！」吉米说着，正了正领带，「嗯，这总算有点内容。」

===Original===
“How do you mean﻿—that’s something? Are you shocked at my views?”

“I don’t believe they are your views. You’ve been reading one of these stern, soured fellows who analyse things.”

Ann stamped. The sound was inaudible, but Jimmy noticed the movement.

“Cold?” he said. “Let’s walk on.”

Ann’s sense of humour reasserted itself. It was not often that it remained dormant for so long. She laughed.
===Chinese===
「你这是什么意思——有点内容？你被我的观点惊着了？」

「我不信这些是你自己的观点。你准是读了哪个板着脸、酸溜溜、逢事都要分析一通的家伙的书。」

安跺了跺脚。跺脚声一点也听不见，可吉米注意到了这个动作。

「冷吗？」他说。「咱们接着走。」

安的幽默感重新占了上风。它沉睡这么久的时候可不多。她笑了。

===Original===
“I know exactly what you are thinking,” she said. “You believe that I am posing, that those aren’t my real opinions.”

“They can’t be. But I don’t think you are posing. It’s getting on for dinnertime, and you’ve got that wan, sinking feeling that makes you look upon the world and find it a hollow fraud. The bugle will be blowing in a few minutes, and half an hour after that you will be yourself again.”

“I’m myself now. I suppose you can’t realise that a pretty girl can hold such views.”

Jimmy took her arm.

“Let me help you,” he said. “There’s a knothole in the deck. Watch your step. Now, listen to me. I’m glad you’ve brought up this subject﻿—I mean the subject of your being the prettiest girl in the known world﻿—”
===Chinese===
「我完全知道你在想什么，」她说。「你认为我在摆姿态，认为那些不是我真正的意见。」

「不可能是。不过我并不认为你在摆姿态。眼看到开饭的钟点了，你正犯着那种面色发虚、心里发空的劲儿，看这世界一眼，就觉得它是个空心的骗局。再过几分钟开饭号就要吹了，再过半个钟头，你就又还原成你自己了。」

「我现在就是我自己。我猜你是没法领会，一个漂亮姑娘也能抱这种观点。」

吉米挽住她的手臂。

「让我扶着你，」他说。「甲板上有个木节孔。留神脚下。好，听我说。我很高兴你提出了这个话题——我是说，你是已知世界里最漂亮的姑娘这个话题——」

===Original===
“I never said that.”

“Your modesty prevented you. But it’s a fact, nevertheless. I’m glad, I say, because I have been thinking a lot along those lines myself, and I have been anxious to discuss the point with you. You have the most glorious hair I have ever seen!”

“Do you like red hair?”

“Red-gold.”

“It is nice of you to put it like that. When I was a child all except a few of the other children called me Carrots.”
===Chinese===
「我可从来没这么说。」

「是你的谦逊拦住了你。可这是个事实，不折不扣。我说我很高兴，是因为我自己也一直顺着这些思路想了许多，而且一直盼着同你探讨这个论点。你的头发，是我这辈子见过的最辉煌的头发！」

「你喜欢红头发？」

「红金色的。」

「你这么说真客气。我小时候，别的孩子除了少数几个，全管我叫『胡萝卜头』。」

===Original===
“They have undoubtedly come to a bad end by this time. If bears were sent to attend to the children who criticised Elijah, your little friends were in line for a troupe of tigers. But there were some of a finer fibre? There were a few who didn’t call you Carrots?”

“One or two. They called me Brick-Top.”

“They have probably been electrocuted since. Your eyes are perfectly wonderful!”
===Chinese===
「那些人到如今无疑都没落得好下场。要是非议以利亚的孩子们会被派狗熊去收拾，你的那些小朋友都有资格领受一整队老虎了。不过也有几个材质优良的？也有那么几个不叫你胡萝卜头的？」

「有一两个。他们管我叫『红砖顶』。」

「他们后来八成被送上电椅了。你的眼睛真是美妙绝伦！」

===Original===
Ann withdrew her arm. An extensive acquaintance of young men told her that the topic of conversation was now due to be changed.

“You will like America,” she said.

“We are not discussing America.”

“I am. It is a wonderful country for a man who wants to succeed. If I were you, I should go out West.”

“Do you live out West?”
===Chinese===
安抽回了手臂。与青年男子打交道的广泛经验告诉她：谈话的题目现在该换了。

「你会喜欢美国的，」她说。

「咱们谈的不是美国。」

「我在谈。对一个想成功的男人来说，那是个好得不得了的国家。我要是你，就上西部去。」

「你住在西部吗？」

===Original===
“No.”

“Then why suggest my going there? Where do you live?”

“I live in New York.”

“I shall stay in New York, then.”
===Chinese===
「不。」

「那你干吗撺掇我去那儿？你住在哪儿？」

「我住在纽约。」

「那我就待在纽约。」

===Original===
Ann was wary, but amused. Proposals of marriage﻿—and Jimmy seemed to be moving swiftly towards one﻿—were no novelty in her life. In the course of several seasons at Bar Harbor, Tuxedo, Palm Beach, and in New York itself, she had spent much of her time foiling and discouraging the ardour of a series of sentimental youths who had laid their unwelcome hearts at her feet.

“New York is open for staying in about this time, I believe.”

Jimmy was silent. He had done his best to fight a tendency to become depressed and had striven by means of a light tone to keep himself resolutely cheerful, but the girl’s apparently total indifference to him was too much for his spirits. One of the young men who had had to pick up the heart he had flung at Ann’s feet and carry it away for repairs had once confided to an intimate friend, after the sting had to some extent passed, that the feelings of a man who made love to Ann might be likened to the emotions which hot chocolate might be supposed to entertain on contact with vanilla ice-cream. Jimmy, had the comparison been presented to him, would have endorsed its perfect accuracy. The wind from the sea, until now keen and bracing, had become merely infernally cold. The song of the wind in the rigging, erstwhile melodious, had turned into a damned depressing howling.

“I used to be as sentimental as anyone a few years ago,” said Ann, returning to the dropped subject. “Just after I left college, I was quite maudlin. I dreamed of moons and Junes and loves and doves all the time. Then something happened which made me see what a little fool I was. It wasn’t pleasant at the time, but it had a very bracing effect. I have been quite different ever since. It was a man, of course, who did it. His method was quite simple. He just made fun of me, and Nature did the rest.”
===Chinese===
安警惕起来，但也不无兴致。求婚——而吉米似乎正朝着一桩求婚疾步前进——在她的人生里可不是新鲜事。在巴尔港、塔克西多、棕榈滩，以及纽约本地经过了好几个社交季，她把许多时间花在挫败和劝退一连串多情少年的热忱上——这些人不请自来，把心捧到她脚边。

「我记得，纽约在这个时节是照常收客的。」

吉米沉默了。他已经尽了最大努力对抗消沉的趋势，极力用轻快的口吻让自己坚决地保持愉快，可这姑娘对他显然全然的冷漠，对他的心气儿实在打击太大了。曾有这么一位青年——他扔到安脚边的那颗心不得不自己拾起来，抱走去返修——在痛感多少平复之后，曾向一位密友吐露：向安求爱的男人的感受，可以比作热巧克力在碰到香草冰淇淋时想必会怀有的那几种情绪。这个比方若是摆到吉米面前，他准会连声赞同它精确无比。从海上来的风，方才还凛冽提神，如今简直是冷得要命；风在索具间的歌唱，先前还悠扬动听，如今已变成一阵该死的、叫人丧气的嚎叫。

「几年以前，我跟谁一样多愁善感，」安说，回到了搁下的话题上。「刚出大学那阵子，我简直感伤得要命。成天梦着月亮啊、六月啊、爱情啊、鸽子啊。后来出了一件事，让我看清自己是个多小的傻瓜。当时并不好受，可是强身健体的效果极佳。打那以后我就完全变了。做这件事的当然是个男人。他的方法简单得很。他不过取笑了我一场，其余的都由天性代劳。」

===Original===
Jimmy scowled in the darkness. Murderous thoughts towards the unknown brute flooded his mind.

“I wish I could meet him!” he growled.

“You aren’t likely to,” said Ann. “He lives in England. His name is Crocker. Jimmy Crocker. I spoke about him just now.”

Through the howling of the wind cut the sharp notes of a bugle. Ann turned to the saloon entrance.

“Dinner!” she said brightly. “How hungry one gets on board ship!” She stopped. “Aren’t you coming down, Mr. Bayliss?”

“Not just yet,” said Jimmy thickly.
===Chinese===
吉米在黑暗里沉下了脸。对那个无名畜生的杀气涌满了他的头脑。

「真希望我能会一会他！」他低吼道。

「你怕是没这个机会，」安说。「他住在英国。他姓克罗克。吉米·克罗克。我方才说到过他。」

开饭号尖锐的音符穿透了风啸。安转身朝大厅入口走去。

「开饭啦！」她喜盈盈地说。「在船上人饿得多快呀！」她停了停。「你不下来吗，贝利斯先生？」

「还不到时候，」吉米嗓音发闷地说。

## VIII / 八

Painful Scene in a Café / 咖啡馆里的难堪一幕

===Original===
The noonday sun beat down on Park Row. Hurrying mortals, released from a thousand offices, congested the sidewalks, their thoughts busy with the vision of lunch. Up and down the canyon of Nassau Street the crowds moved more slowly. Candy-selling aliens jostled newsboys, and huge dray-horses endeavoured to the best of their ability not to grind the citizenry beneath their hooves. Eastward, pressing on to the City Hall, surged the usual dense army of happy lovers on their way to buy marriage-licenses. Men popped in and out of the subway entrances like rabbits. It was a stirring, bustling scene, typical of this nerve-centre of New York’s vast body.

Jimmy Crocker, standing in the doorway, watched the throngs enviously. There were men in that crowd who chewed gum, there were men who wore white satin ties with imitation diamond stickpins, there were men who, having smoked seven-tenths of a cigar, were eating the remainder: but there was not one with whom he would not at that moment willingly have exchanged identities. For these men had jobs. And in his present frame of mind it seemed to him that no further ingredient was needed for the recipe of the ultimate human bliss.

The poet has said some very searching and unpleasant things about the man “whose heart has ne’er within him burned as home his footsteps he has turned from wandering on some foreign strand,” but he might have excused Jimmy for feeling just then not so much a warmth of heart as a cold and clammy sensation of dismay. He would have had to admit that the words “High though his titles, proud his name, boundless his wealth as wish can claim” did not apply to Jimmy Crocker. The latter may have been “concentred all on self,” but his wealth consisted of one hundred and thirty-three dollars and forty cents and his name was so far from being proud that the mere sight of it in the files of the New York *Sunday Chronicle*, the record-room of which he had just been visiting, had made him consider the fact that he had changed it to Bayliss the most sensible act of his career.

===Chinese===
正午的太阳火辣辣地晒着帕克罗（Park Row）街。匆匆的凡人从上千间办公室里放出来，把人行道挤得水泄不通，心里全叫午餐的幻象占满了。在拿骚街（Nassau Street）这条峡谷里，人流走得慢一些。卖糖果的外国佬同报童推推搡搡，高头大马的运货骡马竭尽所能，力求不把市民碾进蹄子底下去。往东，朝市政厅方向涌去的，是照例那支稠密而幸福的恋人大军，正赶着去领结婚证书。男人们在地铁入口里出出进进，活像一群兔子。这是一幅热气腾腾、熙来攘往的景象，正是纽约这庞然大物的神经中枢的典型写照。

吉米·克罗克站在门口，满心艳羡地望着人潮。那人群里有嚼口香糖的男人，有系白缎领带、别仿钻领针的男人，有把一支雪茄抽掉十分之七、正在吃余下十分之三的男人：可是其中没有一个，是他此刻不肯与之对调身份的。因为这些男人有差事。而以他眼下的心境看来，人生至福的方子里，再也无需添加别的配料了。

那位诗人对某一种男人说过好些非常一针见血、大煞风景的话——说什么「当他浪游归来转家门，此心可曾在胸中烧过一分」——可是吉米此刻心里的感觉，与其说是心头一暖，不如说是一阵又冷又黏的沮丧，诗人本该体谅他。诗人也不得不承认，「任他头衔何等高，名号何等傲，家财如愿无穷饶」这些诗句，用在吉米·克罗克身上并不对景。后面那句「一心只把自家念」也许没冤枉他，可他的家财统共一百三十三美元四十美分，他的名号也远说不上骄傲——单是它在纽约*《星期日纪事报》*（Sunday Chronicle）的合订档库里露面——那档库他方才正在造访——就足以叫他认定，把名字改成贝利斯实在是他此生最明白的一着棋。

===Original===
The reason for Jimmy’s lack of enthusiasm as he surveyed the portion of his native land visible from his doorway is not far to seek. The *Atlantic* had docked on Saturday night, and Jimmy, having driven to an excellent hotel and engaged an expensive room therein, had left instructions at the desk that breakfast should be served to him at ten o’clock and with it the Sunday issue of the *Chronicle*. Five years had passed since he had seen the dear old rag for which he had reported so many fires, murders, street-accidents, and weddings: and he looked forward to its perusal as a formal taking seisin of his long-neglected country. Nothing could be more fitting and symbolic than that the first morning of his return to America should find him propped up in bed reading the good old *Chronicle*. Among his final meditations as he dropped off to sleep was a gentle speculation as to who was City editor now and whether the comic supplement was still featuring the sprightly adventures of the Doughnut family.

A wave of not unmanly sentiment passed over him on the following morning as he reached out for the paper. The skyline of New York, seen as the boat comes up the bay, has its points, and the rattle of the Elevated trains and the quaint odour of the Subway extend a kindly welcome, but the thing that really convinces the returned traveller that he is back on Manhattan Island is the first Sunday paper. Jimmy, like everyone else, began by opening the comic supplement: and as he scanned it a chilly discomfort, almost a premonition of evil, came upon him. The Doughnut Family was no more. He knew that it was unreasonable of him to feel as if he had just been informed of the death of a dear friend, for Pa Doughnut and his associates had been having their adventures five years before he had left the country, and even the toughest comic supplementary hero rarely endures for a decade: but nevertheless the shadow did fall upon his morning optimism, and he derived no pleasure whatever from the artificial rollickings of a degraded creature called Old Pop Dill-Pickle who was offered as a substitute.

But this, he was to discover almost immediately, was a trifling disaster. It distressed him, but it did not affect his material welfare. Tragedy really began when he turned to the magazine section. Scarcely had he started to glance at it when this headline struck him like a bullet:

===Chinese===
吉米站在门口，眺望祖国那打眼下铺开的一角，何以兴致缺缺，缘由不难查明。*大西洋号*是星期六夜里靠的岸。吉米驱车去了家上等旅馆，开了个价钱不菲的房间，在账台上留话：十点把早饭送进房来，随饭附上《纪事报》的星期日版。他上一回见这份亲爱的小破报，已经是五年前的事了——想当年，多少场火灾、凶杀、车祸、婚礼，都是他替它采访的：他把捧读这张报纸，当作对自己久疏问候的祖国的一场正式领受。回到美国的头一个清晨，恰好在床上倚着读那份好心肠的老《纪事报》，天下再没有比这更得体、更具象征意味的安排了。他入睡前的末几桩冥想里，有一桩温柔的悬想：如今本市新闻主编是谁了？漫画增刊还在连载「多纳圈一家」（the Doughnut Family）那些活蹦乱跳的历险吗？

第二天早晨，他伸手去够报纸，一阵不失丈夫气概的柔情涌遍全身。船进海湾时望见的纽约天际线自有其妙处，高架列车的哐啷声和地铁里那股古雅的气味也堪当一份善意的欢迎；可是真正叫归来的旅人确信自己重又踏上曼哈顿岛的，还得数头一份星期日报纸。吉米跟普天下人一样，先翻开的就是漫画增刊：他一路扫视过去，一阵冷冰冰的不自在——几乎是灾祸的预感——兜头罩了下来。「多纳圈一家」没了。他知道，自己这感觉活像刚接到挚友的死讯，未免不讲道理，因为多纳圈老爹和他的伙伴们早在五年前他离国之前就一直在历险，况且再结实的漫画增刊英雄也少有熬得过十年的：然而那片阴影终究落在他清晨的乐观上，而对于顶替上场的一个格调低下的家伙——名唤「酸黄瓜老爹」（Old Pop Dill-Pickle）——那副装腔作势的欢蹦乱跳，他更是半点趣味都尝不出来了。

不过这一场，他几乎马上就要发现，还只是区区小灾。它叫他难受，却伤不着他的生计。真正的悲剧，是打他翻到杂志版才开的头。他刚瞥了一眼，底下这个大标题就像一颗子弹似的击中了他：

===Original===
Piccadilly Jim At It Again

And beneath it his own name.

Nothing is so capable of diversity as the emotion we feel on seeing our name unexpectedly in print. We may soar to the heights or we may sink to the depths. Jimmy did the latter. A mere cursory first inspection of the article revealed the fact that it was no eulogy. With an unsparing hand the writer had muckraked his eventful past, the text on which he hung his remarks being that ill-fated encounter with Lord Percy Whipple at the Six Hundred Club. This the scribe had recounted at a length and with a boisterous vim which outdid even Bill Blake’s effort in the London *Daily Sun*. Bill Blake had been handicapped by consideration of space and the fact that he had turned in his copy at an advanced hour when the paper was almost made up. The present writer was shackled by no restrictions. He had plenty of room to spread himself in, and he had spread himself. So liberal had been the editor’s views in the respect that, in addition to the letterpress, the pages contained an unspeakably offensive picture of a burly young man in an obviously advanced condition of alcoholism raising his fist to strike a monocled youth in evening dress who had so little chin that Jimmy was surprised that he had ever been able to hit it. The only gleam of consolation that he could discover in this repellent drawing was the fact that the artist had treated Lord Percy even more scurvily than himself. Among other things, the second son of the Duke of Devizes was depicted as wearing a coronet﻿—a thing which would have excited remark even in a London nightclub.

===Chinese===
皮卡迪利·吉姆故态复萌

底下印着他自己的大名。

世上的事，再没有比冷不丁看见自己名字上了报的那份心情更变幻莫测的了。咱们也许直上九霄，也许沉入渊底。吉米沉入了渊底。那篇文章头一遍走马观花，便足以看出它绝非颂词。执笔人下手毫不留情，把他多灾多难的往昔扒了个底朝天，而用来悬挂这篇宏论的钉子，正是他在六百俱乐部同珀西·惠普尔勋爵那场倒霉的遭遇战。这一节，执笔人叙述得篇幅之长、气焰之盛，连比尔·布莱克在伦敦《每日太阳报》上那番功夫都甘拜下风。比尔·布莱克当时受着两重掣肘：一是版面要打算，二是他交稿交得晚，报纸差不多已经拼好版了。眼前这位执笔人却不受半点儿束缚。他有的是天地任凭铺陈，他也着实铺陈了。编辑在这上头的见识又宽宏到那个地步，以致正文之外，版面上还配了一幅不堪入目的图画：一个五大三粗的青年，显然纵酒已入晚期，正抡起拳头，要打一个戴单片眼镜、穿晚礼服、下巴短得几乎不存在的公子哥儿——那下巴之小，害得吉米直纳闷，当初自己怎么居然打得着它。这幅面目可憎的图画里，他能寻见的一线安慰，唯有画家把珀西勋爵糟践得比他自己还狠这一条。桩桩件件之外，德维泽斯公爵的二公子竟被画成头戴宝冠——这种事，就是在伦敦的夜总会里也要惹人议论的。

===Original===
Jimmy read the thing through in its entirety three times before he appreciated a nuance which his disordered mind had at first failed to grasp﻿—to wit, that this character-sketch of himself was no mere isolated outburst but apparently one of a series. In several places the writer alluded unmistakeably to other theses on the same subject.

Jimmy’s breakfast congealed on its tray, untouched. That boon which the gods so seldom bestow, of seeing ourselves as others see us, had been accorded to him in full measure. By the time he had completed his third reading he was regarding himself in a purely objective fashion not unlike the attitude of a naturalist towards some strange and loathesome manifestation of insect life. So this was the sort of fellow he was! He wondered they had let him in at a reputable hotel.

===Chinese===
吉米把那篇东西从头到尾整个儿读了三遍，才咂摸出一层他乱作一团的脑子起初没领会的微妙之处——也就是说，这篇他的人物小传并非孤立的一回发作，而分明是系列宏文里的一篇。好几处地方，执笔人清清楚楚地援引了同一题目底下的其他宏文。

吉米的早饭在托盘上凝成了一坨，原封未动。诸神罕肯赏赐的那桩恩典——让别人眼里的自己给自己瞧见——已经足尺足寸地加在了他身上。到第三遍读完，他已经在用一种纯然客观的眼光打量自己，那架势，跟博物学家端详昆虫生命里某种古怪而可憎的形态殊无二致。原来他是这么一号东西！他奇怪那家体面旅馆居然肯放他进门。

===Original===
The rest of the day he passed in a state of such humility that he could have wept when the waiters were civil to him. On the Monday morning he made his way to Park Row to read the files of the *Chronicle*﻿—a morbid enterprise, akin to the eccentric behaviour of those priests of Baal who gashed themselves with knives or of authors who subscribe to press-clipping agencies.

He came upon another of the articles almost at once, in an issue not a month old. Then there was a gap of several weeks, and hope revived that things might not be as bad as he had feared﻿—only to be crushed by another trenchant screed. After that he set about his excavations methodically, resolved to know the worst. He knew it in just under two hours. There it all was﻿—his row with the bookie, his bad behaviour at the political meeting, his breach-of-promise case. It was a complete biography.

And the name they called him. Piccadilly Jim! Ugh!

===Chinese===
那一天的其余时光，他是在一种极其谦卑的心境里度过的，侍者对他客气一声，他都险些掉下泪来。星期一早晨，他上帕克罗街去读《纪事报》的合订档库——一桩病态的营生，好比巴力（Baal）手下的祭司用刀自割的古怪行径，又好比那些订阅剪报公司的作家。

他几乎立刻就撞上了系列里的另一篇，登在一份不满一个月的报纸上。接着是几个星期的空白，希望又活了过来：事情也许不像他担心的那么糟——随即被另一篇刀锋淋漓的宏文碾了个粉碎。打那以后，他便有条不紊地展开发掘，抱定主意要探个水落石出。差两个钟头不到，他探明了。全在那儿了——他跟赌注贩子的口角，他在政治集会上的恶劣行径，他的婚约毁弃案。一部完完整整的传记。

还有他们送他的那个名号。皮卡迪利·吉姆！呸！

===Original===
He went out into Park Row, and sought a quiet doorway where he could brood upon these matters.

It was not immediately that the practical or financial aspect of the affair came to scourge him. For an appreciable time he suffered in his self-esteem alone. It seemed to him that all these bustling persons who passed knew him, that they were casting sidelong glances at him and laughing derisively, that those who chewed gum chewed it sneeringly and that those who ate their cigars ate them with thinly-veiled disapproval and scorn. Then, the passage of time blunting sensitiveness, he found that there were other and weightier things to consider.

As far as he had had any connected plan of action in his sudden casting-off of the fleshpots of London, he had determined as soon as possible after landing to report at the office of his old paper and apply for his ancient position. So little thought had he given to the minutiae of his future plans that it had not occurred to him that he had anything to do but walk in, slap the gang on the back, and announce that he was ready to work. Work!﻿—on the staff of a paper whose chief diversion appeared to be the satirising of his escapades! Even had he possessed the moral courage﻿—or gall﻿—to make the application, what good would it be? He was a byword in a world where he had once been a worthy citizen. What paper would trust Piccadilly Jim with an assignment? What paper would consider Piccadilly Jim even on space rates? A chill dismay crept over him. He seemed to hear the grave voice of Bayliss the butler speaking in his car as he had spoken so short a while before at Paddington Station.

===Chinese===
他走出去，到了帕克罗街上，寻着一个清静门洞，好把这些事情细细咂摸。

这事儿的实利一面，也就是进账一面，倒没有立刻赶来鞭挞他。有一段不短的工夫，他单在自尊上受罪。在他看来，眼前晃过去的这些忙人个个认得他，都在斜眼瞟他、放声讥笑；嚼口香糖的冷笑着嚼，吃雪茄的带着一层遮掩不住的不以为然与鄙夷吃。后来，时间的流逝磨钝了这份敏感，他发现有别的、更压秤的事情要盘算了。

要说他在突然抛下伦敦的肉锅时还有什么成套的行动方针，那便是：一上岸就尽快去老东家的报馆报到，讨还旧职。他对未来计划的细枝末节想得如此之少，以至于压根儿不曾想到，除了走进去、挨个儿拍拍大伙儿的背、宣布自己已经预备好上班之外，还有什么别的事可做。上班！——在一家看来把讥刺他的荒唐行径当正经消遣的报馆里上班！就算他有那份道德勇气——或者脸皮——去递这份申请，又能讨着什么好？在一个他曾经是个体面公民的世界里，他已经成了一句口头禅。哪家报馆肯把采访任务交给皮卡迪利·吉姆？哪家报馆肯哪怕按行论价收皮卡迪利·吉姆的稿？一阵寒森森的沮丧悄悄爬上他。他仿佛听见管家贝利斯那副郑重的嗓音，在他耳边说起话来，就跟不多时之前，他在帕丁顿车站里说的一样。

===Original===
“Is it not a little rash, Mr. James?”
