Rash was the word. Here he stood, in a country that had no possible use for him, a country where competition was keen and jobs for the unskilled infrequent. What on earth was there that he could do?

Well, he could go home.﻿ ﻿… No, he couldn’t. His pride revolted at that solution. Prodigal Son stuff was all very well in its way, but it lost its impressiveness if you turned up again at home two weeks after you had left. A decent interval among the husks and swine was essential. Besides, there was his father to consider. He might be a poor specimen of a fellow, as witness the *Sunday Chronicle* passim, but he was not so poor as to come slinking back to upset things for his father just when he had done the only decent thing by removing himself. No, that was out of the question.

===Chinese===
「是不是有点儿太莽撞了，詹姆斯先生？」

莽撞，正是这个词。他站在这儿，站在一个全然用他不着的国家里，一个竞争火辣、没手艺的人差事寥寥的国家里。他到底还有什么是能干的呢？

嗯，他可以回家去。……不，不能。他的自尊心对这条出路深恶痛绝。浪子回头的戏码固然有它的妙处，可你离家才两个星期就重新在家里露面，这戏码便失了气派。在豆荚壳和猪群里先混上像样的一段时日，乃是必不可少的。再说，还得替他父亲想想。他这个人也许是个不怎么样的货色，《星期日纪事报》随处可证，可还不至于不怎么样到这个地步：自己刚刚办完平生唯一一件体面事——把自己从老父身边挪开——又溜回去给父亲添乱。不行，此路想都不必想。

===Original===
What remained? The air of New York is bracing and healthy, but a man cannot live on it. Obviously he must find a job. But what job?

What could he do?

A gnawing sensation in the region of the waistcoat answered the question. The solution﻿—which it put forward was, it was true, but a temporary one, yet it appealed strongly to Jimmy. He had found it admirable at many crises. He would go and lunch, and it might be that food would bring inspiration.

===Chinese===
还剩下什么？纽约的空气固然爽利养生，人却不能拿它当饭吃。显而易见，他得找份差事。可是什么差事呢？

他能干什么呢？

马甲一带一阵啃噬似的感觉，替他把问题答了。它提出的解决方案，虽说不过是个权宜之计，对吉米却有无穷的吸引力。多少回危机关头，他都发觉它妙不可言。他要去吃午饭了，说不定饭食会把灵感带来。

===Original===
He moved from his doorway and crossed to the entrance of the subway. He caught a timely express, and a few minutes later emerged into the sunlight again at Grand Central. He made his way westward along Forty-second Street to the hotel which he thought would meet his needs. He had scarcely entered it when in a chair by the door he perceived Ann Chester, and at the sight of her all his depression vanished and he was himself again.

“Why, how do you do, Mr. Bayliss? Are you lunching here?”

“Unless there is some other place that you would prefer,” said Jimmy. “I hope I haven’t kept you waiting.”

===Chinese===
他从门洞里挪出来，穿过马路，走到地铁入口。他赶上一班不早不晚的快车，几分钟后，又在大中央车站（Grand Central）重新冒进了阳光里。他沿着第四十二街一路向西，奔那家他认定能满足自己需要的旅馆去。他前脚刚进门，就在门边一张椅子里望见了安·切斯特；一望见她，他那一腔颓唐登时烟消云散，他又还原成他自己了。

「哎呀，你好哇，贝利斯先生！你在这儿用午饭吗？」

「除非你另有更中意的地方，」吉米说。「但愿没叫你久等。」

===Original===
Ann laughed. She was looking very delightful in something soft and green.

“I’m not going to lunch with you. I’m waiting for Mr. Ralstone and his sister. Do you remember him? He crossed over with us. His chair was next to mine on the promenade deck.”

Jimmy was shocked. When he thought how narrowly she had escaped, poor girl, from lunching with that insufferable pill Teddy﻿—or was it Edgar?﻿—he felt quite weak. Recovering himself, he spoke firmly.

===Chinese===
安笑了。她穿着一身轻软的绿衣裳，模样说不出的讨人喜欢。

「我可不是要跟你吃午饭。我在等拉尔斯通先生（Mr. Ralstone）和他的妹妹。你还记得他吗？他跟咱们同船过来的。在散步甲板上，他的躺椅就在我旁边。」

吉米大吃一惊。一想到她险些儿——可怜的姑娘——就要同那个叫人无法忍受的药丸泰迪共进午餐——要不就是埃德加来着？——他就觉得浑身发软。他定住心神，语气坚决地说。

===Original===
“When were they to have met you?”

“At one o’clock.”

“It is now five past. You are certainly not going to wait any longer. Come with me, and we will whistle for cabs.”

“Don’t be absurd!”

“Come along. I want to talk to you about my future.”

“I shall certainly do nothing of the kind,” said Ann, rising. She went with him to the door. “Teddy would never forgive me.” She got into the cab. “It’s only because you have appealed to me to help you discuss your future,” she said, as they drove off. “Nothing else would have induced me.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「他们约好几点见你？」

「一点钟。」

「现在已经一点过五分了。你无论如何不能再等下去。跟我来，咱们吹哨叫车。」

「别犯傻了！」

「来吧。我想跟你谈谈我的前程。」

「我当然绝不做这种事，」安说着，站起身来。她还是随他走到门口。「泰迪永远不会饶我的。」她上了车。「只是因为你恳求我帮你合计前程，」车子开动时她说，「旁的什么都休想劝动我。……」

===Original===
“I know,” said Jimmy. “I felt that I could rely on your womanly sympathy. Where shall we go?”

“Where do you want to go? Oh, I forget that you have never been in New York before. By the way, what are your impressions of our glorious country?”

“Most gratifying, if only I could get a job.”

“Tell him to drive to Delmonico’s. It’s just around the corner on Forty-fourth Street.”

“There are some things round the corner, then?”

“That sounds cryptic. What do you mean.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，」吉米说。「我料定能指望上你那份女性特有的同情心。咱们上哪儿去？」

「你想上哪儿？哦，忘了你从前没来过纽约。对了，你对我们这个光荣的国家观感如何呀？」

「极为可喜——只要我找得着差事。」

「叫他开去德尔莫尼科（Delmonico's）。就在第四十四街，一拐弯就到。」

「这么说，拐弯的地方到底还是有几样东西的？」

「这话听着高深。你什么意思。」

===Original===
“You’ve forgotten our conversation that night on the ship. You refused to admit the existence of wonderful things just round the corner. You said some very regrettable things that night. About love, if you remember.”

“You can’t be going to talk about love at one o’clock in the afternoon! Talk about your future.”

“Love is inextricably mixed up with my future.”

“Not with your immediate future. I thought you said that you were trying to get a job. Have you given up the idea of newspaper work, then?”

“Absolutely.”

“Well, I’m rather glad.”

===Chinese===
「你把咱们船上那晚的谈话忘啦。你拒不承认拐弯的地方就有美妙东西。你那晚说了些非常遗憾的话。关于爱情，你要是还记得的话。」

「你可不能下午一点钟谈爱情！谈谈你的前程吧。」

「爱情跟我的前程是难解难分地搅和在一块儿的。」

「跟你的眼前前程可搅不到一块儿。我记得你说过，你正想法子找差事来着。这么说，报馆的事你放弃啦？」

「彻底放弃了。」

「嗯，我倒挺高兴。」

===Original===
The cab drew up at the restaurant door, and the conversation was interrupted. When they were seated at their table and Jimmy had given an order to the waiter of absolutely inexcusable extravagance, Ann returned to the topic.

“Well, now the thing is to find something for you to do.”

Jimmy looked round the restaurant with appreciative eyes. The summer exodus from New York was still several weeks distant, and the place was full of prosperous-looking lunchers, not one of whom appeared to have a care or an unpaid bill in the world. The atmosphere was redolent of substantial bank-balances. Solvency shone from the closely shaven faces of the men and reflected itself in the dresses of the women. Jimmy sighed.

“I suppose so,” he said. “Though for choice I’d like to be one of the Idle Rich. To my mind the ideal profession is strolling into the office and touching the old dad for another thousand.”

===Chinese===
车子在餐馆门口停住，谈话就此中断。两人在桌边落座，吉米向侍者报了一单铺张得全然说不过去的菜，然后安回到了正题上。

「好啦，眼下的事，是给你找点儿事做。」

吉米用鉴赏家的眼光把餐馆巡了一遍。纽约的夏季大撤退离当下还有好几个星期，馆子里坐满了满面兴旺气象的午餐客，瞧哪一位都像世上既无一件心事、又无一笔欠账。空气里蒸腾着殷实银行存款的芬芳。偿付能力从男人们刮得精光的脸上放出光来，又反射到女人们的衣裙上。吉米叹了一口气。

「我看也是，」他说。「不过要由着我挑，我情愿当游手好闲的阔人。依我看，天字第一号的理想职业，是溜溜达达走进办公室，跟老爸爸再支一千块钱。」

===Original===
Ann was severe.

“You revolt me!” she said. “I never heard anything so thoroughly disgraceful. You *need* work!”

“One of these days,” said Jimmy plaintively, “I shall be sitting by the roadside with my dinner-pail, and you will come by in your limousine, and I shall look up at you and say ‘*You* hounded me into this!’ How will you feel then?”

“Very proud of myself.”

“In that case, there is no more to be said. I’d much rather hang about and try to get adopted by a millionaire, but if you insist on my working﻿—Waiter!”

===Chinese===
安板起面孔。

「你叫我恶心！」她说。「我从没听见过这么彻头彻尾下作的话。你就是*需要*工作！」

「等哪一天，」吉米哭丧着脸说，「我会提着饭桶坐在路边，你坐着你的豪华大桥车打我跟前过，我抬头望着你说：『*是你*把我撵到这步田地的！』那时候你心里什么滋味？」

「为自己骄傲得很呐。」

「既然这样，那就没什么可说的了。我巴不得四处闲晃，想法子叫个百万富翁把我收养了；可你既然非要我工作不可——侍者！」

===Original===
“What do you want?” asked Ann.

“Will you get me a Classified Telephone Directory,” said Jimmy.

“What for?” asked Ann.

“To look for a profession. There is nothing like being methodical.”

The waiter returned, bearing a red book. Jimmy thanked him and opened it at the A.’s.

===Chinese===
「你要什么？」安问。

「劳驾给拿一本分类电话簿，」吉米说。

「干什么使？」安问。

「找职业使。干什么都讲究个按部就班。」

侍者捧着一本红书回来了。吉米道过谢，翻到 A 字部。

===Original===
“The boy, what will he become?” he said. He turned the pages. “How about an Auditor? What do you think of that?”

“Do you think you could audit?”

“That I could not say till I had tried. I might turn out to be very good at it. How about an Adjuster?”

===Chinese===
「这孩子，将来会成为什么人物？」他说。他哗哗地翻着页。「当个审计员怎么样？你觉得如何？」

「你觉得你会审计吗？」

「这个，不试过说不上。说不定我干这个还挺有一手。当个调整师怎么样？」

===Original===
“An adjuster of what?”

“The book doesn’t say. It just remarks broadly﻿—in a sort of spacious way﻿—‘Adjuster.’ I take it that, having decided to become an adjuster, you then sit down and decide what you wish to adjust. One might, for example, become an Asparagus Adjuster.”

“A what?”

“Surely you know? Asparagus Adjusters are the fellows who sell those rope-and-pulley affairs by means of which the Smart Set lower asparagus into their mouths﻿—or rather Francis the footman does it for them, of course. The diner leans back in his chair, and the menial works the apparatus in the background. It is entirely superseding the old-fashioned method of picking the vegetable up and taking a snap at it. But I suspect that to be a successful Asparagus Adjuster requires capital. We now come to Awning Crank and Spring Rollers. I don’t think I should like that. Rolling awning cranks seems to me a sorry way of spending life’s springtime. Let’s try the B.’s.”

===Chinese===
「调整什么的师？」

「书上没说。它只是大而化之地——以一种海阔天空的口气——印着『调整师』三个字。我琢磨着，等你拿定主意当了调整师，再坐下来慢慢决定你想调整什么。比方说，可以当一名芦笋调整师。」

「一名什么？」

「这你还能不知道？芦笋调整师，就是卖那种绳索滑轮装置的家伙们——上流社会靠这套家伙把芦笋卸进嘴里——不，说得更准确些，当然是由男仆弗朗西斯替他们操作。进餐的往椅背上一仰，底下人在后头摇动器械。这套办法正在全面淘汰那种把菜拿起来啃一口的老式作风。不过我疑心，要当一名成功的芦笋调整师，得有资本。现在咱们翻到『遮阳篷摇柄与弹簧卷筒』了。这个我看不合适。在我看来，把一生之春花用来卷遮阳篷摇柄，未免可怜。咱们试试 B 字部吧。」

===Original===
“Let’s try this omelette. It looks delicious.” Jimmy shook his head.

“I will toy with it﻿—but absently and in a distrait manner, as becomes a man of affairs. There’s nothing in the B.’s. I might devote my ardent youth to Bar-Room Glassware and Bottlers’ Supplies. On the other hand, I might not. Similarly, while there is no doubt a bright future for somebody in Celluloid, Fiberloid, and Other Factitious Goods, instinct tells me that there is none for﻿—” he pulled up on the verge of saying, “James Braithwaite Crocker,” and shuddered at the nearness of the pitfall. “﻿—for﻿—” he hesitated again﻿—“for Algernon Bayliss,” he concluded.

===Chinese===
「咱们尝尝这份煎蛋卷吧。瞧着真好吃。」吉米摇了摇头。

「我要拿它消遣消遣——不过要心不在焉、神思恍惚地消遣，方合一位事务家的气派。B 字部没什么指望。我大可把如火如荼的青春献给『酒吧玻璃器皿与装瓶商杂料』。话又说回来，也可以不献。同理，『赛璐珞、菲布洛伊德及其他人造货色』这一行，无疑会给什么人带来锦绣前程，本能却告诉我，这前程落不到——」他在就要脱口说出「詹姆斯·布雷斯韦特·克罗克」（James Braithwaite Crocker）的边沿猛地收缰，为那陷坑离得如此之近打了个寒噤。「落不到——」他又踌躇起来——「落不到阿尔杰农·贝利斯（Algernon Bayliss）头上，」他这样收了尾。

===Original===
Ann smiled delightedly. It was so typical that his father should have called him something like that. Time had not dimmed her regard for the old man she had seen for that brief moment at Paddington Station. He was an old dear, and she thoroughly approved of this latest manifestation of his supposed pride in his offspring.

“Is that really your name﻿—Algernon?”

“I cannot deny it.”

“I think your father is a darling,” said Ann inconsequently.

===Chinese===
安喜笑颜开。他父亲竟给他起了这么个名字，实在太有这位老先生的作风了。她在帕丁顿车站惊鸿一瞥所生的对那位老人的敬意，岁月并不曾冲淡。他是位可爱的老先生，而这回他那份——据信是——为子自豪的最新流露，她十二分地赞成。

「你当真叫——阿尔杰农？」

「我没法抵赖。」

「我看你父亲真是个可人儿，」安牛头不对马嘴地说。

===Original===
Jimmy had buried himself in the directory again.

“The D.’s,” he said. “Is it possible that posterity will know me as Bayliss the Dermatologist? Or as Bayliss the Drop Forger? I don’t quite like that last one. It may be a respectable occupation, but it sounds rather criminal to me. The sentence for forging drops is probably about twenty years with hard labour.”

“I wish you would put that book away and go on with your lunch,” said Ann.

“Perhaps,” said Jimmy, “my grandchildren will cluster round my knee some day and say in their piping, childish voices, ‘Tell us how you became the Elastic Stocking King, grandpa!’ What do you think?”

===Chinese===
吉米又把自己埋进了电话簿。

「D 字部，」他说。「后世有没有可能管我叫皮肤科医生贝利斯？或者滴剂锻造工贝利斯？后一个我不大喜欢。这兴许是门体面营生，可落在我耳朵里相当像刑事。伪造滴剂，量刑大约得二十年苦役。」

「我但愿你把那本书收起来，好好吃你的午饭，」安说。

「说不定，」吉米说，「有朝一日我的孙儿孙女们会簇拥在我膝下，用他们尖细的童音说：『爷爷，讲讲您是怎么当上弹力袜大王的吧！』你觉得如何？」

===Original===
“I think you ought to be ashamed of yourself. You are wasting your time, when you ought to be either talking to me or else thinking very seriously about what you mean to do.”

Jimmy was turning the pages rapidly.

“I will be with you in a moment,” he said. “Try to amuse yourself somehow till I am at leisure. Ask yourself a riddle. Tell yourself an anecdote. Think of life. No, it’s no good. I don’t see myself as a Fan Importer, a Glass Beveller, a Hotel Broker, an Insect Exterminator, a Junk Dealer, a Kalsomine Manufacturer, a Laundryman, a Mausoleum Architect, a Nurse, an Oculist, a Paperhanger, a Quilt Designer, a Roofer, a Ship Plumber, a Tinsmith, an Undertaker, a Veterinarian, a Wig Maker, an X-ray apparatus manufacturer, a Yeast producer, or a Zinc Spelter.” He closed the book. “There is only one thing to do. I must starve in the gutter. Tell me﻿—you know New York better than I do﻿—where is there a good gutter?”

===Chinese===
「我觉得你不害臊才怪。你这是白白糟蹋光阴，这工夫你本该要么陪我说话，要么正儿八经好好琢磨琢磨你到底打算干什么。」

吉米飞快地翻动书页。

「我一会儿就来陪你，」他说。「在我得空之前，你想法子先消遣消遣。给自己出个谜语。给自己讲段逸事。想想人生。不行，白搭。我瞧不出自己能当扇子进口商、玻璃磨边工、旅馆经纪人、除虫师、废品商、墙粉制造商、洗衣工、陵墓建筑师、护士、眼科大夫、裱糊工、绗缝设计师、盖屋顶工、船舶管子工、白铁匠、承办丧葬工、兽医、假发匠、X 光器械制造商、酵母制造商，或者锌冶炼工。」他把书合上。「只有一条路好走了。我只好在阴沟里挨饿。告诉我——纽约你比我熟——哪儿有一条好阴沟？」

===Original===
At this moment there entered the restaurant an Immaculate Person. He was a young man attired in faultlessly fitting clothes, with shoes of flawless polish and a perfectly proportioned floweret in his buttonhole. He surveyed the room through a monocle. He was a pleasure to look upon, but Jimmy, catching sight of him, started violently and felt no joy at all; for he had recognised him. It was a man he knew well and who knew him well﻿—a man whom he had last seen a bare two weeks ago at the Bachelors’ Club in London. Few things are certain in this world, but one was that, if Bartling﻿—such was the Vision’s name﻿—should see him, he would come over and address him as Crocker. He braced himself to the task of being Bayliss, the whole Bayliss, and nothing but Bayliss. It might be that stout denial would carry him through. After all, Reggie Bartling was a man of notoriously feeble intellect, who could believe in anything.

The monocle continued its sweep. It rested on Jimmy’s profile.

“By Gad!” said the Vision.

===Chinese===
就在此刻，一位一尘不染的人物走进了餐馆。那是个青年男子，穿一身妥帖得无可挑剔的衣服，皮鞋亮得无瑕可指，钮孔里簪着一朵比例臻于完美的小花。他透过一片单片眼镜纵览全厅。他这人观之可喜，可吉米一瞥见他，猛吃一惊，一点儿喜悦也无；因为他认出他来了。那是个他熟识、也熟识他的人——一个他不过两个星期前才在伦敦单身汉俱乐部（Bachelors' Club）见过的人。世上确定的事没几桩，可有一桩是确定的：倘若雷吉·巴特林（Reggie Bartling）——那位幻象就叫这个名字——瞧见了他，必会走将过来，管他叫克罗克。他鼓足了劲，预备一口咬定：本人贝利斯，整个儿的贝利斯，除贝利斯外别无分号。也许硬着头皮死不认账，就能闯过这一关。毕竟，雷吉·巴特林是出了名的脑力孱弱之士，什么都信得过。

单片眼镜继续巡扫。它停在了吉米的侧影上。

「老天爷！」幻象说。

===Original===
Reginald Bartling had landed in New York that morning, and already the loneliness of a strange city had begun to oppress him. He had come over on a visit of pleasure, his suitcase stuffed with letters of introduction, but these he had not yet used. There was a feeling of homesickness upon him, and he ached for a pal. And there before him sat Jimmy Crocker, one of the best. He hastened to the table.

“I say, Crocker, old chap, I didn’t know you were over here. When did you arrive?”

Jimmy was profoundly thankful that he had seen this pest in time to be prepared for him. Suddenly assailed in this fashion, he would undoubtedly have incriminated himself by recognition of his name. But, having anticipated the visitation, he was able to say a whole sentence to Ann before showing himself aware that it was he who was addressed.

===Chinese===
雷金纳德·巴特林（Reginald Bartling）那天早晨刚在纽约登陆，陌生城市的孤寂已经开始压迫他了。他此来是一场寻欢作乐之旅，手提箱里塞满了介绍信，只是一封还没用出去。一阵思乡病罩住了他，他渴望有个伴儿。而就在那儿，他眼前坐着吉米·克罗克，数一数二的好伙伴。他火速奔向那张餐桌。

「我说，克罗克，老伙计，不知道你过这边来了。你什么时候到的？」

吉米深深庆幸，亏得他及时瞧见了这位瘟神，来得及严阵以待。若是像这样冷不丁遭了伏击，他必定一听见报名便自投罗网。可正因为预见到这场降临，他得以先对安说完一整句话，然后才显出觉察：原来叫的是他。

===Original===
“I say! Jimmy Crocker!”

Jimmy achieved one of the blankest stares of modern times. He looked at Ann. Then he looked at Bartling again.

“I think there’s some mistake,” he said. “My name is Bayliss.”

===Chinese===
「我说！吉米·克罗克！」

吉米拿出了一记现代史上数得着的茫然直视。他看看安。然后又看看巴特林。

「我看是弄错了，」他说。「我姓贝利斯。」

===Original===
Before his stony eye the immaculate Bartling wilted. It was a perfectly astounding likeness, but it was apparent to him when what he had ever heard and read about doubles came to him. He was confused. He blushed. It was deuced bad form going up to a perfect stranger like this and pretending you knew him. Probably the chappie thought he was some kind of a confidence johnnie or something. It was absolutely rotten! He continued to blush till one could have fancied him scarlet to the ankles. He backed away, apologising in ragged mutters. Jimmy was not insensible to the pathos of his suffering acquaintance’s position; he knew Reggie and his devotion to good form sufficiently well to enable him to appreciate the other’s horror at having spoken to a fellow to whom he had never been introduced; but necessity forbade any other course. However Reggie’s soul might writhe and however sleepless Reggie’s nights might become as a result of this encounter, he was prepared to fight it out on those lines if it took all summer. And, anyway, it was darned good for Reggie to get a jolt like that every once in a while. Kept him bright and lively.

So thinking, he turned to Ann again, while the crimson Bartling tottered off to restore his nerve centres to their normal tone at some other hostelry. He found Ann staring amazedly at him, eyes wide and lips parted.

===Chinese===
在他那岩石般的眼色跟前，一尘不染的巴特林蔫了。这张脸像得叫人咋舌，不过他平生听来看来的关于「分身」的种种此刻都涌了上来，他也就想明白了。他慌了神。他红了脸。径直凑到素不相识的人跟前去冒充相识，这风度糟糕到了极点。那位老兄八成把他当成某种专耍嘴皮子的骗子之流了。简直糟得没法说！他继续红下去，红到叫人疑心他连脚脖子都是猩红的。他步步后退，用断断续续的咕哝一路赔着不是。吉米并非感受不到这位受难朋友处境之凄凉；他对雷吉、对他那份对体面的痴忠知之甚深，完全能够体会对方因为同一位从未经人引荐的人搭了话而生出的骇怖；然而情势所迫，不容他另走一条路。管他雷吉的灵魂如何辗转，管他雷吉为此要熬过多少个无眠的长夜，他都准备照这条方针奋战到底，哪怕战上一个夏天。再说了，雷吉隔三差五挨上这么一顿闷棍，天知道多有好处。保管他神清气爽、活蹦乱跳。

他这样想着，重新转向安；那厢，红透的巴特林踉跄着去了别家馆子，好把他那些神经中枢恢复到正常的调门。他只见安正惊愕万分地瞪着他，眼睛睁得溜圆，嘴唇微微张着。

===Original===
“Odd, that!” he observed with a light carelessness which he admired extremely and of which he would not have believed himself capable. “I suppose I must be somebody’s double. What was the name he said?”

“Jimmy Crocker!” cried Ann.

Jimmy raised his glass, sipped, and put it down.

“Oh yes, I remember. So it was. It’s a curious thing, too, that it sounds familiar. I’ve heard the name before somewhere.”

===Chinese===
「怪事一桩，可不是！」他发表议论，那副轻飘飘的漫不经心，连他自己都叹服极了，也决计想不到自己竟有这份能耐。「想必我总归是哪个人的分身。他方才叫的什么名字来着？」

「吉米·克罗克！」安叫道。

吉米举起杯子，呷了一口，放下。

「哦，对，我想起来了。正是这个名字。而且怪就怪在，这名字听着耳熟。我在什么地方听见过。」

===Original===
“I was talking about Jimmy Crocker on the ship. That evening on deck.”

Jimmy looked at her doubtfully.

“Were you? Oh yes, of course. I’ve got it now. He is the man you dislike so.”

Ann was still looking at him as if he had undergone a change into something new and strange.

“I hope you aren’t going to let the resemblance prejudice you against *me*?” said Jimmy. “Some are born Jimmy Crockers, others have Jimmy Crockers thrust upon them. I hope you’ll bear in mind that I belong to the latter class.”

“It’s such an extraordinary thing.”

===Chinese===
「我在船上说起过吉米·克罗克。就是有天晚上，在甲板上。」

吉米将信将疑地望着她。

「是吗？哦，对，没错。这我对上号了。他就是你深恶痛绝的那个人。」

安还那样望着他，仿佛他经历了一场蜕变，变成了什么又新鲜又陌生的东西。

「我希望你不至于因为这副相像，连*我*也一并嫌弃了吧？」吉米说。「有的人天生是吉米·克罗克，有的人是硬被派作吉米·克罗克。望你牢牢记住，我属于后一类。」

「这事情真是邪门儿。」

===Original===
“Oh, I don’t know. You often hear of doubles. There was a man in England a few years ago who kept getting sent to prison for things some genial stranger who happened to look like him had done.”

“I don’t mean that. Of course there are doubles. But it is curious that you should have come over here and that we should have met like this at just this time. You see, the reason I went over to England at all was to try to get Jimmy Crocker to come back here.”

“What!”

“I don’t mean that *I* did. I mean that I went with my uncle and aunt, who wanted to persuade him to come and live with them.”

Jimmy was now feeling completely out of his depth.

“Your uncle and aunt? Why?”

===Chinese===
「哦，倒也未必。分身的事你不是常听说嘛。前几年英格兰就有那么一位，老是因为某个碰巧同他长得一模一样的和善陌生人闯下的祸事给抓去坐牢。」

「我不是说这个。分身当然有的是。怪的是你偏偏到了这边来，咱们又偏偏赶在这节骨眼上这样碰了头。你要知道，我当初上英国去，就为的是想法子把吉米·克罗克弄回这边来。」

「什么！」

「我不是说*我*去弄。我是说，我是跟着我姨父姨妈一道去的，他们想劝他搬来跟他们同住。」

吉米此刻只觉得自己完全踩不着底了。

「你的姨父姨妈？为什么？」

===Original===
“I ought to have explained that they are his uncle and aunt, too. My aunt’s sister married his father.”

“But﻿—”

“It’s quite simple, though it doesn’t sound so. Perhaps you haven’t read the *Sunday Chronicle* lately? It has been publishing articles about Jimmy Crocker’s disgusting behaviour in London﻿—they call him Piccadilly Jim, you know﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我早该交代明白，他们也是他的姨父姨妈。我姨妈的姐姐嫁给了他父亲。」

「可是——」

「事情其实简单，只是听上去不简单。你近来到《星期日纪事报》没有？它一直连篇累牍地登吉米·克罗克在伦敦的恶心行径——他们管他叫皮卡迪利·吉姆，你是知道的——」

===Original===
In print, that name had shocked Jimmy. Spoken, and by Ann, it was loathly. Remorse for his painful past tore at him.

“There was another one printed yesterday.”

“I saw it,” said Jimmy, to avert description.

===Chinese===
这名字印在纸上，不过叫吉米心惊；由安说出口来，它可憎可厌。对他那辛酸的往事，悔恨一阵阵撕扯着他。

「昨天又登了一篇。」

「我看见了，」吉米说，免得再听一番描述。

===Original===
“Oh, did you? Well, just to show you what sort of a man Jimmy Crocker is, the Lord Percy Whipple whom he attacked in the club was his very best friend. His stepmother told my aunt so. He seems to be absolutely hopeless.” She smiled. “You’re looking quite sad, Mr. Bayliss. Cheer up! You may look like him, but you aren’t him he?﻿—him?﻿—no, ‘he’ is right. The soul is what counts. If you’ve got a good, virtuous, Algernonish soul, it doesn’t matter if you’re so like Jimmy Crocker that his friends come up and talk to you in restaurants. In fact, it’s rather an advantage, really. I’m sure that if you were to go to my aunt and pretend to be Jimmy Crocker, who had come over after all in a fit of repentance, she would be so pleased that there would be nothing she wouldn’t do for you. You might realise your ambition of being adopted by a millionaire. Why don’t you try it? I won’t give you away.”

“Before they found me out and hauled me off to prison, I should have been near you for a time. I should have lived in the same house with you, spoken to you﻿—!” Jimmy’s voice shook.

Ann turned her head to address an imaginary companion.

“You must listen to this, my dear,” she said in an undertone. “He speaks *wonderfully*! They used to call him the Boy Orator in his hometown. Sometimes that, and sometimes Eloquent Algernon!”

Jimmy eyed her fixedly. He disapproved of this frivolity.

===Chinese===
「哦，是吗？正好让你领教领教吉米·克罗克是号什么人：他在俱乐部里揍的那位珀西·惠普尔勋爵，正是他顶顶要好的朋友。这是他继母亲口告诉我姨妈的。他这人看来是彻底没救了。」她微微一笑。「你瞧着怪难过的，贝利斯先生。打起精神来！你尽管长得像他，可你不是他——是『他』么？——唔——不，用『他』就对了。要紧的是灵魂。只要你揣着一颗善良、贞洁、阿尔杰农式的灵魂，哪怕你像吉米·克罗克像得他的朋友都会走进餐馆来找你搭话，也不碍事。说实在的，这没准儿还是桩便宜。我敢担保，你要是上我姨妈那儿去，冒充吉米·克罗克，就说到底还是良心发现、痛改前非过来了，她一欢喜，天下就没有她不肯为你办的事。你那叫百万富翁收养的宏图，说不定就实现了。干吗不去试试？我保管不拆穿你。」

「趁他们还没把我查出来、押去蹲大狱，我总还能挨着你过上一段日子。我本可以跟你同住一座宅子，跟你说话——！」吉米的声音发颤了。

安扭过头去，对一位假想的同伴致辞。

「这一段你可得听听，亲爱的，」她悄声道。「他说起话来*神妙极了*！在他的家乡，人们从前管他叫少年演说家。有时叫这个，有时叫雄辩的阿尔杰农！」

吉米目不转睛地端详她。他不赞成这种轻浮行径。

===Original===
“One of these days you will try me too high﻿—!”

“Oh, you didn’t hear what I was saying to my friend, did you?” she said in concern. “But I meant it, every word. I love to hear you talk. You have such *feeling*!”

Jimmy attuned himself to the key of the conversation.

“Have you no sentiment in you?” he demanded.

“I was just warming up, too! In another minute you would have heard something worthwhile. You’ve damped me now. Let’s talk about my lifework again.”

“Have you thought of anything?”

===Chinese===
「总有一天，你会欺我太甚——！」

「哦，我跟我朋友说的话，你没听见吧？」她一脸关切地说。「不过我句句出自真心。我爱听你说话。你多么*有感情*啊！」

吉米把自己的心弦调到了这场谈话的调门上。

「你身上就没有一丝一毫的柔情么？」他质问。

「人家刚要进入状态呢！再过一分钟，你就能听上些值得一听的东西了。这会儿全叫你泼凉了。咱们还是再谈谈我的终身大业吧。」

「想出什么名堂来没有？」

===Original===
“I’d like to be one of those fellows who sit in offices, and sign checks, and tell the office-boy to tell Mr. Rockerfeller they can give him five minutes. But of course I should need a checkbook, and I haven’t got one. Oh well, I shall find something to do all right. Now tell me something about yourself. Let’s drop the future for awhile.”

---

An hour later Jimmy turned into Broadway. He walked pensively, for he had much to occupy his mind. How strange that the Petts should have come over to England to try to induce him to return to New York, and how galling that, now that he was in New York, this avenue to a prosperous future was closed by the fact that something which he had done five years ago﻿—that he could remember nothing about it was quite maddening﻿—had caused Ann to nurse this abiding hatred of him. He began to dream tenderly of Ann, bumping from pedestrian to pedestrian in a gentle trance.

===Chinese===
「我倒想当那么一号人物：坐在办公室里，签签支票，吩咐办公室小弟去回洛克菲勒先生（Mr. Rockerfeller）的话，说可以赏他五分钟。当然喽，这先得有本支票簿，而我一本也没有。唉，算了，我总归找得着事做的。现在你给我讲讲你自己吧。咱们把前程撂下片刻。」

---

一个钟头之后，吉米拐上了百老汇（Broadway）。他一路踱得沉思默想，因为他脑子里要办的差事实在不少。佩特夫妇竟然特意渡海到英国去劝他回纽约，这事何其古怪；而他如今人已到了纽约，这条通向锦绣前程的坦道却给堵死了——只因为他五年前干下的某一桩事——那桩事他半点儿也记不起来，真是要把人逼疯——害得安对他存了这份常驻不散的仇恨。他开始柔情缱绻地梦想起安来，一路恍恍惚惚，从一个行人身上撞到另一个行人身上。

===Original===
From this trance the seventh pedestrian aroused him by uttering his name, the name which circumstances had compelled him to abandon.

“Jimmy Crocker!”

Surprise brought Jimmy back from his dreams to the hard world﻿—surprise and a certain exasperation. It was ridiculous to be incognito in a city which he had not visited in five years and to be instantly recognised in this way by every second man he met. He looked sourly at the man. The other was a sturdy, square-shouldered, battered young man, who wore on his homely face a grin of recognition and regard. Jimmy was not particularly good at remembering faces, but this person’s was of a kind which the poorest memory might have recalled. It was, as the advertisements say, distinctively individual. The broken nose, the exiguous forehead, and the enlarged ears all clamoured for recognition. The last time Jimmy had seen Jerry Mitchell had been two years before at the National Sporting Club in London, and, placing him at once, he braced himself, as a short while ago he had braced himself to confound immaculate Reggie.

===Chinese===
第七个行人喊出了那个名字——那个遭时势逼迫被他抛弃了的名字——把他从恍惚里惊了起来。

「吉米·克罗克！」

这一声惊讶把吉米从梦里拽回了坚硬的人世——惊讶，外加几分窝火。到一座五年没来的城市里隐姓埋名，碰上的却是逢二隔三便有人当场把他认出来，这也未免可笑。他没好气地瞧那人。对方是个敦实、宽肩、满脸伤痕的青年，朴实的脸上挂着又认得又亲近的笑。吉米记人相的本事平平，可这一位的长相，只怕记性再不济也忘不掉。用广告上的话说，这脸个性鲜明。断过的鼻子、局促的脑门、放大了的耳朵，全都在嚷着要人相认。吉米上一回见到杰里·米切尔，是两年前在伦敦的国家体育俱乐部（National Sporting Club）；他一眼把他放定了位，随即抖擞起精神，好比不多时之前抖擞精神去挫败那位一尘不染的雷吉一样。

===Original===
“Hello!” said the battered one.

“Hello indeed!” said Jimmy courteously. “In what way can I brighten your life?”

The grin faded from the other’s face. He looked puzzled.

“You’re Jimmy Crocker, ain’t you?”

“No. My name chances to be Algernon Bayliss.”

===Chinese===
「你好啊！」伤痕累累的那位说。

「你也好啊！」吉米彬彬有礼地说。「我能用什么法子照亮您的人生吗？」

笑容从对方脸上褪了下去。他露出一脸困惑。

「你是吉米·克罗克，不是么？」

「不是。敝人碰巧叫阿尔杰农·贝利斯。」

===Original===
Jerry Mitchell reddened.

“ ’Scuse me. My mistake.”

He was moving off, but Jimmy stopped him. Parting from Ann had left a large gap in his life, and he craved human society.

“I know you now,” he said. “You’re Jerry Mitchell. I saw you fight Kid Burke four years ago in London.”

The grin returned to the pugilist’s face, wider than ever. He beamed with gratification.

===Chinese===
杰里·米切尔涨红了脸。

「对不住。认错人了。」

他正要挪步走开，吉米却把他叫住了。同安一别，在他生活里留下老大一块空档，他渴望有人做伴。

「现在我认出你了，」他说。「你是杰里·米切尔。四年前我在伦敦看过你跟基德·伯克（Kid Burke）打的那一场。」

笑容回到拳击手的脸上，比先前更宽了。他喜不自胜地满面生辉。

===Original===
“Gee! Think of that! I’ve quit since then. I’m working for an old guy named Pett. Funny thing, he’s Jimmy Crocker’s uncle that I mistook you for. Say, you’re a dead ringer for that guy! I could have sworn it was him when you bumped into me. Say, are you doing anything?”

“Nothing in particular.”

“Come and have a yarn. There’s a place I know just round by here.”

“Delighted.”

They made their way to the place.

===Chinese===
「嚯，你瞧瞧这事！打那以后我就不干了。如今我给一个姓佩特的老头儿做事。巧的是，我错把你当成的那位吉米·克罗克，正好就是他的外甥。我说，你跟那位简直是一个模子磕出来的！你撞上我那会儿，我都敢赌咒他就是本人。哎，你这会儿有事儿没？」

「没什么正经事。」

「来一块儿聊聊。这附近拐个弯就有个我熟识的地方。」

「乐意奉陪。」

两人便朝那地方去了。

===Original===
“What’s yours?” said Jerry Mitchell. “I’m on the wagon myself,” he said apologetically.

“So am I,” said Jimmy. “It’s the only way. No sense in always drinking and making a disgraceful exhibition of yourself in public!”

Jerry Mitchell received this homily in silence. It disposed definitely of the lurking doubt in his mind as to the possibility of this man really being Jimmy Crocker. Though outwardly convinced by the other’s denial, he had not been able to rid himself till now of a nebulous suspicion. But this convinced him. Jimmy Crocker would never have said a thing like that nor would have refused the offer of alcohol. He fell into pleasant conversation with him. His mind eased.

===Chinese===
「你喝点儿什么？」杰里·米切尔说。「我自己戒了酒，」他带着歉意补了一句。

「我也戒了，」吉米说。「就得这样。老是灌黄汤，当众出乖露丑，成何体统！」

杰里·米切尔默默领受了这篇布道。它把他心里蛰伏的那点疑影——这人兴许真是吉米·克罗克——干脆利落地荡涤干净了。表面上，他早叫对方的否认给说服了，可直到此刻，他才算真正摆脱了那团若有若无的疑云。是这番话叫他信了。吉米·克罗克决计说不出这种话，也决计不会把人家敬的酒推开。他同他愉快地攀谈起来。他的心松快了。

## IX / 九

Mrs. Pett Is Shocked / 佩特太太大惊失色

===Original===
At five o’clock in the afternoon some ten days after her return to America, Mrs. Pett was at home to her friends in the house on Riverside Drive. The proceedings were on a scale that amounted to a reception, for they were not only a sort of official notification to New York that one of its most prominent hostesses was once more in its midst, but were also designed to entertain and impress Mr. Hammond Chester, Ann’s father, who had been spending a couple of days in the metropolis preparatory to departing for South America on one of his frequent trips. He was very fond of Ann in his curious, detached way, though he never ceased in his private heart to consider it injudicious of her not to have been born a boy, and he always took in New York for a day or two on his way from one wild and lonely spot to another, if he could manage it.
The large drawing-room overlooking the Hudson was filled almost to capacity with that strange mixture of humanity which Mrs. Pett chiefly affected. She prided herself on the Bohemian element in her parties, and had become during the past two years a human dragnet, scooping Genius from its hiding-place and bringing it into the open. At different spots in the room stood the six resident geniuses to whose presence in the home Mr. Pett had such strong objections, and in addition to these she had collected so many more of a like breed from the environs of Washington Square that the air was clamorous with the hoarse cries of futurist painters, esoteric Buddhists, *vers libre* poets, interior decorators, and stage reformers, sifted in among the more conventional members of society who had come to listen to them. Men with new religions drank tea with women with new hats. Apostles of Free Love expounded their doctrines to persons who had been practising them for years without realising it. All over the room throats were being strained and minds broadened.
Mr. Chester, standing near the door with Ann, eyed the assemblage with the genial contempt of a large dog for a voluble pack of small ones. He was a massive, weather-beaten man, who looked very like Ann in some ways and would have looked more like her but for the misfortune of having had some of his face clawed away by an irritable jaguar with whom he had had a difference some years back in the jungles of Peru.

===Chinese===
回到美国大约十来天之后的一个下午五点钟，佩特太太在里弗赛德大道的宅邸里会客。这场面的规格之高，简直抵得上一场招待会，因为它不仅是对纽约的一种正式通告——告诉纽约，它最显赫的女主人之一又回到了它的怀抱——同时也是为了款待并镇一镇哈蒙德·切斯特先生，即安的父亲。他已在纽约盘桓两三天，预备踏上他屡试不爽的旅程之一，前往南美洲。他以他那古怪而超然的方式深深地疼爱着安，尽管在他内心深处，从未停止抱怨她投胎时不够审慎，没选个男孩当当；而且每逢从一个荒僻的地方动身赶往另一个荒僻的地方，只要办得到，他总要在纽约落脚一两天。
俯瞰哈得孙河的大客厅几乎被塞到了饱和点，塞满了佩特太太素来最爱罗致的那种成分奇特的人类混合体。她对自己聚会中的波希米亚成分颇感自豪，过去两年来俨然成了一张人肉拖网，专把天才从藏身的旮旯里兜捞出来，拖到大庭广众之下。屋里各处站着六位驻府天才——正是佩特先生对其盘踞家中抱有强烈异议的那几位——除此之外，她还在华盛顿广场（Washington Square）一带罗致了大批同类，以至于空气中喧响着未来主义画家、密宗佛教徒、*自由体*（vers libre）诗人、室内装潢师和戏剧改革家的粗哑啼鸣，他们被筛混进前来聆听教诲的较为循规蹈矩的社会人士中间。怀抱新宗教的男士，与头戴新帽子的女士，同桌饮茶。自由恋爱的使徒们向一些人宣讲教义，而这些人多年来一直在身体力行，自己却毫无察觉。满屋子，嗓子在被扯紧，脑筋在被拓宽。
切斯特先生同安站在门边，用一条大狗望着一群聒噪的小狗时的那种和蔼的轻蔑，打量着这批与会的人群。他是个膀阔腰圆、饱经风霜的汉子，某些地方长得极像安，本来还能更像的，只可惜多年前在秘鲁的丛林里，同一只脾气暴躁的美洲豹有过一场龃龉，被它抓掉了小半张脸。

===Original===
“Do you like this sort of thing?” he asked.
“I don’t mind it,” said Ann.
“Well, I shall be very sorry to leave you, Ann, but I’m glad I’m pulling out of here this evening. Who are all these people?”
Ann surveyed the gathering.
“That’s Ernest Wisden, the playwright, over there, talking to Lora Delane Porter, the feminist writer. That’s Clara What’s-her-name, the sculptor, with the bobbed hair. Next to her﻿—”
Mr. Chester cut short the catalogue with a stifled yawn.

===Chinese===
「你喜欢这种场合吗？」他问。
「我不在乎，」安说。
「呃，离开你我很难过，安，不过今晚就能撤出这儿，我倒是高兴的。这些人都是谁？」
安把这群人扫视了一遍。
「那边是剧作家埃内斯特·威斯登（Ernest Wisden），正在跟女权主义作家洛拉·德莱恩·波特（Lora Delane Porter）说话。那位剪短发的，是雕塑家克拉拉·什么来着（Clara What's-her-name）。她旁边——」
切斯特先生忍住一个哈欠，打断了这份花名册。

===Original===
“Where’s old Pete? Doesn’t he come to these jamborees?”
Ann laughed.
“Poor uncle Peter! If he gets back from the office before these people leave, he will sneak up to his room and stay there till it’s safe to come out. The last time I made him come to one of these parties he was pounced on by a woman who talked to him for an hour about the morality of Finance and seemed to think that millionaires were the scum of the earth.”
“He never would stand up for himself.” Mr. Chester’s gaze hovered about the room, and paused. “Who’s that fellow? I believe I’ve seen him before somewhere.”

===Chinese===
「老皮特呢？这种盛大的联欢他不来吗？」
安笑了。
「可怜的彼得姨父！要是他从事务所回来时这帮人还没走，他就会溜回自己房间，一直待到出来安全了为止。上回我拉他来参加这种聚会，他被一位太太扑了个正着，人家跟他聊了一个钟头金融界的道德问题，而且似乎认定百万富翁是全天下的渣滓。」
「他就是从来不肯为自己挺身而出。」切斯特先生的目光在屋里转来转去，忽然停住了。「那家伙是谁？我好像从前在哪儿见过。」

===Original===
A constant eddying swirl was animating the multitude. Whenever the mass tended to congeal, something always seemed to stir it up again. This was due to the restless activity of Mrs. Pett, who held it to be the duty of a good hostess to keep her guests moving. From the moment when the room began to fill till the moment when it began to empty she did not cease to plough her way to and fro, in a manner equally reminiscent of a hawk swooping on chickens and an earnest collegian bucking the line. Her guests were as a result perpetually forming new ententes and combinations, finding themselves bumped about like those little moving figures which one sees in shopwindows on Broadway, which revolve on a metal disc until, urged by impact with another little figure, they scatter to regroup themselves elsewhere. It was a fascinating feature of Mrs. Pett’s at-homes and one which assisted that mental broadening process already alluded to that one never knew, when listening to a discussion on the sincerity of Oscar Wilde, whether it would not suddenly change in the middle of a sentence to an argument on the inner meaning of the Russian Ballet.
Plunging now into a group dominated for the moment by an angular woman who was saying loud and penetrating things about the suffrage, Mrs. Pett had seized and removed a tall, blonde young man with a mild, vacuous face. For the past few minutes this young man had been sitting bolt upright on a chair with his hands on his knees, so exactly in the manner of an end-man at a minstrel show that one would hardly have been surprised had he burst into song or asked a conundrum.
Ann followed her father’s gaze.

===Chinese===
一股川流不息的漩涡正搅动着这批人群。大伙儿每当有凝成一团的趋势，总有什么东西再把它们搅动起来。这得归功于佩特太太那从不消停的劲头，她认为一位称职女主人的天职，就是让客人保持流动。从屋里开始上人到开始撤人，她一刻不停地来回犁开一条路，那副架势，既像老鹰扑小鸡，又像一名认真的大学球员撞开人墙。其结果是，她的客人们不断缔结新的协约与同盟，被撞得七荤八素，好比百老汇（Broadway）橱窗里那些活动小人儿：在金属圆盘上滴溜溜转着，直到被另一个小人儿一撞，才四散奔逃，去别处重新结队。这是佩特太太会客日的一大妙处，也助推了上文说过的那种脑筋开阔化的进程：你永远说不准，正听着一篇关于奥斯卡·王尔德（Oscar Wilde）之真诚的讨论，它会不会在半句话上突然改换成一场关于俄国芭蕾内在意义的论战。
这会儿，佩特太太一头扎进了一小堆人——那堆人此刻正被一位棱角分明的太太统治着，她就妇女参政问题发表着响亮而穿脑的宏论——佩特太太从里头揪出并撤离了一名高个子金发青年，此人一脸温吞的茫然。几分钟以来，这位青年一直直挺挺地坐在椅子上，两手扣在膝盖上，那副做派活脱脱是黑脸游艺团里坐端角插科的演员，以至于他若是突然引吭高歌或者抛出一则谜语，你都不会觉得意外。
安顺着父亲的目光望去。

===Original===
“Do you mean the man talking to aunt Nesta? There, they’ve gone over to speak to Willie Partridge. Do you mean that one?”
“Yes. Who is he?”
“Well, I like that!” said Ann. “Considering that you introduced him to us! That’s Lord Wisbeach, who came to uncle Peter with a letter of introduction from you. You met him in Canada.”
“I remember now. I ran across him in British Columbia. We camped together one night. I’d never seen him before and I didn’t see him again. He said he wanted a letter to old Pete for some reason, so I scribbled him one in pencil on the back of an envelope. I’ve never met anyone who played a better game of draw poker. He cleaned me out. There’s a lot in that fellow, in spite of his looking like a musical comedy dude. He’s clever.”

===Chinese===
「你是说在跟内斯塔姨妈说话的那个人？瞧，他们走过去跟威利·帕特里奇说话了。你是指他吗？」
「是。他是谁？」
「哟，这话说的！」安说。「想想看，还是您把他介绍给我们的呢！那是威斯比奇勋爵，拿着您写的介绍信来找彼得姨父的。您是在加拿大认识他的。」
「我现在想起来了。我是在不列颠哥伦比亚（British Columbia）碰上他的。我们一起宿了一夜营。之前我从没见过他，之后也没再见过。他说因为某种缘故，想要封给老皮特的介绍信，我就用铅笔在一个信封背面给他涂了一封。打五张抽牌的扑克，我从没见过比他更高明的手。他把我赢了个精光。那家伙肚子里有货，别看他一副歌舞喜剧花花公子的做派。他聪明。」

===Original===
Ann looked at him meditatively.
“It’s odd that you should be discovering hidden virtues in Lord Wisbeach, father. I’ve been trying to make up my mind about him. He wants me to marry him.”
“He does! I suppose a good many of these young fellows here want the same thing, don’t they, Ann?” Mr. Chester looked at his daughter with interest. Her growing-up and becoming a beauty had always been a perplexity to him. He could never rid himself of the impression of her as a long-legged child in short skirts. “I suppose you’re refusing them all the time?”
“Every day from ten to four, with an hour off for lunch. I keep regular office hours. Admission on presentation of visiting card.”
“And how do you feel about this Lord Wisbeach?”

===Chinese===
安若有所思地望着他。
「真怪，父亲，居然是您来发掘威斯比奇勋爵深藏的美德。我一直想对他拿定主意。他要我嫁给他。」
「他要娶你！我猜这儿的好些小伙子都打着同样的主意吧，安，是不是？」切斯特先生饶有兴味地看着女儿。她的长大、她的出落成美人，向来是他一桩想不通的事。他怎么也甩不掉这样的印象：她还是个穿短裙的长腿小姑娘。「我猜你一直在回绝他们？」
「每天十点到四点，午间休息一个钟头。我按正规办公时间办公。凭名片入场。」
「那你觉得这位威斯比奇勋爵怎么样？」

===Original===
“I don’t know,” said Ann frankly. “He’s very nice. And﻿—what is more important﻿—he’s different. Most of the men I know are all turned out of the same mould. Lord Wisbeach﻿—and one other man﻿—are the only two I’ve met who might not be the brothers of all the rest.”
“Who’s the other?”
“A man I hardly know. I met him on board ship.”
Mr. Chester looked at his watch.
“It’s up to you, Ann,” he said. “There’s one comfort in being your father﻿—I don’t mean that exactly; I mean that it *is* a comfort to me *as* your father﻿—to know that I need feel no paternal anxiety about you. I don’t have to give you advice. You’ve not only got three times the sense that I have, but you’re not the sort of girl who would take advice. You’ve always known just what you wanted ever since you were a kid.﻿ ﻿… Well, if you’re going to take me down to the boat, we’d better be starting. Where’s the car?”

===Chinese===
「我不知道，」安坦率地说。「他人很好。而且——更要紧的是——他与众不同。我认识的男人，多半都是一个模子里倒出来的。威斯比奇勋爵——还有另一个男人——是我见过的仅有的两个，兴许不是其余那群人的兄弟。」
「另一个是谁？」
「一个我几乎不了解的男人。我在船上遇见的他。」
切斯特先生看了看表。
「这事全在你，安，」他说。「做你的父亲，有桩安慰——我不是那个意思；我是说，*作为*你的父亲，这*确实*是我的安慰——那就是：我知道自己大可不必为你操那份为人父的心。我用不着给你什么忠告。你的见识不仅三倍于我，而且你也不是那种肯听忠告的姑娘。打从是个毛孩子起，你就一向清楚自己要什么。……好了，你要是打算送我上船，咱们最好这就动身。车在哪儿？」

===Original===
“Waiting outside. Aren’t you going to say goodbye to aunt Nesta?”
“Good God, no!” exclaimed Mr. Chester in honest concern. “What! Plunge into that pack of coyotes and fight my way through to her! I’d be torn to pieces by wild poets. Besides, it seems silly to make a fuss saying goodbye when I’m only going to be away a short time. I shan’t go any further than Colombia this trip.”
“You’ll be able to run back for weekends,” said Ann.
She paused at the door to cast a fleeting glance over her shoulder at the fair-haired Lord Wisbeach, who was now in animated conversation with her aunt and Willie Partridge; then she followed her father down the stairs. She was a little thoughtful as she took her place at the wheel of her automobile. It was not often that her independent nature craved outside support, but she was half conscious of wishing at the present juncture that she possessed a somewhat less casual father. She would have liked to ask him to help her decide a problem which had been vexing her for nearly three weeks now, ever since Lord Wisbeach had asked her to marry him and she had promised to give him his answer on her return from England. She had been back in New York several days now, but she had not been able to make up her mind. This annoyed her, for she was a girl who liked swift decisiveness of thought and action both in others and in herself. She was fond of Mr. Chester in much the same unemotional, detached way that he was fond of her, but she was perfectly well aware of the futility of expecting counsel from him. She said goodbye to him at the boat, fussed over his comfort for awhile in a motherly way, and then drove slowly back. For the first time in her life she was feeling uncertain of herself. When she had left for England, she had practically made up her mind to accept Lord Wisbeach, and had only deferred actual acceptance of him because in her cool way she wished to reexamine the position at her leisure. Second thoughts had brought no revulsion of feeling. She had not wavered until her arrival in New York. Then, for some reason which baffled her, the idea of marrying Lord Wisbeach had become vaguely distasteful. And now she found herself fluctuating between this mood and her former one.

===Chinese===
「就在外头等着。您不去跟内斯塔姨妈道个别吗？」
「老天爷，不去！」切斯特先生真心实意地骇然嚷道。「什么！一头扎进那群郊狼里，一路厮杀着杀到她跟前！我会叫那群野诗人撕成碎片的。再说，我只不过离开一小阵子，郑重其事地道别未免可笑。这趟我最多到哥伦比亚（Colombia）为止。」
「那您还能赶回来过周末呢，」安说。
她在门口驻了驻，回头朝金发的威斯比奇勋爵投去匆匆一瞥——他此刻正同她姨妈和威利·帕特里奇谈得眉飞色舞——随后便跟着父亲下楼去了。她在自己汽车的驾驶座上坐定时，略略出了会儿神。她那独立的天性难得渴求外援，可在此刻，她也半自觉地希望父亲别这么大大咧咧。她本想请他帮着拿捏一个近三周来一直折磨着她的问题——自从威斯比奇勋爵向她求婚、她答应从英国回来后给他答复的时候起。如今回纽约已经好几天了，她却仍旧拿不定主意。这让她恼火，因为她是个无论对别人还是对自己，都崇尚思路和行动雷厉风行的姑娘。她以同他疼爱她差不多的那种不动感情、超然物外的方式喜欢着切斯特先生，但也完全明白，指望他出主意是徒劳的。她在码头上跟他道了别，以一副慈母的架势对他的旅途舒适啰嗦了一阵，然后缓缓开车回去。有生以来头一遭，她对自己失去了把握。动身去英国的时候，她其实已经打定主意接受威斯比奇勋爵，之所以没有当场应允，不过是想以她冷静的做派，从容地重新审度一番局势。再三思量并没有带来感情的翻悔。直到抵达纽约之前，她都没有动摇过。然后，出于某种令她自己莫名其妙的缘故，嫁给威斯比奇勋爵的念头变得隐隐地倒起胃口来。而此刻，她发觉自己就在这种心境和先前那种心境之间摇摆不定。

===Original===
She reached the house on Riverside Drive, but did not slacken the speed of the machine. She knew that Lord Wisbeach would be waiting for her there, and she did not wish to meet him just yet. She wanted to be alone. She was feeling depressed. She wondered if this was because she had just departed from her father, and decided that it was. His swift entrances into and exits from her life always left her temporarily restless. She drove on up the river. She meant to decide her problem one way or the other before she returned home.
Lord Wisbeach, meanwhile, was talking to Mrs. Pett and Willie, its inventor, about Partridgite. Willie, on hearing himself addressed, had turned slowly with an air of absent self-importance, the air of a great thinker disturbed in mid-thought. He always looked like that when spoken to, and there were those﻿—Mr. Pett belonged to this school of thought﻿—who held that there was nothing to him beyond that look and that he had built up his reputation as a budding mastermind on a foundation that consisted entirely of a vacant eye, a mop of hair through which he could run his fingers, and the fame of his late father.
Willie Partridge was the son of the great inventor, Dwight Partridge, and it was generally understood that the explosive, Partridgite, was to be the result of a continuation of experiments which his father had been working upon at the time of his death. That Dwight Partridge had been trying experiments in the direction of a new and powerful explosive during the last year of his life was common knowledge in those circles which are interested in such things. Foreign governments were understood to have made tentative overtures to him. But a sudden illness, ending fatally, had finished the budding career of Partridgite abruptly, and the world had thought no more of it until an interview in the *Sunday Chronicle*, that storehouse of information about interesting people, announced that Willie was carrying on his father’s experiments at the point where he had left off. Since then there had been vague rumours of possible sensational developments, which Willie had neither denied nor confirmed. He preserved the mysterious silence which went so well with his appearance.

===Chinese===
她到了里弗赛德大道的宅子，却没有减低车速。她知道威斯比奇勋爵准在那儿等她，而她还不想这就见他。她想独自待着。她正觉得情绪低落。她琢磨着这是不是因为刚同父亲分手，并且断定正是如此。他在她生命里风驰电掣地进进出出，总害她一时心神不宁。她沿着河继续往上开。她打算在回家之前，把这个问题不管怎样先拿定一个主意。
与此同时，威斯比奇勋爵正同佩特太太以及帕特里奇特——该炸药的发明人威利——谈论着它。威利一听有人跟自己搭话，便慢吞吞地转过身来，带着一种心不在焉的自命不凡，一派大思想家思路被打断于中途的气概。每逢有人同他讲话，他总是这副模样，而且也不乏其人——佩特先生便属于这一学派——坚称那副模样之下别无长物，他作为一颗初露锋芒的智多星攒下的名望，地基无非三样：一双空茫的眼睛，一头可以插指梳理的乱发，再加上他先父的声名。
威利·帕特里奇是大发明家德怀特·帕特里奇（Dwight Partridge）之子，而世人普遍的理解是，帕特里奇特这种炸药，将是其父临终前所做实验继续延伸的成果。德怀特·帕特里奇在世的最后一年里，一直在朝一种威力强大的新炸药的方向搞试验，这在关心此类事情的圈子里是尽人皆知的事。据说好几国政府都曾向他作过试探性的接洽。可是一场来得突然、终以致死的大病，干脆利落地了结了帕特里奇特这颗初生的萌芽，世人也就把它淡忘了，直到《星期日纪事报》（*Sunday Chronicle*）——那座名人情报的宝库——登出一篇访谈，宣布威利正从他父亲停下的地方继续那些实验。自那以后，关于可能出现的轰动性进展，流言蜚语不绝如缕，威利对之一概既不否认也不证实。他保持着神秘的缄默，同他那副仪表相得益彰。

===Original===
Having turned slowly so that his eyes rested on Lord Wisbeach’s ingenuous countenance, Willie paused, and his face assumed the expression of his photograph in the *Chronicle*.
“Ah, Wisbeach!” he said.
Lord Wisbeach did not appear to resent the patronage of his manner. He plunged cheerily into talk. He had a pleasant, simple way of comporting himself which made people like him.
“I was just telling Mrs. Pett,” he said, “that I shouldn’t be surprised if you were to get an offer for your stuff from our fellows at home before long. I saw a lot of our War Office men when I was in England, don’t you know. Several of them mentioned the stuff.”
Willie resented Partridgite as being referred to as “the stuff,” but he made allowance. All Englishmen talked that way, he supposed.
“Indeed?” he said.

===Chinese===
威利慢条斯理地转过身，让目光落定在威斯比奇勋爵那张憨直的脸上，顿了顿，随后他的面部摆出了他在《纪事报》（*Chronicle*）上那张照片里的表情。
「啊，威斯比奇！」他说。
威斯比奇勋爵似乎并不见怪他这副屈尊俯就的做派。他兴冲冲地攀谈起来。他为人处世有一种讨人喜欢的直爽劲儿，让人们都喜欢他。
「我正跟佩特太太说来着，」他说，「要不了多久，国内我们那帮人就找上门来出价盘你这批货，我是不会吃惊的。我在英格兰的时候见了不少我们陆军部的人，你可知道。他们中间有几位都提到过这东西。」
帕特里奇特被唤作「这东西」，威利心中不快，但他宽宏地不予计较。英国人说话都是这路数，他想。
「是吗？」他说。

===Original===
“Of course,” said Mrs. Pett, “Willie is a patriot and would have to give our own authorities the first chance﻿—”
“Rather!”
“﻿—But you know what officials are all over the world. They are so sceptical and they move so slowly.”
“I know. Our men at home are just the same as a rule. I’ve got a pal who invented something-or-other, I forget what, but it was a most decent little contrivance and very useful and all that; and he simply can’t get them to say Yes or No about it. But, all the same, I wonder you didn’t have some of them trying to put out feelers to you when you were in London.”
“Oh, we were only in London a few hours. By the way, Lord Wisbeach, my sister﻿—”﻿—Mrs. Pett paused; she disliked to have to mention her sister or to refer to this subject at all, but curiosity impelled her﻿—“my sister said that you are a great friend of her stepson, James Crocker. I didn’t know that you knew him.”
Lord Wisbeach seemed to hesitate for a moment.

===Chinese===
「当然啦，」佩特太太说，「威利是个爱国志士，总得让我们自家的当局得着优先机会——」
「那当然！」
「——可你知道世界各地的官僚是什么德行。他们疑心那么重，手脚又那么慢。」
「我知道。我们国内那帮人照例也是一路货。我有个朋友发明了个什么玩意儿，我忘了是什么了，反正是个顶体面的小巧装置，还挺有用什么的；可他就是没法让他们给句痛快话，行还是不行。不过话又说回来，奇怪的是你在伦敦那阵子，居然没有他们中的哪一位朝你伸触角试探。」
「哦，我们在伦敦只待了几个钟头。对了，威斯比奇勋爵，我妹妹——」佩特太太顿了顿；她不愿意提起她妹妹，也根本不愿意触及这个话题，可是好奇心驱使着她——「我妹妹说，你是她继子詹姆斯·克罗克的好朋友。我原先不知道你认识他。」
威斯比奇勋爵似乎迟疑了一刹那。

===Original===
“He’s not coming over, is he? Pity! It would have done him a world of good. Yes, Jimmy Crocker and I have always been great pals. He’s a bit of a nut, of course,﻿ ﻿… I beg your pardon!﻿ ﻿… I mean﻿ ﻿…” He broke off confusedly, and turned to Willie again to cover himself. “How are you getting on with the jolly old stuff?” he asked.
If Willie had objected to Partridgite being called “the stuff,” he was still less in favour of its being termed “the jolly old stuff.” He replied coldly.
“I have ceased to get along with the jolly old stuff.”
“Struck a snag?” enquired Lord Wisbeach sympathetically.
“On the contrary, my experiments have been entirely successful. I have enough Partridgite in my laboratory to blow New York to bits!”

===Chinese===
「他不打算过来，是吗？可惜！本来这对他大有好处。是啊，吉米·克罗克和我一直是莫逆之交。当然，他多少有点儿傻……恕我失言！……我是说……」他慌乱地收住话头，重新转向威利来掩饰窘态。「那件妙极的老玩意儿，你搞出什么名堂了？」他问。
如果说帕特里奇特被唤作「这东西」已让威利不悦，那么它被尊称为「妙极的老玩意儿」就更不合他的意了。他冷冷地答道。
「我已经不同那妙极的老玩意儿打交道了。」
「碰上坎儿了？」威斯比奇勋爵满怀同情地问道。
「恰恰相反，我的实验已大功告成。我实验室里的帕特里奇特，足够把整个纽约炸上天！」

===Original===
“Willie!” exclaimed Mrs. Pett. “Why didn’t you tell me before? You know I am so interested.”
“I only completed my work last night.”
He moved off with an important nod. He was tired of Lord Wisbeach’s society. There was something about the young man which he did not like. He went to find more congenial company in a group by the window.
Lord Wisbeach turned to his hostess. The vacuous expression had dropped from his face like a mask. A pair of keen and intelligent eyes met Mrs. Pett’s.
“Mrs. Pett, may I speak to you seriously?”

===Chinese===
「威利！」佩特太太惊呼。「你怎么不早告诉我？你知道我多么感兴趣。」
「我昨天夜里才完工。」
他煞有介事地一点头，挪步走开。他厌倦了威斯比奇勋爵的陪伴。这青年身上有什么东西他不欢喜。他去窗边的一堆人里寻觅气味更相投的伙伴去了。
威斯比奇勋爵转向他的女主人。那副茫然的神情已从脸上卸落，像摘掉一副面具。一双敏锐而聪明的眼睛迎上了佩特太太的目光。
「佩特太太，我能同您严肃地谈一谈吗？」

===Original===
Mrs. Pett’s surprise at the alteration in the man prevented her from replying. Much as she liked Lord Wisbeach, she had never given him credit for brains, and it was a man with brains and keen ones who was looking at her now. She nodded.
“If your nephew has really succeeded in his experiments, you should be awfully careful. That stuff ought not to lie about in his laboratory, though no doubt he has hidden it as carefully as possible. It ought to be in a safe somewhere. In that safe in your library. News of this kind moves like lightning. At this very moment, there may be people watching for a chance of getting at the stuff.”
Every nerve in Mrs. Pett’s body, every cell of a brain which had for years been absorbing and giving out sensational fiction, quivered irrepressibly at these words, spoken in a low, tense voice which gave them additional emphasis. Never had she misjudged a man as she had misjudged Lord Wisbeach.
“Spies?” she quavered.

===Chinese===
此人身上这场剧变，惊得佩特太太答不上话来。她尽管喜欢威斯比奇勋爵，却从不曾把他当作有脑子的，而此刻望着她的，偏偏是个有脑子的男人，还是副顶尖的脑子。她点了点头。
「如果您外甥的实验果真成功了，您可得万分小心。那东西不该搁在他的实验室里到处乱放，尽管他无疑已经把它藏得尽可能周密了。它应当放进哪只保险柜里。就放您书房的那只保险柜里。这类消息传起来快如闪电。此时此刻，说不定就有人正盯着机会下手弄这东西呢。」
佩特太太身上的每一根神经，她那颗多年来吸进又吐出惊险小说的脑袋里的每一个细胞，都随着这番话不可遏止地颤抖起来。这话说得又低又紧，愈发字字千钧。她还从来没有像看错威斯比奇勋爵这样看错过一个人。
「间谍？」她哆嗦着问。

===Original===
“They wouldn’t call themselves that,” said Lord Wisbeach. “Secret Service agents. Every country has its men whose only duty it is to handle this sort of work.”
“They would try to steal Willie’s﻿—?” Mrs. Pett’s voice failed.
“They would not look on it as stealing. Their motives would be patriotic. I tell you, Mrs. Pett, I have heard stories from friends of mine in the English Secret Service which would amaze you. Perfectly straight men in private life, but absolutely unscrupulous when at work. They stick at nothing﻿—nothing. If I were you, I should suspect everyone, especially every stranger.” He smiled engagingly. “You are thinking that that is odd advice from one who is practically a stranger like myself. Never mind. Suspect me, too, if you like. Be on the safe side.”
“I would not dream of doing such a thing, Lord Wisbeach,” said Mrs. Pett horrified. “I trust you implicitly. Even supposing such a thing were possible, would you have warned me like this, if you had been﻿—?”

===Chinese===
「他们不会这么称呼自己的，」威斯比奇勋爵说。「人家自称特工。每个国家都养着专司此职的人。」
「他们会想把威利的——？」佩特太太的声音哽住了。
「他们不会把这看作偷窃。他们的动机是爱国的。我告诉您，佩特太太，我从英国特工部门的朋友那里听来的种种故事，准叫您目瞪口呆。私生活里个个是正人君子，一旦上起工来就绝无半点顾忌。他们无所不为——无所不为。我要是您，就人人都疑，尤其疑每一个生人。」他和蔼可亲地一笑。「您心里在想，这忠告出自我这么个十足的外人嘴里，未免可笑。不要紧。愿意的话，把我也一并疑上。求个稳妥嘛。」
「我做梦也不会做那种事，威斯比奇勋爵，」佩特太太骇然说。「我对您全然信赖。姑且假设有这种可能，您若是那样的人，还会这样提醒我吗？」

===Original===
“That’s true,” said Lord Wisbeach. “I never thought of that. Well, let me say, suspect everybody but me.” He stopped abruptly. “Mrs. Pett,” he whispered, “don’t look round for a moment. Wait.” The words were almost inaudible. “Who is that man behind you? He has been listening to us. Turn slowly.”
With elaborate carelessness, Mrs. Pett turned her head. At first she thought her companion must have alluded to one of a small group of young men who, very improperly in such surroundings, were discussing with raised voices the prospects of the clubs competing for the National League Baseball Pennant. Then, extending the sweep of her gaze, she saw that she had been mistaken. Midway between her and this group stood a single figure, the figure of a stout man in a swallowtail suit, who bore before him a tray with cups on it. As she turned, this man caught her eye, gave a guilty start, and hurried across the room.
“You saw?” said Lord Wisbeach. “He was listening. Who is that man? Your butler apparently. What do you know of him?”
“He is my new butler. His name is Skinner.”
“Ah, your *new* butler? He hasn’t been with you long, then?”

===Chinese===
「倒也是，」威斯比奇勋爵说。「我倒没想到这一层。好吧，这么说吧：除了我，人人都疑。」他猛地打住。「佩特太太，」他耳语道，「这一会儿先别回头。等一等。」这几句话轻得几乎听不见。「您身后那个人是谁？他一直在偷听我们。慢慢转过来。」
佩特太太摆出煞费苦心的漫不经心，转过头去。起初她以为同伴指的必是那一小伙青年——在这般场合实在不成体统，他们正扯着嗓门争论争夺全国联盟棒球锦旗的各俱乐部的胜负前景。随后，她的目光扫描范围扩大，才明白自己弄错了。在她与那伙人之间，孤零零立着一个人影，是个穿燕尾服的胖子，胸前捧着一只放着茶杯的托盘。她一转身，那人撞上她的视线，心虚地一惊，急匆匆横穿房间去了。
「看见了吗？」威斯比奇勋爵说。「他刚才在偷听。那人是谁？看样子是您的男管家。他的底细您了解多少？」
「他是我的新管家。他叫斯金纳（Skinner）。」
「啊，您的*新*管家？那么他来府上还没多久吧？」

===Original===
“He only arrived from England three days ago.”
“From England? How did he get in here? I mean, on whose recommendation?”
“Mr. Pett offered him the place when we met him at my sister’s in London. We went over there to see my sister, Eugenia﻿—Mrs. Crocker. This man was the butler who admitted us. He asked Mr. Pett something about baseball, and Mr. Pett was so pleased that he offered him a place here if he wanted to come over. The man did not give any definite answer then, but apparently he sailed on the next boat, and came to the house a few days after we had returned.”
Lord Wisbeach laughed softly.
“Very smart. Of course they had him planted there for the purpose.”
“What ought I to do?” asked Mrs. Pett agitatedly.

===Chinese===
「他三天前才从英格兰过来。」
「从英格兰来？他怎么进府的？我是说，是谁的引荐？」
「是在伦敦我妹妹家碰到他时，佩特先生给他这个职位的。我们过去看望我妹妹尤金妮娅——克罗克太太。这个人就是开门迎我们进去的管家。他向佩特先生请教了几句棒球的事，佩特先生高兴得不得了，就提出他若愿意过来，府里给他留个位子。当时那人没给什么确切的答复，可他显然搭了下一班船，在我们回来几天之后找上门来了。」
威斯比奇勋爵轻轻地笑了。
「非常高明。他们当然是有意把他安插在那里的。」
「我该怎么办？」佩特太太心慌意乱地问。

===Original===
“Do nothing. There is nothing that you can do, for the present, except keep your eyes open. Watch this man Skinner. See if he has any accomplices. It is hardly likely that he is working alone. Suspect everybody. Believe me﻿ ﻿…”
At this moment, apparently from some upper region, there burst forth an uproar so sudden and overwhelming that it might well have been taken for a premature testing of a large sample of Partridgite; until a moment later it began to resemble more nearly the shrieks of some partially destroyed victim of that death-dealing invention. It was a bellow of anguish, and it poured through the house in a cascade of sound, advertising to all beneath the roof the twin facts that some person unknown was suffering and that whoever the sufferer might be he had excellent lungs.
The effect on the gathering in the drawing-room was immediate and impressive. Conversation ceased as if it had been turned off with a tap. Twelve separate and distinct discussions on twelve highly intellectual topics died instantaneously. It was as if the last trump had sounded. Futurist painters stared pallidly at *vers libre* poets, speech smitten from their lips; and stage performers looked at esoteric Buddhists with a wild surmise.
The sudden silence had the effect of emphasising the strange noise and rendering it more distinct, thus enabling it to carry its message to one at least of the listeners. Mrs. Pett, after a moment of strained attention in which time seemed to her to stand still, uttered a wailing cry and leaped for the door.
“Ogden!” she shrilled; and passed up the stairs two at a time, gathering speed as she went. A boy’s best friend is his mother.

===Chinese===
「什么都别做。眼下您什么也做不了，只有睁大眼睛。盯牢这个斯金纳。看看他有没有同伙。他单枪匹马作案的可能性微乎其微。人人皆疑。请相信我……」
就在这时，显然是从楼上某处，爆发出一阵突如其来、排山倒海的喧闹，大可以被当作是大份样品的帕特里奇特提前试爆了；直到片刻之后，它才变得更接近那件夺命发明的某位遭了半毁的受难者的尖叫。那是一声痛苦的咆哮，它像一挂声浪的瀑布倾泻而下，穿过整座宅子，向屋顶之下的全体人员昭示着一对事实：有个不明人士正在受苦，而这位受苦者不管是谁，肺活量都好极了。
这对客厅里那群人的效果是立竿见影且蔚为壮观的。谈话戛然而止，仿佛龙头一拧就被关掉了。十二场各不相属的讨论，十二个格调高绝的话题，齐刷刷当场断了气。宛如末日号角已然吹响。未来主义画家面色惨白地瞪着*自由体*诗人，话语被击落在唇边；戏剧演员们则带着狂乱的臆测，望着密宗佛教徒。
这突然降临的寂静起了个作用：把那奇异的响动衬托得愈发分明，于是它至少把信息送达了一位听者。佩特太太凝神屏息了片刻——在那片刻里，她觉得时间仿佛停住了——随即发出一声哀号，朝门口飞扑过去。
「奥格登！」她尖声叫道；随即跨上楼梯，一步两级，越走越快。男孩子的最好朋友，就是他的母亲。

## X / 十

Instruction in Deportment / 仪态课

===Original===
While the feast of reason and flow of soul had been in progress in the drawing-room, in the gymnasium on the top floor Jerry Mitchell, awaiting the coming of Mr. Pett, had been passing the time in improving with strenuous exercise his already impressive physique. If Mrs. Pett’s guests had been less noisily concentrated on their conversation, they might have heard the muffled *tap-tap-tap* that proclaimed that Jerry Mitchell was punching the bag upstairs.

It was not until he had punched it for perhaps five minutes that, desisting from his labours, he perceived that he had the pleasure of the company of little Ogden Ford. The stout boy was standing in the doorway, observing him with an attentive eye.

“What are you doing?” enquired Ogden.

Jerry passed a gloved fist over his damp brow.

“Punchin’ the bag.”
===Chinese===
客厅里，那场理性的盛筵、心灵的涌流正开得如火如荼；与此同时，顶层的健身房里，杰里·米切尔一边等候佩特先生大驾光临，一边用剧烈的运动打磨他那本已相当可观的体格，借以消磨时光。倘若佩特太太的客人们交谈时不是那么闹哄哄地全神贯注，他们或许能听见一阵隐隐约约的*咚、咚、咚*——那声音昭告世人：杰里·米切尔正在楼上捶打吊袋。

直捶到约莫五分钟之后，他才停下手头的功夫，发觉自己竟有此荣幸，得小奥格登·福特惠然作陪。那胖小子正站在门口，用一只专注的眼睛观察着他。

「你在干什么？」奥格登问道。

杰里抬起戴着手套的拳头，抹了抹汗津津的额头。

「捶袋子呢。」

===Original===
He began to remove his gloves, eyeing Ogden the while with a disapproval which he made no attempt to conceal. An extremist on the subject of keeping in condition, the spectacle of the bulbous stripling was a constant offence to him. Ogden, in pursuance of his invariable custom on the days when Mrs. Pett entertained, had been lurking on the stairs outside the drawing-room for the past hour, levying toll on the foodstuffs that passed his way. He wore a congested look, and there was jam about his mouth.

“Why?” he said, retrieving a morsel of jam from his right cheek with the tip of his tongue.

“To keep in condition.”

“Why do you want to keep in condition?”

Jerry flung the gloves into their locker.

“Fade!” he said wearily. “Fade!”
===Chinese===
他动手摘手套，一面拿眼瞅着奥格登，那份不以为然丝毫懒得掩饰。身为「保持状态」问题上的极端主义者，眼前这个滚圆少年的模样，在他看来就是一桩屡犯不爽的公然冒犯。每逢佩特太太在家宴客，奥格登照例要在客厅外的楼梯上潜伏整整一个钟头，向打这儿经过的各色食品征收买路钱。此刻他一脸拥塞之相，嘴边上还糊着果酱。

「为什么呀？」他说着，用舌尖从右颊上收复了一星果酱。

「为了保持状态。」

「你为什么要保持状态？」

杰里把手套摔进柜子里。

「消散吧！」他有气无力地说。「消散吧！」

===Original===
“Huh?”

“Beat it!”

“Huh?” Much pastry seemed to have clouded the boy’s mind.

“Run away.”

“Don’t want to run away.”

The annoyed pugilist sat down and scrutinised his visitor critically.
===Chinese===
「嗯？」

「快滚！」

「嗯？」看来大量的油酥点心已把这孩子的心智搅浑了。

「跑开去。」

「不想跑开。」

这位恼火的拳师坐下来，把来访的小客人挑剔地端详了一番。

===Original===
“You never do anything you don’t want to, I guess?”

“No,” said Ogden simply. “You’ve got a funny nose,” he added dispassionately. “What did you do to it to make it like that?”

Mr. Mitchell shifted restlessly on his chair. He was not a vain man, but he was a little sensitive about that particular item in his makeup.

“Lizzie says it’s the funniest nose she ever saw. She says it’s something out of a comic supplement.”
===Chinese===
「我猜，凡是你说不想做的事，你是一件也从来不做的吧？」

「不做的，」奥格登坦白地说。「你这鼻子可真逗，」他又不动声色地补上一句。「你是怎么把它弄成那副样子的？」

米切尔先生在椅子上坐立不安地挪了挪。他不是个爱虚荣的人，但对自身配备中的这一特定零件，多少有些敏感。

「莉齐说，这是她平生所见最逗的鼻子。她说这玩意儿整个是从滑稽连环画副刊里出来的货色。」

===Original===
A dull flush, such as five minutes with the bag had been unable to produce, appeared on Jerry Mitchell’s peculiar countenance. It was not that he looked on Lizzie Murphy, herself no Lillian Russell, as an accepted authority on the subject of facial beauty; but he was aware that in this instance she spoke not without reason, and he was vexed, moreover, as many another had been before him, by the note of indulgent patronage in Ogden’s voice. His fingers twitched a little eagerly, and he looked sullenly at his tactless junior.

“Get out!”

“Huh?”

“Get outa here!”

“Don’t want to get out of here,” said Ogden with finality. He put his hand in his trouser-pocket and pulled out a sticky mass which looked as if it might once have been a cream-puff or a meringue. He swallowed it contentedly. “I’d forgotten I had that,” he explained. “Mary gave it to me on the stairs. Mary thinks you’ve a funny nose, too,” he proceeded, as one relating agreeable gossip.
===Chinese===
杰里·米切尔那张别致的脸面上，泛起了一阵五分钟吊袋都没能打出来的暗红。倒不是说他把莉齐·墨菲（Lizzie Murphy）——她自己也绝非什么莉莲·拉塞尔（Lillian Russell）——尊为面部美容学界公认的权威；而是他心里明白，这回她的话并非全无道理；更何况，一如他之前的无数前辈，奥格登话音里那种纵容的、屈尊俯就的调子实在叫他冒火。他的手指微微发痒地抽动着，阴沉沉地瞪着这位不知轻重的少年。

「出去！」

「嗯？」

「从这儿滚出去！」

「不想从这儿出去，」奥格登一锤定音地说。他把手伸进裤袋，掏出一团黏糊糊的东西，看那模样，从前保不准是一只奶油泡芙，或者一块蛋白酥。他心满意足地把它咽了下去。「我都忘了兜里还有这个，」他解释道。「是玛丽（Mary）在楼梯上给我的。玛丽也觉得你的鼻子逗，」他继续道，俨然是在转述一段可人的八卦。

===Original===
“Can it! Can it!” exclaimed the exasperated pugilist.

“I’m only telling you what I heard her say.”

Mr. Mitchell rose convulsively and took a step towards his persecutor, breathing noisily through the criticised organ. He was a chivalrous man, a warm admirer of the sex, but he was conscious of a wish that it was in his power to give Mary what he would have described as “hers.” She was one of the parlour-maids, a homely woman with a hard eye, and it was part of his grievance against her that his Maggie, alias Celestine, Mrs. Pett’s maid, had formed an enthusiastic friendship with her. He had no evidence to go on, but he suspected Mary of using her influence with Celestine to urge the suit of his leading rival for the latter’s hand, Biggs the chauffeur. He disliked Mary intensely, even on general grounds. Ogden’s revelation added fuel to his aversion. For a moment he toyed with the fascinating thought of relieving his feelings by spanking the boy, but restrained himself reluctantly at the thought of the inevitable ruin which would ensue. He had been an inmate of the house long enough to know, with a completeness which would have embarrassed that gentleman, what a cipher Mr. Pett was in the home and how little his championship would avail in the event of a clash with Mrs. Pett. And to give Ogden that physical treatment which should long since have formed the main plank in the platform of his education would be to invite her wrath as nothing else could. He checked himself, and reached out for the skipping-rope, hoping to ease his mind by further exercise.

Ogden, chewing the remains of the cream-puff, eyed him with languid curiosity.
===Chinese===
「闭嘴！闭嘴！」这位被逼到极限的拳师吼道。

「我不过是把亲耳听见她说的话转告你罢了。」

米切尔先生一个激灵站起身，朝他的迫害者逼近一步，从那只挨了批判的器官里呼哧呼哧喘着粗气。他是个有骑士风度的人，向来热忱崇拜女性，可此刻他却清醒地意识到，自己恨不得能赏玛丽一份他所谓的「她该得的」。她是客厅侍女之一，一个眼光冷硬、姿色平平的女人；他对她的一条罪状，便是他的玛吉——即化名塞莱斯廷的佩特太太女仆——竟同她结下了火热友谊。他手里虽无实据，却疑心玛丽正仗着她对塞莱斯廷的影响，撺掇他的头号情敌——司机比格斯——的求婚事业。即使不论这一节，单凭一般的理由，他也厌恶玛丽厌恶到了极点。奥格登的揭发，更往这份厌恶里添了柴。有一瞬间，他玩味着一个迷人的念头：在这小子屁股上痛痛快快打一顿，出一出胸中恶气；可一想到随之而来的必然毁灭，只好悻悻地按捺住。他在这宅子里寄居得够久了，久到能以一种足以叫那位先生脸红的透彻，看清佩特先生在这个家里是怎样一枚可有可无的零、一旦跟佩特太太交起火来他的撑腰又是怎样地无济于事。而给奥格登来上那么一顿体罚——这本该早就写成他教育纲领里的头号条款——将是招惹太太雷霆之怒的头等妙法，再没有别的能出其右。他忍住了，伸手去够跳绳，指望靠再一番运动平一平心头的火气。

奥格登嚼着奶油泡芙的残余，带着倦怠的好奇打量着他。

===Original===
“What are you doing that for?”

Mr. Mitchell skipped grimly on.

“What are you doing that for? I thought only girls skipped.”

Mr. Mitchell paid no heed. Ogden, after a moment’s silent contemplation, returned to his original train of thought.

“I saw an advertisement in a magazine the other day of a sort of machine for altering the shape of noses. You strap it on when you go to bed. You ought to get pop to blow you to one.”

Jerry Mitchell breathed in a laboured way.
===Chinese===
「你跳这个干什么？」

米切尔先生闷头继续跳。

「你跳这个干什么？我还当只有女孩子才跳绳呢。」

米切尔先生毫不理会。奥格登默默冥想了一阵，又回到他原先的思路上去了。

「前几天我在一本杂志上看见一幅广告，卖一种能改变鼻子形状的机器。上床睡觉的时候绑上就成。你该叫你老子掏腰包给你置一个。」

杰里·米切尔吃力地喘着气。

===Original===
“You want to look nice about the place, don’t you? Well, then! there’s no sense in going around looking like that if you don’t have to, is there? I heard Mary talking about your nose to Biggs and Celestine. She said she had to laugh every time she saw it.”

The skipping-rope faltered in its sweep, caught in the skipper’s legs, and sent him staggering across the room. Ogden threw back his head and laughed merrily. He liked free entertainments, and this struck him as a particularly enjoyable one.

There are moments in the life of every man when the impulse attacks him to sacrifice his future to the alluring gratification of the present. The strong man resists such impulses. Jerry Mitchell was not a weak man, but he had been sorely tried. The annoyance of Ogden’s presence and conversation had sapped his self-restraint, as dripping water will wear away a rock. A short while before, he had fought down the urgent temptation to massacre this exasperating child, but now, despised love adding its sting to that of injured vanity, he forgot the consequences. Bounding across the room, he seized Ogden in a powerful grip, and the next instant the latter’s education, in the true sense of the word, so long postponed, had begun; and with it that avalanche of sound which, rolling down into the drawing-room, hurled Mrs. Pett so violently and with such abruptness from the society of her guests.

Disposing of the last flight of stairs with the agility of the chamois which leaps from crag to crag of the snow-topped Alps, Mrs. Pett finished with a fine burst of speed along the passage on the top floor, and rushed into the gymnasium just as Jerry’s avenging hand was descending for the eleventh time.
===Chinese===
「你总想在这宅子里显得体面点儿，不是吗？这不结了！要是本可以不用的话，成天顶着那么一副模样到处晃就毫无道理了，对不对？我听见玛丽跟比格斯还有塞莱斯廷念叨你的鼻子来着。她说她每回一瞧见它，就非笑不可。」

跳绳在轮挥之中一个踉跄，缠上了跳绳人的一双腿，把他甩得踉踉跄跄横穿房间。奥格登仰头畅笑。他素喜白看的助兴节目，而这一出在他看来尤为可意。

每个男人的一生中，总有那么几个时刻，会被一股冲动袭击，怂恿他为眼前诱人的痛快牺牲掉自己的未来。坚强的人抵制得住这类冲动。杰里·米切尔不是个软弱的人，可他受的磨砺也委实够呛。奥格登人在眼前、话在耳畔的双重折磨，像滴水穿石一般，一滴滴磨蚀了他的自制力。片刻之前，他还压住了把这讨债鬼宰了的迫切诱惑；可如今，被轻贱的爱情在被刺伤的虚荣心上又补了一蜇，他便把后果忘了个一干二净。他一跃而起蹿过房间，一把铁钳似的手劲擒住奥格登——下一瞬间，这孩子那份名副其实、久拖未办的教育，总算开了张；与之俱来的，还有那一阵雪崩般的巨响，滚滚冲下客厅，把佩特太太从宾客群中那么猛烈、那么猝不及防地抛掷了出去。

佩特太太以阿尔卑斯雪岭间从这块岩峰跃向那块岩峰的岩羚羊般的矫捷，一举解决了最后一段楼梯，又在顶层的走廊上来了个漂亮的冲刺，旋即冲进健身房——恰在此刻，杰里那只报仇雪恨的手掌，正朝下落到第十一下。

## XI / 十一

Jimmy Decides to Be Himself / 吉米决定做回自己

===Original===
It was less than a quarter of an hour later﻿—such was the speed with which Nemesis, usually slow, had overtaken him﻿—that Jerry Mitchell, carrying a grip and walking dejectedly, emerged from the back premises of the Pett home and started down Riverside Drive in the direction of his boardinghouse, a cheap, clean, and respectable establishment situated on Ninety-seventh Street between the Drive and Broadway. His usually placid nervous system was ruffled and a-quiver from the events of the afternoon, and his cauliflower ears still burned reminiscently at the recollection of the uncomplimentary words shot at them by Mrs. Pett before she expelled him from the house. Moreover, he was in a mild panic at the thought of having to see Ann later on and try to explain the disaster to her. He knew how the news would affect her. She had set her heart on removing Ogden to more disciplinary surroundings, and she could not possibly do it now that her ally was no longer an inmate of the house. He was an essential factor in the scheme, and now, to gratify the desire of the moment, he had eliminated himself. Long before he reached the brownstone house, which looked exactly like all the other brownstone houses in all the other side-streets of uptown New York, the first fine careless rapture of his mad outbreak had passed from Jerry Mitchell, leaving nervous apprehension in its place. Ann was a girl whom he worshipped respectfully, but he feared her in her wrath.

Having entered the boardinghouse, Jerry, seeking company in his hour of sorrow, climbed the stairs till he reached a door on the second floor. Sniffing and detecting the odour of tobacco, he knocked and was bidden to enter.

“Hello, Bayliss!” he said sadly, having obeyed the call.

He sat down on the end of the bed and heaved a deep sigh.
===Chinese===
不到一刻钟之后——复仇女神平日里步履迟缓，这一回追赶起他来却风驰电掣——杰里·米切尔拎着一只旅行提包，垂头丧气地从佩特宅的后院走出来，沿着里弗赛德大道朝他的寄宿公寓走去。那是一家便宜、干净而又体面的公寓，坐落在九十七街，夹在大道与百老汇之间。他平日里平平静静的神经系统，经过下午这一场折腾，已然乱作一团、簌簌发抖；他那两只菜花耳，一回想起被逐出宅门之前佩特太太朝它们射来的那一串难听话，至今还在火辣辣地发烧。再者，一想到待会儿还得去见安，还得设法向她解释这场大祸，他便隐隐犯起慌来。他清楚这消息会对她产生什么影响。她一心想把奥格登挪到纪律更严明的地方去，可如今她的盟友已不再是这宅子里的人，这事便万难办成了。他本是这项计划里不可或缺的一环，如今，就为了图一时痛快，他把自己这一环给报废了。还没走到那栋褐砂石宅子——它与纽约住宅区其他所有侧街上其他所有褐砂石宅子长得一模一样——杰里·米切尔那场疯狂发作之初的痛快淋漓便已烟消云散，只剩下惴惴不安取而代之。安是他敬若神明的姑娘，可她发起怒来，他又怕得要命。

进了寄宿公寓，杰里想在伤心时刻寻个伴儿，便一路爬上楼梯，来到二楼的一扇门前。他嗅了嗅，闻出里面有烟草味儿，于是敲了敲门，里面唤他进去。

应声进门之后，他悲悲戚戚地招呼了一句：「你好啊，贝利斯！」

他在床沿上坐下，长叹一声。

===Original===
The room which he had entered was airy but small, so small, indeed, that the presence of any furniture in it at all was almost miraculous, for at first sight it seemed incredible that the bed did not fill it from side to side. There were however, a few vacant spots, and in these had been placed a washstand, a chest of drawers, and a midget rocking-chair. The window, which the thoughtful architect had designed at least three sizes too large for the room and which admitted the evening air in pleasing profusion, looked out onto a series of forlorn backyards. In boardinghouses, it is only the windows of the rich and haughty that face the street.

On the bed, a corncob pipe between his teeth, lay Jimmy Crocker. He was shoeless and in his shirtsleeves. There was a crumpled evening paper on the floor beside the bed. He seemed to be taking his rest after the labours of a trying day.

At the sound of Jerry’s sigh he raised his head, but, finding the attitude too severe a strain on the muscles of the neck, restored it to the pillow.

“What’s the matter, Jerry? You seem perturbed. You have the aspect of one whom Fate has smitten in the spiritual solar plexus, or of one who has been searching for the leak in Life’s gaspipe with a lighted candle. What’s wrong?”
===Chinese===
他走进的这间屋子通风倒是良好，就是小——小到屋里居然还摆得下家具，简直近乎奇迹，因为乍一看去，那张床居然没有把屋子从这边塞到那边，就叫人难以置信。不过终究还有几处空当，空当里便安置了一个洗脸架、一个五斗橱，外加一把侏儒尺寸的摇椅。窗户被那位善解人意的建筑师设计得比屋子所需的尺码起码大了三号，暮色中的空气源源涌入，丰盛得讨人喜欢；凭窗望去，是一溜荒凉冷清的后院。在寄宿公寓里，只有阔气而傲慢的房间，窗子才临街开。

床上躺着吉米·克罗克，嘴里叼着一根玉米芯烟斗。他脚上没穿鞋，身上只着衬衫，床边地板上摊着一张揉皱的晚报。看那光景，他是在劳苦一天之后歇息养神。

听见杰里的叹息，他抬起头来，但发觉这姿势对颈部肌肉是过于严酷的考验，便又把它放回了枕头上。

「怎么了，杰里？你似乎心绪不宁。你这副模样，活像被命运在精神的太阳神经丛上狠狠击中了一拳，又像举着点亮的蜡烛去搜人生煤气管上的漏气孔。到底出什么事了？」

===Original===
“Curtains!”

Jimmy, through long absence from his native land, was not always able to follow Jerry’s thoughts when concealed in the wrappings of the peculiar dialect which he affected.

“I get you not, friend. Supply a few footnotes.”

“I’ve been fired.”

Jimmy sat up. This was no imaginary trouble, no mere malaise of the temperament. It was concrete, and called for sympathy.

“I’m awfully sorry,” he said. “No wonder you aren’t rollicking. How did it happen?”
===Chinese===
「完蛋了！」

吉米离乡日久，每逢杰里把心思裹进他那套独特方言的行头里再抖出来，便未必回回跟得上趟。

「我没听懂，朋友。请附几条脚注。」

「我被炒了。」

吉米坐起身来。这可不是凭空想象的烦恼，也不是什么性情上的小恙。这是桩实实在在的祸事，须报以同情。

「我非常遗憾，」他说，「难怪你欢跃不起来。是怎么闹的？」

===Original===
“That half-portion Bill Taft came joshing me about my beezer till it got something fierce,” explained Jerry. “William J. Bryan couldn’t have stood for it.”

Once again Jimmy lost the thread. The wealth of political allusion baffled him.

“What’s Taft been doing to you?”

“It wasn’t Taft. He only looks like him. It was that kid Ogden up where I work. He came butting into the gym, joshing me about﻿—makin’ pers’nal remarks till I kind of lost my goat, and the next thing I knew I was giving him his!” A faint gleam of pleasure lightened the gloom of his face. “I cert’nly give him his!” The gleam faded. “And after that﻿—well, here I am!”

Jimmy understood now. He had come to the boardinghouse the night of his meeting with Jerry Mitchell on Broadway, and had been there ever since, and frequent conversations with the pugilist had put him abreast of affairs at the Pett home. He was familiar with the personnel of the establishment on Riverside Drive, and knew precisely how great was the crime of administering correction to Ogden Ford, no matter what the cause. Nor did he require explanation of the phenomenon of Mrs. Pett dismissing one who was in her husband’s private employment. Jerry had his sympathy freely.

“You appear,” he said, “to have acted in a thoroughly capable and praiseworthy manner. The only point in your conduct which I would permit myself to criticise is your omission to slay the kid. That, however, was due, I take it, to the fact that you were interrupted. We will now proceed to examine the future. I cannot see that it is altogether murky. You have lost a good job, but there are others, equally good, for a man of your calibre. New York is crammed with dyspeptic millionaires who need an efficient physical instructor to look after them. Cheer up, Cuthbert, for the sun is still shining!”
===Chinese===
「那个小号塔夫脱跑来拿我的鼻子打趣，闹得没完没了，」杰里解释道，「换了威廉·J. 布莱恩也咽不下这口气。」

吉米又跟丢了话头。这一连串丰盛的政治典故把他绕晕了。

「塔夫脱对你干什么了？」

「不是塔夫脱。他只是长得像塔夫脱。是我上班那家的少爷奥格登。他闯进健身房来，拿我打趣——净说些人身攻击的话，说得我火气直往上冒，等我回过神来，我已经在收拾他了！」一丝快意的微光点亮了他脸上的阴霾。「我着实收拾了他一顿！」微光随即又黯淡下去。「再往后嘛——得，我就到这儿来了！」

吉米这下明白了。自百老汇街头与杰里·米切尔相遇那晚起，他便住进了这家寄宿公寓，此后一直没挪窝，与这位拳师的一次次长谈，使他随时掌握着佩特宅的动向。里弗赛德大道那户人家的全班人马他都熟门熟路，也完全清楚管教奥格登·福特是何等滔天的罪过——无论起因为何。至于佩特太太把丈夫私人雇用的人扫地出门这一奇观，也无需谁来向他解说。杰里的同情，他慷慨奉送。

「照我看，」他说，「你行事干净利落，值得嘉许。你的举动中唯一容我冒昧批评的一点，是你漏了没把这小子宰掉。不过我料想，那是因为你被人打断了。咱们这就来考察一下前程。我看它并不算漆黑一片。你是丢了一份好差事，可对你这等身手的人，还有别的好差事呢。纽约城里塞满了消化不良的百万富翁，个个都需要一位得力的健身教练来照管他们。振作起来吧，卡斯伯特，太阳依然高照！」

===Original===
Jerry Mitchell shook his head. He refused to be comforted.

“It’s Miss Ann,” he said. “What am I going to say to her?”

“What has she got to do with it?” asked Jimmy, interested.

For a moment Jerry hesitated, but the desire for sympathy and advice was too strong for him. And after all there was no harm in confiding in a good comrade like Jimmy.
===Chinese===
杰里·米切尔摇了摇头。他拒绝受人安慰。

「是安小姐的事，」他说，「我跟她怎么说呢？」

「这事跟她有什么相干？」吉米问，来了兴致。

杰里迟疑了片刻，但渴求同情与指点的欲望终究占了上风。再说，向吉米这样的好哥们交个底，又有什么害处呢。

===Original===
“It’s like this,” he said. “Miss Ann and me had got it all fixed up to kidnap the kid!”

“What!”

“Say, I don’t mean ordinary kidnapping. It’s this way. Miss Ann come to me and we agree that the kid’s a pest that had ought to have some strong-arm keep him in order, so we decide to get him away to a friend of mine who keeps a dogs’ hospital down on Long Island. Bud Smithers is the guy to handle that kid. You ought to see him take hold of a dog that’s all grouch and ugliness and make it over into a dog that it’s a pleasure to have around. I thought a few weeks with Bud was what the doctor ordered for Ogden, and Miss Ann guessed I was right, so we had it all framed. And now this happens and balls everything up! She can’t do nothing with a husky kid like that without me to help her. And how am I going to help her if I’m not allowed in the house?”
===Chinese===
「是这么回事，」他说，「安小姐跟我早把事儿全盘安排好了——把那小子绑架走！」

「什么！」

「嗨，我不是说寻常的绑架。是这么回事：安小姐来找我，我俩都认定这小子是个祸害，得有个耍胳膊的狠角色治得住他，于是我们决定把他弄到我一位朋友那儿去——他在长岛开了一家狗医院。治那小子，正得巴德·史密瑟斯这号人物出手。你真该瞧瞧他怎么收拾一条浑身别扭、满脸凶相的狗，把它改造成一条养着叫人舒心的狗。我想着让奥格登跟巴德过几个星期，正是大夫开的对症药方，安小姐也觉得我说的在理，于是咱们全盘筹划停当。可现在出了这档子事，全砸了锅！没有我搭手，那么一条壮硕的小子，她拿什么治？而我要是连宅门都进不去，又怎么搭手？」

===Original===
Jimmy was conscious of a renewed admiration for a girl whom he had always considered a queen among women. How rarely in this world did one find a girl who combined every feminine charm of mind and body with a resolute determination to raise Cain at the slightest provocation!

“What an absolutely corking idea!”

Jerry smirked modestly at the approbation, but returned instantly to his gloom.

“You get me now? What am I to say to her? She’ll be sore!”

“The problem,” Jimmy had begun, “is one which, as you suggest, presents certain﻿—” when there was a knock at the door and the head of the boardinghouse’s maid-of-all-work popped in.
===Chinese===
吉米心中对这位姑娘再度涌起钦佩——在他眼里，她素来便是女中之王。这世上何其难得：一位姑娘既能集心智与形体的一切女性魅力于一身，又怀着一副说一不二的狠劲，稍有招惹便要掀桌大闹！

「这主意真是绝了！」

杰里受了夸奖，谦虚地咧嘴一笑，随即又坠回愁云惨雾之中。

「这回你听明白了吧？我跟她怎么说呢？她非气炸不可！」

「这个问题嘛，」吉米刚起了个头，「正如你所言，呈现出若干——」话音未落，门外传来敲门声，寄宿公寓那位包打杂的女仆把脑袋探了进来。

===Original===
“Mr. Bayliss, is Mr. Mitchell﻿—? Oh, say, Mr. Mitchell, there’s a lady down below wants to see you. Says her name’s Chester.”

Jerry looked at Jimmy appealingly.

“What’ll I do?”

“Do nothing,” said Jimmy, rising and reaching for his shoes. “I’ll go down and see her. I can explain for you.”

“It’s mighty good of you﻿—”

“It will be a pleasure. Rely on me.”
===Chinese===
「贝利斯先生，米切尔先生是不是——？哦，对了，米切尔先生，楼下来了一位小姐要见您。说姓切斯特。」

杰里向吉米投去求助的目光。

「我怎么办？」

「什么都不必办，」吉米说着，起身去够他的鞋子，「我下楼去见她。我可以替你解释。」

「你真是够意思——」

「这是我的荣幸。包在我身上。」

===Original===
Ann, who had returned from her drive shortly after the Ogden disaster and had instantly proceeded to the boardinghouse, had been shown into the parlour. Jimmy found her staring in a rapt way at a statuette of the Infant Samuel which stood near a bowl of wax fruit on the mantelpiece. She was feeling aggrieved with Fate and extremely angry with Jerry Mitchell, and she turned at the sound of the opening door with a militant expression in her eyes, which changed to one of astonishment on perceiving who it was that had come in.

“Mr. Bayliss!”

“Good evening, Miss Chester. We, so to speak, meet again. I have come as an intermediary. To be brief, Jerry Mitchell daren’t face you, so I offered to come down instead.”

“But how﻿—but why are you here?”

“I live here.” He followed her gaze. It rested on a picture of cows in a field. “Late American school,” he said. “Attributed to the landlady’s niece, a graduate of the Wissahickon, Pa. Correspondence School of Pictorial Art. Said to be genuine.”

“You *live* here?” repeated Ann. She had been brought up all her life among the carefully thought out effects of eminent interior decorators, and the room seemed more dreadful to her than it actually was. “What an awful room!”
===Chinese===
奥格登事件发生后不久，安便兜风归来，随即径直赶到寄宿公寓，被引进了客厅。吉米找到她时，她正出神地凝望着壁炉架上的一尊雕像——婴孩撒母耳——它立在满满一碗蜡制水果近旁。她心里正怨着命运不公，又对杰里·米切尔怒火中烧，听得开门声响，她转过身来，眼中一副跃跃请战的神色；待看清进来的是何人，那神色顿时化作惊愕。

「贝利斯先生！」

「晚上好，切斯特小姐。咱们，可以说，又见面了。我是来充当中间人的。长话短说：杰里·米切尔不敢见你，我便自告奋勇替他下来。」

「可是——可是你怎么会在这儿？」

「我就住在这儿。」他顺着她的目光望去。那目光停在一幅画上，画的是田野里的几头牛。「美国晚期画派，」他说，「传为房东太太的侄女所作，系宾夕法尼亚州威萨希康绘画函授学校的毕业生。据称系真迹。」

「你*住*在这儿？」安重复了一遍。她自幼在赫赫有名的室内设计师精心推敲出的陈设效果中长大，因此这屋子在她眼里比实际情形还要可怕三分。「这屋子真够呛！」

===Original===
“Awful? You must be overlooking the piano. Can’t you see the handsome plush cover from where you are standing? Move a little to the southeast and shade your eyes. We get music here of an evening﻿—when we don’t see it coming and sidestep.”

“Why in the name of goodness do you live here, Mr. Bayliss?”

“Because, Miss Chester, I am infernally hard up! Because the Bayliss bankroll has been stricken with a wasting sickness.”

Ann was looking at him incredulously.

“But﻿—but﻿—then, did you really *mean* all that at lunch the other day? I thought you were joking. I took it for granted that you could get work whenever you wanted to or you wouldn’t have made fun of it like that! Can’t you really find anything to do?”

“Plenty to do. But I’m not paid for it. I walk a great number of blocks and jump into a great number of cars and dive into elevators and dive out again and open doors and say ‘Good morning!’ when people tell me they haven’t a job for me. My days are quite full, but my pocketbook isn’t!”
===Chinese===
「够呛？你一定是漏看了那架钢琴。从你站的地方，望不见那块气派的长毛绒琴罩吧？朝东南方挪两步，再拿手遮遮眼睛。我们这儿隔三差五晚上有音乐——专挑我们猝不及防、来不及侧身闪避的时候。」

「看在老天的份上，你到底为什么要住在这儿，贝利斯先生？」

「因为，切斯特小姐，我手头紧得要命！因为贝利斯家的钱袋害上了痨病，正一天天地消瘦下去。」

安难以置信地望着他。

「可是——可是——那么，那天午餐时你说的话，竟是*当真的*？我还以为你在开玩笑。我一向想当然地认为，你想要工作随时都找得到，不然你也不会那样拿它打趣！你真的找不到任何事情做吗？」

「事情有的是。只是没有报酬。我走过数不清的街区，跳上数不清的街车，一头扎进电梯又一头扎出来，推开一扇扇门，在人家告诉我这里没有活儿的时候道一声『早安！』。我的日子排得满满当当，我的钱袋却瘪得可怜！」

===Original===
Ann had forgotten all about her errand in her sympathy.

“I’m so sorry. Why, it’s terrible! I should have thought you could have found *something*.”
