Jimmy found it hard at first to say anything. He had never properly understood the possibilities of Ogden’s character before. The longer he contemplated him, the more admirable Ann’s scheme appeared. It seemed to him that only a resolute keeper of a home for dogs would be adequately equipped for dealing with this remarkable youth.

“This is a commercial age,” he said.

“You bet it is,” said Ogden. “My middle name is business. Say, are you working this on your own, or are you in with Buck Maginnis and his crowd?”

“I don’t think I know Mr. Maginnis.”

“He’s the guy who kidnapped me the first time. He’s a roughneck. Smooth Sam Fisher got away with me the second time. Maybe you’re in with Sam?”

“No.”

===Chinese===

吉米一时竟不知说什么好。他从前实在没有充分领略过奥格登这号人物的种种能耐。他越端详这孩子，越觉得安的计划可敬可佩。在他看来，唯有铁腕的狗医院院长，才算装备齐全，对付得了这位非凡少年。

「这是一个商业时代。」他说。

「可不咋的，」奥格登说。「我的中名就叫『生意』。我问你，这票买卖你是单干，还是搭了巴克·马金尼斯（Buck Maginnis）那伙人的班子？」

「马金尼斯先生，我想我不认识。」

「头一回绑我的就是他。一条粗汉。第二回把我弄走的是『滑头』萨姆·费希尔（Smooth Sam Fisher）。兴许你是萨姆一伙的？」

「不是。」

===Original===

“No, I guess not. I heard that he had married and retired from business. I rather wish you were one of Buck’s lot. I like Buck. When he kidnapped me, I lived with him and he gave me a swell time. When I left him, a woman came and interviewed me about it for one of the Sunday papers. Sob stuff. Called the piece ‘Even Kidnappers Have Tender Hearts Beneath A Rough Exterior.’ I’ve got it upstairs in my press-clipping album. It was pretty bad slush. Buck Maginnis hasn’t got any tender heart beneath his rough exterior, but he’s a good sort and I liked him. We used to shoot craps. And he taught me to chew. I’d be tickled to death to have Buck get me again. But, if you’re working on your own, all right. It’s all the same to me, provided you meet me on the terms.”

“You certainly are a fascinating child.”

“Less of it, less of it. I’ve troubles enough to bear without having you getting fresh. Well, what about it? Talk figures. If I let you take me away, do we divvy up or don’t we? That’s all you’ve got to say.”

“That’s easily settled. I’ll certainly give you half of whatever I get.”

Ogden looked wistfully at the writing-desk.

“I wish I could have that in writing. But I guess it wouldn’t stand in law. I suppose I shall have to trust you.”

===Chinese===

「不是就好。听说他结了婚，金盆洗手了。我还真盼你是巴克那一伙的。我喜欢巴克。他绑我那回，我跟他同吃同住，他待我阔气极了。我离开他以后，有个女的为这事来采访我，写给哪家星期日报纸。催泪文章。题目叫《绑匪的粗皮囊底下也有一颗温柔的心》。剪报我搁在楼上剪报簿里呢。腻人腻得可以。巴克·马金尼斯那副粗皮囊底下才没有什么温柔的心，不过他是个好汉，我喜欢他。我们常掷骰子。他还教我嚼烟草。巴克要是再绑我一回，我才乐死了呢。不过你要是单干，也成。只要你肯按我的条件来，跟谁干我都无所谓。」

「你可真是个迷人的孩子。」

「少来，少来。我一身麻烦够受了，不劳你来耍贫嘴。怎么样，说数吧。我若让你把我带走，咱们分不分账？就这么一句话。」

「这好办。到手的钱，管保分你一半。」

奥格登眼巴巴地望着写字台。

「这话要是能落成白纸黑字就好了。可我想打官司也站不住。看来只好信你一回了。」

===Original===

“Honour among thieves.”

“Less of the thieves. This is just a straight business proposition. I’ve got something valuable to sell, and I’m darned if I’m going to keep giving it away. I’ve been too easy. I ought to have thought of this before. All right, then, that’s settled. Now it’s up to you. You can think out the rest of it yourself.”

He heaved himself out of the chair, and left the room. Ann, coming down from the gallery, found Jimmy meditating. He looked up at the sound of her step.

“Well, that seems to make it pretty easy for us, doesn’t it?” he said. “It solves the problem of ways and means.”

“But this is awful. This alters everything. It isn’t safe for you to stay here. You must go away at once. They’ve found you out. You may be arrested at any moment.”

“That’s a side-issue. The main point is to put this thing through. Then we can think about what is going to happen to me.”

===Chinese===

「盗亦有道。」

「什么盗不盗的。这不过是桩正派的买卖。我手里有值钱的货色要出手，再白白送人我就见鬼了。我从前太好说话。这招早该想到。行，那就这么定了。接下来看你的了。余下的事儿，你自己琢磨去。」

他把自己从椅子里挣出来，出屋去了。安从楼廊上下来，见吉米正出神。听见她的脚步声，他抬起头。

「喏，这一来咱们的路子可就宽了，是不是？」他说。「路数和本钱的问题全解决了。」

「可这糟透了。这一下全变了。你再待在这儿就不安全了。你必须马上走。他们把你识破了。你随时可能被抓。」

「那是枝节。要点是把这事办成。办成之后，再来操心我会怎么样。」

===Original===

“But can’t you see the risk you’re running?”

“I don’t mind. I want to help you.”

“I won’t let you.”

“You must.”

“But do be sensible. What would you think of me if I allowed you to face this danger﻿—?”

“I wouldn’t think any differently of you. My opinion of you is a fixed thing. Nothing can alter it. I tried to tell you on the boat, but you wouldn’t let me. I think you’re the most perfect, wonderful girl in all the world. I’ve loved you since the first moment I saw you. I knew who you were when we met for half a minute that day in London. We were utter strangers, but I knew you. You were the girl I had been looking for all my life. Good Heavens, you talk of risks. Can’t you understand that just being with you and speaking to you and knowing that we share this thing together is enough to wipe out any thought of risk? I’d do anything for you. And you expect me to back out of this thing because there is a certain amount of danger!”

===Chinese===

「可你难道看不见自己在冒的险？」

「我不在乎。我要帮你。」

「我不许你帮。」

「你非让不可。」

「可你讲点道理行不行。要是我眼睁睁由你去冒这种险——你会把我看成什么人？」

「我对你的看法一丝一毫也不会变。我对你的看法是铁定的，什么都改不了它。船上我就想跟你说，是你不许我说。我认为你是全世界最完美、最奇妙的姑娘。打见你的头一眼起，我就爱上你了。那天在伦敦，我们不过照面半分钟，我就认出了你是谁。我们素昧平生，可我认得你。你正是我寻了一辈子的那个姑娘。老天爷，你还谈什么风险。你难道不明白，光是陪在你身边、跟你说话、知道你我共担着这桩事，就足以把一切风险之念冲刷干净？为你我什么都干得出来。你倒指望我因为区区几分危险就临阵脱逃！」

===Original===

Ann had retreated to the door, and was looking at him with wide eyes. With other young men﻿—and there had been many﻿—who had said much the same sort of thing to her since her débutante days she had been cool and composed﻿—a little sorry, perhaps, but in no doubt as to her own feelings and her ability to resist their pleadings. But now her heart was racing, and the conviction had begun to steal over her that the cool and composed Ann Chester was in imminent danger of making a fool of herself. Quite suddenly, without any sort of warning, she realised that there was some quality in Jimmy which called aloud to some corresponding quality in herself﻿—a nebulous something that made her know that he and she were mates. She knew herself hard to please where men were concerned. She could not have described what it was in her that all the men she had met, the men with whom she had golfed and ridden and yachted, had failed to satisfy: but, ever since she had acquired the power of self-analysis, she had known that it was something which was a solid and indestructible part of her composition. She could not have put into words what quality she demanded in man, but she had always known that she would recognise it when she found it: and she recognised it now in Jimmy. It was a recklessness, an irresponsibility, a cheerful daredevilry, the complement to her own gay lawlessness.

“Ann!” said Jimmy.

“It’s too late!”

She had not meant to say that. She had meant to say that it was impossible, out of the question. But her heart was running away with her, goaded on by the irony of it all. A veil seemed to have fallen from before her eyes, and she knew now why she had been drawn to Jimmy from the very first. They were mates, and she had thrown away her happiness.

“I’ve promised to marry Lord Wisbeach!”

Jimmy stopped dead, as if the blow had been a physical one.

===Chinese===

安已退到门边，睁大了眼睛望着他。换了别的年轻人——这样的人多得很——自打她初入社交界，对她说过大同小异痴话的，她一律冷静自持：或许略带歉意，但对自己的心意、对自己抵御央求的定力，从无半点怀疑。可这会儿她的心怦怦狂奔，而且一种信念开始悄悄爬上心头：那个冷静自持的安·切斯特，正濒临出乖露丑的边缘。全然突然地，毫无半点预兆，她意识到吉米身上有某种特质，正高声呼唤着她自身某种相应的特质——一种云雾般说不清的东西，让她确知他与她是同道。她深知自己在男人一事上挑剔。所有她结识过的男人——同她打过高尔夫、骑过马、驾过游艇的那些人——究竟没能满足她心里的哪一样，她说不上来：但自打她有了自省的能力，她便知道，那是她天性中一块结实、砸不烂的组成部分。她要男人身上具备怎样的品性，她无从诉诸言辞，可她一直知道，一旦遇上便自会认得：而她此刻在吉米身上认出来了。那是一股不管不顾的莽劲、一种不负责任的洒脱、一副乐呵呵的亡命气，恰与她那份快活的不法天性互为表里。

「安！」吉米唤道。

「太迟了！」

她本不打算说这句话的。她本打算说的是：不可能，免谈。可她的心不听使唤地驮着她狂奔，被这一切的造化弄人鞭赶着。仿佛一层帷幕从她眼前揭落，她此刻明白了，为什么她从一开始便被吉米吸引。他们原是同道，而她亲手抛掉了自己的幸福。

「我已答应了要嫁给威斯比奇勋爵！」

吉米猛地僵住，仿佛这一记真落在了肉身上。

===Original===

“You’ve promised to marry Lord Wisbeach!”

“Yes.”

“But﻿—but when?”

“Just now. Only a few minutes ago. When I was driving him to his hotel. He had asked me to marry him before I left for England, and I had promised to give him his answer when I got back. But when I got back, somehow I couldn’t make up my mind. The days slipped by. Something seemed to be holding me back. He pressed me to say that I would marry him, and it seemed absurd to go on refusing to be definite, so I said I would.”

“You can’t love him? Surely you don’t﻿—?”

Ann met his gaze frankly.

===Chinese===

「你答应了要嫁给威斯比奇勋爵！」

「是的。」

「可——可是什么时候？」

「刚刚。几分钟前的事。我开车送他回饭店的路上。我动身去英格兰之前他就求过婚，我答应回来后给他答复。可回来之后，不知怎么，我下不了决心。日子一天天溜过去，总觉得有什么在拽着我。他催我给他一句准话，再含糊下去未免可笑，我就说，我愿意。」

「你不可能是爱他的？你该不至于——？」

安坦然迎住他的目光。

===Original===

“Something seems to have happened to me in the last few minutes,” she said, “and I can’t think clearly. A little while ago it didn’t seem to matter much. I liked him. He was good-looking and good-tempered. I felt that we should get along quite well and be as happy as most people are. That seemed as near perfection as one could expect to get nowadays, so﻿—well, that’s how it was.”

“But you can’t marry him! It’s out of the question!”

“I’ve promised.”

“You must break your promise.”

“I can’t do that.”

“You must!”

===Chinese===

「这几分钟里，我像是出了什么毛病，」她说，「脑子清醒不了。方才还觉得这事无甚要紧。我喜欢他。他相貌堂堂，脾气又好。我觉得我们会处得相当不错，会跟大多数人一样快活。如今这年头，能指望的完美，大约也就到这份上了，所以——喏，就这么回事。」

「可你不能嫁给他！万万不能！」

「我答应了。」

「你必须食言。」

「那不行。」

「必须！」

===Original===

“I can’t. One must play the game.”

Jimmy groped for words. “But in this case﻿—you mustn’t﻿—it’s awful﻿—in this special case﻿—” He broke off. He saw the trap he was in. He could not denounce that crook without exposing himself. And from that he still shrank. Ann’s prejudice against Jimmy Crocker might have its root in a trivial and absurd grievance, but it had been growing through the years, and who could say how strong it was now?

Ann came a step towards him, then paused doubtfully. Then, as if making up her mind, she drew near and touched his sleeve.

“I’m sorry,” she said.

There was a silence.

“I’m sorry!”

===Chinese===

「不行。做人得讲规矩。」

吉米摸索着词句。「可这一回——你不能——这太可怕了——偏偏在这一回——」他顿住了。他看清了自己身陷的圈套。要告发那骗子，就得先暴露自己。这一步他仍旧缩着不敢迈。安对吉米·克罗克的偏见，根子上或许是桩琐碎又荒唐的怨隙，可它滋长了这许多年，如今已多牢靠，谁说得准？

安朝他迈了一步，又迟疑地停住。随即，仿佛下了决心，她走近前来，碰了碰他的衣袖。

「对不起。」她说。

一阵沉默。

「对不起！」

===Original===

She moved away. The door closed softly behind her. Jimmy scarcely knew that she had gone. He sat down in that deep chair which was Mr. Pett’s favourite, and stared sightlessly at the ceiling. And then, how many minutes or hours later he did not know, the sharp click of the door-handle roused him. He sprang from the chair. Was it Ann, come back?

It was not Ann. Round the edge of the door came inquiringly the fair head of Lord Wisbeach.

“Oh!” said his lordship, sighting Jimmy.

The head withdrew itself.

“Come here!” shouted Jimmy.

The head appeared again.

===Chinese===

她走开了。门在她身后轻轻合上。吉米几乎没意识到她走了。他一屁股坐进佩特先生最心爱的那把深椅，两眼发直地瞪着天花板。然后——过了几分钟还是几个钟头他不知道——门把手清脆的一响惊醒了他。他从椅子里弹起来。是安回来了吗？

不是安。门边探进来一颗金发脑袋，满脸探询——正是威斯比奇勋爵的。

「哦！」勋爵大人瞥见吉米，出了一声。

那颗脑袋缩了回去。

「过来！」吉米喝道。

那颗脑袋又出现了。

===Original===

“Talking to me?”

“Yes, I was talking to you.”

Lord Wisbeach followed his superstructure into the room. He was outwardly all that was bland and unperturbed, but there was a wary look in the eye that cocked itself at Jimmy, and he did not move far from the door. His fingers rested easily on the handle behind him. He did not think it probable that Jimmy could have heard of his visit to Mrs. Pett, but there had been something menacing in the latter’s voice, and he believed in safety first.

“They told me Miss Chester was here,” he said by way of relaxing any possible strain there might be in the situation.

“And what the devil do you want with Miss Chester, you slimy, crawling second-story-worker, you damned, oily yegg?” enquired Jimmy.

The sunniest optimist could not have deluded himself into the belief that the words were spoken in a friendly and genial spirit. Lord Wisbeach’s fingers tightened on the door-handle, and he grew a little flushed about the cheekbones.

===Chinese===

「跟我说话？」

「对，就是跟你说话。」

随后，威斯比奇勋爵的其余上层建筑跟着那颗脑袋进了屋。他外表一派和气从容，可那只朝吉米斜睨过来的眼睛里带着戒备，而且他没有远离门口。他的手指虚搭在身后的门把手上。他觉得吉米不至于已经听说他去见过佩特太太，可方才对方嗓音里有些威吓的意味，而他信奉安全第一。

「他们告诉我切斯特小姐在这儿。」他说，权且给局面里可能绷着的火气松松弦。

「那你想拿切斯特小姐怎么样，你这个黏糊糊、贴地爬行的翻窗贼，你这个该死的、满身油滑的撬柜惯偷？」吉米质问道。

最阳光的乐天派也无法骗自己相信，这些话是怀着友善亲热的精神说出来的。威斯比奇勋爵的手指在门把手上扣紧了，颧骨附近泛起些微红。

===Original===

“What’s all this about?” he said.

“You infernal crook!”

Lord Wisbeach looked anxious.

“Don’t shout like that! Are you crazy? Do you want people to hear?”

Jimmy drew a deep breath.

“I shall have to get further away from you,” he said more quietly. “There’s no knowing what may happen if I don’t. I don’t want to kill you. At least, I do, but I had better not.”

===Chinese===

「这是唱的哪一出？」他说。

「你这个地狱里爬出来的骗子！」

威斯比奇勋爵面露忧色。

「别这么嚷嚷！你疯了吗？想叫人听见？」

吉米深深吸了一口气。

「我得离你再远点儿，」他的口气平静了些。「不然难保不出什么事。我不想杀你。至少——是想杀的，可最好还是别。」

===Original===

He retired slowly until brought to a halt by the writing-desk. To this he anchored himself with a firm grip. He was extremely anxious to do nothing rash, and the spectacle of Gentleman Jack invited rashness. He leaned against the desk, clutching its solidity with both hands. Lord Wisbeach held steadfastly to the door-handle. And in this tense fashion the interview proceeded.

“Miss Chester,” said Jimmy, forcing himself to speak calmly, “has just been telling me that she has promised to marry you.”

“Quite true,” said Lord Wisbeach. “It will be announced tomorrow.” A remark trembled on his lips, to the effect that he relied on Jimmy for a fish-slice, but prudence kept it unspoken. He was unable at present to understand Jimmy’s emotion. Why Jimmy should object to his being engaged to Ann, he could not imagine. But it was plain that for some reason he had taken the thing to heart, and, dearly as he loved a bit of quiet fun, Lord Wisbeach decided that the other was at least six inches too tall and fifty pounds too heavy to be bantered in his present mood by one of his own physique. “Why not?”

“It won’t be announced tomorrow,” said Jimmy. “Because by tomorrow you will be as far away from here as you can get, if you have any sense.”

“What do you mean?”

“Just this. If you haven’t left this house by breakfast time tomorrow, I shall expose you.”

===Chinese===

他徐徐后撤，直到被写字台挡住去路。他一把牢牢抓住台沿，抛锚定位。他极力想不干莽撞事，可「绅士杰克」那副尊容本身就在招引人莽撞。他倚着写字台，双手死死攥住它的坚实。威斯比奇勋爵则寸步不让地把守着门把手。会谈便在这般剑拔弩张的态势下继续进行。

「切斯特小姐，」吉米强自镇定地说，「方才告诉我，她已经答应了嫁给你。」

「千真万确，」威斯比奇勋爵说。「明天就要宣布了。」一句话在他唇边打颤，大意是婚事既成，上鱼刀之类的馈赠就指望吉米了，但谨慎使它没有出口。他眼下无法理解吉米的激动。吉米凭什么反对他与安订婚，他想象不出。但对方显然不知为何把这事上了心，而威斯比奇勋爵虽说顶爱来点不动声色的打趣，也断定：眼前这位起码比可打趣的高度高出六英寸、体重多出五十磅，以他自己这副身板，趁对方这副心绪去逗弄，未免不自量力。「有何不可？」

「明天宣布不了，」吉米说。「因为到了明天，只要你还有半点脑子，你早逃到天边去了。」

「你什么意思？」

「就这个意思。明天早饭时分你要还没滚出这宅子，我就揭你的老底。」

===Original===

Lord Wisbeach was not feeling particularly happy, but he laughed at this.

“You!”

“That’s what I said.”

“Who do you think you are, to go about exposing people?”

“I happen to be Mrs. Pett’s nephew, Jimmy Crocker.”

Lord Wisbeach laughed again.

===Chinese===

威斯比奇勋爵此刻心情算不得舒畅，可他闻言笑了。

「就凭你！」

「我说的是就凭我。」

「你以为你是谁，满世界揭人的底？」

「我恰好就是佩特太太的外甥，吉米·克罗克。」

威斯比奇勋爵又笑了。

===Original===

“Is that the line you are going to take?”

“It is.”

“You are going to Mrs. Pett to tell her that you are Jimmy Crocker and that I am a crook and that you only pretended to recognise me for reasons of your own?”

“Just that.”

“Forget it!” Lord Wisbeach had forgotten to be alarmed in his amusement. He smiled broadly. “I’m not saying it’s not good stuff to pull, but it’s old stuff now. I’m sorry for you, but I thought of it before you did. I went to Mrs. Pett directly after lunch and sprang that line of talk myself. Do you think she’ll believe you after that? I tell you I’m ace-high with that dame. You can’t queer me with her.”

“I think I can. For the simple reason that I really am Jimmy Crocker.”

===Chinese===

「你打算走这步棋？」

「正是。」

「你要去找佩特太太，告诉她你是吉米·克罗克、我是个骗子、你先前认出我纯属别有用心装装样子？」

「正是此意。」

「死了这条心吧！」威斯比奇勋爵乐得连惊慌都忘了。他咧嘴大笑。「我不是说这招拿出去不响，可它如今是旧货了。我替你惋惜，不过这条道我比你先想到了。午饭后我径直去找了佩特太太，亲自把这套说辞抖了个干净。你觉得那以后她还会信你？告诉你，我在那位太太跟前红得发紫。你拆不了我的台。」

「我看拆得动。道理简单得很：我真是吉米·克罗克。」

===Original===

“Yes, you are.”

“Exactly. Yes, I am.”

Lord Wisbeach smiled tolerantly.

“It was worth trying the bluff, I guess, but it won’t work. I know you’d be glad to get me out of this house, but you’ve got to make a better play than that to do it.”

“Don’t deceive yourself with the idea that I’m bluffing. Look here.” He suddenly removed his coat and threw it to Lord Wisbeach. “Read the tailor’s label inside the pocket. See the name. Also the address. ‘J. Crocker. Drexdale House. Grosvenor Square. London.’ ”

Lord Wisbeach picked up the garment and looked as directed. His face turned a little sallower, but he still fought against his growing conviction.

===Chinese===

「是啊，你真是。」

「一点儿不错。我是真的。」

威斯比奇勋爵宽宏地一笑。

「虚张声势，值得一试，可它不灵。我知道你巴不得把我轰出这宅子，但要轰成，你还得拿出高明点儿的招来。」

「别自欺欺人，当我是在唬人。看这儿。」他猛地脱下上衣，掷给威斯比奇勋爵。「念念口袋里衬的裁缝标签。看名字。还有地址。『J. 克罗克。伦敦，格罗夫纳广场，德雷斯代尔府。』」

威斯比奇勋爵拾起衣裳，照指示查验。他的脸色蜡黄了几分，但仍在与心里渐长的确信抗争。

===Original===

“That’s no proof.”

“Perhaps not. But, when you consider the reputation of the tailor whose name is on the label, it’s hardly likely that he would be standing in with an impostor, is it? If you want real proof, I have no doubt that there are half a dozen men working on the *Chronicle* who can identify me. Or are you convinced already?”

Lord Wisbeach capitulated.

“I don’t know what fool game you think you’re playing, but I can’t see why you couldn’t have told me this when we were talking after lunch.”

“Never mind. I had my reasons. They don’t matter. What matters is that you are going to get out of here tomorrow. Do you understand that?”

“I get you.”

===Chinese===

「这不算证据。」

「也许不算。可你想想标签上那位裁缝的名声，他会给一个冒牌货做衣裳吗？你要真凭实据，我毫不怀疑，《纪事报》馆里少说有五六个能指认我的人。怎么，这你还不信？」

威斯比奇勋爵缴械了。

「不晓得你自以为在玩什么昏招，可我不明白，午饭后咱们说话那阵，你怎么不把这事告诉我。」

「不提了。我有我的道理。那不重要。重要的是，你明天就得从这儿滚蛋。明白吗？」

「明白。」

===Original===

“Then that’s about all, I think. Don’t let me keep you.”

“Say, listen.” Gentleman Jack’s voice was plaintive. “I think you might give a fellow a chance to get out good. Give me time to have a guy in Montreal send me a telegram telling me to go up there right away. Otherwise you might just as well put the cops on me at once. The old lady knows I’ve got business in Canada. You don’t need to be rough on a fellow.”

Jimmy pondered this point.

“All right. I don’t object to that.”

“Thanks.”

“Don’t start anything, though.”

===Chinese===

「那么我想，就这些了。不多留你了。」

「喂，听我说。」「绅士杰克」的嗓音近乎哀恳。「我看你总得给个同行体面退场的机会。给我点时间，让蒙特利尔（Montreal）的一个哥们儿拍封电报来，叫我即刻北上。不然你还不如干脆这就叫警察来抓我。老太太知道我在加拿大有一摊子事。犯不上这么难为人。」

吉米思忖了一下这个要求。

「行。这个我不反对。」

「谢了。」

「不过，别耍花样。」

===Original===

“I don’t know what you mean.”

Jimmy pointed to the safe.

“Come, come, friend of my youth. We have no secrets from each other. I know you’re after what’s in there, and you know that I know. I don’t want to harp on it, but you’ll be spending tonight in the house, and I think you had better make up your mind to spend it in your room, getting a nice sleep to prepare you for your journey. Do you follow me, old friend?”

“I get you.”

“That will be all then, I think. Wind a smile around your neck and recede.”

The door slammed. Lord Wisbeach had restrained his feelings successfully during the interview, but he could not deny himself that slight expression of them. Jimmy crossed the room and took his coat from the chair where the other had dropped it. As he did so a voice spoke.

===Chinese===

「不懂你什么意思。」

吉米指了指保险柜。

「得了得了，我青年时代的朋友。你我之间没有秘密。我知道你惦记的是那里面的东西，你也知道我知道。我不想旧调重弹，可你今夜总还得在这宅子里过，我劝你拿定主意，就在自己房里过，美美睡上一觉，好为行程养足精神。听懂了吗，老朋友？」

「懂了。」

「那么我想，就到这里。脸上缠一副笑容，退下吧。」

门砰然关上。面谈全程，威斯比奇勋爵一直克制得体，但这一点点情绪的宣泄，他还是不肯亏待自己的。

===Original===

“Say!”

Jimmy spun round. The room was apparently empty. The thing was beginning to assume an uncanny aspect, when the voice spoke again.

“You think you’re darned funny, don’t you?”

It came from above. Jimmy had forgotten the gallery. He directed his gaze thither, and perceived the heavy face of Ogden hanging over the rail like a gargoyle.

“What are you doing there?” he demanded.

“Listening.”

===Chinese===

「喂！」

吉米霍然转身。屋里明明空无一人。事情正开始显出鬼气森森的模样，那声音又开口了。

「你自以为挺逗的吧？」

声音来自头顶。吉米把楼廊忘了个干净。他把目光投上去，看见奥格登那张沉甸甸的脸悬在栏杆上，活像一尊滴水石兽。

「你在那儿干什么？」他质问。

「偷听。」

===Original===

“How did you get there?”

“There’s a door back here that you get to from the stairs. I often come here for a quiet cigarette. Say, you think yourself some josher, don’t you, telling me you were a kidnapper! You strung me like an onion. So you’re really Jimmy Crocker after all? Where was the sense in pulling all that stuff about taking me away and divvying up the ransom? Aw, you make me tired!”

The head was withdrawn, and Jimmy heard heavy steps followed by the banging of a door. Peace reigned in the library.

Jimmy sat down in the chair which was Mr. Pett’s favourite and which Ogden was accustomed to occupy to that gentleman’s displeasure. The swiftness of recent events had left him a little dizzy, and he desired to think matters over and find out exactly what had happened.

The only point which appeared absolutely clear to him in a welter of confusing occurrences was the fact that he had lost the chance of kidnapping Ogden. Everything had arranged itself so beautifully simply and conveniently as regarded that venture until a moment ago; but now that the boy had discovered his identity it was impossible for him to attempt it. He was loth to accept this fact. Surely, even now, there was a way.﻿ ﻿…

Quite suddenly an admirable plan occurred to him. It involved the cooperation of his father. And at that thought he realised with a start that life had been moving so rapidly for him since his return to the house that he had not paid any attention at all to what was really as amazing a mystery as any. He had been too busy to wonder why his father was there.

===Chinese===

「你怎么上去的？」

「这后头有扇门，打楼梯就能上来。我常来这儿清净地抽根烟。喂，你自以为挺能耍宝的吧，居然跟我说你是绑匪！你把我当洋葱串着耍。闹了半天你真是吉米·克罗克？那编什么带走我、分赎金的鬼话，图什么呢？唉，你真让我反胃！」

那颗脑袋缩了回去，吉米听见沉重的脚步声，接着是一扇门的巨响。书房里恢复了太平。

吉米在佩特先生最心爱的那把椅子上坐了下来——奥格登惯常占用它，每每害那位绅士不痛快。接二连三的变故来得飞快，把他弄得有些头晕，他要把事情从头理一遍，弄清楚究竟出了什么。

在一团乱麻般的变故里，唯有一点在他看来绝对分明：他绑走奥格登的机会黄了。直到方才为止，那桩营生方方面面都安排得又漂亮、又简单、又顺手；可如今那小子识破了他的身份，再想动手万万不能了。他不甘心认这个账。就算到了这般田地，总还有条路吧……

蓦地，一条妙计撞进他脑海。这计策需要他父亲从旁协力。这么一想他猛然惊觉：自打回到这宅子，日子过得风驰电掣，他竟对一桩其实再离奇不过的谜团不闻不问——他一直忙得没空琢磨，他父亲好端端为何在此。

===Original===

He debated the best method of getting in touch with him. It was out of the question to descend to the pantry or wherever it was that his father lived in this new incarnation of his. Then the happy thought struck him that results might be obtained by the simple process of ringing the bell. It might produce some other unit of the domestic staff. However, it was worth trying. He rang the bell.

A few moments later the door opened. Jimmy looked up. It was not his father. It was a dangerous-looking female of uncertain age, dressed as a parlourmaid, who eyed him with what seemed to his conscience-stricken soul dislike and suspicion. She had a tight-lipped mouth and beady eyes beneath heavy brows. Jimmy had seldom seen a woman who attracted him less at first sight.

“Jer ring, s’?”

Jimmy blinked and almost ducked. The words had come at him like a projectile.

“Oh, ah, yes.”

“J’ want anything, s’?”

===Chinese===

他盘算着怎样同父亲接上头最妥。径直下到配膳室去——或他父亲这副新化身所栖身的随便什么去处——是万万不行的。随后他灵机一动：只消摇铃，说不定便能如愿。应铃而出的，或许是仆役班子的其他一员。不过，值得一试。他摇了铃。

片刻之后，门开了。吉米抬头一看。来的不是他父亲。是个年纪莫测、模样危险的女子，一身客厅侍女装束，正用一种——在他那问心有愧的灵魂看来——又嫌恶又狐疑的目光打量他。她嘴唇紧抿，浓眉底下一双豆子般的小眼睛。头一眼就这么不招人喜欢的女人，吉米生平少见。

「按铃了，先？」

吉米一眨眼，几乎缩了下脖子。那几个字像发炮弹似的朝他飞来。

「哦，呃，是。」

「要啥，先？」

===Original===

With an effort Jimmy induced his mind to resume its interrupted equilibrium.

“Oh, ah, yes. Would you mind sending Skinner the butler to me.”

“Y’s’r.”

The apparition vanished. Jimmy drew out his handkerchief and dabbed at his forehead. He felt weak and guilty. He felt as if he had just been accused of nameless crimes and had been unable to deny the charge. Such was the magic of Miss Trimble’s eye﻿—the left one, which looked directly at its object. Conjecture pauses baffled at the thought of the effect which her gaze might have created in the breasts of the sex she despised, had it been double instead of single-barrelled. But half of it had wasted itself on a spot some few feet to his right.

Presently the door opened again, and Mr. Crocker appeared, looking like a benevolent priest.


===Chinese===

吉米费了些力气，才说服自己的心智恢复被搅断的平衡。

「哦，呃，是。劳驾，请让管家斯金纳到我这儿来一趟。」

「是，先。」

那幽灵消失了。吉米掏出手帕，按了按额头。他觉得虚弱，而且心虚。仿佛方才有人控告了他种种不可名状的罪名，而他无从辩驳。这便是特琳布尔小姐（Miss Trimble）目光的魔力——左眼那只，直射目标的那只。她那目光若是双管齐发，而非单管射击，在她所不齿的男性胸膛里会激起何等波澜，凭猜想只能知难而退。不过那魔力的一半，已在他右手边几英尺开外的一处虚空里白白耗掉了。

不多时，门又开了，克罗克先生走了进来，一副慈眉善目的教士模样。

## XIX / 十九

Between Father and Son / 父子之间

===Original===

“Well, Skinner, my man,” said Jimmy, “how goes it?”

Mr. Crocker looked about him cautiously. Then his priestly manner fell from him like a robe, and he bounded forward.

“Jimmy!” he exclaimed, seizing his son’s hand and shaking it violently. “Say, it’s great seeing you again, Jim!”

Jimmy drew himself up haughtily.

“Skinner, my good menial, you forget yourself strangely! You will be getting fired if you mitt the handsome guest in this chummy fashion!” He slapped his father on the back. “Dad, this is great! How on earth do you come to be here? What’s the idea? Why the buttling? When did you come over? Tell me all!”

Mr. Crocker hoisted himself nimbly onto the writing-desk, and sat there, beaming, with dangling legs.

===Chinese===

「喂，斯金纳，我的好伙计，」吉米说，「日子过得怎么样啊？」

克罗克先生小心翼翼地朝四下里张望了一圈。随后，他那一身管家祭司般的威仪像一袭长袍似的从他身上滑落，他一个箭步蹿上前去。

「吉米！」他嚷道，一把攥住儿子的手，使劲摇晃。「哎呀，又见到你真是太好啦，吉姆！」

吉米傲然挺直了身子。

「斯金纳，我的好奴仆，你可真是忘乎所以得离奇！要是你跟这位英俊的客人这么亲热地动拳碰掌，可就要被辞退啦！」他拍了拍父亲的背。「爸，这可真妙！你到底是怎么跑到这儿来的？打的什么主意？怎么当起管家来了？什么时候渡海过来的？一五一十全告诉我！」

克罗克先生轻捷地一撑，坐上了写字台，两条腿悬空晃着，满面红光。

===Original===

“It was your letter that did it, Jimmy. Say, Jim, there wasn’t any need for you to do a thing like that just for me.”

“Well, I thought you would have a better chance of being a peer without me around. By the way, dad, how did my stepmother take the Lord Percy episode?”

A shadow fell upon Mr. Crocker’s happy face.

“I don’t like to do much thinking about your stepmother,” he said. “She was pretty sore about Percy. And she was pretty sore about your lighting out for America. But, gee! what she must be feeling like now that I’ve come over, I daren’t let myself think.”

“You haven’t explained that yet. Why did you come over?”

===Chinese===

「全是你那封信闹出来的事，吉米。哎，吉姆，你犯不着光为了我干这种事。」

「呃，我想没有我在跟前碍眼，你当贵族的机会总要大些。对了，爸，我那继母对珀西勋爵那桩公案作何感想？」

一片阴云掠上了克罗克先生那张欢喜的脸。

「关于你继母，我不愿意多想，」他说，「珀西那件事，她已经气得够呛。你拔腿溜到美国来，她又气得够呛。可是，老天爷！如今连我也跑了过来，她心里不定成了什么滋味，我可不敢让自己去想。」

「这个你还没交代呢。你到底为什么要过来？」

===Original===

“Well, I’d been feeling homesick﻿—I always do over there in the baseball season﻿—and then talking with Pett made it worse﻿—”

“Talking with Pett? Did you see him, then, when he was in London?”

“See him? I let him in!”

“How?”

“Into the house, I mean. I had just gone to the front door to see what sort of a day it was﻿—I wanted to know if there had been enough rain in the night to stop my having to watch that cricket game﻿—and just as I got there the bell rang. I opened the door.”

“A revoltingly plebeian thing to do! I’m ashamed of you, dad! They won’t stand for that sort of thing in the House of Lords!”

===Chinese===

「呃，我一直害着思乡病——每逢棒球季，我在那边总是这样——后来又跟佩特聊了一通，病势越发沉重——」

「跟佩特聊了一通？这么说，他在伦敦那阵子你见到他了？」

「见到他？是我放他进的门！」

「怎么讲？」

「我是说，放他进屋。我当时刚走到前门口，想看看天气如何——我想知道夜里雨下得够不够大，能不能免了我去看那场板球赛——我刚走到门口，门铃就响了。我便开了门。」

「这事儿干得可真是俗不可耐！我都替你臊得慌，爸！上议院（House of Lords）里可容不下这种行径！」

===Original===

“Well, before I knew what was happening they had taken me for the butler. I didn’t want your stepmother to know I’d been opening doors﻿—you remember how touchy she was always about it so I just let it go at that and jollied them along. But I just couldn’t help asking the old man how the pennant race was making out, and that tickled him so much that he offered me a job here as butler if I ever wanted to make a change. And then your note came saying that you were going to New York, and﻿—well, I couldn’t help myself. You couldn’t have kept me in London with ropes. I sneaked out next day and bought a passage on the *Carmantic*﻿—she sailed the Wednesday after you left﻿—and came straight here. They gave me this job right away.” Mr. Crocker paused, and a holy light of enthusiasm made his homely features almost beautiful. “Say, Jim, I’ve seen a ballgame every darned day since I landed! Say, two days running Larry Doyle made home-runs! But, gosh! that guy Klem is one swell robber! See here!” Mr. Crocker sprang down from the desk, and snatched up a handful of books, which he proceeded to distribute about the floor. “There were two men on bases in the sixth and What’s-his-name came to bat. He lined one out to centre-field﻿—where this book is﻿—and﻿—”

“Pull yourself together, Skinner! You can’t monkey about with the employer’s library like that.” Jimmy restored the books to their places. “Simmer down and tell me more. Postpone the gossip from the diamond. What plans have you made? Have you considered the future at all? You aren’t going to hold down this buttling job forever, are you? When do you go back to London?”

The light died out of Mr. Crocker’s face.

===Chinese===

「唉，我还没弄明白是怎么回事，他们已经把我当成管家了。我可不想让你继母知道我开过门——你总记得，她一向为这个多么动气——所以我就将错就错，顺着他们打哈哈。可我到底没憋住，问了老佩特一句锦旗赛进行得怎么样。这话把他逗得那么开心，他当场拍板：哪天我想换个东家，就上这儿来当管家。接着你的信到了，说你就要来纽约——这下，我实在把持不住了。你就是拿绳子也休想把我拴在伦敦。第二天我悄悄溜出门，买了张*卡曼提克号*（Carmantic）的船票——它在你动身后那个星期三启的航——然后径直奔了这儿。他们二话不说就把这份差事给了我。」克罗克先生顿了顿，一股神圣的狂热之光映亮了他那其貌不扬的眉眼，几乎使它们俊美起来。「嘿，吉姆，自打上岸，我天天都看一场球赛，一天没落！嘿，拉里·多伊尔（Larry Doyle）连着两天打出本垒打！可是，天哪！克莱姆（Klem）那家伙真是个头号大盗！你瞧！」克罗克先生从写字台上一跃而下，抓起一把书，便朝地板四下里分发起来。「第六局两人上垒，那个谁谁谁上场打击。他打出一记平直球，直飞中外野——就是搁这本书的地方——接着——」

「振作起来，斯金纳！主人家的书房可不许你这么折腾。」吉米把书归回原位。「冷静冷静，再跟我往下说。球场上那些闲话先搁一搁。你作了什么打算？到底考虑过将来没有？这份管家的差事你总不至于干一辈子吧？什么时候回伦敦？」

那道光芒从克罗克先生脸上熄灭了。

===Original===

“I guess I shall have to go back some time. But how can I yet, with the Giants leading the league like this?”

“But did you just light out without saying anything?”

“I left a note for your stepmother telling her I had gone to America for a vacation. Jimmy, I hate to think what she’s going to do to me when she gets me back!”

“Assert yourself, dad! Tell her that woman’s place is the home and man’s the ballpark! Be firm!”

Mr. Crocker shook his head dubiously.

“It’s all very well to talk that way when you’re three thousand miles from home, but you know as well as I do, Jim, that your stepmother, though she’s a delightful woman, isn’t the sort you can assert yourself with. Look at this sister of hers here. I guess you haven’t been in the house long enough to have noticed, but she’s very like Eugenia in some ways. She’s the boss all right, and old Pett does just what he’s told to. I guess it’s the same with me, Jim. There’s a certain type of man that’s just born to have it put over on him by a certain type of woman. I’m that sort of man and your stepmother’s that sort of woman. No, I guess I’m going to get mine all right, and the only thing to do is to keep it from stopping me having a good time now.”

===Chinese===

「我想迟早总得回去。可巨人队正这么领跑全联盟，眼下我怎么走得出？」

「可你就这么一声不吭地溜了？」

「我给你继母留了张条子，说我到美国度假去了。吉米，一想到等她把我弄回去之后要怎么发落我，我就不寒而栗！」

「拿出丈夫气概来，爸！告诉她：女人安身的地方是家里，男人安身的地方是球场！坚决一点！」

克罗克先生将信将疑地摇了摇头。

「离家三千里的时候，说说这种漂亮话自然容易。可你跟我一样清楚，吉姆，你那位继母虽说是个讨人喜欢的女人，却不是那种你能硬气得起来的对象。瞧瞧她这位妹妹吧。我猜你在这宅子里住得还短，没来得及看出来，她有些地方跟尤金妮娅像极了。她才是当家的没错，老佩特让干什么就干什么。我想我这情形也差不多，吉姆。世上有一种男人，天生就注定要吃某一种女人的亏。我正是那种男人，你继母正是那种女人。唉，到头来我那一份横竖是逃不掉的，眼下唯一的办法，就是别让它搅了我眼前的快活日子。」

===Original===

There was truth in what he said, and Jimmy recognised it. He changed the subject.

“Well, never mind that. There’s no sense in worrying oneself about the future. Tell me, dad, where did you get all the ‘dinner-is-served, madam’ stuff? How did you ever learn to be a butler?”

“Bayliss taught me back in London. And, of course, I’ve played butlers when I was on the stage.”

Jimmy did not speak for a moment.

“Did you ever play a kidnapper, dad?” he asked at length.

===Chinese===

他这话不无道理，吉米也听出来了。他换了个话题。

「好了，那个不谈。为将来操心毫无意义。告诉我，爸，你那一套『晚饭备好了，夫人』的功夫是从哪儿学来的？你到底是怎么学会当管家的？」

「在伦敦的时候贝利斯教的。再说，我从前在台上也演过管家。」

吉米一时没有作声。

「爸，你演过绑匪吗？」他终于开口问道。

===Original===

“Sure. I was Chicago Ed. in a crook play called *This Way Out*. Why, surely you saw me in that? I got some good notices.”

Jimmy nodded.

“Of course. I knew I’d seen you play that sort of part some time. You came on during the dark scene and﻿—”

“﻿—switched on the lights and﻿—”

“﻿—covered the bunch with your gun while they were still blinking! You were great in that part, dad.”

===Chinese===

「当然演过。我在一出叫《由此出去》（This Way Out）的盗贼戏里演过芝加哥·埃德（Chicago Ed）。怎么，你肯定看过吧？我还得了几篇漂亮剧评呢。」

吉米点了点头。

「当然。我就说我什么时候看过你演这一路角色。你在暗场的时候上场，接着——」

「——把灯一开，再——」

「——乘他们还在眨眼的当儿，一杆枪把满屋子人都罩住了！那个角色你演得棒极了，爸。」

===Original===

“It was a good part,” said Mr. Crocker modestly. “It had fat. I’d like to have a chance to play a kidnapper again. There’s a lot of pep to kidnappers.”

“You *shall* play one again,” said Jimmy. “I am putting on a little sketch with a kidnapper as the star part.”

“Eh? A sketch? You, Jim? Where?”

“Here. In this house. It is entitled ‘Kidnapping Ogden’ and opens tonight.”

Mr. Crocker looked at his only son in concern. Jimmy appeared to him to be rambling.

===Chinese===

「那是个好角色，」克罗克先生谦虚地说，「油水足。我很想再有机会演一回绑匪。绑匪这个行当，来劲儿得很。」

「你*一定*会再演一回的，」吉米说。「我正在排一出小短剧，绑匪正是头牌角色。」

「嗯？短剧？你，吉姆？在哪儿排？」

「就在这儿。就在这座宅子里。剧名叫《绑走奥格登》，今晚开演。」

克罗克先生忧心忡忡地望着他的独生子。在他看来，吉米说话已经开始颠三倒四了。

===Original===

“Amateur theatricals?” he hazarded.

“In the sense that there is no pay for performing, yes. Dad, you know that kid Ogden upstairs? Well, it’s quite simple. I want you to kidnap him for me.”

Mr. Crocker sat down heavily. He shook his head.

“I don’t follow all this.”

“Of course not. I haven’t begun to explain. Dad, in your rambles through this joint you’ve noticed a girl with glorious red-gold hair, I imagine?”

“Ann Chester?”

===Chinese===

「业余戏剧演出？」他试探着说。

「就演出不拿薪水这一点而言，是的。爸，楼上那个小子奥格登你知道吧？呃，事情非常简单。我想请你替我把他绑走。」

克罗克先生沉重地坐了下去。他摇了摇头。

「这些我全跟不上趟。」

「那当然。我还没开始解释呢。爸，你在这座宅子里转悠的时候，想必见过一位长着一头华丽金红色头发的姑娘吧？」

「安·切斯特？」

===Original===

“Ann Chester. I’m going to marry her.”

“Jimmy!”

“But she doesn’t know it yet. Now, follow me carefully, dad. Five years ago Ann Chester wrote a book of poems. It’s on that desk there. You were using it a moment back as second-base or something. Now, I was working at that time on the *Chronicle*. I wrote a skit on those poems for the Sunday paper. Do you begin to follow the plot?”

“She’s got it in for you? She’s sore?”

“Exactly. Get that firmly fixed in your mind, because it’s the source from which all the rest of the story springs.”

===Chinese===

「安·切斯特。我要娶她。」

「吉米！」

「可她自己还不知道。现在，仔细听我说，爸。五年前，安·切斯特出版过一册诗集。就在那张书桌上——方才你还拿它当二垒包还是什么来着。当时我正在《纪事报》效力，我为星期日版写了一篇拿那些诗开涮的滑稽文章。剧情你开始看明白了没有？」

「她跟你记着仇？她气得不轻？」

「一点不错。把这一点牢牢钉死在脑子里，因为后面全部故事都发源于此。」

===Original===

Mr. Crocker interrupted.

“But I don’t understand. You say she’s sore at you. Well, how is it that you came in together looking as if you were good friends when I let you in this morning?”

“I was waiting for you to ask that. The explanation is that she doesn’t know that I am Jimmy Crocker.”

“But you came here saying that you were Jimmy Crocker.”

===Chinese===

克罗克先生插了嘴。

「可我不明白。你说她跟你怄气。那今早我放你们进门的时候，你们俩怎么会双双走进来，一副交情不错的朋友模样？」

「我正等你问这一句。解释是：她并不知道我就是吉米·克罗克。」

「可你来的时候，自己报的就是吉米·克罗克呀。」

===Original===

“Quite right. And that is where the plot thickens. I made Ann’s acquaintance first in London and then on the boat. I had found out that Jimmy Crocker was the man she hated most in the world, so I took another name. I called myself Bayliss.”

“Bayliss!”

“I had to think of something quick, because the clerk at the shipping office was waiting to fill in my ticket. I had just been talking to Bayliss on the phone and his was the only name that came into my mind. You know how it is when you try to think of a name suddenly. Now mark the sequel. Old Bayliss came to see me off at Paddington. Ann was there and saw me. She said ‘Good evening, Mr. Bayliss’ or something, and naturally old Bayliss replied ‘What ho!’ or words to that effect. The only way to handle the situation was to introduce him as my father. I did so. Ann, therefore, thinks that I am a young man named Bayliss who has come over to America to make his fortune. We now come to the third reel. I met Ann by chance at the Knickerbocker and took her to lunch. While we were lunching, that confirmed congenital idiot, Reggie Bartling, who happened to have come over to America as well, came up and called me by my name. I knew that, if Ann discovered who I really was, she would have nothing more to do with me, so I gave Reggie the haughty stare and told him that he had made a mistake. He ambled away﻿—and possibly committed suicide in his anguish at having made such a bloomer﻿—leaving Ann discussing with me the extraordinary coincidence of my being Jimmy Crocker’s double. Do you follow the story of my life so far?”

===Chinese===

「一点不错。而这正是剧情转浓的地方。我先是在伦敦认识安，后来又在船上遇见她。我当时已经查明，吉米·克罗克是她天下最恨的人，于是我便换了个名字，自称贝利斯。」

「贝利斯！」

「当时情况紧急，非得马上想出一个名字来，因为轮船公司办事处的职员正等着填我的船票，而我刚好在电话里跟贝利斯通过话，脑子里冒出来的就只有这一个名字。你也知道，突然之间要你想一个名字是什么滋味。且看下文。老贝利斯到帕丁顿车站来送我，安在场，看见了他。她说了句『晚上好，贝利斯先生』之类的话，老贝利斯自然回了句『嗬，你好！』之类的话。收拾这个局面只有一个办法：把他介绍成我的父亲。我便照办了。因此，安以为我是一个名叫贝利斯的青年，到美国来闯天下发家致富。现在我们进入第三本。我在尼克博克（the Knickerbocker）碰巧遇见安，请她吃了午饭。吃饭的当口，那个彻头彻尾的天生白痴雷吉·巴特林——他碰巧也到美国来了——走上前来，叫了我的名字。我知道，一旦安发现我的真实身份，她就再也不会理我了，于是我给了雷吉一记居高临下的冷眼，告诉他认错了人。他晃晃悠悠地走开了——说不定正因为闹了这么个大乌龙，正心如刀绞地寻了短见——留下安同我一起讨论我竟与吉米·克罗克长得一模一样这一惊人的巧合。我这段人生故事，听到这里你都跟上了吗？」

===Original===

Mr. Crocker, who had been listening with wrinkled brow and other signs of rapt attention, nodded.

“I understand all that. But how did you come to get into this house?”

“That is reel four. I am getting to that. It seems that Ann, who is the sweetest girl on earth and always on the lookout to do someone a kindness, had decided, in the interests of the boy’s future, to remove young Ogden Ford from his present sphere, where he is being spoiled and ruined, and send him down to a man on Long Island who would keep him for awhile and instil the first principles of decency into him. Her accomplice in this admirable scheme was Jerry Mitchell.”

“Jerry Mitchell!”

“Who, as you know, got fired yesterday. Jerry was to have done the rough work of the job. But, being fired, he was no longer available. I, therefore, offered to take his place. So here I am.”

===Chinese===

克罗克先生一直皱着眉头、带着全神贯注的其他种种迹象听着，这时点了点头。

「这些我都明白了。可你又是怎么进的这座宅子？」

「那是第四本。我正要说到。事情是这样的：安——她是天底下最可爱的姑娘，时时留神着要给人行个方便——已经拿定主意，为了这孩子的前途，要把小奥格登·福特从他目前那个环境里弄出去，因为在那儿他正被宠坏、毁掉；她要把他送到长岛一个人那儿住上一阵子，往他脑子里灌输一点体面做人的起码原则。她这项令人钦佩的计划里的同伙，正是杰里·米切尔。」

「杰里·米切尔！」

「就是那位，你总知道，昨天被辞退了的。粗活儿原本该由杰里来干。可他既然被辞了，就派不上用场了。于是我自告奋勇顶了他的缺。所以我就到了这儿。」

===Original===

“You’re going to kidnap that boy?”

“No. You are.”

“Me!”

===Chinese===

「你要去绑那个孩子？」

「不。是你要去。」

「我！」

===Original===

“Precisely. You are going to play a benefit performance of your world-famed success, Chicago Ed. Let me explain further. Owing to circumstances which I need not go into, Ogden has found out that I am really Jimmy Crocker, so he refuses to have anything more to do with me. I had deceived him into believing that I was a professional kidnapper, and he came to me and offered to let me kidnap him if I would go fifty-fifty with him in the ransom!”

“Gosh!”

“Yes, he’s an intelligent child, full of that sort of bright ideas. Well, now he has found that I am not all his fancy painted me, he wouldn’t come away with me; and I want you to understudy me while the going is good. In the fifth reel, which will be released tonight after the household has retired to rest, you will be featured. It’s got to be tonight, because it has just occurred to me that Ogden, knowing that Lord Wisbeach is a crook, may go to him with the same proposal that he made to me.”

===Chinese===

「正是。你将把你那誉满全球的成名角色芝加哥·埃德拿出来，加演一场义演。容我进一步解释。由于一些不便细说的变故，奥格登已经查明我其实就是吉米·克罗克，所以拒绝再跟我有任何来往。我原本骗得他相信我是个职业绑匪，他找上门来，主动提出：只要赎金跟他五五分账，就让我把他绑走！」

「天哪！」

「是啊，那孩子聪明着呢，满脑子都是这类妙点子。呃，如今他发现我并非他想象中那般人物，就不肯跟我走了；所以趁行情正好，我要你来替上我一角。第五本今晚发行——等全宅的人就寝之后——你将挂头牌。必须就在今晚，因为我刚想到：奥格登既已知道威斯比奇勋爵是个骗子，说不定会带着当初向我提的那个提议去找他。」

===Original===

“Lord Wisbeach a crook!”

“Of the worst description. He is here to steal that explosive stuff of Willie Partridge’s. But as I have blocked that play, he may turn his attention to Ogden.”

“But, Jimmy, if that fellow is a crook﻿—how do you know he is?”

“He told me so himself.”

“Well, then, why don’t you expose him?”

===Chinese===

「威斯比奇勋爵是骗子！」

「而且是头一等的那种。他到这儿来，就是要偷威利·帕特里奇那种炸药的。不过那一手既然已被我堵死，他没准会把心思转到奥格登身上。」

「可是，吉米，那家伙要真是个骗子——你又怎么知道的？」

「他亲口对我说的。」

「那你怎么不揭穿他？」

===Original===

“Because in order to do so, Skinner my man, I should have to explain that I was really Jimmy Crocker, and the time is not yet ripe for that. To my thinking, the time will not be ripe till you have got safely away with Ogden Ford. I can then go to Ann and say ‘I may have played you a rotten trick in the past, but I have done you a good turn now, so let’s forget the past!’ So you see that everything now depends on you, dad. I’m not asking you to do anything difficult. I’ll go round to the boardinghouse now and tell Jerry Mitchell about what we have arranged, and have him waiting outside here in a car. Then all you will have to do is to go to Ogden, play a short scene as Chicago Ed., escort him to the car, and then go back to bed and have a good sleep. Once Ogden thinks you are a professional kidnapper, you won’t have any difficulty at all. Get it into your head that he *wants* to be kidnapped. Surely you can tackle this light and attractive job? Why, it will be a treat for you to do a bit of character acting once more!”

Jimmy had struck the right note. His father’s eyes began to gleam with excitement. The scent of the footlights seemed to dilate his nostrils.

“I was always good at that roughneck stuff,” he murmured meditatively. “I used to eat it!”

“Exactly,” said Jimmy. “Look at it in the right way, and I am doing you a kindness in giving you this chance.”

===Chinese===

「因为要揭穿他，我的斯金纳伙计，我就得交代清楚自己真是吉米·克罗克，而火候还没到。依我看，等你带着奥格登·福特平安脱身，火候才算到。到那时我便可以去对安说：『我从前也许对你使过一记损招，可如今我为你办了件好事，咱们把过去一笔勾销吧！』所以你瞧，如今万事都系在你身上了，爸。我不是要你做什么难事。我这就去寄宿公寓，把咱们安排好的事告诉杰里·米切尔，让他备好一辆汽车等在这宅子外面。到时候你只需去找奥格登，以芝加哥·埃德的身份演一小场戏，把他护送到汽车上，然后回床上睡个好觉。只要奥格登认定你是职业绑匪，你就毫无困难可言。把这个牢牢记在脑子里：他*巴不得*被人绑走。这样一桩轻松又招人的差事，你总该对付得了吧？哎呀，能再过一把性格演员的瘾，对你简直是赏心乐事！」

吉米这一下琴弦算是弹对了。他父亲的眼睛开始兴奋地发亮。脚灯的气味仿佛熏得他鼻孔都张大了。

「那种粗坯角色，我向来拿手，」他悠悠地喃喃道，「当年我嚼这类戏跟吃糖似的！」

「这就对了，」吉米说。「往对的一面看，我给你这个机会，正是成全你一桩美事。」

===Original===

Mr. Crocker rubbed his cheek with his forefinger.

“You’d want me to make up for the part?” he asked wistfully.

“Of course!”

“You want me to do it tonight?”

“At about two in the morning, I thought.”

“I’ll do it, Jim!”

===Chinese===

克罗克先生用食指蹭着脸颊。

「你要不要我为这个角色化装？」他一脸渴望地问。

「当然要！」

「你要我今晚就演？」

「我想，大约凌晨两点吧。」

「我干了，吉姆！」

===Original===

Jimmy grasped his hand.

“I knew I could rely on you, dad.”

Mr. Crocker was following a train of thought.

“Dark wig﻿ ﻿… blue chin﻿ ﻿… heavy eyebrows﻿ ﻿… I guess I can’t do better than my old Chicago Ed. makeup. Say, Jimmy, how am I to get to the kid?”

“That’ll be all right. You can stay in my room till the time comes to go to him. Use it as a dressing-room.”

“How am I to get him out of the house?”

===Chinese===

吉米紧紧握住他的手。

「我就知道你靠得住，爸。」

克罗克先生的思路正顺着一条轨道往前走。

「黑色假发……铁青下巴……两道浓眉……我看再没有比那套芝加哥·埃德的老扮相更妙的了。哎，吉米，我怎么才能到那孩子跟前去？」

「这个好办。你可以先待在我房里，到时候再去他那儿。就把它当化装间使。」

「我怎么把他弄出这座宅子？」

===Original===

“Through this room. I’ll tell Jerry to wait out on the side-street with the car from two o’clock on.”

Mr. Crocker considered these arrangements.

“That seems to be about all,” he said.

“I don’t think there’s anything else.”

“I’ll slip downtown and buy the props.”

“I’ll go and tell Jerry.”

===Chinese===

「穿过这间屋子。我会吩咐杰里从两点钟起就备着汽车，在外面小街上等候。」

克罗克先生把这套安排琢磨了一遍。

「看样子差不多都齐了。」他说。

「我想没有别的了。」

「我溜到市区去买道具。」

「我去知会杰里。」

===Original===

A thought struck Mr. Crocker.

“You’d better tell Jerry to make up, too. He doesn’t want the kid recognising him and squealing on him later.”

Jimmy was lost in admiration of his father’s resource.

“You think of everything, dad! That wouldn’t have occurred to me. You certainly do take to Crime in the most wonderful way. It seems to come naturally to you!”

Mr. Crocker smirked modestly.

===Chinese===

克罗克先生忽然想起一件事。

「你最好也叫杰里化化装。他总不想让那孩子认出来，日后东窗事发把他咬出来。」

吉米对他父亲的足智多谋叹服不已。

「你什么都想到了，爸！这一层我是万万想不到的。你投向『犯罪』这门行当怀抱的速度，实在妙不可言。看来你是天生这块料！」

克罗克先生谦虚地咧嘴一笑。

## XX / 二十

Celestine Imparts Information / 塞莱斯廷透露消息

===Original===

Plot is only as strong as its weakest link. The best-laid schemes of mice and men gang agley if one of the mice is a mental defective or if one of the men is a Jerry Mitchell.﻿ ﻿…

Celestine, Mrs. Pett’s maid﻿—she who was really Maggie O’Toole and whom Jerry loved with a strength which deprived him of even that small amount of intelligence which had been bestowed upon him by Nature﻿—came into the housekeeper’s room at about ten o’clock that night. The domestic staff had gone in a body to the moving-pictures, and the only occupant of the room was the new parlourmaid, who was sitting in a hard chair, reading Schopenhauer.

Celestine’s face was flushed, her dark hair was ruffled, and her eyes were shining. She breathed a little quickly, and her left hand was out of sight behind her back. She eyed the new parlourmaid doubtfully for a moment. The latter was a woman of somewhat unencouraging exterior, not the kind that invites confidences. But Celestine had confidences to bestow, and the exodus to the movies had left her in a position where she could not pick and choose. She was faced with the alternative of locking her secret in her palpitating bosom or of revealing it to this one auditor. The choice was one which no impulsive damsel in like circumstances would have hesitated to make.

===Chinese===

情节这东西有多结实，全看它最薄弱的一环。鼠与人的万全妙计尚且会走岔，倘若那鼠里有一只先天脑子不灵，或那人里有一个名叫杰里·米切尔的，便更是必岔无疑。……

塞莱斯廷——佩特太太的女仆，本名其实是玛吉·奥图尔；杰里对她的爱情是那样炽烈，以致连造物主原本赏给他的那一星半点智力也被剥夺了去——当晚十点钟左右走进了管家室。佣仆们已倾巢出动看电影去了，屋里唯一的人是新来的客厅侍女，她正坐在一把硬椅子上读叔本华（Schopenhauer）。

塞莱斯廷满脸红晕，一头乌发蓬乱，两眼放光。她呼吸微微急促，左手藏在背后不见踪影。她将信将疑地打量了新来的客厅侍女片刻。后者是位外表颇为让人泄气的女人，绝非那种招人倾诉的模样。可塞莱斯廷满腹的话儿非要送出去不可，而众人看电影的大迁徙又害得她无从挑拣听众。她面前的抉择是：要么把秘密锁进自己怦怦乱跳的胸脯，要么透露给眼前这唯一的听众。凡处在同样境地的心热姑娘，对这样的选择是决不会有半分犹豫的。

===Original===

“Say!” said Celestine.

A face rose reluctantly from behind Schopenhauer. A gleaming eye met Celestine’s. A second eye﻿—no less gleaming﻿—glared at the ceiling.

“Say, I just been talking to my feller outside,” said Celestine with a coy simper. “Say, he’s a grand man!”

A snort of uncompromising disapproval proceeded from the thin-lipped mouth beneath the eyes. But Celestine was too full of her news to be discouraged.

“I’m strong fer Jer!” she said.

===Chinese===

「我说！」塞莱斯廷道。

一张脸老大不情愿地从叔本华背后抬了起来。一只灼灼放光的眼睛迎住了塞莱斯廷的目光。另一只眼睛——灼灼不逊于前——怒瞪着天花板。

「我说，我刚才在外头跟我家那位说话来着，」塞莱斯廷羞答答地抿嘴一笑。「我说，他可是个顶呱呱的男人！」

那两只眼睛底下的一张薄嘴唇里，发出一声绝无通融余地的哼声，以示不满。可塞莱斯廷一肚子新闻，才不肯就此打退堂鼓。

「我迷杰里迷得不行！」她说。

===Original===

“Huh?” said the student of Schopenhauer.

“Jerry Mitchell, you know. You ain’t never met him, have you? Say, he’s a grand man!”

For the first time she had the other’s undivided attention. The new parlourmaid placed her book upon the table.

“Uh?” she said.

Celestine could hold back her dramatic surprise no longer. Her concealed left hand flashed into view. On the third finger glittered a ring. She gazed at it with awed affection.

===Chinese===

「嗯？」研读叔本华的那位说。

「杰里·米切尔，知道吧。你压根儿没见过他，对不对？我说，他可是个顶呱呱的男人！」

头一回，她赢得了对方不打折扣的全副注意。新来的客厅侍女把书搁在了桌上。

「哦？」她说。

塞莱斯廷再也按捺不住她的戏剧性惊喜。藏着的那只左手唰地闪现在眼前。无名指上，一枚戒指熠熠生辉。她怀着敬畏的爱意凝望着它。

===Original===

“Ain’t it a beaut!”

She contemplated its sparkling perfection for a moment in rapturous silence.

“Say, you could have knocked me down with a feather!” she resumed. “He telephones me awhile ago and says to be outside the back door at ten tonight, because he’d something he wanted to tell me. Of course he couldn’t come in and tell it me here, because he’d been fired and everything. So I goes out, and there he is. ‘Hello, kid!’ he says to me. ‘Fresh!’ I says to him. ‘Say, I got something to be fresh about!’ he says to me. And then he reaches into his jeans and hauls out the sparkler. ‘What’s that?’ I says to him. ‘It’s an engagement ring,’ he says to me. ‘For you, if you’ll wear it!’ I came over so weak, I could have fell! And the next thing I know he’s got it on my finger and﻿—” Celestine broke off modestly. “Say, ain’t it a beaut, honest!” She gave herself over to contemplation once more. “He says to me how he’s on Easy Street now, or will be pretty soon. I says to him ‘Have you got a job, then?’ He says to me ‘Now, I ain’t got a job, but I’m going to pull off a stunt tonight that’s going to mean enough to me to start that health-farm I’ve told you about.’ Say, he’s always had a line of talk about starting a health-farm down on Long Island, he knowing all about training and health and everything through having been one of them fighters. I asks him what the stunt is, but he won’t tell me yet. He says he’ll tell me after we’re married, but he says it’s sure-fire and he’s going to buy the license tomorrow.”

She paused for comment and congratulations, eyeing her companion expectantly.

===Chinese===

「它可真是个宝贝！」

她狂喜无言，对着那件闪闪发光的完美之物凝望了片刻。

「我说，你拿根羽毛就能把我放倒！」她接着说道。「他刚才给我来了个电话，说让我今晚十点到家后门外头等着，因为他有话要跟我讲。当然喽，他没法进屋里来讲，他不是已经被辞退了嘛，还有种种缘故。我就出去了，他还真在那儿。『喂，小丫头！』他跟我说。『贫嘴！』我跟他说。『我说，我这回可有的贫了！』他跟我说。接着他把手伸进裤兜，掏出那枚亮灿灿的钻戒。『这是什么？』我跟他说。『是枚订婚戒指，』他跟我说。『给你的，只要你肯戴！』我登时浑身发软，差点儿当场倒下！再等我明白过来，他已经把它套上了我的手指，而且——」塞莱斯廷知趣地收住了话头。「我说，它是不是个宝贝，真的！」她又沉浸在凝望之中。「他跟我说，他如今住进安乐街了，或者很快就要住进了。我跟他说『那你找着差事了？』他跟我说『这个嘛，差事还没有，可我今儿晚上要干成一件大事，进项足够我把跟你念叨过的那座健身农庄办起来。』我说，他一天到晚把去长岛开健身农庄挂在嘴上，因为好歹当过拳击手，什么训练啊保健啊，他全都在行。我问他那件大事是什么，可他还不肯说。他说等咱们结了婚就告诉我，还说这事儿十拿九稳，他明天就去领结婚证。」

她停下来等待品评与道贺，满怀期许地望着同伴。

===Original===

“Huh!” said the new parlourmaid briefly, and resumed her Schopenhauer. Decidedly hers was not a winning personality.

“Ain’t it a beaut?” demanded Celestine, damped.

The new parlourmaid uttered a curious sound at the back of her throat.

“He’s a beaut!” she said cryptically.

She added another remark in a lower tone, too low for Celestine’s ears. It could hardly have been that, but it sounded to Celestine like:

“I’ll fix ’m!”

===Chinese===

「哼！」新来的客厅侍女言简意赅地说，随即重新读起她的叔本华。明摆着，她可不是一个招人爱的性子。

「它难道不是个宝贝？」塞莱斯廷追问，热乎劲儿被浇了个半凉。

新来的客厅侍女从喉咙深处发出了一个古怪的声音。

「他才是宝贝呢！」她高深莫测地说。

她又压低嗓门添了一句，低得塞莱斯廷没法听见。这话按理不可能是那个意思，可听在塞莱斯廷耳朵里却像是：

「看我怎么收拾他！」

## XXI / 二十一

Chicago Ed. / 芝加哥·埃德

===Original===

Riverside Drive slept. The moon shone on darkened windows and deserted sidewalks. It was past one o’clock in the morning. The wicked Forties were still ablaze with light and noisy foxtrots; but in the virtuous Hundreds, where Mr. Pett’s house stood, respectable slumber reigned. Only the occasional drone of a passing automobile broke the silence, or the lovesick cry of some feline Romeo patrolling a wall-top.

Jimmy was awake. He was sitting on the edge of his bed watching his father put the finishing touches to his makeup, which was of a shaggy and intimidating nature. The elder Crocker had conceived the outward aspect of Chicago Ed., King of the Kidnappers, on broad and impressive lines, and one glance would have been enough to tell the sagacious observer that here was no white-souled comrade for a nocturnal saunter down lonely lanes and out-of-the-way alleys.

Mr. Crocker seemed to feel this himself.

“The only trouble is, Jim,” he said, peering at himself in the glass, “shan’t I scare the boy to death directly he sees me? Oughtn’t I to give him some sort of warning?”

===Chinese===

里弗赛德大道睡着了。月光照在黑黢黢的窗户和空无一人的便道上。已是凌晨一点多钟。无法无天的四十几街依旧灯火通明、狐步舞曲喧闹不歇；可在清清白白的上百号街区——佩特先生的宅邸便坐落于此——正派的酣睡君临一切。只有偶尔一辆驶过的汽车发出嗡嗡声打破寂静，再不然，便是哪只在墙头巡夜的猫中罗密欧发出害了相思的啼叫。

吉米却醒着。他坐在床沿上，望着父亲给自己的妆容做最后的收拾——那妆容蓬头垢面、凶相慑人。老克罗克先生构思「绑匪之王」芝加哥·埃德的外表时，走的是恢宏而慑人的路数；眼光老到的观察者只消一眼便足以断定：这位绝非能陪您深夜漫步僻静小路、流窜犄角旮旯的白璧无瑕之良伴。

克罗克先生自己似乎也感觉到了这一点。

「唯一的麻烦是，吉姆，」他眯眼打量着镜中的自己，说道，「那孩子一见我，会不会当场吓死？我是不是该先给他来点什么预警？」

===Original===

“How? Do you suggest sending him a formal note?”

Mr. Crocker surveyed his repellent features doubtfully.

“It’s a good deal to spring on a kid at one in the morning,” he said. “Suppose he has a fit!”

“He’s far more likely to give you one. Don’t you worry about Ogden, dad. I shouldn’t think there was a child alive more equal to handling such a situation.”

===Chinese===

「怎么预警？你总不是建议给他递张正式的便函吧？」

克罗克先生满腹狐疑地端详着自己那副面目可憎的五官。

「凌晨一点钟，冷不丁冲一个孩子来这么一手，分量可不轻，」他说。「万一他抽起风来呢！」

「要抽风，也多半是你先抽——你比他更容易被他吓着。别替奥格登担心，老爸。我看天底下再没有哪个孩子，比他更扛得住这种场面了。」

===Original===

There was an empty glass standing on a tray on the dressing-table. Mr. Crocker eyed this sadly.

“I wish you hadn’t thrown that stuff away, Jim. I could have done with it. I’m feeling nervous.”

“Nonsense, dad! You’re all right! I had to throw it away. I’m on the wagon now, but how long I should have stayed on with that smiling up at me I don’t know. I’ve made up my mind never to lower myself to the level of the beasts that perish with the demon Rum again, because my future wife has strong views on the subject: but there’s no sense in taking chances. Temptation is all very well, but you don’t need it on your dressing-table. It was a kindly thought of yours to place it there, dad, but﻿—”

===Chinese===

梳妆台的托盘上立着一只空杯子。克罗克先生凄然望着它。

「你不该把那玩意儿倒掉，吉姆。我本来正用得着。我这会儿正紧张呢。」

「胡说，老爸！您好端端的！我是不得不倒。我如今上了戒酒车，可要让那杯东西在台上笑吟吟地望着我，还能在车上坐多久，我可说不准。我已经下定决心，这辈子再不自贬身价、与那帮和酒魔同归于尽的畜生为伍，因为我未来的内子在这个问题上有极坚定的见解：不过，犯不着冒这个险。诱惑这东西自有它的好处，可您的梳妆台上并不需要它。您把它摆在那儿是一片好心，老爸，可是——」

===Original===

“Eh? I didn’t put it there.”

“I thought that sort of thing came in your department. Isn’t it the butler’s job to supply drinks to the nobility and gentry? Well, it doesn’t matter. It is now distributed over the neighbouring soil, thus removing a powerful temptation from your path. You’re better without it.” He looked at his watch. “Well, it ought to be all right now.” He went to the window. “There’s an automobile down there. I suppose it’s Jerry. I told him to be outside at one sharp and it’s nearly half-past. I think you might be starting, dad. Oh, by the way, you had better tell Ogden that you represent a gentleman of the name of Buck Maginnis. It was Buck who got away with him last time, and a firm friendship seems to have sprung up between them. There’s nothing like coming with a good introduction.”

Mr. Crocker took a final survey of himself in the mirror.

===Chinese===

「嗯？那不是我摆的。」

「我还当这一类事归您那个部门管呢。给贵族和士绅们供应酒水，不就是管家的分内事吗？也罢，不要紧。它如今已经匀撒在左近的泥土里，您路上的一个强大诱惑就此解除。没了它，您反而更好。」他看了看表。「嗯，现在该没问题了。」他走到窗前。「下头停着一辆汽车。我猜是杰里。我吩咐他一点整在外面候着，现在都快一点半了。我看您可以动身了，老爸。哦，对了，你最好告诉奥格登，你代表着一位名叫巴克·马金尼斯的绅士。上回把他弄到手的正是巴克，两人之间似乎结下了一段牢固的友谊。带着一封漂亮的介绍信登门，天底下再没有比这更妙的了。」

克罗克先生对着镜子，把自己最后端量了一番。

===Original===

“Gee! I’d hate to meet myself on a lonely road!”

He opened the door, and stood for a moment listening.

From somewhere down the passage came the murmur of a muffled snore.

“Third door on the left,” said Jimmy. “Three﻿—count ’em!﻿—three. Don’t go getting mixed.”

===Chinese===

「乖乖！要是在僻静的路上迎面撞见我自己，非吓个好歹不可！」

他打开房门，伫立片刻，凝神细听。

走廊深处的什么地方，传来一阵闷在被窝里的鼾声。

「左手第三间，」吉米说。「三间——数着点儿！——三间。可别数岔了。」

===Original===

Mr. Crocker slid into the outer darkness like a stout ghost, and Jimmy closed the door gently behind him.

Having launched his indulgent parent safely on a career of crime, Jimmy switched off the light and returned to the window. Leaning out, he gave himself up for a moment to sentimental musings. The night was very still. Through the trees which flanked the house the dimmed headlights of what was presumably Jerry Mitchell’s hired car shone faintly like enlarged fireflies. A boat of some description was tooting reflectively far down the river. Such was the seductive influence of the time and the scene that Jimmy might have remained there indefinitely, weaving dreams, had he not been under the necessity of making his way down to the library. It was his task to close the French windows after his father and Ogden had passed through, and he proposed to remain hid in the gallery there until the time came for him to do this. It was imperative that he avoid being seen by Ogden.

Locking his door behind him, he went downstairs. There were no signs of life in the house. Everything was still. He found the staircase leading to the gallery without having to switch on the lights.

It was dusty in the gallery, and a smell of old leather enveloped him. He hoped his father would not be long. He lowered himself cautiously to the floor, and, resting his head against a convenient shelf, began to wonder how the interview between Chicago Ed. and his prey was progressing.

===Chinese===

克罗克先生像一尊胖幽灵，溜进了外面的黑暗，吉米在他身后轻轻把门带上。

把自家那位慈纵的老父安然送上了犯罪生涯，吉米关掉灯，回到窗前。他探身窗外，一时沉入了多愁善感的遐思。夜极静。透过宅子两侧的树木，那辆想来是杰里·米切尔雇来的汽车上拨暗了的车头灯微微闪亮，好似放大了的萤火虫。下游远处河面上，有一条不知什么船在若有所思地鸣笛。此情此景的魅力如此醉人，若不是还必须下楼到书房走一遭，吉米本可以无限期地倚在那里编织美梦。他的任务是在父亲和奥格登穿窗而过之后把落地长窗关上，而他打算在那之前一直藏身于书房的楼廊里。务必不能让奥格登看见他，这是头等要紧的事。

他锁上身后的房门，下了楼。宅子里不见一丝生人气。万籁俱寂。他没开灯，便摸到了通往楼廊的楼梯。

楼廊里灰尘扑扑，一股旧皮革的气味将他裹住。他但愿父亲别耽搁太久。他小心翼翼地放低身子坐到地板上，脑袋往近便的一个书架上一靠，开始琢磨芝加哥·埃德与他的猎物之间的会谈进展如何。

---

===Original===

Mr. Crocker, meanwhile, masked to the eyes, had crept in fearful silence to the door which Jimmy had indicated. A good deal of the gay enthusiasm with which he had embarked on this enterprise had ebbed away from him. Now that he had become accustomed to the novelty of finding himself once more playing a character part, his intimate respectability began to assert itself. It was one thing to play Chicago Ed. at a Broadway theatre, but quite another to give a benefit performance like this. As he tiptoed along the passage, the one thing that presented itself most clearly to him was the appalling outcome of this act of his, should anything go wrong. He would have turned back, but for the thought that Jimmy was depending on him and that success would mean Jimmy’s happiness. Stimulated by this reflection, he opened Ogden’s door inch by inch and went in. He stole softly across the room.

He had almost reached the bed, and had just begun to wonder how on earth, now that he was there, he could open the proceedings tactfully and without alarming the boy, when he was saved the trouble of pondering further on this problem. A light flashed out of the darkness with the suddenness of a bursting bomb, and a voice from the same general direction said “Hands up!”

When Mr. Crocker had finished blinking and had adjusted his eyes to the glare, he perceived Ogden sitting up in bed with a revolver in his hand. The revolver was resting on his knee, and its muzzle pointed directly at Mr. Crocker’s ample stomach.

===Chinese===

且说克罗克先生，此刻已蒙面蒙到眼眶，一路屏气敛息、战战兢兢地摸到了吉米指给他的那扇门前。他投身这项事业时那股兴冲冲的热忱，已经消退了大半。如今他既已习惯了自己又演上性格角色的新鲜，骨子里的正派便开始抬头。在百老汇的戏台上扮芝加哥·埃德是一回事，来这么一场义演可完全是另一回事。他踮着脚尖沿走廊蹭过去时，心里最清楚的一件事便是：此举万一出了岔子，下场将何等骇人。若不是想到吉米全指望着他、想到事成便意味着吉米的幸福，他早掉头回去了。受此念激励，他把奥格登的房门一寸一寸推开，走了进去。他蹑手蹑脚地穿过房间。

他差不多已经走到床边，刚开始犯愁：人既到了此地，究竟怎样才能把这开场白说得得体、又不惊动那孩子——正在此时，他省去了再为这道难题冥思的麻烦。一道光如炸弹炸响般猝然划破黑暗，同一大方向上有个声音喝道：「举起手来！」

克罗克先生眨完了眼，把目光调适到那片强光上，这才看清奥格登正坐在床上，手里握着一支左轮枪。左轮枪支在他膝上，枪口端端正正地指着克罗克先生那片开阔的肚皮。

===Original===

Exhaustive as had been the thought which Jimmy’s father had given to the possible developments of his enterprise, this was a contingency of which he had not dreamed. He was entirely at a loss.

“Don’t do that!” he said huskily. “It might go off!”

“I should worry!” replied Ogden coldly. “I’m at the right end of it. What are you doing here?” He looked fondly at the lethal weapon. “I got this with cigarette-coupons, to shoot rabbits when we went to the country. Here’s where I get a chance at something part-human.”

“Do you want to murder me?”

===Chinese===

吉米的父亲对自己这项事业可能的发展，思虑不可谓不穷尽，可这一层却是他做梦也不曾料到的变数。他全然没了主意。

「可别来那个！」他哑着嗓子说。「那玩意儿会走火！」

「我才犯不上担心！」奥格登冷冷地答道。「我待的可是它安全的那一头。你上这儿来干什么？」他怜爱地望着那件致命的武器。「这是我拿香烟赠券换的，本来预备下乡时打兔子。这回可有机会朝个有几分人样的东西开火了。」

「你想谋杀我不成？」

===Original===

“Why not?”

Mr. Crocker’s makeup was trickling down his face in sticky streams. The mask, however, prevented Ogden from seeing this peculiar phenomenon. He was gazing interestedly at his visitor. An idea struck him.

“Say, did you come to kidnap me?”

Mr. Crocker felt the sense of relief which he had sometimes experienced on the stage when memory had failed him during a scene and a fellow-actor had thrown him the line. It would be exaggerating to say that he was himself again. He could never be completely at his ease with that pistol pointing at him; but he felt considerably better. He lowered his voice an octave or so, and spoke in a husky growl.

===Chinese===

「有何不可？」

克罗克先生脸上的油彩正黏糊糊地一道道往下淌。好在面罩遮着，奥格登瞧不见这一奇观。他正饶有兴味地打量来客。一个念头击中了他。

「我说，你是来绑我票的吧？」

克罗克先生感到一阵宽慰，与他在舞台上偶尔经历的那种如出一辙——戏演到一半忘了词，同台的演员把台词抛了过来。若说他这下完全恢复了常态，那未免夸张。有那支枪指着他，他无论如何也自在不起来；但他觉得好受多了。他把嗓音压低八度上下，哑着喉咙瓮声瓮气地说起话来。

===Original===

“Aw, cheese it, kid. Nix on the rough stuff!”

“Keep those hands up!” advised Ogden.

“Sure! Sure!” growled Mr. Crocker. “Can the gunplay, bo! Say, you’ve soitanly grown since de last time we got youse!”

Ogden’s manner became magically friendly.

===Chinese===

「哎，省省吧，小子。可别来硬的！」

「手举着别放！」奥格登提醒道。

「成！成！」克罗克先生低吼道。「把枪收了吧，老兄！我说，打上回咱们把你弄到手，你可着实见长啊！」

奥格登的态度魔法般地友好起来。

===Original===

“Are you one of Buck Maginnis’ lot?” he enquired almost politely.

“Dat’s right!” Mr. Crocker blessed the inspiration which had prompted Jimmy’s parting words. “I’m wit Buck.”

“Why didn’t Buck come himself?”

“He’s woiking on anudder job!”

===Chinese===

「你是巴克·马金尼斯那伙的吧？」他问得几乎算得上客气。

「正是！」克罗克先生暗自感激那促使吉米临别赠言的灵光。「我是跟巴克一伙的。」

「那巴克怎么自己没来？」

「他手头正张罗另一票呢！」

===Original===

To Mr. Crocker’s profound relief Ogden lowered the pistol.

“I’m strong for Buck,” he said conversationally. “We’re old pals. Did you see the piece in the paper about him kidnapping me last time? I’ve got it in my press-clipping album.”

“Sure,” said Mr. Crocker.

“Say, listen. If you take me now, Buck’s got to come across. I like Buck, but I’m not going to let myself be kidnapped for his benefit. It’s fifty-fifty, or nothing doing. See?”

===Chinese===

奥格登放下了左轮枪，克罗克先生深深地松了一口气。

「我顶巴克顶得不行，」他用闲谈的口气说。「我们是老交情了。上回他绑我的票，报上登的那篇你看了没有？我把它收进我的剪报簿了。」

「可不是。」克罗克先生说。

「哎，听着。你这会儿要是把我带走，巴克就得掏钱。我是喜欢巴克，可我不能白让他借我的绑票发财。五五分账，不然免谈。懂吗？」

===Original===

“I get you, kid.”

“Well, if that’s understood, all right. Give me a minute to get some clothes on, and I’ll be with you.”

“Don’t make a noise,” said Mr. Crocker.

“Who’s making any noise? Say, how did you get in here?”

===Chinese===

「懂了，小子。」

「行，这条讲定了就好。给我一分钟，套上几件衣裳，我就跟你走。」

「别弄出声响。」克罗克先生说。

「谁弄声响啦？对了，你是打哪儿进来的？」

===Original===

“T’roo de libery windows.”

“I always knew some yegg would stroll in that way. It beats me why they didn’t have bars fixed on them.”

“Dere’s a buzz-wagon outside, waitin’.”

“You do it in style, don’t you?” observed Ogden, pulling on his shirt. “Who’s working this with you? Anyone I know?”

===Chinese===

「打书房的落地长窗溜进来的。」

「我早知道早晚有个翻窗贼会这么溜进来。真搞不懂，他们干吗不给窗子装上铁栏杆。」

「外头有辆嗡嗡车，候着呢。」

「你们办事还挺讲究排场，是吧？」奥格登一边套衬衫一边评价道。「这回跟你搭伙的是谁？有我认得的吗？」

===Original===

“Naw. A new guy.”

“Oh? Say, I don’t remember you, if it comes to that.”

“You don’t?” said Mr. Crocker a little discomposed.

“Well, maybe I wouldn’t, with that mask on you. Which of them are you?”

===Chinese===

「没有。一个新面孔。」

「哦？我说，真要论起来，我可想不起你这个人。」

「想不起？」克罗克先生说，心里有点儿发慌。

「呃，有你脸上那玩意儿罩着，想得起才怪。你是他们中间的哪一位？」

===Original===

“Chicago Ed.’s my monaker.”

“I don’t remember any Chicago Ed.”

“Well, you will after dis!” said Mr. Crocker, happily inspired.

Ogden was eyeing him with sudden suspicion.

===Chinese===

「芝加哥·埃德，就是俺的名号。」

「什么芝加哥·埃德，我没听说过。」

「这回过后，你就忘不掉喽！」克罗克先生说，福至心灵，好不欢畅。

奥格登忽然起了疑心，直勾勾地打量起他来。

===Original===

“Take that mask off and let’s have a look at you.”

“Nothing doin’.”

“How am I to know you’re on the level?”

Mr. Crocker played a daring card.

===Chinese===

「把那面罩摘了，让我瞧瞧你的尊容。」

「没门儿。」

「那我凭什么信你这人不掺假？」

克罗克先生打出了一张大胆的牌。

===Original===

“All right,” he said, making a move towards the door. “It’s up to youse. If you t’ink I’m not on de level, I’ll beat it.”

“Here, stop a minute,” said Ogden hastily, unwilling that a promising business deal should be abandoned in this summary manner. “I’m not saying anything against you. There’s no need to fly off the handle like that.”

“I’ll tell Buck I couldn’t get you,” said Mr. Crocker, moving another step.

“Here, stop! What’s the matter with you?”

===Chinese===

「行啊，」他说着朝房门挪动。「随你的便。你要是觉得俺不地道，俺这就开路。」

「哎哎，且慢，」奥格登忙道，一桩大有指望的买卖竟要这般干脆利落地告吹，他可不甘心。「我没说你半个不字。犯不着这么一点就炸。」

「俺就回巴克说，你这号弄不走。」克罗克先生说，又挪了一步。

「哎，站住！你这人是怎么回事？」

===Original===

“Are youse comin’ wit me?”

“Sure, if you get the conditions. Buck’s got to slip me half of whatever he gets out of this.”

“Dat’s right. Buck’ll slip youse half of anyt’ing he gets.”

“All right, then. Wait till I’ve got this shoe on, and let’s start. Now I’m ready.”

===Chinese===

「你到底跟不跟俺走？」

「走啊，只要条件不变。这回不管巴克从里头捞到什么，都得塞给我一半。」

「没跑。他捞着啥，都分你一半。」

「那好。等我把这只鞋蹬上，咱就动身。好了，这就绪了。」

===Original===

“Beat it quietly.”

“What did you think I was going to do? Sing?”

“Step dis way!” said Mr. Crocker jocosely.

They left the room cautiously. Mr. Crocker for a moment had a sense of something missing. He had reached the stairs before he realised what it was. Then it dawned upon him that what was lacking was the applause. The scene had deserved a round.

===Chinese===

「放轻脚步溜。」

「你还当我预备干什么？唱着走？」

「这边请！」克罗克先生打趣道。

两人小心翼翼地出了房间。有一阵子，克罗克先生只觉缺了点什么。走到楼梯口他才明白缺的是什么。他恍然大悟：缺的是掌声。方才那场戏，本该博得满堂彩。

===Original===

Jimmy, vigilant in the gallery, heard the library door open softly and, peering over the rail, perceived two dim forms in the darkness. One was large, the other small. They crossed the room together.

Whispered words reached him.

“I thought you said you came in this way.”

“Sure.”

===Chinese===

吉米在楼廊上望风，听见书房的门轻轻开了，从栏杆上探头下望，看见黑暗中两条模糊的影子。一条大，一条小。两条影子一齐穿过了房间。

低语声传进他耳朵。

「我记得你是说打这儿进来的。」

「是啊。」

===Original===

“Then why’s the shutter closed?”

“I fixed it after I was in.”

There was a faint scraping sound, followed by a click. The darkness of the room was relieved by moonlight. The figures passed through. Jimmy ran down from the gallery, and closed the windows softly. He had just fastened the shutters, when from the passage outside there came the unmistakeable sound of a footstep.

===Chinese===

「那窗板怎么关上了？」

「我进来之后随手关的。」

一阵轻微的刮擦声，接着咔哒一响。月光泻进屋来，房里的黑暗得了缓解。两条影子穿窗而过。吉米从楼廊跑下来，轻轻关上长窗。他刚把窗板扣牢，外面的过道里便传来一声脚步——一记绝不会听错的脚步声。

## XXII / 二十二

In the Library / 书房里

===Original===

Jimmy’s first emotion on hearing the footstep was the crude instinct of self-preservation. All that he was able to think of at the moment was the fact that he was in a questionable position and one which would require a good deal of explaining away if he were found, and his only sensation was a strong desire to avoid discovery. He made a silent, scrambling leap for the gallery stairs, and reached their shelter just as the door opened. He stood there, rigid, waiting to be challenged, but apparently he had moved in time, for no voice spoke. The door closed so gently as to be almost inaudible, and then there was silence again. The room remained in darkness, and it was this perhaps that first suggested to Jimmy the comforting thought that the intruder was equally desirous of avoiding the scrutiny of his fellows. He had taken it for granted in his first panic that he himself was the only person in that room whose motive for being there would not have borne inspection. But now, safely hidden in the gallery, out of sight from the floor below, he had the leisure to consider the newcomer’s movements and to draw conclusions from them.

An honest man’s first act would surely have been to switch on the lights. And an honest man would hardly have crept so stealthily. It became apparent to Jimmy, as he leaned over the rail and tried to pierce the darkness, that there was sinister work afoot; and he had hardly reached this conclusion when his mind took a further leap and he guessed the identity of the soft-footed person below. It could be none but his old friend Lord Wisbeach, known to “the boys” as Gentleman Jack. It surprised him that he had not thought of this before. Then it surprised him that, after the talk they had only a few hours earlier in that very room, Gentleman Jack should have dared to risk this raid.

At this moment the blackness was relieved as if by the striking of a match. The man below had brought an electric torch into play, and now Jimmy could see clearly. He had been right in his surmise. It was Lord Wisbeach. He was kneeling in front of the safe. What he was doing to the safe, Jimmy could not see, for the man’s body was in the way; but the electric torch shone on his face, lighting up grim, serious features quite unlike the amiable and slightly vacant mask which his lordship was wont to present to the world. As Jimmy looked, something happened in the pool of light beyond his vision. Gentleman Jack gave a muttered exclamation of satisfaction, and then Jimmy saw that the door of the safe had swung open. The air was full of a penetrating smell of scorched metal. Jimmy was not an expert in these matters, but he had read from time to time of modern burglars and their methods, and he gathered that an oxyacetylene blowpipe, with its flame that cuts steel as a knife cuts cheese, had been at work.

Lord Wisbeach flashed the torch into the open safe, plunged his hand in, and drew it out again, holding something. Handling this in a cautious and gingerly manner, he placed it carefully in his breast pocket. Then he straightened himself. He switched off the torch, and moved to the window, leaving the rest of his implements by the open safe. He unfastened the shutter, then raised the catch of the window. At this point it seemed to Jimmy that the time had come to interfere.

===Chinese===

听见脚步声，吉米心头涌起的第一股情绪，是自我保全的粗粝本能。此刻他脑中转得动的只有一件事：自己眼下所处的是一个说不清道不明的位置，一旦被人撞见，得费好一番口舌才能洗刷干净；他唯一的感觉，便是一股避免暴露的强烈愿望。他悄没声息、手脚并用地朝楼廊的小楼梯纵身一蹿，恰好赶在房门开动的一刻窜进了那方庇护之地。他直挺挺地立在那里，等着喝问声起，可看来他动作够快，因为无人出声。门轻轻合上，轻得几乎听不见，随后屋里又归于寂静。房间依旧一片漆黑——也许正是这一点，最先让吉米生出一个宽慰的念头：这位不速之客，同样巴望着避开众人的耳目。慌乱之初，他想当然地认定，这屋里动机经不起盘查的，只他一人。可如今，他安然藏身楼廊，楼下望不见他，便有了闲工夫来琢磨来人的举动，并从中得出结论。

一个正派人进门，头一件事必定是开灯。而且一个正派人，也断不至于走得这般蹑手蹑脚。吉米俯在栏杆上，竭力望穿黑暗，渐渐看清了：这里头正在进行某种见不得人的勾当；而这结论刚一落定，他的思路又往前一跃，猜出了底下那位脚步轻柔之人的身份。除了他的老朋友威斯比奇勋爵——「弟兄们」口中的「绅士杰克」——再无别人。叫他诧异的是，自己方才竟没想到这一层。随即更叫他诧异的是，就在几个钟头之前，两人还在这同一间屋里通过话，「绅士杰克」竟还敢铤而走险，来闯这一趟营生。

就在此刻，黑暗里透出一线光亮，仿佛有人擦亮了一根火柴。楼下那人用上了手电筒，吉米这下看得真切了。他猜得没错，正是威斯比奇勋爵。他正跪在保险柜跟前。他对保险柜干了些什么，吉米看不见，因为那人的身子挡着；可手电筒的光照在他脸上，映出一张狰狞而专注的面孔，同勋爵大人惯常呈给世人的那副和蔼里透着几分空空洞洞的面具，判若两人。吉米正望着，他视野外那片光池里有了动静。「绅士杰克」满意地低呼了一声，随即吉米看见保险柜的门荡开了。空气里弥漫着一股刺鼻的烧灼金属味。这类事体吉米并非行家，但现代窃贼及其手法，他也不时在书报上读到过，于是他料定：一柄氧乙炔吹管方才在此效过力，那火焰切起钢铁来，恰如餐刀切奶酪。

威斯比奇勋爵把手电筒的光探进敞开的保险柜，一只手伸进去，又抽了出来，攥着一样东西。他小心翼翼、战战兢兢地把那物件捧弄了一阵，郑重其事地放进胸前的口袋。随后他直起身来，关掉手电，移步向窗口走去，其余的家伙什儿都撂在敞开的保险柜旁。他解开门窗板，又拨起了窗子的插销。到这一步，吉米觉得，出面的时机到了。

===Original===

“Tut, tut!” he said in a tone of mild reproof.

The effect of the rebuke on Lord Wisbeach was remarkable. He jumped convulsively away from the window, then, revolving on his own axis, flashed the torch into every corner of the room.

“Who’s that?” he gasped.

“Conscience!” said Jimmy.

===Chinese===

「啧，啧！」他用温和责备的口气说道。

这声训斥在威斯比奇勋爵身上产生的效果，煞是可观。他一个痉挛，从窗口跳开，接着原地打转，手电筒的光扫遍了屋里每一个角落。

「谁在那儿？」他倒抽一口气。

「良心！」吉米说。

===Original===

Lord Wisbeach had overlooked the gallery in his researches. He now turned his torch upwards. The light flooded the gallery on the opposite side of the room from where Jimmy stood. There was a pistol in Gentleman Jack’s hand now. It followed the torch uncertainly.

Jimmy, lying flat on the gallery floor, spoke again.

“Throw that gun away, and the torch, too,” he said. “I’ve got you covered!”

The torch flashed above his head, but the raised edge of the gallery rail protected him.

===Chinese===

威斯比奇勋爵先前的搜查漏掉了楼廊。此刻他把手电筒扬了起来。光一下子灌满了屋子另一侧的楼廊——吉米站的是相反的一边。「绅士杰克」手里如今多了一把手枪，跟着手电筒犹犹豫豫地转。

吉米平伏在楼廊地板上，又开了口。

「把枪扔了，手电筒也一块儿扔，」他说，「我的枪口可正罩着你呢！」

手电筒的光在他头顶上一晃，好在楼廊栏杆翘起的边沿护住了他。

===Original===

“I’ll give you five seconds. If you haven’t dropped that gun by then, I shall shoot!”

As he began to count, Jimmy heartily regretted that he had allowed his appreciation of the dramatic to lead him into this situation. It would have been so simple to have roused the house in a prosaic way and avoided this delicate position. Suppose his bluff did not succeed. Suppose the other still clung to his pistol at the end of the five seconds. He wished that he had made it ten instead. Gentleman Jack was an enterprising person, as his previous acts had showed. He might very well decide to take a chance. He might even refuse to believe that Jimmy was armed. He had only Jimmy’s word for it. Perhaps he might be as deficient in simple faith as he had proved to be in Norman blood! Jimmy lingered lovingly over his count.

“Four!” he said reluctantly.

There was a breathless moment. Then, to Jimmy’s unspeakable relief, gun and torch dropped simultaneously to the floor. In an instant Jimmy was himself again.

===Chinese===

「我给你五秒钟。到时候枪还没撒手，我可要开枪了！」

开始计数的时候，吉米打心眼儿里懊悔，不该由着那股讲究戏剧效果的雅兴，把自己领进了这般境地。若用平淡无奇的法子唤醒全宅，原是何等省事，也不至于落进这个微妙的处境。万一他的虚张声势不灵呢？万一五秒数完，对方仍死攥着手枪不放呢？他悔的是方才没说十秒。「绅士杰克」是个敢作敢为的角色，先前的种种行状已有明证。他很可能决定赌上一把。他甚至可能压根不信吉米手里有枪。凭的不过只是吉米的一面之词。没准儿他这人连朴素的信任也缺斤短两，正如事实证明他缺的是诺曼血统！吉米数起数来缠绵不舍，一个个数字都舍不得撒手。

「四！」他老大不情愿地说。

有一瞬间人人屏息。随后，枪和手电筒同时落了地，吉米那颗悬着的心，松得无以言表。一转眼，吉米又是吉米了。

===Original===

“Go and stand with your face to that wall,” he said crisply. “Hold your hands up!”

“Why?”

“I’m going to see how many more guns you’ve got.”

“I haven’t another.”

“I’d like to make sure of that for myself. Get moving!”

===Chinese===

「过去，脸冲着那面墙站好，」他干脆利落地说。「把手举起来！」

「凭什么？」

「我要查查你身上还藏着几把枪。」

「再没第二把了。」

「这话我要亲自验一验。这就走！」

===Original===

Gentleman Jack reluctantly obeyed. When he had reached the wall, Jimmy came down. He switched on the lights. He felt in the other’s pockets, and almost at once encountered something hard and metallic.

He shook his head reproachfully.

“You are very loose and inaccurate in your statements,” he said. “Why all these weapons? I didn’t raise my boy to be a soldier! Now you can turn around and put your hands down.”

Gentleman Jack’s appeared to be a philosophical nature. The chagrin consequent upon his failure seemed to have left him. He sat on the arm of a chair and regarded Jimmy without apparent hostility. He even smiled a faint smile.

===Chinese===

「绅士杰克」老大不情愿地照办了。他走到墙边站定，吉米便下了楼廊。他打开电灯，伸手往对方口袋里一摸，几乎立刻就碰上了一件坚硬的金属物件。

他摇了摇头，满面责备之色。

「你说话可真是不严不实、马马虎虎，」他说。「搞这么多家伙干什么？我可不是把我家小子养大去当兵的！好了，你可以转过身来，把手放下了。」

「绅士杰克」似乎天生一副哲人脾性。失手带来的懊恼仿佛已经离他而去。他坐在一张椅子的扶手上，望着吉米，看不出丝毫敌意。他甚至还淡淡一笑。

===Original===

“I thought I had fixed you, he said. You must have been smarter than I took you for. I never supposed you would get on to that drink and pass it up.”

Understanding of an incident which had perplexed him came to Jimmy.

“Was it you who put that highball in my room? Was it doped?”

“Didn’t you know?”

“Well,” said Jimmy, “I never knew before that virtue got its reward so darned quick in this world. I rejected that highball not because I suspected it but out of pure goodness, because I had made up my mind that I was through with all that sort of thing.”

===Chinese===

「我还当我把你料理妥帖了呢，」他说。「你一定比我想象的精明。我压根儿没料到，你竟识破了那杯酒，没有沾唇。」

吉米恍然读懂了一桩先前令他纳闷的事。

「搁在我房里那杯海波酒，是你送的？里头下了药？」

「你不知道？」

「咳，」吉米说，「我从前竟不知道，美德在这世上的报偿来得这么快。我回绝那杯海波酒，可不是因为起了疑心，而是出于纯粹的良知善性——因为我已经打定主意，跟那一类东西一刀两断了。」

===Original===

His companion laughed. If Jimmy had had a more intimate acquaintance with the resourceful individual whom the “boys” called Gentleman Jack, he would have been disquieted by that laugh. It was an axiom among those who knew him well, that when Gentleman Jack chuckled in the reflective way, he generally had something unpleasant up his sleeve.

“It’s your lucky night,” said Gentleman Jack.

“It looks like it.”

“Well, it isn’t over yet.”

“Very nearly. You had better go and put that test-tube back in what is left of the safe now. Did you think I had forgotten it?”

===Chinese===

他的对头笑了。倘若吉米同这位被「弟兄们」唤作「绅士杰克」的足智多谋之士交情更深些，这一笑准会让他心里发毛。深知此君底细的人们中间，通行着一条公理：「绅士杰克」每每一面若有所思地嘿嘿轻笑，袖管里多半就藏着什么不妙的东西。

「今儿晚上是你走运，」「绅士杰克」说。

「看样子是。」

「嘿，还没完呢。」

「差不多了。你最好这就过去，把那支试管放回保险柜剩下的残骸里去。莫不是以为我把它忘了？」

===Original===

“What test-tube?”

“Come, come, old friend! The one filled with Partridge’s explosive, which you have in your breast-pocket.”

Gentleman Jack laughed again. Then he moved towards the safe.

“Place it gently on the top shelf,” said Jimmy.

The next moment every nerve in his body was leaping and quivering. A great shout split the air. Gentleman Jack, apparently insane, was giving tongue at the top of his voice.

===Chinese===

「什么试管？」

「行了行了，老朋友！就是装着帕特里奇特的那支，此刻正在你胸前的口袋里。」

「绅士杰克」又笑了。随后他朝保险柜走去。

「轻轻地搁到最上一层架子上，」吉米说。

说时迟那时快，他全身的神经都狂跳乱颤起来。一声大喊划破长空。「绅士杰克」显然是疯了，正扯开顶格的嗓门狂呼大喊。

===Original===

“Help! Help! Help!”

The conversation having been conducted up to this point in undertones, the effect of this unexpected uproar was like an explosion. The cries seemed to echo round the room and shake the very walls. For a moment Jimmy stood paralysed, staring feebly; then there was a sudden deafening increase in the din. Something living seemed to writhe and jump in his hand. He dropped it incontinently, and found himself gazing in a stupefied way at a round, smoking hole in the carpet. Such had been the effect of Gentleman Jack’s unforeseen outburst that he had quite forgotten that he held the revolver, and he had been unfortunate enough at this juncture to pull the trigger.

There was a sudden rush and a swirl of action. Something hit Jimmy under the chin. He staggered back, and when he had recovered himself found himself looking into the muzzle of the revolver which had nearly blown a hole in his foot a moment back. The sardonic face of Gentleman Jack smiled grimly over the barrel.

“I told you the night wasn’t over yet!” he said.

===Chinese===

「救命啊！救命啊！救命啊！」

方才这场谈话一直是压着嗓子进行的，这突如其来的喧嚣炸开，效果不啻一声爆响。喊声仿佛在屋里四处回荡，震得墙壁簌簌发抖。有一瞬间吉米僵在当地，茫然瞪视；紧接着喧声陡然又拔高一层，震耳欲聋。他手里仿佛有个活物在扭动蹦跳。他当下便撒了手，回过神来，正呆呆望着地毯上一个圆圆的、还在冒烟的窟窿。「绅士杰克」这出乎意料的发作，害得他把攥着左轮枪这茬忘了个干净，偏偏在这个节骨眼上，倒霉催地扣了扳机。

一阵抢攻，一团乱影。什么东西结结实实打在吉米下巴底下。他踉跄倒退，等稳住神，发现自己正对着那柄左轮的枪口——方才它险些在他脚上轰出一个窟窿。「绅士杰克」那张讥诮的脸，正伏在枪管上头狞笑。

「我说过，这一晚还没完呢！」他说。

===Original===

The blow under the chin had temporarily dulled Jimmy’s mentality. He stood, swallowing and endeavouring to pull himself together and to get rid of a feeling that his head was about to come off. He backed to the desk and steadied himself against it.

As he did so, a voice from behind him spoke.

“Whassall this?”

He turned his head. A curious procession was filing in through the open French window. First came Mr. Crocker, still wearing his hideous mask; then a heavily bearded individual with round spectacles, who looked like an automobile coming through a haystack; then Ogden Ford, and finally a sturdy, determined-looking woman with glittering but poorly coordinated eyes, who held a large revolver in her unshaking right hand and looked the very embodiment of the modern female who will stand no nonsense. It was part of the nightmare-like atmosphere which seemed to brood inexorably over this particular night that this person looked to Jimmy exactly like the parlourmaid who had come to him in this room in answer to the bell and who had sent his father to him. Yet how could it be she? Jimmy knew little of the habits of parlourmaids, but surely they did not wander about with revolvers in the small hours?

===Chinese===

下巴底下那一记，暂时把吉米的神志打钝了。他站在那里，咽着唾沫，努力振作精神，驱散那种脑袋眼看就要搬家的感觉。他退到写字台前，扶着台沿稳住身形。

正这时，背后有个声音开了腔。

「这……这是怎么了？」

他扭过头去。一支古怪的队伍正从敞开的落地长窗鱼贯而入。头里是克罗克先生，脸上还罩着那副狰狞的面具；随后是一个大胡子、圆眼镜的人物，那模样活像一辆刚从干草堆里拱出来的汽车；再后是奥格登·福特；压尾是一位结结实实、神情果决的妇人，一双眼睛精光四射，只是两只不大听调遣，右手稳稳端着一柄大号左轮，通身俨然是「绝不容人废话」的现代女性之化身。这天夜里仿佛有一层梦魇般的气氛，执拗地盘踞在一切之上——其中一桩便是：此人在吉米眼里，活脱脱就是先前应铃到这间屋里来过、又把他父亲给他打发来的那个客厅侍女。可她又怎么会是她呢？客厅侍女的习性，吉米所知寥寥，但她们总不至于深更半夜提着左轮枪到处巡游吧？

===Original===

While he endeavoured feverishly to find reason in this chaos, the door opened and a motley crowd, roused from sleep by the cries, poured in. Jimmy, turning his head back again to attend to this invasion, perceived Mrs. Pett, Ann, two or three of the geniuses, and Willie Partridge, in various stages of negligee and babbling questions.

The woman with the pistol, assuming instant and unquestioned domination of the assembly, snapped out an order.

“Shutatdoor!”

Somebody shut the door.

“Now, whassall this?” she said, turning to Gentleman Jack.

===Chinese===

他正抓耳挠腮地想在这团乱麻里理出个道理，房门开了，一群被喊声惊醒的七长八短的人涌了进来。吉米把头又扭回去应付这路入侵，看见佩特太太、安、两三位天才，还有威利·帕特里奇，睡衣各自穿到了不同的进度，嘴里七嘴八舌地问着话。
