那位持枪的妇人，当仁不让、不容分说地接管了全场的统治权，厉声喝出一道命令。

「关……关上门！」

有人把门关上了。

「好了，这……这到底是怎么回事？」她转向「绅士杰克」，说道。

## XXIII / 二十三

Stirring Times for the Petts / 佩特家的风云时刻

===Original===

Gentleman Jack had lowered his revolver, and was standing waiting to explain all, with the insufferable look of the man who is just going to say that he has only done his duty and requires no thanks.

“Who are you?” he said.

“Nev’ min’ who I am!” said Miss Trimble curtly. “Siz Pett knows who I am.”

“I hope you won’t be offended, Lord Wisbeach,” said Mrs. Pett from the group by the door. “I engaged a detective to help you. I really thought you could not manage everything by yourself. I hope you do not mind.”

===Chinese===

「绅士杰克」已经放低了左轮枪，站在那儿等着把一切解释清楚，脸上挂着一副叫人受不了的神气——正是那种马上就要宣称「自己不过尽了本分、无需言谢」的人所特有的神气。

「你是谁？」他说。

「甭管我是谁！」特琳布尔小姐没好气地说。「佩特太太认得我。」

「希望您别见怪，威斯比奇勋爵，」佩特太太从门边那群人里说道。「我雇了一位侦探来帮您的忙。我实在觉得，这么多事您一个人照应不过来。希望您不介意。」

===Original===

“Not at all, Mrs. Pett. Very wise.”

“I’m so glad to hear you say so.”

“An excellent move.”

Miss Trimble broke in on these amiable exchanges.

===Chinese===

「一点儿也不，佩特太太。高明之至。」

「听您这么说，我太高兴了。」

「一步好棋。」

特琳布尔小姐打断了这一番友好的寒暄。

===Original===

“Whassall this? Howjer mean﻿—help me?”

“Lord Wisbeach most kindly offered to do all he could to protect my nephew’s explosive,” said Mrs. Pett.

Gentleman Jack smiled modestly.

“I hope I have been of some slight assistance! I think I came down in the nick of time. Look!” He pointed to the safe. “He had just got it open! Luckily I had my pistol with me. I covered him, and called for help. In another moment he would have got away.”

===Chinese===

「这算啥名堂？你咋说——帮你？」

「威斯比奇勋爵好意主动提出，要竭尽所能保护我外甥的炸药。」佩特太太说。

「绅士杰克」谦逊地一笑。

「但愿我帮上了一点儿小忙！我看我来得正是时候。瞧！」他指了指保险柜。「他刚把柜门弄开！幸亏我随身带着手枪。我用枪逼住了他，又喊人来帮忙。再晚一步，他就跑掉了。」

===Original===

Miss Trimble crossed to the safe and inspected it with a frown, as if she disliked it. She gave a grunt and returned to her place by the window.

“Made good job ’f it!” was her comment.

Ann came forward. Her face was glowing and her eyes shone.

“Do you mean to say that you found Jimmy breaking into the safe? I never heard anything so absurd!”

===Chinese===

特琳布尔小姐走到保险柜跟前，皱着眉头把它端详了一番，仿佛很看它不顺眼。她哼了一声，回到窗边自己的位置上。

「活儿干得挺利索！」她评点了一句。

安走上前来。她满面红光，双眼发亮。

「照这么说，是你发现吉米在撬保险柜了？这么荒唐的事，我还是头一回听说！」

===Original===

Mrs. Pett intervened.

“This is not James Crocker, Ann! This man is an impostor, who came into the house in order to steal Willie’s invention.” She looked fondly at Gentleman Jack. “Lord Wisbeach told me so. He only pretended to recognise him this afternoon.”

A low gurgle proceeded from the open mouth of little Ogden. The proceedings bewildered him. The scene he had overheard in the library between the two men had made it clear to him that Jimmy was genuine and Lord Wisbeach a fraud, and he could not understand why Jimmy did not produce his proofs as before. He was not aware that Jimmy’s head was only just beginning to clear from the effects of the blow on the chin. Ogden braced himself for resolute lying in the event of Jimmy calling him as a witness. But he did not intend to have his little business proposition dragged into the open.

===Chinese===

佩特太太出面干预了。

「这位不是詹姆斯·克罗克，安！这人是个冒名顶替的骗子，进这个家门，图的就是偷威利的发明。」她慈爱地望着「绅士杰克」。「威斯比奇勋爵亲口告诉我的。今天下午，他不过是装作认出了他。」

小奥格登张着嘴，喉咙里发出一声低低的咕噜。眼下的种种，把他闹糊涂了。他在书房里偷听到那两个月人的一场对话，早让他心里透亮：吉米是真货，威斯比奇勋爵是假货；他不明白的是，吉米怎么不像先前那样拿出证据来。他不知道，吉米的脑袋挨了下巴上那一拳，药劲儿这才刚开始散去。万一吉米点他出来作证，奥格登已打定主意，撒谎撒到底。只是他那桩小小的生意筹划，他可不想叫人拖到大庭广众之下去。

===Original===

Ann was looking at Jimmy with horror-struck eyes. For the first time it came to her how little she knew of him and how very likely it was﻿—in the face of the evidence it was almost certain﻿—that he should have come to the house with the intention of stealing Willie’s explosive. She fought against it, but a voice seemed to remind her that it was he who had suggested the idea of posing as Jimmy Crocker. She could not help remembering how smoothly and willingly he had embarked on the mad scheme. But had it been so mad? Had it not been a mere cloak for this other venture? If Lord Wisbeach had found him in this room, with the safe blown open, what other explanation could there be?

And then, simultaneously with her conviction that he was a criminal, came the certainty that he was the man she loved. It had only needed the spectacle of him in trouble to make her sure. She came to his side with the vague idea of doing something to help him, of giving him her support. Once there, she found that there was nothing to do and nothing to say. She put her hand on his, and stood waiting helplessly for she knew not what.

It was the touch of her fingers which woke Jimmy from his stupor. He came to himself almost with a jerk. He had been mistily aware of what had been said, but speech had been beyond him. Now, quite suddenly, he was a whole man once more. He threw himself into the debate with energy.

===Chinese===

安正用惊骇的眼神望着吉米。她头一回意识到，自己对他了解得何其之少；也头一回意识到，他完全可能是揣着偷威利炸药的图谋进这门的——面对眼前的证据，这几乎就是板上钉钉。她极力抗拒这念头，可有个声音仿佛在提醒她：出主意假扮吉米·克罗克的，正是他本人。她不由得记起，当初一头扎进那疯狂计划时，他是何等顺溜、何等情愿。可那当真只是疯狂吗？何尝不是给底下这桩勾当披的一件外衣？倘若威斯比奇勋爵是在这间屋里、守着轰开的保险柜撞见他的，除此之外，还能有什么别的解释？

就在这时，与「他是个罪人」的确信一同降临的，是另一桩确信：他就是她爱的那个男人。原来只消看他落一回难，她便认准了。她走到他身边，心里模模糊糊想着帮上点什么，给他撑撑腰。可一到跟前，她发现无事可做，也无话可说。她把手搭在他的手上，站在那里，无可奈何地等着——等什么，她自己也不知道。

正是她指尖的触碰，把吉米从恍惚中唤醒了。他几乎是猛地一下回过神来。方才众人说些什么，他影影绰绰有点知觉，可要开口说话，万万不能。此刻，说时迟那时快，他又是一条完完整整的好汉了。他精神抖擞地投入了辩论。

===Original===

“Good Heavens!” he cried. “You’re all wrong. I found *him* blowing open the safe!”

Gentleman Jack smiled superciliously.

“A likely story, what! I mean to say, it’s a bit thin!”

“Ridiculous!” said Mrs. Pett. She turned to Miss Trimble with a gesture. “Arrest that man!”

===Chinese===

「老天爷！」他嚷道。「你们全弄拧了。撬保险柜的，我撞见的是*他*！」

「绅士杰克」傲然一笑。

「编得倒像真的，是吧！我是说，这也未免太站不住脚了！」

「荒唐！」佩特太太说。她朝特琳布尔小姐一挥手。「把那人抓起来！」

===Original===

“Wait a mom’nt,” replied that clearheaded maiden, picking her teeth thoughtfully with the muzzle of her revolver. “Wait mom’nt. Gotta look ’nto this. Hear both these guys’ st’ries.”

“Really,” said Gentleman Jack suavely, “it seems somewhat absurd﻿—”

“Ney’ mind how ’bsurd ’t sounds,” returned the fair Trimble rebukingly. “You close y’r face ’n lissen t’ me. Thass all you’ve gotta do.”

“I know you didn’t do it!” cried Ann, tightening her hold on Jimmy’s arm.

===Chinese===

「等一小会儿，」那位头脑清明的小姐答道，一边若有所思地用手枪口剔着牙。「等一会儿。这事得查一查。两个家伙的说法，都得听听。」

「说真的，」「绅士杰克」温文尔雅地说，「这么办未免有点儿荒唐——」

「甭管听着多离谱，」千娇百媚的特琳布尔训诫般答道，「闭上你的嘴，听着我说。你要做的就这一件事。」

「我知道这不是你干的！」安喊道，把吉米的胳膊抓得更紧了。

===Original===

“Less ’f it, please. Less ’f it!” Miss Trimble removed the pistol from her mouth and pointed it at Jimmy. “What’ve you to say? Talk quick!”

“I happened to be down there﻿—”

“Why?” asked Miss Trimble, as if she had touched off a bomb.

Jimmy stopped short. He perceived difficulties in the way of explanation.

“I happened to be down there,” he resumed stoutly, “and that man came into the room with an electric torch and a blowpipe and began working on the safe﻿—”

===Chinese===

「少来这套，劳驾。少来这套！」特琳布尔小姐把手枪从嘴边挪开，指住了吉米。「你有什么要说的？快讲！」

「我碰巧正好在楼下——」

「为啥下去？」特琳布尔小姐问，那口气像点着了一颗炸弹。

吉米戛然收住。他看出了解释路上的一重重难关。

「我碰巧正好在楼下，」他又硬着头皮说了下去，「那人拿着手电筒和吹管进了屋，对着保险柜动起了手——」

===Original===

The polished tones of Gentleman Jack cut in on his story.

“Really now, is it worth while?” He turned to Miss Trimble. “I came down here, having heard a noise. I did not *happen* to be here for some unexplained purpose. I was lying awake and something attracted my attention. As Mrs. Pett knows, I was suspicious of this worthy and expected him to make an attempt on the explosive at any moment: so I took my pistol and crept downstairs. When I got here, the safe was open and this man making for the window.”

Miss Trimble scratched her chin caressingly with the revolver, and remained for a moment in thought. Then she turned to Jimmy like a striking rattlesnake.

“Y’ gotta pull someth’g better th’n that,” she said. “I got y’r number. Y’re caught with th’ goods.”

===Chinese===

「绅士杰克」那圆滑光润的嗓音，把他的故事拦腰截断。

「说真的，这又何苦呢？」他转向特琳布尔小姐。「我是听见了响动才下来的。我可没有怀揣什么说不清道不明的目的*碰巧*待在这儿。我本来醒着没睡，有点动静引起了我的注意。佩特太太知道，我早就在提防这位正人君子，料他随时会打那炸药的主意：于是我带上手枪，蹑手蹑脚下了楼。我到的时候，保险柜已经开了，这人正扑向窗口。」

特琳布尔小姐拿左轮枪爱抚地搔着下巴，出了一会儿神。随后她倏地转向吉米，活像一条出击的响尾蛇。

「你另编点儿好的吧，这个不顶事儿，」她说。「你的斤两我掂得清清楚楚。这叫人赃并获。」

===Original===

“No!” cried Ann.

“Yes!” said Mrs. Pett. “The thing is obvious.”

“I think the best thing I can do,” said Gentleman Jack smoothly, “is to go and telephone for the police.”

“You think of everything, Lord Wisbeach,” said Mrs. Pett.

“Not at all,” said his lordship.

===Chinese===

「不！」安喊道。

「是的！」佩特太太说。「事情明摆着。」

「我看，我此刻顶好做的一件事，」「绅士杰克」圆滑地说，「就是去打电话叫警察。」

「您真是事事想得周到，威斯比奇勋爵。」佩特太太说。

「哪里哪里。」勋爵大人说。

===Original===

Jimmy watched him moving to the door. At the back of his mind there was a dull feeling that he could solve the whole trouble if only he could remember one fact which had escaped him. The effects of the blow he had received still handicapped him. He struggled to remember, but without result. Gentleman Jack reached the door and opened it: and as he did so a shrill yapping, hitherto inaudible because of the intervening oak and the raised voices within, made itself heard from the passage outside. Gentleman Jack closed the door with a hasty bang.

“I say that dog’s out there!” he said plaintively.

The scratching of Aida’s busy feet on the wood bore out his words. He looked about him, baffled.

“That dog’s out there!” he repeated gloomily.

===Chinese===

吉米望着他朝门口挪动。他脑海深处有个发闷的感觉，挥之不去：只要想起一件眼下溜掉了的事实，这满盘麻烦便可一举了结。挨的那一拳，效力至今还绊着他的腿。他拼命去想，却毫无结果。「绅士杰克」走到门边，拉开门：就在拉开的一瞬，一阵尖利的狗吠从外头过道里钻出声来——先前隔着一扇橡木门，加上屋里人声又高，一直听不见。「绅士杰克」慌忙砰的一声摔上了门。

「我说，那条狗就在外头！」他哭丧着脸说。

阿伊达那四只忙碌的爪子挠着木地板的声响，替他的话作了证。他环顾四周，一筹莫展。

「那条狗就在外头！」他没精打采地又说了一遍。

===Original===

Something seemed to give way in Jimmy’s brain. The simple fact which had eluded him till now sprang into his mind.

“Don’t let that man get out!” he cried. “Good Lord! I’ve only just remembered. You say you found me breaking into the safe! You say you heard a noise and came down to investigate! Well, then, what’s that test-tube of the explosive doing in your breast-pocket?” He swung round to Miss Trimble. “You needn’t take my word or his word. There’s a much simpler way of finding out who’s the real crook. Search us both.” He began to turn out his pockets rapidly. “Look here﻿—and here﻿—and here! Now ask him to do the same!”

He was pleased to observe a spasm pass across Gentleman Jack’s hitherto composed countenance. Miss Trimble was eyeing the latter with sudden suspicion.

“Thasso!” she said. “Say, Bill, I’ve f’gott’n y’r name﻿—’sup to you to show us! Less’ve a look ’t what y’ got inside there.”

===Chinese===

吉米的脑袋里，仿佛有什么东西轰然断裂。那个至今躲着他绕着走的简单事实，一下子蹦进了脑海。

「别放那人出去！」他喊道。「老天爷！我这才想起来。你说你撞见我在撬保险柜！你说你听见响动，下来查探！好啊，那你胸袋里那管炸药试管，算怎么回事？」他霍地转向特琳布尔小姐。「我的话也罢，他的话也罢，您都不必信。要查明谁是真正的骗子，有个简单得多的法子。把我们俩都搜一搜。」他动手飞快地翻起自己的口袋。「瞧这儿——还有这儿——还有这儿！现在叫他照样来一遍！」

他乐见一阵痉挛掠过「绅士杰克」那张至此纹丝不乱的脸。特琳布尔小姐正用陡然生疑的目光，打量着后者。

「这就对喽！」她说。「哎，比尔，你贵姓来着，我忘了——这回该你亮亮了！让我们开开眼，瞧瞧你那怀里揣的啥。」

===Original===

Gentleman Jack drew himself up haughtily.

“I really could not agree to﻿—”

Mrs. Pett interrupted indignantly.

“I never heard of such a thing! Lord Wisbeach is an old friend﻿—”

===Chinese===

「绅士杰克」倨傲地挺直了身子。

「恕我实在不能同意——」

佩特太太愤然插话。

「我从没听说过这种事！威斯比奇勋爵是多年的老朋友——」

===Original===

“Less’f it!” ordered Miss Trimble, whose left eye was now like the left eye of a basilisk. “Y’ *gotta* show us, Bill, so b’ quick ’bout ’t!”

A tired smile played over Gentleman Jack’s face. He was the bored aristocrat, mutely protesting against something that “wasn’t done.” He dipped his slender fingers into his pocket. Then, drawing out the test-tube, and holding it up, he spoke with a drawling calm for which even Jimmy could not help admiring him.

“All right! If I’m done, I’m done!”

===Chinese===

「省省吧！」特琳布尔小姐喝令，她的左眼此刻俨然蛇怪的左眼。「你*非*亮不可，比尔，麻利点儿！」

一丝倦怠的笑意在「绅士杰克」脸上漾开。他是那位百无聊赖的贵族，正无声地抗议着一桩「上流社会干不出来」的事。他把修长的手指探进口袋。随后，掏出那支试管，高高举起，慢条斯理、心平气和地开了口——这份从容，连吉米都不能不佩服。

「行！栽了就是栽了！」

===Original===

The sensation caused by his action and his words was of the kind usually described as profound. Mrs. Pett uttered a strangled shriek. Willie Partridge yelped like a dog. Sharp exclamations came simultaneously from each of the geniuses.

Gentleman Jack waited for the clamour to subside. Then he resumed his gentle drawl.

“But I’m not done,” he explained. “I’m going out now through that window. And if anybody tries to stop me, it will be his﻿—or her﻿—” he bowed politely to Miss Trimble﻿—“last act in the world. If anyone makes a move to stop me, I shall drop this test-tube and blow the whole damned place to pieces.”

If his first speech had made a marked impression on his audience, his second paralysed them. A silence followed as of the tomb. Only the yapping of the dog Aida refused to be stilled.

===Chinese===

他的举动与言语所引发的轰动，属于通常被形容为「深刻」的那一类。佩特太太发出一声噎在喉咙里的尖叫。威利·帕特里奇狗吠似的叫了一声。各位天才不约而同，各自迸出一声尖厉的惊叹。

「绅士杰克」等喧腾平息下去。随后又续上了他那温和的拖腔。

「不过我还没栽呢，」他解释道。「我这就从那扇窗子出去。谁要是想拦我，那便是他——或是她——」他朝特琳布尔小姐彬彬有礼地一躬身——「在这世上做的最后一桩事。谁敢动一动拦我，我就撒手摔了这试管，把这整个鬼地方炸个粉碎。」

如果说他头一番话已叫听众深受震动，第二番便把他们震瘫了。随之而来的是坟墓一般的寂静。唯独小狗阿伊达的尖叫，怎么也不肯平息。

===Original===

“Y’ stay where y’ are!” said Miss Trimble, as the speaker moved towards the window. She held the revolver poised, but for the first time that night﻿—possibly for the first time in her life﻿—she spoke irresolutely. Superbly competent woman though she was, here was a situation that baffled her.

Gentleman Jack crossed the room slowly, the test-tube held aloft between forefinger and thumb. He was level with Miss Trimble, who had lowered her revolver and had drawn to one side, plainly at a loss to know how to handle this unprecedented crisis, when the door flew open. For an instant the face of Howard Bemis, the poet, was visible.

“Mrs. Pett, I have telephoned﻿—”

Then another voice interrupted him.

“Yipe! Yipe! Yipe!”

===Chinese===

「你给我待在原地！」那位说话人朝窗口挪动时，特琳布尔小姐喝道。她举着手枪，摆好了架势，可这一晚头一回——兴许是她这辈子头一回——她说起话来没了决断。她纵是本领通天的干练女杰，眼前这个局面也叫她束手无策。

「绅士杰克」缓缓横穿房间，拇指食指之间高擎着那支试管。他走到与特琳布尔小姐并齐的地方——她已垂下手枪，闪在一旁，明摆着不知如何应付这场前所未有的危机——正在此时，门砰地弹开了。刹那间，露出诗人霍华德·比米斯的那张脸。

「佩特太太，电话我打过了——」

随后，另一个声音打断了他。

「汪！汪！汪！」

===Original===

Through the opening the dog Aida, rejoicing in the removal of the obstacle, raced like a fur muff mysteriously endowed with legs and a tongue. She tore across the room to where Gentleman Jack’s ankles waited invitingly. Ever since their first meeting she had wanted a fair chance at those ankles, but someone had always prevented her.

“Damn!” shouted Gentleman Jack.

The word was drowned in one vast cataclysm of noise. From every throat in the room there proceeded a shout, a shriek, or some other variety of cry, as the test-tube, slipping from between the victim’s fingers, described a parabola through the air.

Ann flung herself into Jimmy’s arms, and he held her tight. He shut his eyes. Even as he waited for the end the thought flashed through his mind that, if he must die, this was the manner of death which he would prefer.

===Chinese===

小狗阿伊达从门缝里窜了进来，为拦路障碍的解除而欢天喜地，一路飞奔，活像一只莫名生出了腿和舌头的皮草手筒。她横穿房间，直扑「绅士杰克」那双正殷勤恭候的脚踝。自打头一回见面，她就一直想寻个公平机会会一会那副脚踝，无奈总有人横加阻挠。

「该死！」「绅士杰克」大喝一声。

这一个字，随即淹没在一场天崩地裂的大喧嚣里。屋里每一副喉咙都迸出了呼喊、尖叫，或别的种种喊叫——与此同时，那支试管从苦主的指间滑脱，在半空划出一道抛物线。

安一头扑进吉米怀里，他紧紧搂住了她。他闭上眼睛。即便在静候末日降临的当口，仍有一个念头从他心头电闪而过：倘若非死不可，这般死法，正合他的心意。

===Original===

The test-tube crashed on the writing-desk, and burst into a million pieces.﻿ ﻿…

Jimmy opened his eyes. Things seemed to be much about the same as before. He was still alive. The room in which he stood was solid and intact. Nobody was in fragments. There was only one respect in which the scene differed from what it had been a moment before. Then, it had contained Gentleman Jack. Now it did not.

A great sigh seemed to sweep through the room. There was a long silence. Then, from the direction of the street, came the roar of a starting automobile. And at that sound the bearded man with the spectacles who had formed part of Miss Trimble’s procession uttered a wailing cry.

“Gee! He’s beat it in my bubble! And it was a hired one!”

===Chinese===

试管砸在写字台上，爆成了千百万片碎片。……

吉米睁开眼睛。四下里似乎与先前大同小异。他还活着。他立足的这间屋子结结实实，完好无损。没有谁碎成几瓣。眼前的光景，只有一处与片刻之前不同：方才，屋里还有一位「绅士杰克」；如今，没有了。

一声长叹似乎扫过整间屋子。继而是长长的寂静。然后，从临街的方向，传来一辆汽车发动的轰鸣。一听这声音，那位戴眼镜的胡子男人——特琳布尔小姐一行人马中的一员——发出一声哀号。

「哎呀！他坐上我的泡泡车跑啦！那还是租来的呢！」

===Original===

The words seemed to relieve the tension in the air. One by one the company became masters of themselves once more. Miss Trimble, that masterly woman, was the first to recover. She raised herself from the floor﻿—for with a confused idea that she would be safer there she had flung herself down﻿—and, having dusted her skirt with a few decisive dabs of her strong left hand, addressed herself once more to business.

“I let ’m bluff me with a fake bomb!” she commented bitterly. She brooded on this for a moment. “Say, shut th’t door ’gain, someone, and t’run this mutt out. I can’t think with th’t yapping going on.”

Mrs. Pett, pale and scared, gathered Aida into her arms. At the same time Ann removed herself from Jimmy’s. She did not look at him. She was feeling oddly shy. Shyness had never been a failing of hers, but she would have given much now to have been elsewhere.

Miss Trimble again took charge of the situation. The sound of the automobile had died away. Gentleman Jack had passed out of their lives. This fact embittered Miss Trimble. She spoke with asperity.

===Chinese===

这句话似乎疏解了空气里的紧张。众人一个接一个，重新做回了自己的主人。特琳布尔小姐，那位干练绝伦的女杰，头一个缓过劲来。她从地板上直起身——方才她凭着一团模糊的念头，觉得趴在那儿更保险，便一头扑倒了——用她那只强有力的左手三下五除二掸了掸裙子，重新投身正业。

「我竟让一颗假炸弹给唬住了！」她愤愤然评点。她就这事闷了片刻。「哎，哪位去把那扇门再关上，把这小畜生轰出去。它这么叫个没完，我没法儿想事。」

佩特太太脸色煞白、惊魂未定，把阿伊达搂进怀里。与此同时，安也从吉米的怀抱里抽身出来。她不看他。她心里生出一种异样的羞怯。羞怯从来不是她的毛病，可此刻，只要能挪到别的什么地方去，让她付出多大代价都行。

特琳布尔小姐再度接管了局面。汽车声已经远逝。「绅士杰克」从他们的生活里退场了。这个事实令特琳布尔小姐满怀郁愤。她语带尖酸。

===Original===

“Well, *he’s* gone!” she said acidly. “Now we can get down t’ cases again. Say!” She addressed Mrs. Pett, who started nervously. The experience of passing through the shadow of the valley of death and of finding herself in one piece instead of several thousand had robbed her of all her wonted masterfulness. “Say, list’n t’ me. There’s been a double game on here t’night. That guy that’s jus’ gone was th’ first part of th’ entertainment. Now we c’n start th’ sec’nd part. You see these ducks?” She indicated with a wave of the revolver Mr. Crocker and his bearded comrade. “They’ve been trying t’ kidnap y’r son!”

Mrs. Pett uttered a piercing cry.

“Oggie!”

“Oh, can it!” muttered that youth, uncomfortably. He foresaw awkward moments ahead, and he wished to concentrate his faculties entirely on the part he was to play in them. He looked sideways at Chicago Ed. In a few minutes, he supposed, Ed. would be attempting to minimise his own crimes, by pretending that he, Ogden, had invited him to come and kidnap him. Stout denial must be his weapon.

“I had m’ suspicions,” resumed Miss Trimble, “that someth’ng was goin’ t’ be pulled off tonight, ’nd I was waiting outside f’r it to break loose. This guy here,” she indicated the bearded plotter, who blinked deprecatingly through his spectacles, “h’s been waiting on the c’rner of th’ street for the last hour with ’n automobile. I’ve b’n watching him right along. I was onto h’s game! Well, just now out came the kid with this plug-ugly here.” She turned to Mr. Crocker. “Say you! Take off th’t mask. Let’s have a l’k at you!”

===Chinese===

「哼，*他*倒是跑啦！」她酸溜溜地说。「这就可以接着办正事了。哎！」她冲着佩特太太发话，后者神经质地一激灵。穿越死亡幽谷的阴影、又发觉自己囫囵一个而非碎成几千瓣——这番经历，把她素来的专断威风剥了个干净。「哎，听我说。今儿晚上，这宅子里演的是一出双份的戏。刚走的那位，是头一折。现在咱们开演第二折。瞧见这两位活宝了吗？」她用左轮枪一挥，指住克罗克先生和他的胡子同伙。「他们俩，一直在打你儿子的绑票主意！」

佩特太太发出一声穿云裂帛的尖叫。

「奥吉！」

「噢，闭嘴！」那位少年浑身不自在地嘟囔道。他预感到前头还有难堪的关口，只想把全副心思集中到自己届时要扮演的角色上。他斜眼瞟了瞟芝加哥·埃德。他料想，用不了几分钟，埃德就会图谋给自己的罪行做减法，装作是他奥格登请人家上门来绑自己的票。死不认账，必须成为他的武器。

「我早起了疑心，」特琳布尔小姐接着说，「琢磨着今儿晚上要出事儿，便在外头候着它发作。这位爷，」她指了指那位胡子同谋——那人透过镜片，一个劲儿惶恐地眨着眼，「在街角上守了整整一个钟头，还带了辆汽车。我一路把他盯到底。他那点把戏，我心里门儿清！果然，方才这小子和身边这位凶神一块儿出来了。」她转向克罗克先生。「喂，说你呢！摘了那面罩。让我们瞧瞧你的尊容！」

===Original===

Mr. Crocker reluctantly drew the cambric from his face.

“Goosh!” exclaimed Miss Trimble in strong distaste. “Say, ’ve you got some kind of a plague, or wh’t is it? Y’look like a coloured comic supplement!” She confronted the shrinking Mr. Crocker and ran a bony finger over his cheek. “Makeup!” she said, eyeing the stains disgustedly. “Grease paint! Goosh!”

“Skinner!” cried Mrs. Pett.

Miss Trimble scanned her victim more closely.

“So ’tis, if y’ do a bit ’f excavating.” She turned on the bearded one. “ ’nd I guess all this shrubbery is fake, ’f you come down to it!” She wrenched at the unhappy man’s beard. It came off in her hands, leaving a square chin behind it. “If this ain’t a wig, y’ll have a headache t’morrow,” observed Miss Trimble, weaving her fingers into his luxuriant head-covering and pulling. “Wish y’ luck! Ah! ’twas a wig. Gimme those spect’cles.” She surveyed the results of her handiwork grimly. “Say, Clarence,” she remarked, “y’re a wise guy. Y’ look handsomer with ’em on. Does anyone know *this* duck?”

===Chinese===

克罗克先生老大不情愿，把那块细麻布从脸上扯了下来。

「嗬！」特琳布尔小姐惊呼一声，满脸嫌恶。「哎，你是染了什么瘟病啊，还是怎么的？瞧你这模样，跟彩色滑稽增刊上跑下来的似的！」她迎住那缩成一团的克罗克先生，一根瘦骨嶙峋的手指在他脸颊上抹了一道。「化的妆！」她说，嫌恶地瞅着指头上的斑斑颜色。「油彩！嗬！」

「斯金纳！」佩特太太叫道。

特琳布尔小姐把她这位猎物端详得更仔细了。

「刨去表面那层，还真是。」她转向那大胡子。「再者说，你这满脸的灌木丛，较起真来，八成也是假的！」她朝那倒霉蛋的胡子猛地一拽。胡子应手落在她掌中，底下留下一方见棱见角的下巴。「这要不是假发，明儿你就该头疼了。」特琳布尔小姐说着，手指头织进他那头茂盛的头盖，使劲便拔。「祝你走运！啊！果然是假发。眼镜也交给我。」她冷冷检阅了自己的手艺。「哎，克拉伦斯，」她点评道，「你老兄是个人精。戴上眼镜倒俊些。*这一位*活宝，有人认得不？」

===Original===

“It is Mitchell,” said Mrs. Pett. “My husband’s physical instructor.”

Miss Trimble turned, and, walking to Jimmy, tapped him meaningly on the chest with her revolver.

“Say, this is gett’n interesting! This is where y’ ’xplain, y’ng man, how ’twas you happened to be down in this room when th’t crook who’s just gone was monkeyin’ with the safe. L’ks t’ me as if you were in with these two.”

===Chinese===

「是米切尔，」佩特太太说。「我丈夫的健身教练。」

特琳布尔小姐转过身，走到吉米跟前，用左轮枪意味深长地在他胸口点了点。

「哎，这可越来越有意思了！小伙子，轮到你交代了：方才跑掉的那骗子摆弄保险柜的时候，你怎么偏偏就在这间屋里。照我看，你跟这两位是一伙的。」

===Original===

A feeling of being on the verge of one of those crises which dot the smooth path of our lives came to Jimmy. To conceal his identity from Ann any longer seemed impossible. He was about to speak, when Ann broke in.

“Aunt Nesta,” she said, “I can’t let this go on any longer. Jerry Mitchell isn’t to blame. I told him to kidnap Ogden!”

There was an awkward silence. Mrs. Pett laughed nervously.

===Chinese===

吉米生出一种感觉：自己正站在那种危机之一的边缘——那种点缀在我们人生坦途上的危机。要对安继续隐瞒身份，看来已无可能。他正要开口，安抢先了一步。

「内斯塔婶婶，」她说，「这事我不能再瞒下去了。杰里·米切尔没有错。是我叫他绑架奥格登的！」

一阵尴尬的沉默。佩特太太神经质地笑了。

===Original===

“I think you had better go to bed, my dear child. You have had a severe shock. You are not yourself.”

“But it’s true! I did tell him, didn’t I, Jerry?”

“Say!” Miss Trimble silenced Jerry with a gesture. “You beat ’t back t’ y’r little bed, honey, like y’r aunt says. Y’ say y’ told this guy t’ steal th’ kid. Well, what about this here Skinner? Y’ didn’t tell *him*, did y’?”

“I﻿—I﻿—” Ann began confusedly. She was utterly unable to account for Skinner, and it made her task of explaining difficult.

===Chinese===

「我看你最好上床歇着去，亲爱的孩子。你受了大刺激。你这说的是胡话。」

「可这是真的！我确实跟他说了，是不是，杰里？」

「哎！」特琳布尔小姐一挥手，止住了杰里。「小甜心，照你婶婶说的，乖乖回你的小床去。你说你叫这位爷去偷这孩子。那么这位斯金纳又怎么说？*他*总不是你叫来的吧，嗯？」

「我——我——」安慌乱地开了头。她对斯金纳其人完全无从解释，这就叫她的说明任务难上加难。

===Original===

Jimmy came to the rescue. He did not like to think how Ann would receive the news, but for her own sake he must speak now. It would have required a harder-hearted man than himself to resist the mute pleading of his father’s grease-painted face. Mr. Crocker was a game sport: He would not have said a word without the sign from Jimmy, even to save himself from a night in prison, but he hoped that Jimmy would speak.

“It’s perfectly simple,” said Jimmy, with an attempt at airiness which broke down miserably under Miss Trimble’s eye. “Perfectly simple. I really am Jimmy Crocker, you know.” He avoided Ann’s gaze. “I can’t think what you are making all this fuss about.”

“Th’n why did y’ sit in at a plot to kidnap this boy?”

===Chinese===

吉米出面救场了。安听了这消息会作何感想，他不敢深想，可为着她好，此刻他必须开口。要抗拒他父亲那张油彩面孔的无声恳求，得比他心肠更硬的人才办得到。克罗克先生是条有担当的好汉：哪怕为免一晚上的牢狱之灾，没有吉米的示意，他也一个字不会说——可他巴望吉米开口。

「这事再简单不过，」吉米说，本想装出一副轻飘飘的劲头，可叫特琳布尔小姐的眼风一扫，惨兮兮地破了功。「再简单不过。实说了吧，我真是吉米·克罗克。」他避开安的目光。「我真想不通，你们各位这是大惊小怪个什么。」

「那你干吗掺和进绑架这小子的勾当？」

===Original===

“That, of course﻿—ha, ha!﻿—might seem at first sight to require a little explanation.”

“Y’ admit it, then?”

“Yes. As a matter of fact, I did have the idea of kidnapping Ogden. Wanted to send him to a dogs’ hospital, if you understand what I mean.” He tried to smile a conciliatory smile, but, encountering Miss Trimble’s left eye, abandoned the project. He removed a bead of perspiration from his forehead with his handkerchief. It struck him as a very curious thing that the simplest explanations were so often quite difficult to make. “Before I go any further, I ought to explain one thing. Skinner there is my father.”

Mrs. Pett gasped.

===Chinese===

「那个嘛，当然——哈，哈！——乍看之下，兴许是得稍微解释一下。」

「这么说，你认了？」

「认了。实话说，我确实起过绑架奥格登的念头。想把他送进狗医院，如果您明白我的意思。」他试着堆出一个息事宁人的笑，可一撞上特琳布尔小姐的左眼，便放弃了这项工程。他掏出手帕，抹去额上一粒汗珠。他忽然觉得，这是桩顶顶奇怪的事：最简单的解释，做起来往往最费劲。「往下讲之前，我该先说明一件事。那边那位斯金纳，是我父亲。」

佩特太太倒抽一口气。

===Original===

“Skinner was my sister’s butler in London.”

“In a way of speaking,” said Jimmy, “that is correct. It’s rather a long story. It was this way, you see.﻿ ﻿…”

Miss Trimble uttered an ejaculation of supreme contempt.

“I n’ver saw such a lot of babbl’ng crooks in m’ life! ’t beats me what y’ hope to get pulling this stuff. Say!” She indicated Mr. Crocker. “This guy’s wanted f’r something over in England. We’ve got h’s photographs ’n th’ office. If y’ ask me, he lit out with the spoons ’r something. Say!” She fixed one of the geniuses with her compelling eye. “ ’Bout time y’ made y’rself useful. Go’n call up th’ Astorbilt on th’ phone. There’s a dame there that’s been making the enquiries f’r this duck. She told Anderson’s﻿—and Anderson’s handed it on to us﻿—to call her up any hour of the day ’r night when they found him. You go get her on the wire and t’ll her t’ come right up here’n a taxi and identify h’m.”

===Chinese===

「斯金纳是我姐姐在伦敦的管家。」

「从某种说法上讲，」吉米说，「没错。这事说来话长。是这样的，您知道。……」

特琳布尔小姐发出一声轻蔑到极点的感叹。

「我这辈子也没见过这么一窝子嚼舌根的骗子！你们扯这套淡，图个什么，我真纳闷。哎！」她指了指克罗克先生。「这位爷，在英国犯了事儿，正被查拿。他的照片，我们事务所里存着呢。要叫我说，他是卷了人家几把银汤匙什么的跑的路。哎！」她那双不由分说的眼睛，钉住了其中一位天才。「也该轮到你派上点儿用场了。去，打电话到阿斯托比尔特饭店。那儿有位太太，一直在打听这位爷的下落。她托付过安德森事务所——安德森事务所又转到了我们手上——说是无论白天黑夜，几点找到人都给去个电话。你去把她接上线，叫她立马坐出租车上这儿来，认一认人。」

===Original===

The genius paused at the door.

“Whom shall I ask for?”

“Mrs. Crocker,” snapped Miss Trimble. “Siz Bingley Crocker. Tell her we’ve found th’ guy she’s been looking for!”

The genius backed out. There was a howl of anguish from the doorway.

===Chinese===

那位天才在门口停了停。

「请问找哪一位？」

「克罗克太太，」特琳布尔小姐脆生生地答。「宾利·克罗克太太。告诉她，她找的那位，我们逮着了！」

那位天才倒退着出去。门口传来一声痛切的哀嚎。

===Original===

“I *beg* your pardon!” said the genius.

“Can’t you look where you’re going!”

“I am exceedingly sorry﻿—”

“Brrh!”

===Chinese===

「实在*对不起*！」那位天才说。

「你走路不长眼睛吗！」

「我万分抱歉——」

「哼！」

===Original===

Mr. Pett entered the room, hopping. He was holding one slippered foot in his hand and appeared to be submitting it to some form of massage. It was plain that the usually mild and gentle little man was in a bad temper. He glowered round him at the company assembled.

“What the devil’s the matter here?” he demanded. “I stood it as long as I could, but a man can’t get a wink of sleep with this noise going on!”

“Yipe! Yipe! Yipe!” barked Aida from the shelter of Mrs. Pett’s arms.

Mr. Pett started violently.

“Kill that dog! Throw her out! Do *something* to her!”

Mrs. Pett was staring blankly at her husband. She had never seen him like this before. It was as if a rabbit had turned and growled at her. Coming on top of the crowded sensations of the night, it had the effect of making her feel curiously weak. In all her married life she had never known what fear was. She had coped dauntlessly with the late Mr. Ford, a man of a spirited temperament; and as for the mild Mr. Pett she had trampled on him. But now she felt afraid. This new Peter intimidated her.

===Chinese===

佩特先生单脚蹦着进了屋。他一只手托着那只穿拖鞋的脚，似乎正在对它施行某种按摩。明摆着，这位素来温良和善的小个子男人，正在气头上。他环视满屋众人，怒目而视。

「这屋里到底闹的什么鬼？」他质问道。「能忍的我都忍了，可这么个闹法，人连个囫囵觉都睡不成！」

「汪！汪！汪！」阿伊达躲在佩特太太的怀抱里，冲他吠个不停。

佩特先生猛地一激灵。

「弄死那条狗！把她扔出去！随你们对她*怎么着*都行！」

佩特太太怔怔地瞪着丈夫。她从未见过他这副模样。这情形，好比一只家兔掉过头来朝她咆哮。叠加上今夜种种应接不暇的感受，这一下叫她生出一种说不出的虚弱。成婚这些年，她从来不知恐惧为何物。对付已故的福特先生——一位性烈如火的男子——她应付得毫无惧色；至于温厚的佩特先生，更是被她踩在脚下。可此刻，她感到了怕。这个崭新的彼得，叫她胆寒。

## XXIV / 二十四

Sensational Turning of a Worm / 一条虫豸的轰动转身

===Original===

To this remarkable metamorphosis in Mr. Peter Pett several causes had contributed. In the first place, the sudden dismissal of Jerry Mitchell had obliged him to go two days without the physical exercises to which his system had become accustomed, and this had produced a heavy, irritable condition of body and mind. He had brooded on the injustice of his lot until he had almost worked himself up to rebellion. And then, as sometimes happened with him when he was out of sorts, a touch of gout came to add to his troubles. Being a patient man by nature, he might have borne up against these trials, had he been granted an adequate night’s rest. But, just as he had dropped off after tossing restlessly for two hours, things had begun to happen noisily in the library. He awoke to a vague realisation of tumult below.

Such was the morose condition of his mind as the result of his misfortune that at first not even the cries for help could interest him sufficiently to induce him to leave his bed. He knew that walking in his present state would be painful, and he declined to submit to any more pain just because some party unknown was apparently being murdered in his library. It was not until the shrill barking of the dog Aida penetrated right in among his nerve-centres and began to tie them into knots that he found himself compelled to descend. Even when he did so, it was in no spirit of kindness. He did not come to rescue anybody or to interfere between any murderer and his victim. He came in a fever of militant wrath to suppress Aida. On the threshold of the library, however, the genius, by treading on his gouty foot, had diverted his anger and caused it to become more general. He had not ceased to concentrate his venom on Aida. He wanted to assail everybody.

===Chinese===

彼得·佩特先生身上这场惊人的蜕变，是好几路原因合力促成的。头一条：杰里·米切尔遭了突然辞退，害他一连两天没能做他那副筋骨早已过惯的体育锻炼，身心一齐陷入了滞重易怒的状态。他闷头咀嚼自己命运的不公，嚼着嚼着，几乎把自己鼓捣到了要造反的地步。紧接着，又赶上他身子不爽时偶尔会招来的那一点痛风，来给他的烦恼火上浇油。他天性是个有耐性的人，这些磨难原也扛得住——只消赏他一夜囫囵觉。可偏偏他辗转反侧熬了两个钟头、刚刚合眼，书房里就开始大张旗鼓地出了动静。他悠悠醒转，恍恍惚惚地察觉楼下正闹成一片。

他的种种不幸把他熬成了这么一副阴郁心境，以至于起初连呼救声都提不起他足够的兴致，诱他下床。他知道以眼下的状态，走一趟路必然吃苦头，而他拒绝再受任何苦——哪怕有个什么来历不明的主儿分明正在他书房里遭人谋杀，也不干他的事。直到那条狗阿伊达的尖利吠声径直钻进他的神经中枢、把它们打成一个又一个结，他才发觉自己非下楼不可了。即便真下了楼，他也全无半点善心。他不是来搭救谁的，也不是来横插在任何凶手和他的猎物之间的。他是揣着一腔鏖战般的狂怒，下来镇压阿伊达的。然而在书房门槛上，那位天才一脚踩上他的痛风脚，把他的怒火拨转了方向，使之普及众生。他对阿伊达的毒意并未稍减，只是如今他要开火的对象，是屋里每一个人。

===Original===

“What’s the matter here?” he demanded, red-eyed. “Isn’t somebody going to tell me? Have I got to stop here all night? Who on earth is this?” He glared at Miss Trimble. “What’s she doing with that pistol?” He stamped incautiously with his bad foot, and emitted a dry howl of anguish.

“She is a detective, Peter,” said Mrs. Pett timidly.

“A detective? Why? Where did she come from?”

Miss Trimble took it upon herself to explain.

“ ’ter Pett, siz Pett sent f’r me t’ watch out so’s nobody kidnapped her son.”

===Chinese===

「这儿到底出了什么事？」他红着眼睛喝问。「就没有一个人肯告诉我？难道要我在这儿站一宿？这位究竟是谁？」他瞪着特琳布尔小姐。「她端着那把手枪干什么？」他一不小心，跺了跺那只坏脚，随即发出一声干巴巴的痛嚎。

「她是个侦探，彼得。」佩特太太怯生生地说。

「侦探？为什么？她打哪儿来的？」

特琳布尔小姐当仁不让，亲自出面解释。

「佩先生，佩太太派我来照应着，别叫人绑走她儿子。」

===Original===

“Oggie,” explained Mrs. Pett. “Miss Trimble was guarding darling Oggie.”

“Why?”

“To﻿—to prevent him being kidnapped, Peter.”

Mr. Pett glowered at the stout boy. Then his eye was attracted by the forlorn figure of Jerry Mitchell. He started.

“Was this fellow kidnapping the boy?” he asked.

“Sure,” said Miss Trimble. “Caught h’m with th’ goods. He w’s waiting outside there with a car. I held h’m and this other guy up w’th a gun and brought ’em back!”

===Chinese===

「奥吉。」佩特太太解释道。「特琳布尔小姐护的就是心肝宝贝奥吉。」

「为什么？」

「为——为了不叫他被人绑走，彼得。」

佩特先生怒目而视，瞪着那敦实的男孩。随后他的目光又被杰里·米切尔那副凄惶的身影勾了过去。他吃了一惊。

「刚才是这家伙在绑这孩子？」他问。

「没错儿。」特琳布尔小姐说。「人赃并获。他本来在外头备着一辆汽车等着。我拿枪把他跟另外那个家伙一并制住，押了回来！」

===Original===

“Jerry,” said Mr. Pett, “it wasn’t your fault that you didn’t bring it off, and I’m going to treat you right. You’d have done it if nobody had butted in to stop you. You’ll get the money to start that health-farm of yours all right. I’ll see to that. Now you run off to bed. There’s nothing to keep you here.”

“Say!” cried Miss Trimble, outraged. “D’ya mean t’ say y’ aren’t going t’ pros’cute? Why, aren’t I tell’ng y’ I caught h’m kidnapping th’ boy?”

“I told him to kidnap the boy!” snarled Mr. Pett.

“Peter!”

Mr. Pett looked like an undersized lion as he faced his wife. He bristled. The recollection of all that he had suffered from Ogden came to strengthen his determination.

===Chinese===

「杰里，」佩特先生说，「事情没办成，不怪你，我会好好待你。要不是有人横插一杠子拦着，你早办成了。你开你那座健身农庄的钱，一分少不了。这话我作数。现在你上床睡觉去。这儿没你的事了。」

「喂！」特琳布尔小姐怒不可遏地嚷道。「你的意思是，不起诉了？我不是告诉你了吗，我当场抓住他绑架这小子？」

「绑架这孩子是我指使的！」佩特先生咆哮道。

「彼得！」

佩特先生面对妻子，活像一头尺寸不足的狮子。他毛发倒竖。他从奥格登那里受过的一切气，此刻一齐涌上心头，给他撑硬了决心。

===Original===

“I’ve tried for two years to get you to send that boy to a good boarding-school, and you wouldn’t do it. I couldn’t stand having him loafing around the house any longer, so I told Jerry Mitchell to take him away to a friend of his who keeps a dogs’ hospital on Long Island and to tell his friend to hold him there till he got some sense into him. Well, you’ve spoiled that for the moment with your detectives, but it still looks good to me. I’ll give you a choice. You can either send that boy to a boarding-school next week, or he goes to Jerry Mitchell’s friend. I’m not going to have him in the house any longer, loafing in my chair and smoking my cigarettes. Which is it to be?”

“But, Peter!”

“Well?”

“If I send him to a school, he may be kidnapped.”

“Kidnapping can’t hurt him. It’s what he needs. And, anyway, if he is I’ll pay the bill and be glad to do it. Take him off to bed now. Tomorrow you can start looking up schools. Great Godfrey!” He hopped to the writing-desk and glared disgustedly at the debris on it. “Who’s been making this mess on my desk? It’s hard! It’s darned hard! The only room in the house that I ask to have for my own, where I can get a little peace, and I find it turned into a beer-garden, and coffee or some damned thing spilled all over my writing-desk!”

===Chinese===

「这两年我一直劝你把那孩子送进一所像样的寄宿学校，你偏不干。他成天在宅子里游手好闲，我再也忍不下去了，所以我让杰里·米切尔把他带去长岛他一个开狗医院的朋友那儿，交代他那位朋友把他扣在那儿，直到往他脑子里灌进点道理为止。得，你这会儿让你的侦探们把这事一时搅黄了，可在我看来这主意依然不赖。我给你个选择：要么下礼拜把那孩子送进寄宿学校，要么他就去杰里·米切尔的朋友那儿。他在这座宅子里我是再留不得了——赖在我的椅子里，抽我的香烟。你挑哪样？」

「可是，彼得！」

「怎么？」

「我要是送他上学，他可能被人绑走。」

「绑架坏不了他。那正是他缺的一课。再说了，真绑了，账单我来付，还巴不得付呢。这就带他上床去。明天你就可以着手查学校。老天爷！」他单脚蹦到写字台前，厌恶地瞪着台面上的狼藉。「是谁把我的桌子搅成这副德行？岂有此理！简直是岂有此理！这宅子里我唯一讨来归自己的一间屋，好图一点清静，结果一看，成了啤酒花园，咖啡还是什么见鬼的玩意儿泼了我一写字台！」

===Original===

“That isn’t coffee, Peter,” said Mrs. Pett mildly. This caveman whom she had married under the impression that he was a gentle domestic pet had taken all the spirit out of her. “It’s Willie’s explosive.”

“Willie’s explosive?”

“Lord Wisbeach﻿—I mean the man who pretended to be Lord Wisbeach﻿—dropped it there.”

“Dropped it there? Well, why didn’t it explode and blow the place to Hoboken, then?”

Mrs. Pett looked helplessly at Willie, who thrust his fingers into his mop of hair and rolled his eyes.

===Chinese===

「那不是咖啡，彼得。」佩特太太温和地说。这位穴居人——她当年误以为他是一头温驯的家庭宠物才嫁的——把她的一腔气性全泄光了。「那是威利的炸药。」

「威利的炸药？」

「威斯比奇勋爵——我是说那个冒充威斯比奇勋爵的人——把它掉在那儿的。」

「掉在那儿的？那它怎么没炸？怎么没把这地方轰上霍博肯去？」

佩特太太无助地望向威利。威利把手指插进他那蓬乱头发里，眼珠子直打转。

===Original===

“There was fortunately some slight miscalculation in my formula, uncle Peter,” he said. “I shall have to look into it tomorrow. Whether the trinitrotoluol﻿—”

Mr. Pett uttered a sharp howl. He beat the air with his clenched fists. He seemed to be having a brainstorm.

“Has this﻿—this *fish* been living on me all this time﻿—have I been supporting this﻿—this *buzzard* in luxury all these years while he fooled about with an explosive that won’t explode! He pointed an accusing finger at the inventor. Look into it tomorrow, will you? Yes, you can look into it tomorrow after six o’clock! Until then you’ll be working﻿—for the first time in your life﻿—working in my office, where you ought to have been all along.” He surveyed the crowded room belligerently. “Now perhaps you will all go back to bed and let people get a little sleep. Go home!” he said to the detective.

Miss Trimble stood her ground. She watched Mrs. Pett pass away with Ogden, and Willie Partridge head a stampede of geniuses, but she declined to move.

“Y’ gotta cut th’ rough stuff, ’ster Pett,” she said calmly. “I need my sleep, j’st ’s much ’s everyb’dy else, but I gotta stay here. There’s a lady c’ming right up in a taxi fr’m th’ Astorbilt to identify this gook. She’s after’m f’r something.”

===Chinese===

「万幸，我的配方里存在一点小小的误差，彼得叔叔。」他说。「我明天得研究研究。至于三硝基甲苯是不是——」

佩特先生发出一声凄厉的嚎叫。他抡起攥紧的双拳捶打空气。瞧那架势，像是怒火攻了心窍。

「这个——这条*鱼*，一直赖在我身上白吃白住——这些年我供养着锦衣玉食的，就是这——这只*秃鹫*？供养他瞎摆弄一种炸不响的炸药！」他伸出一根指头，控诉般地指向那位发明家。「明天研究，是吧？对，明天六点以后你尽管研究！在那之前你得干活——你这辈子头一回——在我的办公室里干活，你打一开始就该待在那儿。」他剑拔弩张地扫视满屋子的人。「现在，各位请回床上去，让人睡一小会儿觉行不行？回家去吧！」他对侦探说。

特琳布尔小姐岿然不动。她眼看着佩特太太带着奥格登退场，威利·帕特里奇领着一众天才夺路而散，自己却拒不移步。

「强硬的那一套就免了吧，佩先生。」她不动声色地说。「我跟旁人一样缺觉，可我必须守在这儿。有位太太正从阿斯托比尔特打出租车赶来，认一认这个家伙。她为着一桩什么事在追他。」

===Original===

“What! Skinner?”

“ ’s what he calls h’mself.”

“What’s he done?”

“I d’no. Th’ lady’ll tell us that.”

There was a violent ringing at the front door bell.

===Chinese===

「什么！斯金纳？」

「他自己这么称呼。」

「他干了什么？」

「不知道。那位太太会告诉咱们的。」

前门门铃猛地响了起来。

===Original===

“I guess that’s her,” said Miss Trimble. “Who’s going to let ’r in? I can’t go.”

“I will,” said Ann.

Mr. Pett regarded Mr. Crocker with affectionate encouragement.

“I don’t know what you’ve done, Skinner,” he said, “but I’ll stand by you. You’re the best fan I ever met, and if I can keep you out of the penitentiary, I will.”

“It isn’t the penitentiary!” said Mr. Crocker unhappily.

===Chinese===

「估计是她到了。」特琳布尔小姐说。「谁去放她进来？我去不了。」

「我去。」安说。

佩特先生望着克罗克先生，目光里满是慈爱的鼓励。

「斯金纳，你干了什么，我不知道，」他说，「可我站在你这边。你是我这辈子见过的最铁的球迷，只要能保你不蹲监狱，我一定保。」

「不是监狱的事！」克罗克先生愁眉苦脸地说。

===Original===

A tall, handsome, and determined-looking woman came into the room. She stood in the doorway, looking about her. Then her eyes rested on Mr. Crocker. For a moment she gazed incredulously at his discoloured face. She drew a little nearer, peering.

“D’yo ’dentify ’m, ma’am?” said Miss Trimble.

“Bingley!”

“Is ’t th’ guy y’ wanted?”

“It’s my husband!” said Mrs. Crocker.

===Chinese===

一个身材高挑、容貌端庄、神情果决的女人走进屋来。她立在门口，环顾四周。随后她的目光落在了克罗克先生身上。她难以置信地盯着他那张变了色的脸，端详片刻，又凑近了些细瞧。

「认得出他吗，太太？」特琳布尔小姐说。

「宾利！」

「就是你要找的那个家伙？」

「他是我丈夫！」克罗克太太说。

===Original===

“Y’ can’t arrest ’m f’r *that*!” said Miss Trimble disgustedly.

She thrust her revolver back into the hinterland of her costume.

“Guess I’ll be beatin’ it,” she said with a sombre frown. She was plainly in no sunny mood. “ ’f all th’ hunk jobs I was ever on, this is th’ hunkest. I’m told off ’t watch a gang of crooks, and after I’ve lost a night’s sleep doing it, it turns out ’t’s a nice, jolly fam’ly party!” She jerked her thumb towards Jimmy. “Say, this guy says he’s that guy’s son. I s’pose it’s all right?”

“That is my stepson, James Crocker.”

Ann uttered a little cry, but it was lost in Miss Trimble’s stupendous snort. The detective turned to the window.

“I guess I’ll beat ’t,” she observed caustically, “before it turns out that I’m y’r l’il daughter Genevieve.”

===Chinese===

「*这个*可没法逮捕他！」特琳布尔小姐一脸嫌弃地说。

她把左轮枪重新塞回她那身行头的大后方。

「我看我该开路了。」她说着，眉头阴沉沉地拧起。她显然没有半点晴朗的心情。「我这辈子接手过的差事里，顶蹩脚的就数这桩。派我来盯一帮贼，我熬掉一整夜瞌睡盯下来，到头来原来是一场和和美美的家庭聚会！」她朝吉米猛地一努拇指。「喂，这家伙说他是那家伙的儿子。总该没毛病吧？」

「那是我的继子，詹姆斯·克罗克。」

安发出一声低低的惊呼，可它淹没在特琳布尔小姐那声惊天动地的鼻息里。侦探转身朝窗口走去。

「我看我还是趁早开路，」她冷嘲热讽地说，「免得待会儿再查出，我是你们家的小千金吉纳维夫。」

## XXV / 二十五

Nearly Everybody Happy / 差不多皆大欢喜

===Original===

Mrs. Crocker turned to her husband.
“Well, Bingley?” she said, a steely tinkle in her voice.
“Well, Eugenia?” said Mr. Crocker.
A strange light was shining in Mr. Crocker’s mild eyes. He had seen a miracle happen that night. He had seen an even more formidable woman than his wife dominated by an even meeker man than himself, and he had been amazed and impressed by the spectacle. It had never even started to occur to him before, but apparently it could be done. A little resolution, a little determination﻿ ﻿… nothing more was needed. He looked at Mr. Pett. And yet Mr. Pett had crumpled up Eugenia’s sister with about three firm speeches. It could be done.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

克罗克太太转向丈夫。
「怎么样，宾利？」她说道，嗓音里带着钢铁相击般的叮当声。
「怎么样，尤金妮娅？」克罗克先生说。
克罗克先生那双温良的眼睛里，正闪耀着一道异样的光。这一晚，他亲眼见证了一桩奇迹。他看见一个比他妻子更厉害的女人，被一个比他自己更温良的男人镇得服服帖帖，这场面叫他又是惊叹又是心折。这事他此前连想都不曾往那上头想过，可看样子，居然是办得到的。只消一点决断，一点狠劲……此外再不需要什么。他望着佩特先生。而佩特先生，竟只用了大约三句斩钉截铁的话，就把尤金妮娅的姐姐收拾得偃旗息鼓。这是办得到的。……

===Original===

“What have you to say, Bingley?”
Mr. Crocker drew himself up.
“Just this!” he said. “I’m an American citizen, and the way I’ve figured it out is that my place is in America. It’s no good talking about it, Eugenia. I’m sorry if it upsets your plans, but I﻿—am﻿—not﻿—going﻿—back﻿—to﻿—London!” He eyed his speechless wife unflatteringly. “I’m going to stick on here and see the pennant race out. And after that I’m going to take in the World’s Series.”
Mrs. Crocker opened her mouth to speak, closed it, reopened it. Then she found that she had nothing to say.
“I hope you’ll be sensible, Eugenia, and stay on this side, and we can all be happy. I’m sorry to have to take this stand, but you tried me too high. You’re a woman, and you don’t know what it is to go five years without seeing a ball game; but take it from me it’s more than any real fan can stand. It nearly killed me, and I’m not going to risk it again. If Mr. Pett will keep me on as his butler, I’ll stay here in this house. If he won’t, I’ll get another job somewhere. But, whatever happens, I stick to this side!”

===Chinese===

「你有什么话说，宾利？」
克罗克先生挺了挺胸。
「就这一句！」他说。「我是个美国公民，我左算右算，结论是我的地盘就在美国。说再多也没用，尤金妮娅。要是这搅了你的计划，我很抱歉，可是我——不——回——伦——敦——了！」他毫不客气地打量着哑口无言的妻子。「我要在这儿扎下根来，把锦旗赛看到底。看完之后，我还要再把世界大赛也领略一番。」
克罗克太太张开嘴要说话，又闭上，再张开。随后她发现自己无话可说。
「我希望你放明白些，尤金妮娅，留在这边，那咱们就都能快快活活。我很抱歉不得不端出这个架势，可你把我逼得太狠了。你是个女人，不懂一连五年看不上一场球赛是什么滋味；可你信我一句，这超出了任何一个真球迷受得了的限度。那几乎要了我的命，我可不打算再冒一回险。佩特先生要是肯留我继续当他的管家，我就待在这座宅子里。他若不肯，我就到别处另谋一份差事。可不管怎么样，我死守这边！」

===Original===

Mr. Pett uttered a whoop of approval.
“There’s always been a place for you in my house, old man!” he cried. “When I get a butler who﻿—”
“But, Bingley! How can you be a butler?”
“You ought to watch him!” said Mr. Pett enthusiastically. “He’s a wonder! He can pull all the starchy stuff as if he’d lived with the Duke of Whoosis for the last forty years, and then go right off and fling a pop-bottle at an umpire! He’s all right!”

===Chinese===

佩特先生欢呼一声，以示赞许。
「我这宅子里一向留着你的位置，老伙计！」他嚷道。「想我居然请到一位管家，他还能——」
「可是，宾利！你怎么能当管家呢？」
「你真该瞧瞧他！」佩特先生热情地说。「他是个奇才！那套端足架子的功夫，他使得仿佛过去四十年一直在某某公爵府里当差，转过身来却又径直抄起汽水瓶掷向裁判！他实在没得说的！」

===Original===

The eulogy was wasted on Mrs. Crocker. She burst into tears. It was a new experience for her husband, and he watched her awkwardly, his resolute demeanour crumbling under this unexpected assault.
“Eugenia!”
Mrs. Crocker wiped her eyes.
“I can’t stand it!” she sobbed. “I’ve worked and worked all these years, and now, just as success has nearly come﻿—Bingley, *do* come back! It will only be for a little longer.”
Mr. Crocker stared.

===Chinese===

这番颂扬对克罗克太太全然白费。她放声大哭起来。这于她丈夫是平生头一遭，他手足无措地望着她，那一副决绝的气概，在这场出其不意的攻势之下节节瓦解。
「尤金妮娅！」
克罗克太太拭了拭眼睛。
「我受不住啦！」她抽噎道。「这些年我操劳了又操劳，如今眼看成功就要到手——宾利，你*千万*回来吧！用不了多久的，就再一小阵子。」
克罗克先生瞪眼瞧着。

===Original===

“A little longer? Why, that Lord Percy Whipple business﻿—I know you must have had excellent reasons for soaking him, Jimmy, but it did put the lid on it﻿—surely, after that Lord Percy affair there’s no chance﻿—?”
“There is! There is! It has made no difference at all! Lord Percy came to call next day with a black eye, poor boy!﻿—and said that James was a sportsman and that he wanted to know him better! He said he had never felt so drawn towards anyone in his life and he wanted him to show him how he made some blow which he called a right hook. The whole affair has simply endeared James to him, and Lady Corstorphine says that the Duke of Devizes read the account of the fight to the Premier that very evening and they both laughed till they nearly got apoplexy.”
Jimmy was deeply touched. He had not suspected such a sporting spirit in his antagonist.
“Percy’s all right,” he said enthusiastically. “Dad, you ought to go back. It’s only fair.”

===Chinese===

「就一小阵子？哎呀，那桩珀西·惠普尔勋爵的公案——吉米，我知道你揍他必定有极充分的理由，可那到底把盖子扣死了——经过了珀西勋爵那一场，想必再没有指望——？」
「有指望！有指望！那事一点儿也没碍着！珀西勋爵第二天就带着一只乌青眼登门来拜，可怜的孩子！——还说詹姆斯是个有体育精神的人，他想跟他进一步结交！他说他这辈子从没对哪个人这么心生亲近，还想请他演示一下他管它叫『右勾拳』的那一记是怎么打的。这一整场风波简直让詹姆斯在他心里愈发可亲可爱，科斯托芬夫人说，德维泽斯公爵当天晚上就把那场打斗的报道念给首相听，两人笑得险些中了风。」
吉米深受感动。他万不曾料到，他的对手竟有这般体育精神。
「珀西是个好样的，」他热诚地说。「爸，你该回去。这才公平。」

===Original===

“But, Jimmy! Surely *you* can understand? There’s only a game separating the Giants and the Phillies, with the Braves coming along just behind. And the season only half over!”
Mrs. Crocker looked imploringly at him.
“It will only be for a little while, Bingley. Lady Corstorphine, who has means of knowing, says that your name is certain to be in the next Honours List. After that you can come back as often as you like. We could spend the summer here and the winter in England, or whatever you pleased.”
Mr. Crocker capitulated.
“All right, Eugenia. I’ll come.”

===Chinese===

「可是，吉米！*你*总该明白吧？巨人队和费城人队如今只差一场，勇士队又在后头步步进逼。何况赛季才刚过半呢！」
克罗克太太哀恳地望着他。
「就再一小阵子，宾利。科斯托芬夫人有门路知道消息，她说你的名字笃定会列入下一份授勋名录。在那之后，你爱回来多少回就回来多少回。咱们可以夏天待在这边，冬天住在英格兰，或者随你怎么乐意。」
克罗克先生竖白旗了。
「好吧，尤金妮娅。我回去。」

===Original===

“Bingley! We shall have to go back by the next boat, dear. People are beginning to wonder where you are. I’ve told them that you are taking a rest in the country. But they will suspect something if you don’t come back at once.”
Mr. Crocker’s face wore a drawn look. He had never felt so attached to his wife as now, when she wept these unexpected tears and begged favours of him with that unfamiliar catch in her voice. On the other hand﻿ ﻿… A vision rose before him of the Polo Grounds on a warm afternoon.﻿ ﻿… He crushed it down.
“Very well,” he said.
Mr. Pett offered a word of consolation.

===Chinese===

「宾利！咱们得搭下一班船回去，亲爱的。人家都开始纳闷你上哪儿去了。我跟他们说你正在乡间静养。可你要是不马上回去，他们就要起疑心了。」
克罗克先生脸上带出一副愁苦之色。他从未像此刻这样眷恋自己的妻子——她淌着这出乎意料的泪，嗓音里带着那陌生的哽咽向他恳求。可另一方面……一幅幻象在他眼前升起：一个暖洋洋午后的波罗球场。……他把它按了下去。
「好吧。」他说。
佩特先生献上一句宽慰。

===Original===

“Maybe you’ll be able to run over for the World’s Series?”
Mr. Crocker’s face cleared.
“That’s true.”
“And I’ll cable you the scores every day, dad,” said Jimmy.
Mrs. Crocker looked at him with a touch of disapproval clouding the happiness of her face.

===Chinese===

「兴许你还能赶过来看世界大赛呢？」
克罗克先生的脸豁然开朗。
「这话不假。」
「而且我天天拍电报把比分报给你，爸。」吉米说。
克罗克太太望着他，一丝不以为然的阴云，罩上了她满脸的喜色。

===Original===

“Are you staying over here, James? There is no reason why you should not come back, too. If you make up your mind to change your habits﻿—”
“I have made up my mind to change them. But I’m going to do it in New York. Mr. Pett is going to give me a job in his office. I am going to start at the bottom and work my way still further down.”
Mr. Pett yapped with rapture. He was experiencing something of the emotion of the preacher at the camp-meeting who sees the Sinners’ Bench filling up. To have secured Willie Partridge, whom he intended to lead gradually into the realms of high finance by way of envelope-addressing, was much. But that Jimmy, with a choice in the matter, should have chosen the office filled him with such content that he only just stopped himself from dancing on his bad foot.
“Don’t worry about me, dad. I shall do wonders. It’s quite easy to make a large fortune. I watched uncle Pete in his office this morning, and all he does is sit at a mahogany table and tell the office-boy to tell callers that he has gone away for the day. I think I ought to rise to great heights in that branch of industry. From the little I have seen of it, it seems to have been made for me!”

===Chinese===

「你要留在这边吗，詹姆斯？你也完全可以一起回来。只要你下定决心改掉你那些积习——」
「我已经下定决心改了。不过我要在纽约改。佩特先生要在他公司里给我一个职位。我打算从最底层干起，再接再厉，一路向下。」
佩特先生欢喜得汪汪直叫。他此刻的心境，颇似野营布道会上那位眼见罪人席渐渐坐满的布道师。能把威利·帕特里奇招致麾下——他本打算领着这孩子从写信封入手，循序渐进，引入高级金融的堂奥——已是了不得的收获。而吉米明明去留可以自便，竟甘愿选中了公司，这份满足直灌他的心田，他好不容易才忍住，没用那只坏脚跳起舞来。
「别替我操心，爸。我会干出一番名堂来的。发大财实在容易。今天上午我在皮特叔叔的办公室里瞧着他办事，他成天干的无非是坐在红木大桌后头，吩咐办公室的小杂役去告诉来客，说他今儿已经出门去了。我想在这一行里，我理应扶摇直上。就我浅见所及，这一行简直像是专为我量身设的！」

## XXVI / 二十六

Everybody Happy / 皆大欢喜

===Original===

Jimmy looked at Ann. They were alone. Mr. Pett had gone back to bed, Mrs. Crocker to her hotel. Mr. Crocker was removing his makeup in his room. A silence had followed their departure.
“This is the end of a perfect day!” said Jimmy.
Ann took a step towards the door.
“Don’t go!”
Ann stopped.
“Mr. Crocker!” she said.

===Chinese===

吉米望着安。屋里只剩下他们两人。佩特先生回床上去了，克罗克太太回旅馆去了，克罗克先生正在自己房里卸妆。他们一走，随之而来的是一片寂静。
「这完美的一天，就此落幕了！」吉米说。
安朝门口迈了一步。
「别走！」
安停住了脚。
「克罗克先生！」她说。

===Original===

“Jimmy,” he corrected.
“Mr. Crocker!” repeated Ann firmly.
“Or Algernon, if you prefer it.”
“May I ask﻿—” Ann regarded him steadily. “May I ask.”
“Nearly always,” said Jimmy, “when people begin with that, they are going to say something unpleasant.”
“May I ask why you went to all this trouble to make a fool of me? Why could you not have told me who you were from the start?”

===Chinese===

「叫吉米。」他纠正道。
「克罗克先生！」安斩钉截铁地又说了一遍。
「你若更喜欢，叫阿尔杰农也行。」
「我可以请问——」安定定地注视着他。「我可以请问。」
「十之八九，」吉米说，「人们一拿这话起头，接下去准没什么中听的。」
「我可以请问：你何苦费这么大的周章来愚弄我？为什么不从一开始就告诉我你是谁？」

===Original===

“Have you forgotten all the harsh things you said to me from time to time about Jimmy Crocker? I thought that, if you knew who I was, you would have nothing more to do with me.”
“You were quite right.”
“Surely, though, you won’t let a thing that happened five years ago make so much difference?”
“I shall never forgive you!”
“And yet, a little while ago, when Willie’s bomb was about to go off, you flung yourself into my arms!”

===Chinese===

「你难道忘了，你从前时不时对我数落的那些关于吉米·克罗克的难听话？我原以为，你要是知道了我是什么人，就再也不会同我来往了。」
「你想得一点没错。」
「不过话说回来，五年前的一桩旧事，你总不至于让它起这么大的作用吧？」
「我永远不会原谅你！」
「可是方才，威利的炸弹眼看要炸的时候，你可是扑进了我怀里！」

===Original===

Ann’s face flamed.
“I lost my balance.”
“Why try to recover it?”
Ann bit her lip.
“You did a cruel, heartless thing. What does it matter how long ago it was? If you were capable of it then﻿—”

===Chinese===

安的脸腾地烧红了。
「我一时没站稳。」
「那又何必非要站稳呢？」
安咬住了嘴唇。
「你干下的是一件残酷无情的事。事情隔了多久又有什么要紧？你当时既然干得出来——」

===Original===

“Be reasonable. Don’t you admit the possibility of reformation? Take your own case. Five years ago you were a minor poetess. Now you are an amateur kidnapper﻿—a bright, lovable girl at whose approach people lock up their children and sit on the key. As for me, five years ago I was a heartless brute. Now I am a sober serious businessman, specially called in by your uncle to help jack up his tottering firm. Why not bury the dead past? Besides﻿—I don’t want to praise myself, I just want to call your attention to it﻿—think what I have done for you. You admitted yourself that it was my influence that had revolutionised your character. But for me, you would now be doing worse than write poetry. You would be writing *vers libre*. I saved you from that. And you spurn me!”
“I hate you!” said Ann.
Jimmy went to the writing-desk and took up a small book.
“Put that down!”
“I just wanted to read you ‘Love’s Funeral’! It illustrates my point. Think of yourself as you are now, and remember that it is I who am responsible for the improvement. Here we are. ‘Love’s Funeral’. ‘My heart is dead.﻿ ﻿…’ ”

===Chinese===

「讲点道理嘛。你难道不承认人有改过自新的可能？就拿你自己的情形来说：五年前，你是个无足轻重的小诗人；如今你成了业余绑匪——一个聪明可爱、人还未到家家便锁起孩子、坐上钥匙的姑娘。再说我：五年前，我是个没心肝的恶棍；如今我是个清醒严肃的生意人，还是你叔叔特意请来、给那摇摇欲坠的公司支千斤顶的。何不把死去的往事埋葬了呢？再者——我不想自吹自擂，只想请你留意这个事实——想想我为你做了多少。你自己也承认过，正是我的影响使你改头换面。倘若没有我，你如今干的事会比写诗更糟——你会去写*自由体诗*。是我把你从那深渊里救出来的。而你竟把我一脚踢开！」
「我恨你！」安说。
吉米走到写字台前，拿起一本小书。
「把书放下！」
「我不过想给你念念《爱的葬礼》！它正好印证我的论点。想想你如今这副模样，并且记住，这份长进全亏了我。找着了。《爱的葬礼》。『我心已死。……』」

===Original===

Ann snatched the book from his hands and flung it away. It soared up, clearing the gallery rails, and fell with a thud on the gallery floor. She stood facing him with sparkling eyes. Then she moved away.
“I beg your pardon,” she said stiffly. “I lost my temper.”
“It’s your hair,” said Jimmy soothingly. “You’re bound to be quick-tempered with hair of that glorious red shade. You must marry some nice, determined fellow, blue-eyed, dark-haired, clean-shaven, about five foot eleven, with a future in business. He will keep you in order.”
“Mr. Crocker!”
“Gently, of course. Kindly﻿—lovingly. The velvet thingummy rather than the iron what’s-its-name. But nevertheless firmly.”
Ann was at the door.

===Chinese===

安一把从他手里夺过书，甩了出去。那书腾空飞起，越过楼廊栏杆，啪的一声闷响落在楼廊地板上。她面对着他站定，双眼灼灼发亮。随后她走开了。
「请原谅，」她生硬地说。「我失态了。」
「这得怪你的头发，」吉米抚慰道。「顶着一头那样红得光彩夺目的头发，脾气急是在所难免的。你一定得嫁一个正派而有决断的人：蓝眼睛，黑头发，脸刮得干干净净，身高五英尺十一英寸上下，在商界大有前程。他会把你管教得服服帖帖。」
「克罗克先生！」
「当然是温柔地管。和蔼地——怜爱地。要天鹅绒的那什么，不要铁打的那玩意儿。不过仍旧要坚定不移。」
安已经到了门口。

===Original===

“To a girl with your ardent nature someone with whom you can quarrel is an absolute necessity of life. You and I are affinities. Ours will be an ideally happy marriage. You would be miserable if you had to go through life with a human doormat with ‘Welcome’ written on him. You want someone made of sterner stuff. You want, as it were, a sparring-partner, someone with whom you can quarrel happily with the certain knowledge that he will not curl up in a ball for you to kick, but will be there with the return wallop. I may have my faults﻿—” He paused expectantly. Ann remained silent. “No, no!” he went on. “But I am such a man. Brisk give-and-take is the foundation of the happy marriage. Do you remember that beautiful line of Tennyson’s﻿—‘We fell out, my wife and I’? It always conjures up for me a vision of wonderful domestic happiness. I seem to see us in our old age, you on one side of the radiator, I on the other, warming our old limbs and thinking up snappy stuff to hand to each other﻿—sweethearts still! If I were to go out of your life now, you would be miserable. You would have nobody to quarrel with. You would be in the position of the female jaguar of the Indian jungle, who, as you doubtless know, expresses her affection for her mate by biting him shrewdly in the fleshy part of the leg, if she should snap sideways one day and find nothing there.”
Of all the things which Ann had been trying to say during this discourse, only one succeeded in finding expression. To her mortification, it was the only weak one in the collection.
“Are you asking me to marry you?”
“I am.”
“I won’t!”
“You think so now, because I am not appearing at my best. You see me nervous, diffident, tongue-tied. All this will wear off, however, and you will be surprised and delighted as you begin to understand my true self. Beneath the surface﻿—I speak conservatively﻿—I am a corker!”

===Chinese===

「以你这样热烈的天性，一个能跟你吵架的人实在是生活的绝对必需品。你我二人正是天造地设的一对。我们的婚姻将是一场理想的美满婚姻。倘若你得跟一个身上印着『欢迎光临』字样的人形门垫厮守一生，那你才叫可怜。你要的是一个用更硬扎的材料造就的人。可以说，你要的是一位陪练搭档，一个能让你开开心心吵架的对象，而且你尽可放心：他绝不会蜷缩成一团任你踢，他必定原样奉还一记重拳。我或许自有种种缺点——」他满怀期待地停了停。安一声不吭。「不，不！」他继续说道。「但我正是这样一个人。爽利的你来我往，正是美满婚姻的地基。你可记得丁尼生那行美妙的诗——『我们吵翻了，我和我妻子』？它总在我心头唤起一幅妙不可言的家庭幸福图景。我仿佛看见我们到了垂暮之年，你坐在暖气片的这一边，我坐在那一边，烘着我们老迈的四肢，各自琢磨些俏皮话递给对方——依旧是一对恩爱情侣！若是我此刻从你的生活里退场，你可就要痛苦了。你将无人可吵。你的处境将等同印度丛林中的母美洲豹——想必你知道，她向配偶表达爱意的方式，是照着他腿上多肉的地方狠狠咬上一口——倘若有一天她侧头咬去，却发现那里空空如也。」
在他发表这番宏论期间，安想说的许多话里，只有一句成功地冲出了口。令她羞恼的是，那一句恰是全部存货中最不济的一件。
「你这是在向我求婚吗？」
「正是。」
「我不答应！」
「你现在这样想，是因为我未将最佳状态示人。你眼里的我紧张、怯场、张口结舌。不过这一切都会消退，等你开始领会真正的我时，你自会又惊又喜。在这层皮相底下——我说得保守些——我可是个响当当的妙人！」

===Original===

The door banged behind Ann. Jimmy found himself alone. He walked thoughtfully to Mr. Pett’s armchair and sat down. There was a feeling of desolation upon him. He lit a cigarette and began to smoke pensively. What a fool he had been to talk like that! What girl of spirit could possibly stand it? If ever there had been a time for being soothing and serious and pleading, it had been these last few minutes. And he talked like that!
Ten minutes passed. Jimmy sprang from his chair. He thought he had heard a footstep. He flung the door open. The passage was empty. He returned miserably to his chair. Of course she had not come back. Why should she?
A voice spoke.
“Jimmy!”
He leaped up again, and looked wildly round. Then he looked up. Ann was leaning over the gallery rail.

===Chinese===

门在安身后砰然关上。吉米发觉自己孤零零一人。他心事重重地踱到佩特先生的扶手椅前，坐了下来。一阵凄凉之感笼罩着他。他点燃一支香烟，开始闷闷地抽。方才那样讲话，他真是头号蠢驴！稍有几分志气的姑娘怎么受得了？倘若世上有过一个时刻，最该温存体贴、一本正经、低声下气，那便是方才那几分钟。而他偏偏那样讲话！
十分钟过去了。吉米从椅子里蹦起来。他觉得自己听见了脚步声。他猛地推开门。过道里空无一人。他灰溜溜地回到椅子上。她当然没有回来。她凭什么回来？
一个声音响了。
「吉米！」
他又一跃而起，慌乱地环顾四周。随后他抬起头来。安正俯身倚在楼廊栏杆上。

===Original===

“Jimmy, I’ve been thinking it over. There’s something I want to ask you. Do you admit that you behaved abominably five years ago?”
“Yes!” shouted Jimmy.
“And that you’ve been behaving just as badly ever since?”
“Yes!”
“And that you are really a pretty awful sort of person?”
“Yes!”

===Chinese===

「吉米，我把这事通盘想过了。有个问题要问你。你承不承认，五年前你的行为恶劣透顶？」
「承认！」吉米高声道。
「承不承认，打那以后你一直表现得同样恶劣？」
「承认！」
「承不承认，你实在是个糟糕透顶的家伙？」
「承认！」

===Original===

“Then it’s all right. You deserve it!”
“Deserve it?”
“Deserve to marry a girl like me. I was worried about it, but now I see that it’s the only punishment bad enough for you!” She raised her arm.
“Here’s the dead past, Jimmy! Go and bury it! Good night!”
A small book fell squashily at Jimmy’s feet. He regarded it dully for a moment. Then, with a wild yell which penetrated even to Mr. Pett’s bedroom and woke that sufferer just as he was dropping off to sleep for the third time that night he bounded for the gallery stairs.
At the further end of the gallery a musical laugh sounded, and a door closed. Ann had gone.

===Chinese===

「那就妥了。你罪有应得！」
「罪有应得？」
「该当娶一个我这样的姑娘。我先前还为这事担忧来着，如今可看明白了：这是唯一罚得够重、配得上你的惩罚！」她扬起手臂。
「这就是那死去的往事，吉米！拿去埋了吧！晚安！」
一本小书啪唧一声落在吉米脚边。他怔怔地望了它片刻。随后，他发出一声狂呼，朝楼廊楼梯纵身奔去——那声狂呼甚至直透佩特先生的卧室，在那位受害者当夜第三次正要朦胧入睡之际将他惊醒。
楼廊尽头响起一串音乐般的笑声，一扇门随即关上。安走了。
